# Proposal: Settle checkpoint object-store ownership at the gateway-side ArtifactStore boundary, make the chunked-object model the single upload path, ship the agent-pod eviction trigger, and wire produce → store → restore end to end (T-4.4.12, T-4.4.13, T-4.4.14, T-4.4.16, T-4.4.20, T-4.2.1, T-4.6.4, T-JRN.9)

- **Status:** **WITHDRAWN (2026-07-14). Superseded by the presigned-capability design. Retained for the record; do not implement.**
- **Date:** 2026-07-13.

> **Why this was withdrawn.** The proposal rests on the premise that the spec's assignment of the checkpoint `PutObject` calls to the pod-side adapter is a defect, on the grounds that the agent pod has no object-store egress and no credential mechanism. Both halves of that premise are false, and the review loop never re-examined them because a proposal's premises are an input to adversarial review rather than a target of it.
>
> The capacity model was built for pod-side upload. `spec/17_deployment-topology.md` §17.8.2 budgets MinIO throughput for "a MinIO I/O burst of up to 400 simultaneous uploads" from agent pods during drain (`spec/10_gateway-internals.md:167`), and `spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:35` names "the adapter [supplying] the tar size from the workspace-size probe before upload begins" as the size source for the checkpoint quota reservation. The spec's storage quota, its MinIO sizing, and its drain arithmetic all assume the pod holds the socket.
>
> The credential mechanism already admits non-LLM providers. `spec/04_system-components.md:1167` defines `vcs.<provider>.{read|write}` lease scopes, so the §4.9 lease is not LLM-only and an object-store credential is not a new class of thing.
>
> Routing checkpoint bytes through the gateway therefore does not correct a defect; it relocates a data plane that the deployment topology was sized around, and it re-sizes every gateway capacity figure in §17 as a side effect. The successor design keeps the bytes on the pod-to-MinIO path the spec already assumes and moves only the *authority* to the gateway: the gateway runs the tenant-prefix check, the quota reservation, the tier guard, and the T4 SSE-KMS key selection, and then mints a presigned per-chunk capability bound by signature to one object key, one method, and one expiry. The pod holds a capability rather than a credential, and no payload byte crosses the gateway.
>
> What survives from this document and carries into the successor: the partial-manifest schema gap against §10.1, gateway-side `checkpoint_id` minting at intent-row INSERT, wiring the adapter's produce and restore handlers in `cmd/lenny-adapter`, the §6.2 recycle unblock, and the finding that `manifest_reason = 'terminated_during_resume'` is unsatisfiable because the resume path is read-then-delete. The agent-pod eviction trigger and its prerequisites move to their own proposal.
- **Scope:** A spec-and-code reconciliation of the §4.4 checkpoint pipeline. The spec assigns the checkpoint `PutObject` / `DeleteObject` / `AbortMultipartUpload` calls to the agent pod in five places while §13 forbids the agent pod every credential and every network path those calls require, and the pipeline is unwired in every production binary: the adapter's `Checkpoint` and `Resume` handlers return `codes.Unimplemented`, the gateway drives no eviction checkpoint for a terminating agent pod, the partial-manifest table has no producer, and the eviction-fallback writer has no call site. The change reassigns every object-store verb on the checkpoint path to the gateway-side `ArtifactStore` (**S1**), makes the chunked-object model the single upload path for every trigger with `partial` demoted to a column (**S2**), reassigns the eviction-checkpoint trigger to the gateway, ships the agent-pod eviction trigger over a termination signal from the agent pod's existing preStop hook, and adds the success finalisation arm (**S3**), removes the phantom `terminated_during_resume` manifest producer the spec mandates but its own cleanup rules make impossible (**S4**), widens `Checkpoint` to server-streaming and `Resume` to client-streaming on the existing `Adapter` service and adds one `NotifyPodTerminating` RPC to the existing `GatewayControl` service (**P1**), implements the adapter-side produce and restore handlers and deletes the nil sink/source/aborter seam (**C1**), adds the gateway-side upload and restore drivers behind the `ArtifactStore` (**C2**), replaces `session_partial_checkpoint_manifest` with the unified `checkpoint_manifest` table (**C4**), builds the eviction-trigger seam and deletes the phantom `triggerForSource` (**C5**), makes the resume classification an output of the restore driver (**C6**), and wires the agent-pod termination signal, the §4.4 eviction-fallback writer, the `spec/04:264` resume precedence rule, and the `session_eviction_state` supersede (**C7**). It touches `spec/04_system-components.md`, `spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md`, `spec/06_warm-pod-model.md`, `spec/07_session-lifecycle.md`, `spec/10_gateway-internals.md`, `spec/11_policy-and-controls.md`, `spec/12_storage-architecture.md`, `spec/15_external-api-surface.md`, `spec/16_observability.md`, `spec/17_deployment-topology.md`, `spec/18_build-sequence.md`, and `spec/19_resolved-decisions.md`; `schemas/lenny-adapter.proto`, `schemas/lifecycle-events.schema.json`, `schemas/examples/`, and the generated `pkg/proto/adapter/v1`; the `docs/` reference, operator-guide, runbook, client-guide, runtime-author-guide, getting-started, and api pages the sweep in §11 enumerates; `charts/lenny` values, CRDs, and the MinIO lifecycle job; `TESTING.md`; `pkg/adapter`, `cmd/lenny-adapter`, `pkg/admission/pool_config_validator`, `pkg/admission/webhook`, `pkg/alerting/rules`, `pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1`, `pkg/blobstore`, `pkg/checkpoint`, `pkg/controller/poolscaling`, `pkg/controller/sandbox`, `pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec`, `pkg/gateway/checkpoint`, `pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier`, `pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination`, `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle`, `pkg/gateway/runtime/poolstore`, `pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore`, `pkg/gateway/sessionserver`, `pkg/gateway/storage/evictionfallback`, `pkg/gateway/storage/evictionstatestore`, `pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore`, `pkg/gateway/storage/retentiongc`, `pkg/observability/metrics`, `cmd/lenny-gateway`, and `migrations/`. The three client SDK session types gain the `failureReason` field the §15.1 envelope gains, and no file under `sdks/runtime/` changes. It adds no NetworkPolicy rule, no MinIO ingress allow, no credential-lease provider, and no object-store client in the agent pod.

This document stages the proposed spec, proto, code, and test changes. It does not modify any spec, code, or doc file. Apply the changes in the "Proposed changes" section after sign-off.

## 1. Problem

`spec/04_system-components.md` §4.4 states that a session survives the loss of its pod. The checkpoint pipeline that would implement that behavior is unwired in every production binary, and the spec contradicts itself about which process issues the object-store calls.

### (A) The spec puts the S3 client inside the agent pod, and §13 forbids it

Five normative or descriptive passages place the object-store calls in the agent pod:

- `spec/10_gateway-internals.md:128`: "The partial-manifest flow MUST write the manifest row to Postgres **before** the adapter issues the first chunk `PutObject`."
- `spec/10_gateway-internals.md:131`: "Only after the intent-row INSERT commits does the gateway instruct the adapter to begin issuing single-part `PutObject` calls under `partial_object_key_prefix`."
- `spec/10_gateway-internals.md:139`: "The adapter uploads the workspace tar stream in fixed-size chunks."
- `spec/04_system-components.md:249`: "the adapter MUST delete any partially uploaded MinIO objects for that checkpoint attempt. For multipart uploads, the adapter calls the S3 `AbortMultipartUpload` API; for single-part uploads that may have completed server-side, the adapter issues a `DeleteObject` call."
- `spec/12_storage-architecture.md:294`: "If MinIO is unreachable during a checkpoint upload, the adapter retries with exponential backoff."

The agent pod cannot issue any of these calls. `spec/13_security-model.md:84-113` (`allow-pod-egress-base`) permits agent-namespace egress to the gateway on TCP 50051 and to CoreDNS on 53. `spec/13_security-model.md:220` admits only the gateway, `lenny-ops`, and `lenny-backup` pods to MinIO on TCP 9443. `spec/13_security-model.md:11-13` forbids standing credentials in the agent pod and pins file delivery to "Gateway-mediated only". The §4.9 credential-lease provider set (`spec/04_system-components.md:1117-1118`) is LLM-only, and `presign` appears nowhere in `spec/`.

A pod-side S3 client would also relocate two invariants the spec pins at the store boundary. `spec/12_storage-architecture.md:307` states that "no S3 call is issued unless the caller's authenticated `tenant_id` matches the path prefix. This validation lives at the interface boundary, not in individual callers", restated at `spec/04_system-components.md:310`. `spec/12_storage-architecture.md:309-315` mandates fail-closed per-tenant T4 SSE-KMS key selection with no fallback to the deployment-wide key. Both are implemented in `pkg/blobstore` (`ErrCrossTenant` at `pkg/blobstore/blobstore.go:64`, `ErrClassificationControlViolation` at `:80`, `TenantScoped.check` at `:851`), which is linked only into `cmd/lenny-gateway`. `pkg/adapter` and `cmd/lenny-adapter` reference no blobstore and no S3 client.

### (B) The produce and restore handlers are unimplemented in every shipped binary

`adapter.Server.Checkpoints` (`pkg/adapter/server.go:216`) and `Server.Restorer` (`:259`) are optional fields. `adapter.New` (`pkg/adapter/server.go:398`) leaves them nil and `cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go` never assigns them, so `pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:161-168` and `pkg/adapter/resume.go:44-47` return `codes.Unimplemented` in production. The only implementations of `CheckpointSink`, `CheckpointSource`, and `CheckpointAborter` are test fakes (T-4.4.12, T-4.4.13).

### (C) The gateway selects no eviction trigger

`pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:343-352` (`triggerForSource`) returns `checkpoint.TriggerPeriodic` from all three arms, and its doc comment at `:337-342` defers to a `snapshotWithTrigger` whose only occurrence in the repository is that comment. `checkpoint.TriggerEviction` and `checkpoint.TriggerPreScaleDown` (`pkg/checkpoint/checkpoint.go:84-86`) and `checkpoint.RetryBudgetFor` (`:118-122`) have no non-test caller, so `lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds{trigger="eviction"}` is unreachable (T-4.4.14, T-4.4.20).

### (D) The partial-manifest table has no producer and does not match §10.1

`session_partial_checkpoint_manifest` (migration `0062`, index `0150`) carries seven columns against the set `spec/10_gateway-internals.md:141` requires. Its own migration header (`0062 up.sql:14-22`) records that the §10.1 columns are "deliberately omitted". It is named differently from the single unified `checkpoint_manifest` table the spec assumes (`spec/10_gateway-internals.md:147`, `:151`, `:155`), and its unique index is scoped `(tenant_id, session_id)` with a `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` predicate rather than the spec's `(session_id, slot_id) WHERE partial = TRUE`. It has a wired consumer (`PartialCleaner`, `cmd/lenny-gateway/sessionsrv.go:217`, itself constructed with a nil chunk deleter per T-4.4.16) and no producer. `manifest_reason` exists nowhere in the tree, so the `terminated_during_resume` row that `spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:214` and `spec/04_system-components.md:157` mandate is never written (T-4.2.1).

### (E) The eviction-fallback writer and its resume-side lookup are both dormant

`pkg/gateway/storage/evictionfallback` (table `session_eviction_state`, migration `0045`) has no non-test caller. Its resume-side consumer is guarded by `if s.evictionStateLookup != nil` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:3215`) and `sessionserver.Options.EvictionStateLookup` (`sessionserver.go:1256`) is never assigned in `cmd/`, so `classifyResume` returns `ResumeFull` unconditionally, as its own comment at `start.go:3206` states. `Options.PartialManifestLookup` (consumed at `start.go:3221`) is nil for the same reason. A MinIO outage during eviction therefore loses the session outright rather than degrading to the `conversation_only` resume `spec/04_system-components.md:264` and `spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:138` require.

### (F) Blast radius of the unimplemented Checkpoint

Because `Checkpoint` returns `Unimplemented`, `sealWorkspace` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:74`) burns its full `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` window (300s, `seal.go:21`) on a permanent error, `failWorkspaceSeal` marks the session `failed` with reason `workspace_seal_timeout`, and `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/binder.go:1829` gates recycling on `disposition != dispositionFailed`. §6.2 occupancy-zero recycling is therefore unreachable on single-session recycling pools.

### (G) The restore side already places the object-store calls in the gateway

The gateway is already the object-store caller on the restore side: `spec/10_gateway-internals.md:155` has the coordinator list, `GetObject`, and "Stream the chunks to the adapter", and `spec/04_system-components.md:237` has the gateway issue the cleanup `DeleteObject` calls. The gateway already holds the `ArtifactStore` (`cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1035` `blobproviderflags.Resolve`, `:1062` `var blobs blobstore.Store`) with the SSE-KMS resolver wired, and it already dials every agent pod over the permitted gateway→pod 50051 channel (`spec/13_security-model.md:50`, `:216`; `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1838` `DialAdapter`). No streaming byte channel exists between the adapter and the gateway.

## 2. Decisions

- **The gateway is the only object-store caller, in the spec and in code.** Every `PutObject`, `GetObject`, `DeleteObject`, and `AbortMultipartUpload` verb on the checkpoint path moves behind the gateway-side `ArtifactStore`, where `spec/12_storage-architecture.md:307` and `:309-315` already pin tenant-prefix validation and fail-closed T4 SSE-KMS key selection. The adapter links no S3 client, holds no object-store credentials, and needs no new egress. `spec/13_security-model.md:11-13` stands unchanged: no NetworkPolicy edit, no MinIO ingress allow, and no new §4.9 credential-lease provider.
- **Pod-side scoped credentials and presigned URLs are rejected.** A per-pod STS credential requires a new §4.9 lease provider, an agent→MinIO egress rule, and a MinIO ingress allow. Presigned URLs require the same two network changes and relocate the tenant-prefix and T4 SSE-KMS enforcement out of the `ArtifactStore` boundary into URL minting, which `spec/12_storage-architecture.md:307` forbids in terms ("not in individual callers"). `presign` appears nowhere in `spec/` and has no implementation in `pkg/`. Neither alternative provides anything the gateway-mediated path lacks.
- **The byte channel reuses the existing gateway→adapter `Adapter` service.** `Checkpoint` becomes server-streaming (adapter → gateway archive frames) and `Resume` becomes client-streaming (gateway → adapter checkpoint frames). The gateway is already the gRPC client on both, over the channel `spec/13_security-model.md:50` and `:216` permit and `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1838` already dials. A new adapter→gateway upload RPC on `GatewayControl` (`schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:215`) is rejected: it inverts the driver (`spec/10_gateway-internals.md:130` requires the gateway to mint `checkpoint_id` and commit the intent row before the first chunk) and adds a second byte path for one concern.
- **The chunked-object model becomes the single upload path for every checkpoint trigger** (periodic, sealed, pre-scale-down, eviction, and barrier flush). `partial` stops being a separate pipeline and becomes a column: a stream whose terminal `CheckpointSummary` arrives inside its deadline, with every declared byte committed, finalises with `partial = false`, and one cut short or closed without a summary finalises with `partial = true`. This settles the full-checkpoint object-key layout, collapses full-restore and partial-reassembly into the single concatenate-then-decompress pipeline `spec/10_gateway-internals.md:155` already specifies, and bounds gateway heap to one in-flight chunk (16 MiB) even though `blobstore.Store.Put` buffers its body with `io.ReadAll` (`pkg/blobstore/s3/s3.go:231`, `pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:321`). `pkg/blobstore` therefore needs no streaming or multipart `Put`.
- **Every chunk object is one catalogued `artifact_store` row.** Production hands the gateway the `cataloging` decorator (`cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1085-1090`), whose `Put` inserts one `artifact_store` row per object key at Put time (`pkg/blobstore/cataloging/cataloging.go:143-171`) and whose `SoftDelete` transitions that row and releases the tenant's quota bytes (`:240-258`). Checkpoint chunks account for themselves through the same catalog as every other artifact, so `spec/10_gateway-internals.md:135`'s carve-out that keeps partial-chunk bytes outside `artifact_store` is removed rather than extended, and no row is ever keyed on a bare prefix (a prefix does not parse as a `lenny-blob://` URI, and the `cataloging` GC sweeps skip rows whose URI fails `ParseURI`, `cataloging.go:386-391`).
- **`chunk_count` is reserved before the chunk it names is committed.** Dropping the prefix listing makes the counter the sole enumeration source for restore, the abort sweep, the supersede delete, the latest-2 rotation, the TTL GC, and the §12.5 backstop. The gateway therefore commits the counter `UPDATE` for index `n` before it issues the `PutObject` for index `n`, so the derived key set `[0, chunk_count)` is always a superset of the committed objects and a `SoftDelete` on a reserved-but-uncommitted key is a no-op (`miniostore.Store.SoftDelete` returns nil on a missing object, `pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:788-793`). The fire-and-forget counter framing at `spec/10_gateway-internals.md:131` was sound only while cleanup discovered chunks by `ListObjectsV2`, and it is rewritten by S1.
- **At most one checkpoint attempt per `(session_id, slot_id)` commits chunks, and the supersede transaction is fenced on both generation and settlement.** `Checkpointer.snapshot` (`pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:236`) holds no per-session lock and gates only on the replica-local `Registry` binding (`:237-239`), so the periodic ticker (`:122-133`), `Seal` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:91`), the barrier dispatcher (`pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier/barrier.go:184`), and the eviction path can overlap on one session. Every one of those producers, including the barrier dispatcher, mints its `checkpoint_id`, supersedes, and commits its intent row inside a per-`(session_id, slot_id)` single-flight lock that the upload driver owns, so same-generation attempts on one replica are ordered by the lock rather than by the generation fence, which admits equality. The lock preempts: a producer that finds an attempt in flight for the pair cancels that attempt's upload context and waits for it to settle its finalisation before it mints, so the row it supersedes is always a row whose attempt has stopped running. The lock is process-local while the manifest row and the chunk objects are shared across replicas, and the spec explicitly models a draining or lease-expired replica still dispatching barriers and still writing (`spec/10_gateway-internals.md:165`), so two Postgres terms carry the cross-replica invariant. The supersede transaction (`spec/10:137`) carries one predicate, stated identically everywhere it appears:

  ```
  coordination_generation < $new_generation
  OR (coordination_generation = $new_generation
      AND (manifest_reason IS NOT NULL OR checkpoint_timeout_at < now()))
  ```

  An attempt that finds an active row at a **higher** generation aborts rather than clobbering it, which is the CPS-006 fence: a stale coordinator's write must never beat a fenced newer writer. An attempt at a **strictly higher** generation supersedes the row unconditionally, including a row whose upload is still running, because the §10.1 handoff has already fenced that upload's writer at the pod (`spec/10:32-37`, whose gate P1 puts on `Checkpoint` and `Resume` and C1 enforces, since the shipped fence covers `CheckpointBarrier` alone): the displaced coordinator's remaining chunk `Put`s are wasted, its counter `UPDATE`s and its finalising `UPDATE` no-op under the `deleted_at IS NULL` guard and abort the attempt (S3), and its late chunks are reachable from the soft-deleted row through the widened §12.5 backstop. Without that arm the C7 termination handler and the `spec/10:165` replacement coordinator could never supersede the row of the coordinator they replaced, and every such session would take the minimal-state fallback. At **equal** generation the settled-row term `(manifest_reason IS NOT NULL OR checkpoint_timeout_at < now())` decides. `manifest_reason` is NULL on an intent row and non-NULL on a finalised partial row (C4), so the first arm admits a row whose attempt has already stopped running, including one a lock preemption or an early stream truncation cut short well inside its own deadline, and the second arm admits a row whose attempt has passed its own deadline and is therefore abandoned. A same-generation row that carries no `manifest_reason` and whose deadline has not elapsed is an upload still running on another replica behind no fence, is never superseded, and aborts the attempt that would have superseded it. Keying the term on the recorded deadline alone would not separate the two states, because nothing rewrites `checkpoint_timeout_at` when an attempt settles early, so a preempted or truncated attempt would leave an active row that no later attempt could supersede and every drain-time barrier flush and eviction checkpoint would abort on the guard. `partial_manifest_active_uniq` (C4) is the third half of the guard.
- **The §11.2 storage-quota reservation stays at the gateway and keeps the adapter's probe as its size input.** `spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:35` sits inside §11.2 "Budgets and Quotas" (`spec/11:15`), which is the section number `spec/12:321` cross-references; §11.1 is "Admission and Fairness" (`spec/11:3`) and carries no quota rule. `spec/11:35` mandates an atomic Redis reservation before any checkpoint byte reaches MinIO and names the adapter's workspace-size probe as the size source for checkpoint writes. The adapter still runs the probe (`spec/04_system-components.md:255`), and it now reports the probed size to the gateway in the leading `CheckpointStart` frame so the gateway can `Reserve` before the first chunk `Put` (`pkg/gateway/quota/storagequota/failover.go:91`, already consumed by `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload.go:724`). The same `spec/11:35` paragraph mandates a hard stream cap alongside the reservation, because a declared size is a self-report and `spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:41` refuses to let a pod's self-report drive quota accounting. The gateway therefore wraps the inbound archive stream in an `io.LimitedReader` at the tenant's remaining quota headroom and aborts with `STORAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` when the adapter streams past it, exactly as the workspace-upload path already does (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload.go:337`, `:442`). `probed_bytes` sizes the reservation; the cap enforces the quota.
- **`checkpoint_id` is minted by the gateway at intent-row INSERT.** `spec/10_gateway-internals.md:130` already requires this, but `schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:1026` has the adapter return it and `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:265` stores the adapter's value. An adapter-minted id cannot exist before the intent row, and the intent row before the first chunk is the ordering that closes the chunk-orphan window.
- **Atomicity is restated as a state transition rather than a write ordering.** §4.4's "the metadata record in Postgres ... is written only after both are successfully uploaded to MinIO" becomes: no manifest row is ever `partial = false` until the adapter has declared the archive complete with its terminal `CheckpointSummary` frame and every byte it declared has committed. `spec/04_system-components.md:235` already says a partial manifest "is not a valid checkpoint; it is a recovery aid".
- **The retention catalog, the barrier metadata table, and the manifest keep distinct jobs.** `session_checkpoints` (migrations `0067`/`0112`/`0132`) stays the retention and rotation catalog, already wired at `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:311` (`recordRetention`). `session_checkpoint_meta` (`0148`) stays `CheckpointBarrier` metadata. `session_partial_checkpoint_manifest` (`0062`) and its index (`0150`) are rewritten in place into the single `checkpoint_manifest` table the spec already assumes, holding both full and partial rows. The retention INSERT, the latest-2 rotation, and the `sessions.last_successful_checkpoint_at` and `last_checkpoint_workspace_bytes` stamps stay best-effort post-snapshot writes, as `checkpointer.go:305-311` already documents ("a catalog or rotation failure does not unwind the successful snapshot"); they are not folded into the finalising transaction. All four follow a `partial = false` finalisation only. A `partial = true` finalisation writes neither the session record nor the retention catalog, because `spec/04:235` already states that a partial manifest "is not a valid checkpoint; it is a recovery aid" and a truncated byte count in `last_checkpoint_workspace_bytes` is the next attempt's frozen recovery denominator and its Stage 2 cap-tier input.
- **The gateway's own preStop hook is the second eviction-checkpoint trigger, and it is already wired.** `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/prestop` declares `CheckpointFn` as "the per-session eviction-checkpoint surface" that "runs the §4.4 eviction-trigger checkpoint synchronously" (`prestop.go:207-212`), selects the §10.1 Stage 2 tier cap per session (`prestop.go:341-380`), and production binds it to the `Checkpointer` (`cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:559-561`, `:617`). That call already enters `Checkpointer.Checkpoint` and is therefore already labeled `trigger="periodic"` with the periodic retry budget. C5 routes it through `snapshotWithTrigger(…, checkpoint.TriggerEviction)` so it selects `RetryBudgetFor(TriggerEviction)` (`{500ms, 5s, 30s}`) and emits `lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds{trigger="eviction"}`. The two eviction triggers share one driver, one retry budget, and one metric label, and differ only in what a failure costs: the gateway drain leaves the pod and its workspace running, and agent-pod termination does not.
- **The unhealthy-threshold drain is rejected as the eviction seam.** `DrainSandbox` (`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:598`) stamps `lenny.dev/drain-request` and does not terminate a pod carrying live sessions: on entering `draining`, "Existing slots run to completion (or failure) … The pod transitions to `terminated` only after all remaining slots have completed or failed" (`spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:178`). Its only caller is the slot-bind failure handler (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:2192-2193` inside `applySlotRetryPolicy`), whose session has already had its reservation released (`start.go:2168`) and therefore has no registry binding to checkpoint (`checkpointer.go:237-239` returns `ErrNoBinding`); it carries a pod name with no pod-to-sessions enumeration; it is reachable only on `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pools (`start.go:1985`); and it runs on the inbound `POST /v1/sessions` request context, which would put a 60s checkpoint timeout plus a 30s eviction retry budget per live slot on an unrelated client's request and cancel the checkpoint on client disconnect.
- **The agent-pod eviction trigger ships, driven by a termination signal from the agent pod's existing preStop hook.** Every agent pod already runs a preStop hook that execs `lenny-adapter prestop`. The hook is attached to the `adapter` container in the sidecar model and to the single container in the embedded model, which are the only two `Lifecycle` sites in the podspec (`pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:560`, `:645`, both calling `preStopDrainHook` at `:1229`). That subcommand today only SIGTERMs the adapter and polls for its exit (`cmd/lenny-adapter/prestop.go:31-36`, `:67-93`). The adapter's SIGTERM path now first calls a new blocking `GatewayControl.NotifyPodTerminating` RPC (P1) on the gateway channel it already holds, and the gateway drives an eviction-trigger checkpoint for every session bound to that pod through the C2 upload driver against that pod's own adapter, over the same gateway→pod 50051 channel it already dials. One signal covers node drain, the kubelet Eviction API, a direct pod delete, and a cluster upgrade, because all four run the pod's preStop hook and its termination grace period. The trigger selects `checkpoint.TriggerEviction`, so `checkpoint.RetryBudgetFor(TriggerEviction)` (`{500ms, 5s, 30s}`, `pkg/checkpoint/checkpoint.go:118-122`) governs its MinIO retries and `lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds{trigger="eviction"}` carries its duration. The signal needs no pod RBAC, no Kubernetes client in the pod, and no new network path (S3, P1, C7).
- **The sidecar `runtime` container gets a wait-only preStop hook of its own, because a Full-level runtime must survive to quiesce.** The sidecar `runtime` container (`podspec.go:562-573`) carries no `Lifecycle` today, so the kubelet SIGTERMs the runtime process at the start of pod termination, concurrently with the adapter's preStop hook. The gateway-driven eviction `Checkpoint` is a cooperative quiesce for any Full-level runtime (`pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:203-206` routes it through `checkpointViaLifecycle` → `Lifecycle.RequestCheckpoint`, which waits for `checkpoint_ready`), and a runtime that has already exited on SIGTERM cannot answer, so the quiesce fails with `codes.Internal` (`checkpoint.go:409-411`) on exactly the path the trigger exists for. **`preStopDrainHook` cannot be reused on that container.** Its exec command is `["lenny-adapter", "prestop", …]` (`podspec.go:1229-1245`) and the container runs the third-party `Runtime.spec.image` (`podspec.go:236-237`, `:562-573`), whose only contract is the socket protocol (`spec/04:867`, `:875`), so the binary is absent and the kubelet's exec fails with `FailedPreStopHook` and SIGTERMs the container at t=0. Even with the binary present, `runPreStop` signals and polls **PID 1 of its own container** (`cmd/lenny-adapter/prestop.go:12-16`, `:99-101`), which in the `runtime` container is the runtime process it exists to keep alive, and it can never observe the adapter's PID because `shareProcessNamespace` is deliberately unset (`podspec.go:485-487`) and `spec/04:243` and `spec/13:16-23` forbid setting it. C7 therefore gives the `runtime` container a **wait-only** hook: an initContainer running the adapter image stages the statically linked adapter binary (`Dockerfile:21`, `CGO_ENABLED=0`) into a shared `emptyDir` that the `runtime` container mounts read-only, and the container's preStop hook execs that copy as `lenny-adapter prestop-wait --timeout=Ns`, a new subcommand that polls the adapter's **existing** TCP listener on `127.0.0.1:50051` (the two containers share the pod's network namespace) and returns when the listener stops accepting, which is when the adapter process has exited. It signals nothing, so it cannot terminate the runtime, and it needs no binary in the runtime image, no shared PID namespace, no new port, and no new NetworkPolicy edge. The adapter's own `prestop` hook and its `preStopDeps` are unchanged.
- **The termination notification is the adapter's first SIGTERM act, ahead of the existing teardown.** The adapter's SIGTERM handler runs `ShutdownDemoteSDK` (`cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:397`), then `lifecycle.Close()` (`:398-400`), then `srv.GracefulStop()` (`:401`). `lifecycle.Close()` fails every pending lifecycle request and rejects every later one with `errLifecycleClosed` (`pkg/adapter/lifecyclechannel.go:558-561`, `:605-614`), so a `Checkpoint` the gateway opens after it can never reach a Full-level runtime's quiesce. `NotifyPodTerminating` therefore blocks ahead of all three, and the existing teardown runs only after it returns (C7).
- **A gateway-side pod informer is rejected as the agent-pod eviction seam.** The rejection does not rest on RBAC: the gateway `ClusterRole` already carries `get`, `list`, `watch`, and `patch` on core `pods` and is bound cluster-wide (`charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:176-178`, `:191-203`), so an informer would need no new grant. It is rejected on the two grounds that hold. It needs a per-replica ownership and dedup design, because every gateway replica would receive every delete event while only the session's coordinator may checkpoint it, and `pkg/gateway` carries no informer or watch machinery to build that on. And it races the kubelet, which starts the termination grace clock on the same `DELETE` that produces the watch event, so the gateway learns of the eviction concurrently with the pod rather than before it and has no way to hold the pod open. The preStop signal has none of these properties: the kubelet blocks the container's `SIGTERM` on the preStop hook's exit, so the pod is held open for exactly as long as the checkpoint runs.
- **The `preStop` hook holds the pod open until the gateway-driven checkpoint settles.** The kubelet does not send `SIGTERM` to a container until that container's preStop hook returns, and it does not `SIGKILL` until `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` elapses. The blocking `NotifyPodTerminating` RPC is what turns that into a checkpoint barrier: the adapter completes no part of its teardown (`cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:396-401`) until the RPC returns, so the gRPC server is still serving and the intra-pod lifecycle channel is still open when the gateway opens the `Checkpoint` stream back into it, and the `lenny-adapter prestop` poller does not return until the adapter process exits. The hook on the sidecar `runtime` container holds that container's SIGTERM for the same window. The pod-local query surface the hook previously lacked is not needed: the signal travels adapter→gateway on the existing channel rather than preStop→adapter over a new pod-local one.
- **The notification is served by whichever replica the Service routes it to, and that replica takes the coordination lease from whoever holds it.** The adapter dials the load-balanced `lenny-gateway` ClusterIP Service once at startup (`pkg/adapter/gatewaylink.go:54`, `charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:1601-1618`, which sets no `sessionAffinity`), the chart runs two replicas by default (`charts/lenny/values.yaml:2074`), and `spec/10_gateway-internals.md:22` states that sticky routing is an optimization. The receiving replica is therefore the session's coordinator only by chance, while `podsession.Registry` is process-local (`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/registry.go:8-17`), `Checkpointer.snapshot` returns `ErrNoBinding` without a local binding (`checkpointer.go:236-239`), and the pod's generation fence rejects a non-coordinator's RPCs (`spec/10:37`). The handler therefore runs the §10.1 coordinator handoff for every session bound to the terminating pod before it checkpoints: it dials that pod's adapter through a new exported `podsession.Binder.AdoptForCheckpoint` (the binder's two unexported dial paths are the only producers of an `*adapterclient.Client` in the tree, `binder.go:1096-1100` and `:1625-1631`, and `sessions.pod_assignment` carries the Sandbox name rather than an address, `start.go:753`), takes the coordination lease, and, in one transaction, bumps `coordination_generation` through the `spec/10:33` CAS and reserves that slot's eviction checkpoint through the C2 driver's reservation entry point, then fences the pod with `CoordinatorFence` (`spec/10:35`) over that connection and binds the pod in its own registry. The reservation runs inside the handoff rather than at execution time because the generation cannot exclude a peer's *next* attempt: the peer re-reads the post-CAS value from the session row and writes at the handler's own generation, which the pod's entry gate admits and which the supersede predicate's equal-generation arm refuses to displace, so a periodic tick landing between the CAS and the handler's serial slot loop would take the active row and force the slot into the minimal-state fallback. The committed intent row is what excludes the peer, at the same generation, by the settled-row term (C7). The handler removes every binding for the terminating pod from its registry and closes every client it dialed when the RPC returns, including a binding that was already there, because the pod is destroyed by construction and `PodExecutor.Release` (the only `Registry.Remove` caller, `pod.go:313-316`) runs on the session's teardown rather than on the loss of its pod. A binding naming a destroyed pod would otherwise make `Checkpointer.Sweep` reserve a checkpoint per tick against a pod that cannot answer, committing an intent row that the supersede predicate then refuses to displace on the session's replacement pod; the bindings held by **other** replicas are retired by the reservation's own stale-binding check, which compares the binding's Sandbox name against the session's `pod_assignment` (C2). **The handoff as shipped cannot take a lease from a live holder, so one store primitive is added.** `leasestore.Store.Acquire` returns `ErrHeld` when a different holder owns the lease (`pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/leasestore.go:125-141`), the coordination sweeper stands down on that error (`pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:132-137`), and the Postgres `coordination_lease` table is only a mirror with no compare-and-swap on its `Store` interface (`pkg/gateway/coordination/coordlease/coordlease.go:5-9`, `:61-84`), so the shipped takeover path fires only when a lease lapses on its TTL. `spec/10:30` and `:178` describe acquiring an unheld or lapsed lease and say nothing about taking one from a live holder. C7 therefore adds `Steal` to the `LeaseStore` interface, a takeover that overwrites the holder unconditionally and returns the displaced one, implemented on all three of the interface's implementations (the Redis Lua compare-and-set on `*Store`, the `pg_advisory_xact_lock`-guarded `UPDATE` on `pgstore.Store`, and the primary-then-fallback routing on `*Failover`, which is the value production actually wires, `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1272`), and S3 states the rule that permits it. The steal is safe on the protocol the spec already carries. The displaced coordinator observes the takeover through the sweeper's own renewal call, which is `Acquire` rather than `Renew`: `Acquire` is idempotent for the current holder and is what refreshes the TTL (`pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:10-14`), and the sweeper already stands down on `ErrHeld` by skipping the session (`coordination.go:132-137`), so after the steal it stops renewing and stops mirroring the session. `leasestore.Renew` has no coordination-path caller at all. **The sweeper's `ErrHeld` arm drops no `podsession.Registry` binding, and C7 leaves it as it stands.** It is the generic "another replica coordinates this session" arm rather than a displacement signal: `Sweeper.Sweep` enumerates every non-terminal session of every tenant and races to `Acquire` each one (`coordination.go:109`, `:133`), nothing on the session-start path acquires the coordination lease, and the registry binding is created by whichever replica served `POST /v1/sessions` or the resume (`start.go:2232`, `:3150`), so lease holder and binding holder are uncorrelated and a replica that never coordinated a session takes `ErrHeld` on it every sweep. Unbinding there would drop live bindings for sessions the replica is actively serving, which would strand the pod and its concurrency slot (`PodExecutor.Release` is the only `Registry.Remove` caller and returns nil when the binding is absent, `pod.go:313-316`), make the pod unreachable for message dispatch, interrupt, upload-to-session, expiry warnings, and tree recovery, and stop the session being checkpointed by anyone. The wasted work the unbind was meant to save is already bounded by the predicates: a displaced replica's next periodic attempt reads the post-CAS `sessions.coordination_generation` in the transaction where it would commit its intent row, meets the handler's reserved row at equal generation with no `manifest_reason` and inside its deadline, and aborts on the settled-row term at the reservation, before it writes a row or opens an RPC. The bindings that name the destroyed pod are retired on a different signal, which is the one every replica can see: the reservation compares the binding's Sandbox name against the session's `pod_assignment` and drops the binding when they disagree, and a `Checkpoint` RPC that fails because the pod is gone drops it on the abort path (C2). The handler retires the bindings in its own registry directly (C7). `Release` is already a no-op for a non-holder (`leasestore.go:170-172`); and `CoordinatorFence` is a hard precondition of every gateway→pod RPC (`spec/10:35`). The fence as shipped stops no stream that is already running: its only in-flight-cancellation rule fires on a generation gap greater than 1 (`spec/10:36`) while the handoff CAS bumps by exactly one, and the adapter does not implement even that (`pkg/adapter/coordination.go:62-64`). S3 therefore restates `spec/10:36` so an accepted fence cancels every in-flight RPC of a superseded generation before it acks, P1 puts `coordination_generation` on `CheckpointRequest` and `ResumeStart`, and C1 builds both. The displaced coordinator's in-flight `Checkpoint` is then cancelled at the pod as the fence acks, releasing the adapter's pod-global op-lock before the new coordinator's eviction checkpoint contends for it. A **later** request from that coordinator is not stopped by the entry gate, because it carries the post-CAS generation the gate admits; it is stopped by the handler's reserved intent row, which its reservation meets at equal generation and aborts on. The steal is scoped to the termination path, which is the one case in which waiting for a TTL to lapse is not an option, because the pod is inside its grace period. A gateway-to-gateway forwarding RPC is rejected: no peer surface exists in `schemas/`, `pkg/gateway`, or `spec/10`, `coordination_lease.coordinator_replica` records an OTel `service.instance.id` rather than an address (`pkg/gateway/coordination/coordlease/coordlease.go:72-74`), and it would add a new RPC, an addressing scheme, a NetworkPolicy edge, and spec text where one store primitive on an existing interface suffices.
- **The §4.4 eviction-fallback writer, the resume precedence rule, and the `session_eviction_state` supersede ship with the trigger.** Agent-pod termination is the class that destroys a workspace, so `evictionfallback.Writer` gets its call site here (C7): a MinIO failure after the eviction retry budget writes the `session_eviction_state` minimal-state row, increments `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total`, and the session resumes `conversation_only` with `workspaceLost: true`. Three properties travel with it and are staged rather than recorded as future work. The writer runs on a context detached from the §10.1 Stage 2 per-session tier cap, because 30s of MinIO retries plus the 60s Postgres write (`spec/04:278`) cannot fit inside a 30s cap and `spec/04:278` bounds the Postgres write by `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` instead. `classifyResume` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:3214-3219`) gains the `spec/04:264` precedence rule before `Options.EvictionStateLookup` is wired, so any restorable checkpoint beats the minimal-state row rather than losing to a bare presence check. The checkpoint driver deletes the session's `session_eviction_state` row when a later checkpoint finalises `partial = false`, so one fallback write does not make every later resume of that session `conversation_only` forever.
- **A failed gateway-drain checkpoint is still not an eviction fallback.** The two eviction paths this change wires have different consequences and the spec states both. On the gateway replica's own preStop drain (C5) the agent pod keeps running with its workspace intact and the session is re-adopted through a coordinator handoff (`spec/10_gateway-internals.md:178`), so a failed checkpoint falls through to the last successful checkpoint and writes no `session_eviction_state` row. On agent-pod termination (C7) the workspace is destroyed with the pod, so a failed checkpoint takes the minimal-state fallback. Hanging the writer off the gateway-drain path would write `workspace_lost = true` for sessions whose pods are still running (C6).
- **The pluggable `CheckpointSink` / `CheckpointSource` / `CheckpointAborter` interfaces are deleted rather than implemented.** They exist to be nil in every production binary (`pkg/adapter/server.go:216`, `:217-220`, `:259`) and to be faked in tests. With the gateway owning storage there is one canonical adapter-side implementation of produce and one of restore, so the seam, the nil branches, and the two `codes.Unimplemented` returns go away together.
- **The partial-workspace resume arm goes live here**, because C4 gives the partial-manifest path a producer for the first time. The classification comes from the restore driver's own report rather than from a resume-time re-read of the manifest table, which both cleanup paths soft-delete before the classifier runs (C6).
- **No agent-pod egress rule, no MinIO ingress allow, and no new credential-lease provider.** This is the security argument the whole design rests on, so the sites that would have to change under a pod-side S3 client are enumerated here to record that they do not. `spec/13_security-model.md:11` ("Egress | Default-deny NetworkPolicy; allow only gateway + required internal services"), `:78`, and the `allow-pod-egress-base` policy body at `:80-114` permit agent-namespace egress to the gateway on TCP 50051 and to CoreDNS on 53 and nothing else; `spec/13:220` admits only gateway, `lenny-ops`, and `lenny-backup` pods to MinIO on TCP 9443; `spec/13:216` already grants the gateway MinIO egress on that port; `spec/13:180` and `:184` pin the `lenny.dev/managed`, `lenny.dev/delivery-mode`, `lenny.dev/egress-profile`, and `lenny.dev/dns-policy` label immutability those policies select on; `spec/13:209` (NET-047, NET-050) pins the rendered selector requirement for the MinIO component. The rendered chart carries no MinIO egress rule for agent pods in any of its policies (`charts/lenny/templates/agent-network-policies.yaml:34` `default-deny-all`, `:69-99` `allow-pod-egress-base`, `:113-134` LLM proxy, `:142-169` OTLP, `:177-208` provider-direct, `:215` internet, `:275-303` kube-dns). The agent pod is issued no object-store credential anywhere in the repository: `spec/15:2525-2526` scopes pod credential delivery to `/run/lenny/credentials.json` and to the adapter↔gateway mTLS transport, and the §4.9 lease-provider set (`spec/04:1117-1118`) is LLM-only. **Every one of those sites is unchanged by this proposal.** They appear in the target lists of no staged change, and the tier-11 assertion in §6 fails the build if a NetworkPolicy, a MinIO ingress rule, or a credential-lease provider for agent pods appears. Moving the object-store calls to the gateway is what makes the code match the policy those sites already state; the contradiction the change removes is in §4, §10, and §12 rather than in §13. The one new pod-originated call this change adds is `GatewayControl.NotifyPodTerminating`, which travels adapter→gateway on TCP 50051, the port `allow-pod-egress-base` already permits.
- **The retention catalog stays separate from `checkpoint_manifest`.** `session_checkpoints` (migrations `0067`/`0112`/`0132`) remains the retention and rotation catalog and `checkpoint_manifest` remains the upload and restore manifest, so a successful checkpoint writes two rows. Folding rotation into `checkpoint_manifest` (a `retained` column plus the rotation sweep) would yield one row and one lifecycle, and it would touch the §12.5 GC rules and the `artifact_store` tombstone sweep, which both key on `session_checkpoints`, for a consolidation this change does not need. The double write is a Postgres INSERT on a path that already performs several.
- **`manifest_reason` stays as a single-valued column.** S4 reduces the value set to `timeout` alone, and the column is kept rather than dropped, so a later reason (a `pre_scale_down` cap fire, an operator-forced abort) is a value rather than a migration. Dropping it would also remove the settled-row term the supersede transaction keys on (`manifest_reason IS NOT NULL OR checkpoint_timeout_at < now()`, S2), which is what separates a row whose attempt has stopped running from one still uploading on another replica.
- **Classified `fix`.** The dominant action reconciles a self-contradictory spec to the security posture it already commits to and wires the already-specified surfaces end to end.

## 3. How the pieces fit end to end

A checkpoint runs as one gateway-driven pipeline for every trigger.

1. The gateway takes the per-`(session_id, slot_id)` single-flight lock, cancelling and settling any attempt already in flight for that pair, mints `checkpoint_id`, supersedes any prior active partial row for that pair under the single predicate `coordination_generation < $new_generation OR (coordination_generation = $new_generation AND (manifest_reason IS NOT NULL OR checkpoint_timeout_at < now()))` (aborting the attempt instead when an active row sits at a higher generation, which is the signature of a newer coordinator's upload in flight on another replica, or when an active row of the same generation carries no `manifest_reason` and is still inside its own deadline, which is the signature of an unfenced upload still running on another replica), and commits the intent row with `partial = true`, `chunk_count = 0`, the `chunk_size_bytes` and `chunk_encoding` it selects, and `object_key_prefix = {tenant_id}/checkpoint/{session_id}/{checkpoint_id}-chunk-`. The gateway is the sole authority for `chunk_encoding` (constant `tar.gz`, the only encoding `workspace.ArchiveTree` produces, `pkg/adapter/workspace/tree.go:45-57`), because the value is committed at INSERT, is the suffix of every derived chunk key, and is the decode selector on every later read and sweep; the adapter carries it on `CheckpointRequest` and MUST stream an archive in that encoding. Exactly one `checkpoint_id` and one intent row exist per attempt, whatever the trigger. The lock is what orders two attempts of the same generation on one replica, and the generation and settled-row guards are what keep an attempt from superseding a row whose upload is still running on another replica. On the barrier-flush path the barrier coordinator performs this whole step through the driver's reservation entry point, which takes the same lock, before it sends `CheckpointBarrier`, so that the request carries the id the ack echoes back and so that a drain-time barrier cannot supersede a periodic checkpoint of the same session that is still uploading on the same replica. The driver is entered at step 2 with that id and that row (C2), and the lock is held until the flush the ack drives settles or the shared `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` deadline fires. The agent-pod eviction handler uses the same two entry points for the same reason: it reserves every slot of the terminating pod through the reservation entry point, inside the transaction that advances that session's `coordination_generation`, and only then executes the reserved attempts serially (C7). The reservation does not block quiescence: when the lock cannot be taken inside its bounded slice of that deadline, the degraded arm below fires, the barrier is dispatched with an empty `checkpoint_id`, and no flush runs. The barrier path is where a stale coordinator is most likely to write, because `spec/10:165` states that the in-memory lease cache produces false-positive barriers for sessions just handed off, and the pod's generation fence rejects such a barrier only when the gateway calls `Send` (`pkg/adapter/coordination.go:153-159`), strictly after the intent row and its supersede would have committed. The coordinator therefore reads the authoritative `sessions.coordination_generation` for the session in the same transaction that commits the intent row, writes it as the row's `coordination_generation`, and takes the generation-fenced supersede, so a stale replica's false-positive barrier aborts on the guard instead of soft-deleting the live row and chunk-deleting the newer coordinator's upload under it. The `coordination_lease` mirror is not the source: it is written by the coordination sweeper with the generation it read **before** `RecordHandoff` bumped it (`pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:109`, `:145`, `:150`, with the bump at `:169-171`) and the upsert is best-effort (`:178-194`), so the mirror lags the CAS by at least one sweep. Sourcing the row from it would stamp the superseding attempt with the same generation as its victim, the strictly-higher arm would never fire, and every handed-off session would collide with `partial_manifest_active_uniq` and fall to the minimal-state fallback. The mirror keeps its one job, which is enumerating the barrier-target set (`spec/10:165`). A Postgres failure on that transaction does not suppress the barrier: the gateway dispatches `CheckpointBarrier` with an empty `checkpoint_id`, the pod quiesces and acks, no flush runs, and the session falls back to its last successful checkpoint, because quiescence is what bounds the interruption window (`spec/10:180`) and `spec/10:165` already requires the barrier to fire under a degraded read.
2. The gateway opens the server-streaming `Checkpoint` RPC to the adapter, carrying the minted `checkpoint_id` and the row's `chunk_encoding`. The adapter runs its pre-checkpoint workspace-size probe, sends the probed byte count in the leading `CheckpointStart` frame, archives the two-root bundle (the resolved workspace root plus the `/sessions` tmpfs, `pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:311-323`) into a single continuous byte stream in the requested encoding and sends it back as `archive_chunk` frames, and closes with a terminal `CheckpointSummary` carrying the total archived byte count.
3. The gateway reserves the probed byte count against the tenant's storage quota (`spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:35`) before the first chunk `Put`, and wraps the archive stream in an `io.LimitedReader` at the tenant's remaining quota headroom so an under-declared `probed_bytes` cannot stream past the quota. It then slices the frame stream at `chunk_size_bytes` boundaries and, for each index `n`, commits the `chunk_count = n + 1` `UPDATE` first and then the single-part `PutObject` through `blobstore.NewTenantScoped(tenantID, blobs)`, advancing `workspace_bytes_uploaded` once the chunk commits. The `cataloging` store inserts the chunk's `artifact_store` row as part of that `Put`. Heap holds one chunk. Every chunk `Put`, `Get`, and `SoftDelete` on this path goes through the context-carrying store surface C2 adds, because the shipped `blobstore.Store` verbs take no context (`pkg/blobstore/blobstore.go:307-321`) and `miniostore.Store.Put` opens on `context.Background()` (`pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:259`) and sleeps through its own fixed ladder of 1s, 5s, and 30s (`miniostore.go:44`), so no deadline the driver holds could otherwise cut an in-flight chunk (C2).
4. The gateway finalises the row with `partial = false`, the committed `chunk_count`, and `workspace_bytes_uploaded` only when it has received the terminal `CheckpointSummary` inside the deadline and `workspace_bytes_uploaded` equals `CheckpointSummary.size_bytes`. The finalising `UPDATE` carries the `checkpoint_id = $id AND deleted_at IS NULL` guard, and a zero-row result is an abort rather than a success: a newer coordinator superseded the attempt and deleted its chunks after its last chunk committed, so the writer stamps no session record, inserts no retention row, releases its reservation remainder, and takes the abort sweep. A stream that reaches EOF without a summary, or whose committed byte count disagrees with the summary, is a truncated archive and finalises `partial = true` with `manifest_reason = 'timeout'`, exactly as a deadline fire does. Stream EOF alone is not the completion signal: a non-conforming adapter can end a server stream with an OK status after a truncated archive, and a `partial = false` row over an incomplete chunk set is the session's full checkpoint and the fallback target every other branch resolves to. On a deadline fire the committed chunks are retained for the resume path; the unused remainder of the reservation is released the same way, and the committed chunks keep their bytes on the tenant's counter through their own `artifact_store` rows. If no chunk committed, the row is soft-deleted, the whole reservation is released, and the checkpoint falls through to the last successful checkpoint. On an abort with `N` committed chunks the gateway releases only `probed_bytes − workspace_bytes_uploaded` and then deletes the chunks, so each chunk's `SoftDelete` releases its own `artifact_size_bytes` and the two accounting layers never cover the same bytes twice. The finalising UPDATE, the `chunk_count == 0` soft-delete, the abort sweep, and the quota release all run on a context detached from the deadline that cut the attempt (`context.WithoutCancel` plus a bounded budget of its own), because the deadline that ends the stream is the deadline of the context the driver is running on and every Postgres, object-store, and Redis call issued after it fires would otherwise return `context.DeadlineExceeded` (C5).
5. On resume, the gateway selects the manifest row through the single `spec/10:155` selector (`SelectForResume`, C4): the newest active row for `(tenant_id, session_id, slot_id)` under `deleted_at IS NULL` in the total order `(coordination_generation DESC, checkpoint_started_at DESC, checkpoint_id DESC)`. The selector applies the §10.1 restorability gates itself: a `partial = true` row whose `chunk_count == 0`, whose `workspace_bytes_uploaded` is zero, or whose `workspace_bytes_uploaded` is below the §10.1 recovery threshold is not a restore source, and the selector falls through to the newest active `partial = false` row for the same `(tenant_id, session_id, slot_id)` rather than reporting nothing. It returns `ErrNotFound` only when neither a restorable partial nor an active full row exists. That selection is the resume's entry condition: a restorable row of either class takes the restore path and names the row on `podsession.ResumeRequest`, and `ErrNotFound` takes the snapshotless rebuild. The fall-through is what keeps a live full checkpoint reachable: the intent row of every attempt of every trigger is a `partial = true` row, so an in-flight or crash-abandoned attempt leaves an active zero-chunk row that is strictly newer than the session's last successful full row under a stable coordinator, and routing that row to the snapshotless rebuild would return an empty workspace to a session that holds a restorable full checkpoint. The session's `workspace_snapshot_ref` column is not the gate, because a session whose first-ever checkpoint truncated has no such ref and would otherwise never reach the restore driver (C2). The order is total rather than `MAX(coordination_generation)` alone because `coordination_generation` advances only on coordinator handoff (`spec/04_system-components.md:157`), so a session's two retained full checkpoints and any later partial normally carry the same generation and `MAX` alone does not identify a row. Restated on the same order, `spec/10:155`'s full-wins fencing rule keeps its purpose (a stale-coordinator write must not beat a fenced newer writer) and stops firing on equality: a `partial = false` row outranks a `partial = true` row only when it is strictly newer in that order, so a partial finalised after the session's last full checkpoint under a stable coordinator is the row the resume reads. It derives the chunk keys as `object_key_prefix + {n}.{chunk_encoding}` for `n` in `[0, chunk_count)`, `GetObject`s them in ascending index order, and pushes them to the adapter over the client-streaming `Resume` RPC. The adapter feeds the concatenated stream into one decompress-then-untar pipeline into a staging directory and renames it onto the workspace root on success. The threshold is `baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes * partialRecoveryThresholdFraction` when `baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes` is non-NULL and zero when it is NULL, which is the degenerate arm `spec/10:141` and `spec/10:155` already define for a session whose first checkpoint truncated before any full checkpoint existed. A `partial = true` row's chunks are deleted and the row soft-deleted after the restore, per `spec/04_system-components.md:237`. A contiguity failure on the selected row, of either class, is non-terminal while another full checkpoint remains: the driver resolves the newest active `partial = false` row **strictly below the row it failed on** through `SelectLatestFullBefore` (C4), restores that checkpoint's chunk set, and reports `full`. A session whose manifest rows all name absent objects has lost its workspace: it resumes `conversation_only` when it holds a §4.4 `session_eviction_state` row (`spec/04:264`), and otherwise the resume fails with `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` and the session transitions to `failed`, which is the rule `spec/25:4095` already states. The snapshotless rebuild is reserved for the different state in which `SelectForResume` returns `ErrNotFound` and the session has no manifest row at all (C2, C6, C7). The exclusion is what makes the arm terminate: under S2 a `partial = false` row is a chunk set and can itself fail contiguity, and it is the row `SelectForResume` returns in the dominant case, so a selector that returned the newest full row unconditionally would return the row that just failed. The restore driver reports the mode it took (`partial_workspace` on a reassembly above the threshold, `full` on every restore of a full checkpoint) together with the recovery fraction it computed (the adapter's `ResumeResponse.restored_bytes` over the row's frozen `baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes`, and nil when that baseline is NULL), and the gateway classifies `session.resumed` and populates its `workspaceRecoveryFraction` from that report, because both cleanup paths destroy the row before the classifier runs and a post-hoc re-read of the table would answer `full` for every partial recovery and would have no denominator to divide by (C6). A `partial = false` row is read-only on restore, and its chunks and its manifest row are retired together: the latest-2 rotation, the TTL GC, and terminal-session checkpoint deletion each delete the derived chunk keys and soft-delete the row under the `deleted_at IS NULL` guard in the same step, and the §12.5 tombstone hard-prune removes the row after `gc.tombstoneRetentionSeconds`.

The agent-pod eviction trigger enters that pipeline at step 1 rather than replacing any of it. When the kubelet begins terminating an agent pod (a node drain, the kubelet Eviction API, a direct pod delete, or a cluster upgrade), it runs each container's preStop hook and holds that container's `SIGTERM` until its hook returns. The `adapter` container (or the single container of an embedded pod) runs `lenny-adapter prestop`, which signals the adapter to drain and polls for its exit. The sidecar `runtime` container runs the wait-only `lenny-adapter prestop-wait` hook from the staged binary, which signals nothing and returns when the adapter's TCP listener stops accepting, so the runtime process is still alive to answer the quiesce (C7). The adapter, before it runs any part of its existing teardown (`ShutdownDemoteSDK`, `lifecycle.Close()`, and `srv.GracefulStop()`, `cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:396-401`), opens a blocking `GatewayControl.NotifyPodTerminating` RPC to the gateway carrying its pod name and the remaining grace budget it computes from the `LENNY_TERMINATION_GRACE_SECONDS` the podspec renders. The receiving replica resolves the sessions bound to that pod from `sessions.pod_assignment` in Postgres rather than from its process-local registry, dials that pod's adapter through `podsession.Binder.AdoptForCheckpoint` and binds it in its own registry for every session it is not already bound to (removing the binding and closing the client on the way out), takes over coordination of every session bound to that pod through the §10.1 handoff (a `leasestore.Steal` of the coordination lease when it does not already hold it, and in every case the `spec/10:33` `coordination_generation` CAS **and the reservation of that slot's eviction checkpoint in the same transaction as the CAS**, followed by the `CoordinatorFence` RPC over that connection, because a session's periodic uploads are driven by whichever replica holds its pod binding rather than by its lease holder), and then for each slot in slot-ID order executes the attempt it reserved, entering the upload driver with `checkpoint.TriggerEviction` and the already-minted `checkpoint_id` under that slot's §10.1 Stage 2 tier cap, driving the archive stream back over the `Checkpoint` RPC into the adapter that is still serving because it has not yet stopped its gRPC server. The generation bump fences any upload the previous coordinator still has **in flight** for those sessions: the new attempt's generation is strictly higher, so the supersede's `coordination_generation < $new_generation` arm admits the victim's row even though its upload is still running; the `CoordinatorFence` ack cancels the victim's in-flight `Checkpoint` stream at the pod and releases the pod's operation lock before the new coordinator opens its own (the `spec/10:36` rule S3 restates and C1 builds); and the victim's next counter `UPDATE` or its finalising `UPDATE` affects zero rows and aborts, which is the supersede path step 4 already defines. The victim's *next* attempt is a different matter and the generation does not exclude it, because that attempt re-reads the post-CAS value from the session row and presents the generation the pod's entry gate admits: what excludes it is the intent row the handler committed for that slot, which its reservation meets at equal generation, inside its deadline, with no `manifest_reason`, and aborts on. That is why the reservation runs inside the handoff rather than at the head of each slot's turn in the serial loop, whose last slot can begin minutes after the CAS. A slot whose upload exhausts the eviction retry budget, whose coordination the handler cannot take over, or which has not settled when the declared budget elapses, takes the §4.4 minimal-state fallback: the gateway writes the `session_eviction_state` row, increments `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total`, and that session resumes `conversation_only` with `workspaceLost: true`. That write runs on a **gateway-owned** context, detached from both the slot's tier cap and the pod's grace period, under its own §4.4 60-second Postgres budget. It needs only the gateway and Postgres, and it cannot fit inside the pod's remaining grace period on the arm that matters: at the moment `budget_ms` elapses, every second that is left belongs to the adapter's demote-and-exit reserve and the kubelet's drain margin. The gateway therefore returns the RPC at `budget_ms` and completes the fallback write, and on its exhaustion the `session.lost` publish, afterwards. The adapter then runs its teardown and exits, both preStop hooks observe the exit, and the kubelet proceeds with termination; the fallback record and the total-loss event become durable on the gateway's own clock, independently of the pod's lifetime. Nothing in this path is a new network edge: the signal travels adapter→gateway on TCP 50051, the archive travels gateway→adapter on the same channel, and the runtime container's hook polls a pod-local loopback address.

Five invariants close the orphan window. No chunk is ever written before its manifest row is durable, so the §12.5 backstop sweep always finds a row for a committed chunk. The counter `UPDATE` for index `n` commits before the `PutObject` for index `n`, so the derived key set `[0, chunk_count)` is always a superset of the committed chunks and every sweep that enumerates from the row reaches every object the attempt wrote. Before it lowers `chunk_count` to the committed count, the finalising `UPDATE` resolves the at-most-one index whose `PutObject` outcome the driver did not observe, by `Stat`ing its derived key: a definitely-present object is counted, a definitely-absent or byte-length-mismatched one is soft-deleted and the count is lowered, and an index the `Stat` cannot resolve (the common case when the object store is the thing that failed) keeps the reserved count and is left in the derived key set. A chunk that landed server-side after its attempt was cut is therefore never outside the row's derived key set (S1). A soft-deleted row keeps naming its chunks until the §12.5 tombstone hard-prune removes it, and the §12.5 backstop selects soft-deleted rows inside the tombstone-retention window as well as active ones, so a chunk a superseded writer commits after its row was soft-deleted is still reachable from that row and is still swept. A fourth rule keeps the backstop from destroying a live upload rather than reclaiming an abandoned one: the terminal-session arm sweeps only rows whose `checkpoint_timeout_at` has already passed, so the seal checkpoint of a session that is terminal by construction is never swept while it is still running.

## 4. Proposed changes

### S1. Reassign every checkpoint object-store verb from the adapter to the gateway

its diagnosis and resolution blocks at `:126-143` already name the gateway and are confirmed unchanged); `docs/operator-guide/disaster-recovery.md` (`:251`, `:279`); `docs/operator-guide/upgrades.md` (`:47-53`); the page contains no other checkpoint, preStop, or eviction content); `docs/runbooks/workspace-seal-stuck.md` (`:51`, `:54`, `:102`, the three lines that attribute the seal's object-store dial and its uploader credential to the pod; `:53`, `:88`, and `:121` are confirmed unchanged, because pod-side archive errors stay pod-side); `docs/runtime-author-guide/integration-levels.md` (`:145-175`).

The resume selector and the full-wins fencing rule (`spec/10:155`) keep their actor, which is already the coordinator; S2 restates their predicate as a total order over `(coordination_generation, checkpoint_started_at, checkpoint_id)`. The supersede-on-write transaction (`spec/10:137`) keeps its actor and is edited by S2 for the renamed column, the derived chunk keys, and the `coordination_generation` guard.

**Anchor and change (spec/10:124, the BarrierAck floor).** The `SandboxWarmPool` admission webhook's BarrierAck-floor rule rests its rationale on a pod-side upload and defines the ack counter in terms of one: "if a pod legitimately needs 90s to upload a 512 MB workspace within its tier cap, a BarrierAck timeout shorter than 90s would declare it unresponsive while it is still making progress", and "Observability: BarrierAck timeouts are counted by `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_total{outcome="timeout"}` ..., enabling operators to distinguish legitimate slow uploads from unresponsive pods". After this change the gateway slices and `PutObject`s, and the pod acks on quiescence without uploading anything, so a slow upload can never produce an ack timeout. Rewrite the rationale so the deadline covers the gateway-driven flush rather than a pod-side upload: the gateway's flush of a 512 MB workspace can legitimately need the full tier cap, and a BarrierAck timeout shorter than that cap would cut the flush before it completes. Rewrite the observability sentence so `outcome = "timeout"` means the pod did not acknowledge quiescence by the deadline, and point the cut-flush case at `lenny_checkpoint_partial_total{trigger = "barrier_ack_timeout"}`, matching the staged `:169`, `:177`, and `spec/16:41`. The floor rule itself (`checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds >= max_tiered_checkpoint_cap`), its `422 INVALID_POOL_CONFIGURATION` rejection message, the default equality at 90s, and the webhook's in-process purity (`spec/13`) are unchanged.

**Anchor and change (spec/10:128).** The sentence currently reads:

```
   **Intent-row-first ordering (orphan prevention).** The partial-manifest flow MUST write the manifest row to Postgres **before** the adapter issues the first chunk `PutObject`. The flow is:
```

Replace "the adapter issues the first chunk `PutObject`" with "the gateway issues the first chunk `PutObject`".

**Anchor and change (spec/10:131).** Step 2 is titled **Chunk uploads (adapter)** and opens:

```
   2. **Chunk uploads (adapter).** Only after the intent-row INSERT commits does the gateway instruct the adapter to begin issuing single-part `PutObject` calls under `partial_object_key_prefix`.
```

Retitle the step to **Chunk uploads (gateway)** and replace the opening sentence with the gateway-driven flow:

```
   2. **Chunk uploads (gateway).** Only after the intent-row INSERT commits does the gateway open the `Checkpoint` stream to the adapter. The adapter archives the workspace and streams the tar bytes back to the gateway, and the gateway commits each `chunk_size_bytes` boundary as a single-part `PutObject` under `object_key_prefix` through the `ArtifactStore`. The agent pod issues no object-store call and holds no object-store credential ([§13.1](13_security-model.md#131-threat-model) "No standing credentials", "File delivery: Gateway-mediated only"); tenant-prefix validation and per-tenant SSE-KMS key selection are enforced at the `ArtifactStore` boundary ([§12.5](12_storage-architecture.md#125-artifact-store) Tenant isolation).
```

The monotonic-counter `UPDATE` sentence that follows is rewritten in the same step, because the fire-and-forget framing it carries is safe only while cleanup discovers chunks by `ListObjectsV2`, which S2 removes. Keep the conditional `UPDATE ... SET chunk_count = $n + 1, workspace_bytes_uploaded = $bytes WHERE checkpoint_id = $id AND deleted_at IS NULL AND chunk_count < $n + 1` statement and its monotonicity guard, and replace the ordering and the fire-and-forget clause:

```
   The counter `UPDATE` for index `n` commits **before** the gateway issues the `PutObject` for index `n`, and `workspace_bytes_uploaded` is advanced once that chunk commits. `chunk_count` is therefore an upper bound on the committed chunks and the derived key set `[0, chunk_count)` is always a superset of the objects the attempt wrote: every cleanup path (the abort sweep, supersede-on-write, the resume-time cleanup, the latest-2 rotation, the TTL GC, and the §12.5 backstop) enumerates chunk keys from the row, so a chunk that is committed but not counted would be unreachable and permanently orphaned. A failed counter `UPDATE` aborts the attempt rather than continuing the upload. A cleanup delete on a reserved index whose `PutObject` never committed is a no-op, because the `ArtifactStore` soft-delete returns success on a missing object.
```

The finalising `UPDATE` (step 3) writes the committed `chunk_count`, which is at most the reserved value. Lowering it is safe only because the driver first **resolves** the one index whose `PutObject` outcome it did not observe. An attempt cut between the counter reservation for index `n` and the completion of that index's `PutObject` can leave the object committed server-side while the driver never saw the response, which S3 already establishes for the deadline path ("a `PutObject` whose body was fully sent but whose response was lost, or one cut by the per-attempt deadline, leaves the object committed server-side", C2). Writing `chunk_count = n` over that state would put the object at `{prefix}{n}.{chunk_encoding}` outside `[0, chunk_count)` for every manifest-derived path (restore, resume-time cleanup, supersede, rotation, TTL GC, and the §12.5 backstop), and it would have no `artifact_store` row either, because `cataloging.Store.Put` inserts the catalog row only after the inner `Put` returns success (`pkg/blobstore/cataloging/cataloging.go:143-171`) and `SoftDeleteSession` enumerates catalog rows (`cataloging.go:423-425`), so the terminal-session sweep would never reach it. The object would be permanently orphaned and its bytes would sit on `storage_bytes_used` forever, which is the exact failure the reserve-before-Put ordering exists to exclude, on the deadline path that is this design's common case.

The chunk `Put`s of one attempt are serial, so at most one index is ever unresolved. Before the finalising `UPDATE`, and on the same detached bookkeeping context every other post-deadline write runs on (S3), the driver therefore `Stat`s that index's derived key and takes one of three branches. The `Stat` reports the object present at the byte length the driver was writing: the chunk counts, `workspace_bytes_uploaded` advances by that length, and `chunk_count` keeps the reserved value. The `Stat` returns `ErrNotFound`, or reports a definite byte-length mismatch: the driver soft-deletes the key (a no-op on a missing object) and writes the lower committed count. The `Stat` returns any other error: the index is **unresolved**, and the driver keeps the reserved `chunk_count` and soft-deletes nothing. The third branch is not an edge case. `miniostore.Store.statBlob` maps only `isNotFound(err)` to `blobstore.ErrNotFound` and wraps every other error (`pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:513-517`), and the dominant cause of an unresolved index is an object store that became unreachable mid-`PutObject`, under which the resolving `Stat` fails too and the paired `SoftDelete` would fail on its own `StatObject` precheck rather than no-opping (`miniostore.go:785-793`). Lowering the count on that branch is the exact failure this resolution exists to prevent: the object may have committed server-side, and a `chunk_count` below its index puts it outside `[0, chunk_count)` for every manifest-derived path and outside the `artifact_store` catalog, so no sweep can ever reach it. Keeping the reserved count instead preserves the superset invariant, leaves the index reachable to the abort sweep, the supersede delete, and the §12.5 backstop, and costs at most one no-op delete on a key that was never written. The finalising `UPDATE` therefore writes a `chunk_count` whose derived key set is exactly the committed objects when the index resolves, and a superset of them when it does not, and the invariant that `[0, chunk_count)` is a superset of the committed chunks holds at every point in the attempt's life. A resolved chunk never makes a truncated archive `partial = false`: the summary gate is unchanged, so an attempt whose last chunk resolves as committed still finalises `partial = true` unless the terminal `CheckpointSummary` arrived and the byte counts agree (S3). An unresolved index always finalises `partial = true`, because `workspace_bytes_uploaded` advances only on a chunk whose `Put` the driver observed, so it cannot equal the summary's byte count.
