The unresolved index is always the **highest** index of the row, because the chunk `Put`s of one attempt are serial and the counter for index `n` is reserved before the `Put` for index `n` is issued. The restore reads that rule rather than treating the reservation as a gap: on a `partial = true` row, an object absent at index `chunk_count − 1` is the reserved-but-unresolved index and the driver reassembles the objects it did read, whose byte total is the row's `workspace_bytes_uploaded` and therefore the numerator the §10.1 recovery threshold is computed against; an object absent at any index below `chunk_count − 1` is a contiguity failure and takes the fall-back arm unchanged. S2's restatement of `spec/10:155` carries that sentence, and C2 implements it.

**Anchor and change (the `chunk_count` column at spec/10:141).** The schema paragraph defines the column as the count of chunks that have already committed: "`chunk_count` (number of chunks committed — equal to the highest `{n}` + 1; initialised to `0` at intent-row INSERT and updated monotonically as chunks commit — the intent-row-first ordering above means both `chunk_count = 0` and `workspace_bytes_uploaded = 0` are valid initial states that the §12.5 backstop treats as 'no chunks to delete, only the row to soft-delete')". That definition is the fire-and-forget ordering restated on the column, and an implementer following it would advance the counter after each `Put` and reintroduce the committed-but-uncounted chunk this change calls unreachable. Restate the column as the reserved index count: `chunk_count` is the highest reserved chunk index + 1, the counter `UPDATE` for index `n` commits before the `PutObject` for index `n`, so on an in-flight or crash-abandoned row `[0, chunk_count)` is a superset of the committed objects, and the finalising `UPDATE` rewrites the column to the committed count after the gateway has resolved, with a `Stat` on the derived key, the at-most-one index whose `PutObject` outcome it did not observe: a resolved-present chunk is counted and a resolved-absent one is soft-deleted, so the column never excludes a committed object. Keep the `chunk_count = 0` initial-state sentence verbatim. The neighbouring `workspace_bytes_uploaded` clause ("sum of chunk sizes successfully committed to MinIO ... updated monotonically as chunks commit") stays correct under this ordering and is unchanged.

**Anchor and change (spec/10:139).** The paragraph currently reads, in part:

```
   **Chunked-object storage model (not S3 multipart upload).** Partial checkpoints are stored as a **sequence of separate, independently committed MinIO objects** — not as parts of a single S3 multipart upload. The adapter uploads the workspace tar stream in fixed-size chunks (`partialChunkSizeBytes`, default: 16 MiB, ...), naming each chunk deterministically as ...
```

Replace the actor: "The gateway slices the adapter-streamed tar byte stream into fixed-size chunks (`chunkSizeBytes`, default: 16 MiB, ...) and commits each via a single-part `PutObject`, naming each chunk deterministically as ...". The chunk-key format itself is rewritten by S2.

**Anchor and change (the knob rename).** `partialChunkSizeBytes` governs every checkpoint once S2 makes the chunked path the single upload path, so the knob is renamed to `chunkSizeBytes` everywhere it is named: the `spec/10:139` paragraph above, the `chunk_size_bytes` column description at `spec/10:141` ("the `partialChunkSizeBytes` value in effect for this manifest"), the chunk-boundary paragraph at `spec/10:153`, and the §17.8.1 quick-reference row at `spec/17_deployment-topology.md:919`, whose label becomes "Checkpoint chunk size (`chunkSizeBytes`)". The row is the authoritative default that `spec/10:139` cross-references, and the name appears nowhere in `charts/` or `pkg/` today, so the rename is confined to the spec.

**Anchor and change (spec/10:153).** The paragraph currently reads, in part:

```
   **Chunk boundaries do not align with tar members.** The adapter writes a single continuous `tar` (or `tar.gz`) byte stream during the checkpoint upload and slices that stream into fixed-size chunks at arbitrary byte offsets — the `partialChunkSizeBytes` boundary.
```

Split the two actions: the adapter writes the single continuous byte stream and the gateway slices it into fixed-size chunks at arbitrary byte offsets, at the `chunkSizeBytes` boundary. The rest of the paragraph (straddling members, concatenate-then-decode) is unchanged.

**Anchor and change (spec/10:167).** Step 3 of the CheckpointBarrier protocol currently reads, in part:

```
3. **Checkpoint flush:** After quiescing, the adapter triggers a best-effort checkpoint (full workspace snapshot) and sends `CheckpointBarrierAck(barrier_id, checkpoint_ref)` to the gateway.
```

Replace with: the adapter acknowledges the barrier as soon as it has quiesced, sending `CheckpointBarrierAck(barrier_id, checkpoint_ref)` where `checkpoint_ref` echoes the gateway-minted `checkpoint_id` (or is empty). The gateway commits the session's intent row and mints its `checkpoint_id` before it dispatches that session's `CheckpointBarrier`, and carries the id on the request so the ack correlates against the row. On each ack the gateway opens the `Checkpoint` stream for that session and drives the barrier-flush checkpoint through the same intent-row-first chunked path as every other trigger. One `checkpoint_id` and one intent row serve the whole attempt: the flush the gateway drives on the ack neither mints a second id nor commits a second row, and no attempt supersedes the row it is itself executing, so the single-active-partial invariant at `spec/10:147` and the deterministic read at `spec/10:171` hold on the barrier path. The ack signals quiescence and carries no upload result, because `CheckpointBarrier` is a unary RPC and an ack that waited for the archive stream to close would require the adapter's unary handler to block on a gateway-initiated stream it cannot observe. The parallel-checkpoint and single-deadline sentences that follow keep their meaning and gain the per-pod serialization rule: the gateway fans the barrier out to all coordinated pods in parallel and holds one wall-clock deadline of `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` covering both the acks and the flushes it drives, and the flushes of the several sessions of one concurrent-session pod run one at a time, in slot-ID order, which is the `spec/05:542` rule S1 states over every eviction checkpoint. Pods still checkpoint in parallel with each other; the slots of one pod do not. The MinIO I/O burst these sentences describe is issued by the gateway replica rather than by the pods.

**Anchor and change (spec/10:165 and the degraded barrier dispatch).** The intent-row commit is a Postgres write, and making it a hard precondition of `Send` would make a Postgres outage suppress the barrier entirely, which is the outcome `spec/10:165` exists to prevent: on a failed `coordination_lease` read the replica "falls back to its in-memory lease cache so the barrier still fires — a degraded barrier set is strictly better than no barrier at all when the replica is already committed to terminating". The barrier's load-bearing function is step-2 quiescence, which bounds the rolling-update interruption window to at most one in-flight tool call per session (`spec/10:180`); the checkpoint flush is best-effort and may be skipped. The staged `:167` text therefore carries a degraded arm, and `:165` names it: when the intent-row INSERT or the same-transaction `coordination_lease` generation read fails, or when the reservation cannot take the per-`(session_id, slot_id)` lock inside its bounded slice of the deadline, the gateway dispatches `CheckpointBarrier` anyway with an empty `checkpoint_id`, the pod quiesces and acks with an empty `checkpoint_ref`, the gateway drives no flush for that session, and the session falls back to its last successful checkpoint (which is the rule-4 outcome at `:174`).

The degraded dispatch is a per-session event with no signal today, and it is not `lenny_prestop_barrier_target_source_total{source="cache_fallback"}`. That counter is emitted once per preStop `CheckpointBarrier` fan-out and reports where the barrier-target **set** came from (`spec/16:44`, `pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_podlifecycle.go:136-144`); two of the three degraded triggers (a failed intent-row INSERT and a lock the reservation cannot take) occur with a healthy `coordination_lease` read, so stamping `cache_fallback` for them would tell an operator that Postgres was unreachable during preStop and that the fan-out ran on a stale session set, which is false, and it would put per-session events on a per-fan-out counter. The skipped flush is instead counted on `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total{trigger="eviction", reason="manifest_write_failed"}`, the counter S1 widens below to cover every trigger and whose meaning (a checkpoint that could not be persisted, with the `trigger` label carrying which path) is exactly this outcome; the barrier flush is the gateway drain's checkpoint, so `eviction` is the value the `trigger` label already carries (C5), and `manifest_write_failed` is the `reason` value the degraded dispatch takes, because the failure is a Postgres write or a lock the reservation could not take rather than an exhausted MinIO retry. `spec/16:200` and its mirrors declare that value, and `spec/16:44` stays as written. No metric is added.

**Anchor and change (spec/16:44, the barrier-target-source counter's label set).** `spec/16:44` declares `lenny_prestop_barrier_target_source_total` with the labels `pool` and `source`, while `pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_podlifecycle.go:142-144` registers it with `source` alone and `IncPreStopBarrierTargetSource(source string)` (`gatewaymetrics.go:1278-1282`) takes only the source. The tier-11 label-parity gate in §6 fails on that disagreement, so one of the two is corrected. The **code** is the party that is right here, because the `pool` label is not expressible under the increment rule the same row states. The counter is emitted once per preStop `CheckpointBarrier` fan-out (`spec/16:44`, "emitted once per preStop `CheckpointBarrier` fan-out invocation"), from `Coordinator.Dispatch` (`pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier/barrier.go:164-166`) before the per-target loop at `:168`, over every session the draining replica coordinates, and those sessions span every pool the replica holds. A single `pool` value on that increment would name one of many pools arbitrarily. `spec/16:44` therefore drops `pool` and declares `source` alone, matching the shipped registration, the shipped helper, and the per-fan-out increment rule the row itself carries. The registration, the helper, the `barrier.Metrics` method, and the counter's meaning are all unchanged, and `spec/16:44` is added to S1's target list. Its docs mirror is corrected in the same edit: `docs/reference/metrics.md:40` declares the same `pool`, `source` label set, so leaving it alone would make it the only surviving statement in the tree that the counter carries a `pool` label and would replace a spec-versus-code disagreement with a spec-versus-docs one. Its Labels column becomes `source`: `postgres`, `cache_fallback`, and the §6 tier-11 label-parity assertion compares the `spec/16` row, the `docs/reference/metrics.md` row, and the Go registration for this counter, as it already does for `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total`. The contrast with `lenny_prestop_cap_selection_total` (`spec/16:43`) is not an argument for the `pool` label: that counter is emitted once per **per-session** Stage 2 tier selection (`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/prestop/prestop.go:451`, `h.Metrics.IncPreStopCapSelection(pool, …)`), where a single pool is in hand.

The pool value the barrier-ack counter and its histogram need is a separate matter, and C2 puts it in hand at the site that emits them. `barrier.Target` (`barrier.go:59-70`) carries `TenantID`, `SessionID`, `CoordinationGeneration`, and `PodAddr` and no pool, and its producer `MirrorTargetLister.Targets` (`wiring.go:112-135`) builds each `Target` from a `coordlease.Lease`, whose store interface exposes tenant, session, generation, and replica alone (`pkg/gateway/coordination/coordlease/coordlease.go:60-85`). `Target` therefore gains `Pool string`, populated by `MirrorTargetLister.Targets` and by the in-memory-cache `Fallback` lister (`wiring.go:141-143`) from the session record rather than from the `coordination_lease` mirror, which carries no pool column. `Coordinator.dispatchOne` (`barrier.go:183-210`) then holds the pool for `IncCheckpointBarrierAck(pool, outcome)` and the ack-duration observation. The same widening carries the `slot_id` P1 puts on `CheckpointBarrierRequest` and supplies the `(session_id, slot_id)` key the driver's reservation entry point needs at `dispatchOne`, so `Target` gains `SlotID string` in the same edit. `pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier/wiring.go` and both target producers are in C2's target list for this.

**Anchor and change (spec/10:169-175).** The BarrierAck-timeout partial-capture path (CPS-007) keeps its handler logic and its `manifest_reason = "timeout"` finalisation, and its trigger predicate is restated so it keys on the gateway's own deadline rather than on a missing pod ack. Reword `:169` so the deadline is the shared `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` wall clock: a session whose pod never acked has quiesced-unresponsively and the gateway opened no `Checkpoint` stream for it, while a session whose flush is still uploading when the deadline fires is cut there. In both cases the gateway consults the session's manifest row, "which exists in Postgres from before the gateway issued its first chunk `PutObject`". Rules 1 and 2 (`:171`, `:172`) stand unchanged. Rule 5 (`:175`) keeps its fallback to the last successful periodic checkpoint and its handler logic; its closing clause states the §12.5 backstop's row-selection predicate, which S2 widens, so S2 edits that clause. Rule 3 (`:173`, `chunk_count == 0`) is reworded to "plausible when the pod never acked the barrier, or when the adapter's archive stream produced no bytes before the deadline". Rule 4 (`:174`) is restated, because its antecedent moves: the gateway now commits the intent row before it dispatches the barrier, so a pod that hangs during quiescence or never acks always has a row and lands on rule 3, and its current rationale ("the pod never began the chunked upload path — e.g., it hung during quiescence or its checkpoint flush never reached the first chunk") describes no actor once the gateway is the uploader. Rule 4 becomes the residual case: no intent row exists for `(session_id, slot_id)` because the INSERT for that session never committed, so either no barrier was dispatched for it or the degraded arm above dispatched one with an empty `checkpoint_id` and drove no flush, and the gateway falls back to the last successful periodic checkpoint. Its data-durability bound and its equivalence to the pre-CPS-007 behavior are unchanged. The same deadline is the `checkpoint_timeout_at` the gateway writes on the intent row (`spec/10:130`), so one value drives the finalisation and the `trigger = "barrier_ack_timeout"` label.

**Anchor and change (spec/10:177 and spec/16:41, the barrier-ack outcome set).** `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_total{pool, outcome}` tracks per-pod barrier ack results and its `success` value is what lets operators compute the barrier success rate (`spec/16:41`). `spec/10:177` currently emits `outcome = "partial_captured"` in rule 2, and `spec/16:41` defines that value as marking a BarrierAck timeout. After the ack is decoupled from the flush, rule 2's dominant input is a session whose pod acked inside the deadline and whose gateway-driven flush the shared deadline then cut, so a pod that never timed out would be counted as a BarrierAck timeout, and a pod counted under both `success` and `partial_captured` would be double-counted on a counter whose success rate is computed per pod. Remove `partial_captured` from the ack counter's value set at `spec/10:177` and `spec/16:41`, leaving `success` (ack received inside the deadline), `timeout` (no ack by the deadline), and `error` (transport error) as the per-pod ack outcomes, and state that the partial finalisation rule 2 produces is counted on `lenny_checkpoint_partial_total{trigger = "barrier_ack_timeout"}` (`spec/10:157`, `spec/16:195`), one increment per manifest row finalised on that path. No metric is added; one value moves to the counter that carries the event.

`spec/16:42`, the row immediately below, defines the companion histogram over a subject the decoupling removes: `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_duration_seconds` is "wall-clock time from `CheckpointBarrier` send to `CheckpointBarrierAck` receipt per pod; measures how much of the `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` budget is consumed by **checkpoint flushing** during rolling updates". After this change the ack returns on quiescence, strictly before the gateway opens the `Checkpoint` stream and drives the flush, so the interval the histogram measures contains no flush at all and an operator sizing a drain budget against it would read a number that excludes the work it is told it covers. Restate the row: the histogram measures the wall-clock from `CheckpointBarrier` send to `CheckpointBarrierAck` receipt per pod, which is how long the pod takes to quiesce tool-call dispatch; the gateway-driven flush the ack triggers runs inside the same `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` wall clock and is not part of this histogram (`lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds{trigger="eviction"}` covers it). The histogram's name, type, and `pool` label are unchanged, and C2 gives it its emitter alongside the ack counter's.

The histogram has a second operator-facing consumer in the same file, and it is corrected in the same edit. The §16.5 `PDBBlockedEvictions` row (`spec/16:456`) tells the operator to diagnose clause (b), "the preStop tiered checkpoint cap is consistently hitting its upper bound", by correlating with `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_duration_seconds`. After the decoupling that histogram contains no flush, so it stays near zero whether or not the Stage 2 tier cap is saturating and the instruction cannot surface the condition it names. Clause (b) is retargeted at the metric that carries the flush: correlate with `lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds{trigger="eviction"}`, and `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_duration_seconds` measures pod quiescence alone. `spec/16:456` is added to S1's target list, and the §6 tier-11 assertion's scope covers §16.5 alert-catalog rows as well as the §16.1 inventory rows.

`lenny_checkpoint_partial_total` cannot carry it as shipped. `spec/16:195` declares the counter with `pool`, `recovered`, `manifest_reason`, and `trigger`, but `pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_podlifecycle.go:112-117` registers it with `pool` alone, `IncCheckpointPartial(pool string)` (`gatewaymetrics.go:1247-1256`) takes nothing else, and the helper has no caller anywhere in `pkg/` or `cmd/`, so the counter counts nothing today. C2 widens the counter and its helper to the `spec/16:195` label set and gives it its emitter on the `partial = true` finalisation, with the `recovered` flip on resume. Without that, removing `partial_captured` from the ack counter would leave the barrier-timeout partial capture with no signal at all. `pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics` is added to C2's target list and to §10.

`lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_total` is in worse shape than that and is fixed in the same change. It has no Prometheus registration and no emitter anywhere in the tree: `gatewaymetrics_checkpoint.go` registers `lenny_checkpoint_stale_sessions` (`:62`), `lenny_partial_manifest_cleanup_total` (`:75`), `lenny_checkpoint_partial_manifests_superseded_total` (`:87`), `lenny_checkpoint_orphaned_objects_total` (`:100`), `lenny_checkpoint_size_exceeded_total` (`:111`), `lenny_session_eviction_total_loss_total` (`:122`), `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_partial_keys_logged_total` (`:133`), and `lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds` (`:147`) and no barrier-ack counter, and `barrier.Coordinator`, the component that sends the barrier and reads the ack (`barrier.go:184-210`), holds a `Metrics` interface with exactly one method, `IncPreStopBarrierTargetSource` (`barrier.go:100-101`). The counter's only occurrence in Go is a name-and-type row in the metric catalog (`pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go:127`), which registers nothing (`catalog.go:3-7`: "The catalog is a type-level enumeration of what the platform exports"). The barrier success rate the `spec/16:41` rationale rests on is therefore uncomputable today, the staged `spec/10:124` observability sentence would direct operators to diagnose unresponsive pods with a counter that emits nothing, and the §6 tier-7 assertion on `outcome="timeout"` and `outcome="success"` would have no target. C2 registers `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_total{pool, outcome}` beside the other checkpoint metrics in `gatewaymetrics_checkpoint.go` with the surviving `success | timeout | error` value set, widens `barrier.Metrics` (`barrier.go:100`) with an `IncCheckpointBarrierAck(pool, outcome string)` method, and emits it from `barrier.Coordinator.dispatchOne` on the ack, on the `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` expiry, and on a transport error. `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_duration_seconds` (`spec/16:42`) is registered and observed from the same site, over the send-to-ack interval.

**Anchor and change (spec/04:249).** The paragraph currently reads, in part:

```
**Checkpoint abort cleanup:** When a checkpoint is aborted — whether by watchdog timeout, adapter crash, or any other failure — the adapter MUST delete any partially uploaded MinIO objects for that checkpoint attempt. For multipart uploads, the adapter calls the S3 `AbortMultipartUpload` API; for single-part uploads that may have completed server-side, the adapter issues a `DeleteObject` call for the checkpoint's object key.
```

Replace the first two sentences with the gateway sweep, scoped to attempts that leave no usable manifest row:

```
**Checkpoint abort cleanup:** A checkpoint attempt is *aborted* when it leaves no row a resume can consume: an adapter crash, a stream error, a watchdog timeout, a gateway crash before finalisation, a finalisation that finds `chunk_count == 0`, or a supersede by a newer coordinator, which the superseded writer observes as a zero-row counter `UPDATE` on its next chunk index (aborting the attempt before that chunk's `PutObject` is issued) or, when no chunk index remains, as a zero-row finalising `UPDATE` under the same `deleted_at IS NULL` guard. A deadline fire is not an abort — it is the partial finalisation defined in [§10.1](10_gateway-internals.md#101-horizontal-scaling), which retains the committed chunks so the resume path can reassemble them. On an abort the gateway MUST delete every chunk object it committed for that attempt and soft-delete the manifest row under the `deleted_at IS NULL` guard. The chunk keys are derivable from the row's `object_key_prefix`, `chunk_count`, and `chunk_encoding`, so the gateway issues a per-key soft-delete through the `ArtifactStore` for every index in `[0, chunk_count)`. The sweep runs against the row the attempt itself committed, whose three columns the driver holds, and it runs whether or not that row is still active: on the supersede arm another writer has already soft-deleted the row, so the sweep's own soft-delete `UPDATE` affects zero rows and the pass is chunk-delete-only. The `ArtifactStore` soft-delete tombstones the object, transitions its `artifact_store` row, and releases its bytes from the tenant's storage-quota counter ([§12.5](12_storage-architecture.md#125-artifact-store)); the [§12.5](12_storage-architecture.md#125-artifact-store) tombstone hard-prune physically removes the object and the row after `gc.tombstoneRetentionSeconds`. A raw bucket delete on this path is not permitted: it would bypass the `artifact_store` row and the quota decrement and leave the aborted attempt's bytes on the tenant's counter permanently. The delete is idempotent on a reserved-but-uncommitted index, because a soft-delete on a missing object is a no-op. `AbortMultipartUpload` is not used on this path: chunks are committed as independent single-part objects and no multipart upload is ever opened.
```

Two sentences in the remainder of the paragraph are false under the intent-row-first ordering and are replaced in the same edit. "The checkpoint metadata record in Postgres is never written for an aborted checkpoint (per the atomicity guarantee above), so no Postgres cleanup is needed" becomes: an aborted attempt always leaves the `partial = true` intent row it committed before its first chunk, the gateway soft-deletes that row under the `deleted_at IS NULL` guard, and the [§12.5](12_storage-architecture.md#125-artifact-store) backstop reclaims any row that escapes. "The orphaned partial object is harmless (it cannot be mistaken for a valid checkpoint because no metadata references it)" becomes: an orphaned chunk is one whose manifest row was already soft-deleted, so no resume can select it; it cannot be mistaken for a valid checkpoint because only a `partial = false` row is a checkpoint, and it consumes storage until an operator sweeps it.

Keep the rest of the paragraph as written, including the best-effort framing, the `lenny_checkpoint_orphaned_objects_total` counter (now emitted by the gateway, C1 and C2), and the mandate that any `recover()` wrapping the checkpoint goroutine re-panics or marks the liveness endpoint unhealthy.

**Anchor and change (spec/04:263 and :264).** Two metric emitters are attributed to the adapter:

```
- **Non-eviction checkpoints** ... The adapter logs the failure and increments the `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` metric ...
- **Eviction checkpoints** ... The adapter also increments `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total` ...
```

Reassign both emitters to the gateway. `lenny_checkpoint_size_exceeded_total` (`spec/04:255`, labeled by `pool` and `level`) stays adapter-emitted: the pre-checkpoint workspace-size probe runs in the pod.

**Anchor and change (`lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` covers every trigger).** The counter is scoped to non-eviction checkpoints in two places (`spec/04:263`, `spec/16_observability.md:200`, "increments when a **non-eviction** checkpoint upload fails after all retries"), and `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total` is the eviction-class counter. After S3 and C5 two eviction triggers exist, and C6 establishes that one of them, the gateway's own drain, performs no Postgres minimal-state fallback, so a failed gateway-drain checkpoint can increment neither counter as they are defined and would have no signal at all. The counter already carries a `trigger` label (`pool`, `level`, `trigger`), so it is the surface that already distinguishes the classes and no new metric is introduced. Drop the "non-eviction" scoping at `spec/04:263` and `spec/16:200`: `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` increments when **any** checkpoint upload fails after all retries, and the `trigger` label carries which. `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total` keeps its meaning (a fallback to the Postgres minimal-state record actually occurred) and gets its first emitter from the agent-pod eviction path C7 wires, where the fallback is the correct behavior.

`CheckpointStorageUnavailable` fires on that counter (`rate(lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total[5m]) > 0`, `pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:349-354`), and both of its operator-facing strings are scoped to eviction: `Summary: "Checkpoint upload to MinIO failed during eviction"` (`rules.go:352`) and `Description: "Checkpoint upload to MinIO failed after all retries during eviction; the Postgres minimal-state fallback was attempted."` (`rules.go:353`). The description promises a Postgres minimal-state fallback and a `conversation_only` resume that the gateway-drain class never performs, and the summary asserts an eviction that a failed periodic or seal checkpoint upload did not have. The summary is the first line an operator sees in Alertmanager, so correcting only the description would leave a routine gateway rolling update paging with an eviction headline over a body that describes any trigger. The alert catalog is single-sourced in `pkg/alerting/rules` and rendered by `make generate`, so both fields are corrected there and the mirrors follow. The summary becomes "Checkpoint upload to MinIO failed after all retries", with no trigger scoping. The description says that a checkpoint upload to MinIO failed after all retries, that the `trigger` label carries which checkpoint path failed, that the session falls back to its last successful checkpoint, and that for the agent-pod eviction class the Postgres minimal-state fallback applies and resumed sessions are `conversation_only` with `workspaceLost: true`.

**The alert's expression is scoped to the object-store `reason` values in the same edit, because the counter gains two increment conditions that are not object-store failures.** The shipped expression carries no `reason` selector (`rate(lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total[5m]) > 0`, `rules.go:350`), the alert is critical, it is mapped to the `objectStore` health component (`pkg/alerting/rules/health_components.go:61`), and `/v1/admin/health` derives `unhealthy` for a component from a firing critical alert (`spec/25:448`), overriding the MinIO `HeadBucket` probe. Left unscoped, the degraded barrier dispatch (`reason="manifest_write_failed"`, a Postgres or lock-contention failure the design deliberately admits during a drain) and the zero-chunk fall-through (`reason="no_chunks_committed"`, an unresponsive pod or an empty archive stream) would page on-call with a MinIO headline, flip `objectStore` to `unhealthy` while MinIO is healthy, and route the operator to `docs/runbooks/minio-failure.md`, which is exactly the misreading the two `reason` values were added to prevent. The expression therefore becomes `rate(lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total{reason!~"manifest_write_failed|no_chunks_committed"}[5m]) > 0` in `pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:350`, and the same scoped condition is mirrored at `spec/16:417` and `docs/reference/metrics.md:470`. The two excluded reasons stay an unalerted diagnostic dimension on the counter: they are per-session events on paths that already fall back to the session's last successful checkpoint, they carry no object-store health signal, and adding a second alert for them would need its own runbook and health component for a condition an operator diagnoses from the Postgres and pod-health alerts that fire alongside. `docs/operator-guide/observability.md:127` is corrected in the same edit: its condition ("MinIO checkpoint upload failed after retries") and its remediation ("Check MinIO health, network") stay accurate only for the object-store reasons, so the row names the scoped expression. The alert's name, severity, runbook target, and health-component mapping are unchanged. `spec/16:417` (the alert-catalog row, which today reads "Checkpoint upload to MinIO failed after all retries during eviction; Postgres minimal state fallback was attempted") drops both the "during eviction" scoping and the unconditional fallback claim. `spec/16:418`, the `MinIOUnavailable` row immediately below it, carries the same scoping in its cross-reference ("the checkpoint-path eviction fallback has its own alert — `CheckpointStorageUnavailable`") and is corrected in the same edit, because after the widening a firing `CheckpointStorageUnavailable` means a checkpoint upload failed on some trigger rather than that an eviction fallback occurred: the parenthetical names the alert by what it now covers ("checkpoint upload failures on any trigger have their own alert — `CheckpointStorageUnavailable`, whose `trigger` label carries which path failed"). The same row's body is a bucket-A site and is corrected in the same edit: it reads "at least one gateway **or pod replica** is failing `ArtifactStore` PUTs after exhausting its retry budget", and the pod's only `ArtifactStore` PUT and only retry budget in the shipped spec is the checkpoint upload S1 removes (`spec/12:294`). Leaving it would put "The agent pod issues no object-store call" at `spec/10:131` and a pod-issued `ArtifactStore` PUT with its own retry budget at `spec/16:418` in one applied spec, and the tier-11 assertion in §6 fails on it. The row drops "or pod replica" and attributes the PUTs and the retry budget to the gateway alone. `pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:363` carries the same sentence as the alert's operator-facing `Description`, single-sourced and rendered by `make generate` into `docs/alerting/rules.yaml`, `charts/lenny/files/alerting-rules.yaml`, and `pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml`, so it is corrected there and the mirrors follow. There is no pod-side emitter for `lenny_artifact_upload_error_total`: the counter is registered on the gateway registry alone (`pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_retention.go:162`). The alert's name, severity, and runbook target are unchanged; its expression gains the `reason` exclusion above. `docs/reference/metrics.md:470` (the docs mirror of that alert row, which today reads "Postgres fallback attempted"), `docs/runbooks/minio-failure.md:106`, and the catalog help text at `pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go:289` carry the same correction. The runbook states the same unscoped fallback twice, and the second site is its opening summary at `docs/runbooks/minio-failure.md:31` ("Workspace uploads fail, artifact retrieval returns 5xx, and checkpoint storage falls back to Postgres -- which is a bounded emergency mode"), which is the first line an operator paged by `CheckpointStorageUnavailable` reads, since the alert's `runbook_url` resolves to this page (`pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:349-354`, and the page's front matter names the alert at `minio-failure.md:8`). It is corrected in the same edit and in the same terms as `:106`: an object-store outage blocks workspace uploads and artifact retrieval, checkpoint uploads fail after all retries on whichever trigger is running (`lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total{trigger}`), and each session falls back to its last successful checkpoint; the Postgres minimal-state record applies to the agent-pod eviction class alone, which S3 and C7 wire. The rendered mirrors of the summary (`docs/alerting/rules.yaml:66`, `charts/lenny/files/alerting-rules.yaml:63`, and `pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml:1550`) are regenerated by `make generate` rather than hand-edited. The alert's name, severity, and runbook target are unchanged; its expression gains the `reason` exclusion above.

The counter's description is mirrored on four surfaces, and all four are corrected together with `spec/16:200`: `docs/reference/metrics.md:190` ("Non-eviction checkpoint upload failures after all retries."), `docs/operator-guide/observability.md:96` ("Non-eviction checkpoint upload failures"), and the catalog help text at `pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go:289`. Each becomes "Checkpoint upload failures after all retries (any trigger; the `trigger` label carries which)". `docs/operator-guide/observability.md` is an authored operator page rather than a generated one, so leaving it would tell on-call that the counter excludes the eviction class on both of the eviction triggers this change wires.

The rest of the non-eviction bullet at `:263` is rewritten with it. It currently has the adapter act on the outcome of upload retries ("If all upload retries fail, the adapter resumes the agent immediately — via `checkpoint_complete` on the lifecycle channel (Full-level), `SIGCONT` (embedded adapter) … The failed checkpoint is discarded."), which the adapter can no longer observe: its handler returns when its archive stream closes. Restate it as two actors: the adapter resumes the runtime as soon as its archive stream ends (`checkpoint_complete`, `SIGCONT`, or no-op by integration level), and the gateway owns the upload retries, discards the failed checkpoint, logs the failure, and increments `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total`.

`checkpoint_complete` is redefined from an upload result to an archive-capture result in the same edit, because the adapter can only report whether it streamed the archive. The `status` (`ok` | `failed`) field keeps its values and means "the archive stream completed" rather than "the snapshot upload succeeded", across the §4.7 lifecycle-event row (`spec/04:721`), `schemas/lifecycle-events.schema.json:91-103` ("Confirms the snapshot upload result and releases the runtime from quiescence"), `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md:541`, and the integration-level page `docs/runtime-author-guide/integration-levels.md:145-175`. Without this edit a runtime author branching on `status: "ok"` would treat a checkpoint whose upload later fails at the gateway as durable.

The eviction bullet at `:264` is retitled and clause (a) of its rationale is rewritten, because after S3 no agent-pod preStop hook produces a checkpoint. The title "**Eviction checkpoints** (preStop hook)" becomes "**Eviction checkpoints** (gateway-driven, §4.6.1)". Clause (a) rests on the pod's own termination ("the pod is terminating regardless — there is no agent to resume, so retry cost is just wall-clock time") and is replaced: the checkpoint runs on the terminating gateway replica rather than in the pod, so the retry cost is drain wall-clock time and no agent is waiting on it. Clause (b) keeps `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` (240s at Tier 1/2, 300s at Tier 3) as the figure the headroom argument rests on, reattributed to the gateway pod, because that is the only budget large enough to cover the combined MinIO and Postgres retry budgets (30s plus 60s) and the adjacent Postgres-fallback paragraph (`spec/04:278`) already argues its own 60s budget against the same figure. The gateway preStop drain timeout (60s at Tier 1/2, 120s at Tier 3, `spec/17_deployment-topology.md:966`) is not the figure to cite: it is smaller than the combined budgets. Clause (c), the 30-second budget, and the `CheckpointStorageUnavailable` alert are unchanged. The per-session §10.1 Stage 2 tier cap is a second, tighter ceiling on the gateway-drain path and is stated in C5.

The bullet's fallback clauses are scoped to the termination class that destroys a workspace, rather than left applying to the gateway-driven checkpoint the retitled bullet now names. As written, the bullet mandates that a failed eviction checkpoint writes a minimal session state record to Postgres, increments `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total`, and resumes the session `conversation_only` with `workspaceLost: true`. On the gateway-drain trigger the agent pod keeps running with its workspace intact (`spec/10:178`), so none of that happens and none of it should (C6). The bullet therefore states two arms explicitly. On a **gateway-drain** eviction checkpoint, a failure after all retries increments `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total{trigger="eviction"}`, writes no `session_eviction_state` row, publishes no `session.lost`, and falls through to the session's last successful checkpoint (`spec/10:180`); the session is re-adopted by the replacement coordinator on its still-running pod. On an **agent-pod** eviction (node drain, the kubelet Eviction API, cluster upgrade), the existing minimal-state record, the `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total` counter, and the `conversation_only` / `workspaceLost: true` resume apply unchanged; that trigger ships here (S3, C7), so both arms are live and each names the pod class it applies to. Four adjacent passages repeat the unscoped mandate and are corrected with it. `spec/04:266` is the paragraph that defines the record ("**Eviction fallback: Postgres minimal state record.** When an eviction checkpoint cannot be persisted to MinIO, the gateway writes a minimal state record to the `session_eviction_state` table in Postgres."), and its antecedent is satisfied on exactly the path the corrected `:264` bullet forbids, because C5 makes the gateway-drain checkpoint an eviction checkpoint. Its opening sentence is scoped to the class the record represents: "When an **agent-pod** eviction checkpoint (node drain, the kubelet Eviction API, cluster upgrade) cannot be persisted to MinIO, the gateway writes a minimal state record …", followed by a sentence stating that a failed gateway-drain checkpoint writes no such record and falls through to the last successful checkpoint per §10.1. The dependent paragraphs at `:278` (the 60-second Postgres retry budget) and `:284` (the total-loss path) hang off `:266` and inherit that scope, so each names the agent-pod eviction class in its opening sentence rather than any eviction. `spec/10:132`'s `chunk_count == 0` arm (S3), the second sentence of `spec/12:294`, and the eviction-fallback sentence of `spec/12:315` are reworded the same way.

**Anchor and change (spec/15:2531, the runtime-SDK contract).** The §15.7 "What the SDKs provide" bullet reads "**Workspace utilities.** Helpers for materializing files into `/workspace`, respecting the workspace plan ([§14](14_workspace-plan-schema.md)), and uploading checkpoints ([§7.1](07_session-lifecycle.md#71-normal-flow) seal-and-export)." §15.7 is the normative description of the runtime SDKs (`sdks/runtime/{go,python,typescript}`) that runtime authors implement against, and those SDKs run inside the agent pod, so the bullet assigns a checkpoint upload to the pod. Left as it is, the applied spec would say at `spec/10:131` and `spec/04:489` that the pod issues no object-store call and at `spec/15:2531` that the pod-side SDK ships checkpoint-upload helpers, and the tier-11 assertion below would fail on it. The referenced §7.1 seal-and-export step is itself a pod-side export (`spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:212`), so the bullet is about the checkpoint path rather than about the client upload API. Restate it so the SDK provides workspace materialization helpers alone: the checkpoint archive is streamed to the gateway over the `Checkpoint` RPC and the gateway performs the object-store write.

**Anchor and change (spec/12:294).** The sentence currently reads:

```
**MinIO unavailability during checkpoint:** If MinIO is unreachable during a checkpoint upload, the adapter retries with exponential backoff as described in [Section 4.4](04_system-components.md#44-event--checkpoint-store).
```

Replace "the adapter retries" with "the gateway retries". The paragraph's closing sentence ("any pod eviction during an outage that exhausts all MinIO retries falls back to the Postgres minimal state record … sessions resume with conversation context but workspace state is lost") is scoped in the same edit to agent-pod eviction, which is the class that destroys a workspace and the class S3 and C7 wire; a gateway drain during an outage leaves the pod and its workspace running and the session falls back to its last successful checkpoint. `spec/12:315`'s eviction-fallback sentence ("The session's eviction path falls back to the Postgres minimal-state record") is scoped the same way in the same edit.

**Anchor and change (spec/05:542).** The eviction-checkpoint-ordering rule inside the §5.2 checkpoint-granularity bullet reads "The adapter processes one slot's checkpoint upload at a time, in slot-ID order." The adapter no longer uploads, so the serialization rule needs the new actor or the write-amplification argument and the preStop budget formula it feeds (enforced by the `SandboxWarmPool` validation webhook, same paragraph) rest on a process that issues no `PutObject`. Reword the two sentences: eviction checkpoints for concurrent-session pods are serialized across slots by the gateway, which drives one slot's `Checkpoint` stream and its chunk uploads at a time, in slot-ID order; the adapter archives and streams the slot it is asked for. The MinIO write-amplification rationale and the per-slot tiered cap are unchanged. The CRD budget formula in the same bullet is amended by S3, which makes the serialized Postgres fallback budget a normative term rather than deployer advice.

**Anchor and change (spec/04:253 and the checkpoint-duration SLO, spec/16:39, spec/16:629, spec/16:647, docs/operator-guide/observability.md:225).** Five sites define the checkpoint-duration SLO over a pod-side upload. `spec/04:253` states that "Checkpoint duration is dominated by workspace tar creation and MinIO upload" with an expected scaling of "~1 second per 100MB on typical node-local SSD with gigabit-class MinIO connectivity", which asserts a network path from the pod to MinIO that §13 forbids and that this change removes. `spec/16:39` defines `lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds` as "end-to-end checkpoint wall time from the initial quiescence request through snapshot upload complete", and `spec/16:629`, `spec/16:647` (the `CheckpointDurationBurnRate` row), and the `docs/operator-guide/observability.md:225` SLO-table row restate the same boundary. After this change the upload is two hops (pod → gateway over the `Checkpoint` stream, gateway → MinIO through the `ArtifactStore`), and the histogram is emitted by the gateway across both. Restate `spec/04:253` so checkpoint duration is dominated by workspace tar creation in the pod, the archive relay to the gateway, and the gateway's chunk `PutObject`s, and so the scaling constant is stated against the gateway's MinIO connectivity plus the intra-cluster pod→gateway hop rather than against the pod's own MinIO connectivity. Restate the `spec/16:39` span as running from the initial quiescence request through the final chunk's commit, measured at the gateway. The P95 < 2s target for workspaces ≤ 100 MB, the `le="2"` histogram bucket, the `CheckpointDurationHigh` and `CheckpointDurationBurnRate` alerts, and the `docs/alerting/openslo.yaml` SLI are unchanged: the added hop is an intra-cluster gRPC relay on the same channel the gateway already uses for every other adapter RPC, both legs are already inside the 2s budget the shipped one-hop path assumed, and the chunk `Put`s pipeline against the archive stream rather than following it. The staged text says so, because an operator reading a burn-rate alert must know which hop the histogram covers.

**Anchor and change (docs/reference/metrics.md's Checkpoint-metrics section and :352, the emitter attribution).** The `## Checkpoint metrics` section (`:181`, table at `:185-195`) attributes no emitter and states no scrape-target consequence for any of its rows, so a reader has no way to tell that the counters `spec/04:263` and `:264` assign to the adapter are unscrapable in the shipped topology. The section gains that statement in the same edit that moves the emitters: the upload, retry, abort-cleanup, and eviction-fallback counters are gateway-emitted and sit on the gateway's scrape target, and `lenny_checkpoint_size_exceeded_total` stays adapter-emitted in the pod and therefore outside the default scrape set, mirroring the staged `spec/16:199` row. The `## Adapter metrics` section (`:169-177`) is **confirmed unchanged**: its scoping paragraph at `:172` ("These counters are emitted by the adapter process inside each agent pod. The default scrape target set does not reach agent pods…") introduces its own two-row table, which carries `lenny_adapter_sopeercred_disabled_total` (`:176`) and `lenny_adapter_sopeercred_selftest_failed_total` (`:177`) and no checkpoint counter, so it is accurate as written and this change does not touch it. `:352` describes `lenny_artifact_upload_error_total` as counting `ArtifactStore` PUTs that failed "after the adapter's per-request retry budget was exhausted"; the retry budget on the checkpoint path is now the gateway's `checkpoint.RetryBudgetFor(trigger)` ladder (C2), so the row names the gateway's per-request retry budget. Its `auth_denied` `error_type` value (`spec/16:262`) described a pod-credential rejection that cannot occur once the pod holds no object-store credential; the value is retained because the gateway's own MinIO credential can be rejected, and `:262` says so rather than describing a pod credential.

**Anchor and change (the §16.1 metric inventory rows whose owner or subject changes).** Nine rows in the `spec/16_observability.md` inventory attribute a checkpoint metric to the adapter or define it over a single MinIO object. They are corrected together, because the inventory is the source `docs/reference/metrics.md:181-195` and `docs/operator-guide/observability.md:90-97` both mirror and the tier-11 assertion compares all three.

- `spec/16:46` and `spec/16:200`: `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total` and `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` are gateway-emitted. `:200` also drops its "non-eviction" scoping (above) and gains the `reason` label the counter is registered and emitted with, over the full value set the shipped emitters and this change produce: `retry_exhausted | kms_unavailable | tier_store_mismatch | minio_unreachable | auth | quota_exceeded | other | manifest_write_failed | no_chunks_committed` (below).
- `spec/16:198`: `lenny_checkpoint_orphaned_objects_total` "increments when checkpoint abort cleanup fails to delete partial MinIO objects". The abort sweep is the gateway's (S1, C2), and a checkpoint is now a chunk set, so a partially failed sweep needs a stated increment rule rather than an implied one. Restate: the gateway emits the counter, and it increments **once per chunk object the abort sweep could not soft-delete**, so a sweep that fails on three of thirty-two derived keys increments by three. The `pool` and `trigger` labels are unchanged. Without the per-key rule an implementer could increment once per failed sweep, and the counter would understate the orphaned-object count by up to `chunk_count`.
- `spec/16:201`: `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_partial_keys_logged_total` with its `keys_committed: 0 | 1+` bucketing. The emitter moves to the gateway with the manual-recovery key logging (`spec/04:280`). The bucketing is unchanged: its purpose is to separate a total MinIO failure (no chunk committed) from a partial upload, which the two buckets still express under the chunked model.
- `spec/16:194`: `lenny_partial_manifest_cleanup_total{outcome}`. "Partial manifest" stops being a distinct object class once every checkpoint writes a manifest row, so the counter is restated as counting cleanups of `partial = true` rows, which is the class the resume-time cleaner and the §12.5 backstop retire. Its `success | failed_deleted | gc_collected` value set is unchanged.
- `spec/16:196`: `lenny_checkpoint_partial_manifests_superseded_total{pool}`. Supersede-on-write now runs on every checkpoint attempt of every trigger rather than on the partial path alone, so the row states that it counts supersedes of active `partial = true` rows, which under S2 is the intent row of a prior attempt. Its label set is unchanged.
- `spec/16:197`: `lenny_gc_tombstones_pruned_total{table}`. Its `table` label keeps its two values and `partial_manifest` becomes `checkpoint_manifest`, matching the renamed table (C4). The row records that the per-checkpoint row count is now `chunk_count` `artifact_store` rows plus one `checkpoint_manifest` row, so the counter's volume scales with `chunkSizeBytes`; no label is added.
- `spec/16:84`: `lenny_checkpoint_storage_bytes_total{tenant_id, pool}` drives `CheckpointStorageHigh`. The gauge is bytes and is unchanged in value, and the row states that it is now summed over the chunk rows of each checkpoint rather than over one row per checkpoint, so an operator reading it against `artifact_store` finds the same total.
- `spec/16:199`: `lenny_checkpoint_size_exceeded_total{pool, level}` stays adapter-emitted, because the pre-checkpoint workspace-size probe runs in the pod (`spec/04:255`). The row says so explicitly, since it is the only checkpoint counter that keeps the adapter as its emitter.
- `spec/16:361`: the audit/trace ownership table gives `session.checkpoint` the owner "Gateway + Pod". The pod's half becomes quiesce-and-stream, and the row says so.
- `spec/16:204`: `lenny_session_suspension_checkpoint_failed_total` is already gateway-owned (`spec/06:220`) and is unchanged; it is listed to record that it was checked.

Two pre-existing label defects surface on these rows and are corrected in the same edit rather than inherited. `spec/12:315` documents `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` with a `reason="kms_unavailable"` label that its declaration at `spec/16:200` (`pool`, `level`, `trigger`) does not carry. The code is the party that is right: the counter is registered with four labels including `reason` (`pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_sessionlifecycle.go:282-286`), and **four** call sites emit it. Three are the ones the label's declared value set would suggest: `reason="retry_exhausted"`, hard-coded by the only pool/level/trigger-carrying helper (`IncCheckpointStorageFailure`, `gatewaymetrics.go:608-613`); `reason="kms_unavailable"` from the T4 fail-closed write rejection (`gatewaymetrics.go:616-631`, wired through `cmd/lenny-gateway/metricsbackfill.go:243-244`); and `reason="tier_store_mismatch"`. The fourth is a rollup: `IncArtifactUploadError` (`gatewaymetrics.go:764-782`) rolls every failed `ArtifactStore` PUT into the same counter with the store's own error classification, and it is wired in production (`cmd/lenny-gateway/metricsbackfill.go:247-248`). Its values are `minio_unreachable`, `auth`, `quota_exceeded`, and `other` (`pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:372`, and `classifyPutError` at `:441-455`), and none of them is in the three-value set. Declaring the narrow set would publish an operator-facing enum that the shipped code already violates on the most common failure, a MinIO outage, and an operator filtering on the declared values while `CheckpointStorageUnavailable` fires would see an empty series. It matters more after C2, because every checkpoint chunk `Put` then goes through that store and the rollup becomes the dominant increment path for checkpoint failures. `spec/12:315` describes shipped behavior and the incomplete declaration is `spec/16:200`, so the reconciliation runs in that direction: `spec/16:200`, the `docs/reference/metrics.md:190` label column, and the catalog help text at `pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go:289` declare the full value set (`retry_exhausted | kms_unavailable | tier_store_mismatch | minio_unreachable | auth | quota_exceeded | other | manifest_write_failed | no_chunks_committed`), state that the rollup and KMS increments carry empty `pool`, `level`, and `trigger` labels (so the sentence "the `trigger` label carries which checkpoint path failed" is scoped to the driver-emitted increments), and `spec/12:315` keeps its `reason="kms_unavailable"` identification.

The last two values are new with this change, and they are what keep its two new increment conditions inside the enum. The degraded barrier dispatch (a failed intent-row INSERT, a failed same-transaction `coordination_lease` read, or a lock the reservation cannot take) is a Postgres or contention failure rather than an exhausted MinIO retry, and it takes `reason="manifest_write_failed"`. The `chunk_count == 0` fall-through (the pod never acked, or the archive stream produced no bytes) takes `reason="no_chunks_committed"`. Neither has a value in the shipped set, and the only pool/level/trigger-carrying helper hard-codes `retry_exhausted` (`gatewaymetrics.go:608-613`), so without these values an implementer would stamp `retry_exhausted` and an operator paged during a Postgres failover overlapping a gateway drain would read that MinIO exhausted its retries while MinIO was healthy. C2 therefore gives `IncCheckpointStorageFailure` a `reason` parameter (`IncCheckpointStorageFailure(pool, level, trigger, reason string)`) rather than a second helper, and its existing call sites pass `retry_exhausted`. The `T4KmsKeyUnusable` alert is not a substitute for it: it fires on the leader-elected probe gauge (`pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:368-372`, `lenny_t4_kms_probe_last_success_timestamp`) rather than on a rejected checkpoint write. `spec/16:274` declares `lenny_session_eviction_total_loss_total` with labels `pool` and `reason: minio_unavailable | postgres_unavailable | both_unavailable` while `spec/04:287` declares it with `pool` and `had_prior_checkpoint`; C7 gives the counter its first emitter, so the two declarations must agree before it can be emitted. `spec/04:287`'s `had_prior_checkpoint` is the one the operator-facing text at `:287` reasons about and the one `SessionEvictionTotalLoss` (`docs/alerting/rules.yaml:242`) describes, so `spec/16:274` is corrected to `pool` and `had_prior_checkpoint`.

**Anchor and change (the operator pages that name the wrong actor or the wrong counter).** Five authored operator pages describe an actor this change removes, a knob that never existed, or a counter whose meaning it widens, and each is a page an operator reads while the checkpoint path is failing.

- `docs/runbooks/checkpoint-stale.md` is the page the `CheckpointStale` alert routes to and it becomes the primary gateway-side-I/O runbook. Its diagnosis (`:33`, `:63`, `:66`) attributes checkpoint latency to the pod's object-store path; restate it so the bottleneck is the gateway's chunk `PutObject` latency, keeping `lenny_minio_put_duration_seconds` as the signal. Its remediation section references three names that exist nowhere in `spec/` or `charts/`: `checkpoint.maxSizeBytes` (`:103`), `checkpoint.postgresFallbackMaxBytes` (`:107`), and `checkpointWorker.concurrency` (`:116`, described as "the per-gateway checkpoint-worker count"). The first two are removed from the page, because the size ceiling an operator can act on is `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` (`spec/04:255`). `checkpointWorker.concurrency` is the one of the three that names something the gateway-side upload path actually needs, and S2's Helm-value block below defines it: the page's reference is retargeted at that key rather than deleted. The page's Step 4 diagnosis (`:72`, the P95 of `lenny_checkpoint_size_bytes` grouped by pool) is kept: the histogram is declared at `spec/16:39` and registered in the metric catalog (`pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go:125`), and it is the only per-checkpoint size signal an operator has, which `lenny_checkpoint_storage_bytes_total` (a per-tenant aggregate storage gauge, `spec/16:84`) cannot answer. Only its framing changes: the histogram is gateway-emitted after this change and is measured over the committed chunk set, which S1 states at `spec/16:39` in the same edit that restates the duration histogram's span. The `:133-134` escalation targets are unchanged.
- `docs/runbooks/minio-failure.md`: `:13` and `:15` describe workspace upload/download failures and exhausted checkpoint upload retries, which stay true with the new retrier; `:105` ("In-flight sessions continue running. The pod-local workspace is intact. Only upload/download to the artifact store is blocked.") stays true and becomes load-bearing, because it is the state a gateway-drain checkpoint failure leaves the session in; `:152` ("the workspace tarball was never uploaded") is restated over the chunk set, since a failed checkpoint can leave a committed prefix of chunks under a `partial = true` row. `:31` and `:106` are corrected by the `CheckpointStorageUnavailable` block above.
- `docs/operator-guide/upgrades.md:47-53` states that "the controller sends a `CheckpointBarrier` to all active pods". The barrier is dispatched by the draining gateway replica's preStop hook (`spec/10:165`) rather than by a controller. Correct the actor; the `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_total` monitoring instruction at `:313` is unchanged.
- `docs/operator-guide/disaster-recovery.md:279` states that "Before draining a node, the WarmPoolController performs a MinIO health check webhook." The check is the `lenny-drain-readiness` `ValidatingAdmissionWebhook` on `pods/eviction`, which calls the gateway's `GET /internal/drain-readiness` (`spec/12:298`, `:303`). Correct the actor on that line; the forced-drain override sentence at `:281` is unchanged. In the complete-MinIO-outage list, `:251` ("Eviction checkpoints fall back to Postgres minimal state") is scoped to the agent-pod eviction class, because a gateway drain during an outage leaves the pod and its workspace running and the session falls through to its last successful checkpoint (S1's split of the two arms). `:249` ("Checkpoint uploads fail -- sessions continue without new checkpoints") and the `:106-107` data-loss impact rows stay true and are confirmed unchanged.
- `docs/operator-guide/troubleshooting.md`'s "Checkpoint Failures" section (`:115-143`) names no pod actor: its diagnosis shells into the gateway Deployment to probe MinIO (`:126-127`) and its resolution rows name MinIO, `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`, workspace hygiene, and the runtime's `checkpoint_ready` latency (`:140-143`), all of which stay accurate. The edit it does need is on its two symptom rows. `:119` names `CheckpointStorageUnavailable`, whose expression gains the `reason!~"manifest_write_failed|no_chunks_committed"` selector and whose text stops being eviction-scoped (above), and `:120` names `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total`, which widens to every trigger and gains the `reason` label (above). Both rows are restated so an operator reading a `manifest_write_failed` or a `no_chunks_committed` increment is not sent to restore a healthy MinIO. The diagnosis and resolution blocks are unchanged.

**Anchor and change (the seal path shares the sink, spec/07:112 and docs/runbooks/workspace-seal-stuck.md).** `spec/07:112`'s seal-and-export invariant ("The workspace is always exported to durable storage before the pod is released") is satisfied by the same `Checkpoint` stream and the same gateway-side upload as every other trigger (C1, C2), so the seal does not become a second object-store owner. The invariant text is unchanged, and this proposal records that it was checked, because a spec in which the seal wrote pod-side and every other trigger wrote gateway-side would have two owners for one object class. `docs/runbooks/workspace-seal-stuck.md` splits the failure into a pod-side and a store-side arm, and only the store-side arm changes actor. `:53` (`archive_fs_error` — "pod-side tar / stream error; pod may be unhealthy"), `:88` ("Step 3 — Pod-side error"), and `:121` ("Platform engineering for pod-side archive errors that recur") keep their meaning verbatim, because the adapter still creates the archive and pod-side archive errors stay pod-side under C1. The two lines that attribute the seal's object-store write to a pod-side uploader are `:54` ("`credential_denied` — uploader's credential is revoked or mis-scoped") and `:102` ("If uploader credentials were revoked, rotate and confirm the uploader ServiceAccount has `WriteObject` on the artifacts bucket"), and both are restated on the gateway: the uploader is the gateway, the credential is the one its `ArtifactStore` holds, and the agent pod holds no object-store credential on the seal path or on any other. `:51` (`minio_dial_tcp` / `s3_request_timeout`) is restated in the same edit so the dial is the gateway's. Without these two edits the page would still send on-call to rotate a pod-side uploader ServiceAccount for a write the pod no longer performs.

**Anchor and change (schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:100-103 and docs/api/internal.md:229-231, the protocol-root assignment).** The `Checkpoint` RPC's doc comment states that it "requests an immediate checkpoint of the current session state **to the artifact store**", which is the pod-writes-MinIO assertion at the protocol root, and `docs/api/internal.md:229` and `:231` have the adapter "perform the snapshot" and "take the snapshot immediately". Restate all three so the adapter archives the session's workspace and streams it to the gateway, which writes it to the artifact store. P1 rewrites the RPC signature in the same edit; this block records the doc-comment prose so a third-party adapter author reading only the proto does not implement an object-store client.

### S2. Make the chunked-object model the single upload path, define the full-checkpoint object-key layout, and state the artifact-store accounting

**Target:** `spec/10_gateway-internals.md` §10.1 (`:126`, `:130`, `:132`, `:133`, `:135`, `:137`, `:139`, `:141`, `:147`, `:151`, `:155`, `:157`, `:175`); `spec/04_system-components.md` §4.4 (`:235`, `:237`, `:249`, `:280`); `spec/07_session-lifecycle.md` §7.2 (`:138`); `spec/11_policy-and-controls.md` §11.2 (`:35`); `spec/12_storage-architecture.md` (`:289`, `:292`, `:305`, `:307`, `:321`, `:325`, `:326`, `:328`, `:330`, `:331`, `:334`, `:336`, `:337`, `:338`, `:339`, `:340`, `:345`, `:346`, `:348`, `:349`, `:351`, `:353`, `:766`, `:768`, `:832`); `spec/15_external-api-surface.md` (`:1026`); `spec/16_observability.md` (`:607`); `spec/17_deployment-topology.md` (`:529`, `:900`, `:919`, `:920`, `:1208-1212`, `:1216`, `:1221-1225`, `:1455`, `:1461`); `spec/18_build-sequence.md` (`:398-415`); `spec/19_resolved-decisions.md` (`:7`); `charts/lenny/values.yaml`, `charts/lenny/values.schema.json`, `charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml`, `charts/lenny/templates/minio-bucket-lifecycle-job.yaml`; `docs/operator-guide/multi-tenancy.md` (`:279-284`); `docs/reference/configuration.md` (`:31`, `:337`); `docs/client-guide/wire-format.md` (`:183`); `docs/client-guide/streaming.md` (`:64`); `docs/runtime-author-guide/lifecycle.md` (`:230-236`); `docs/operator-guide/disaster-recovery.md` (`:271-273`); `docs/getting-started/concepts.md` (`:98`, `:434-440`); `docs/getting-started/architecture.md` (`:204-210`); `TESTING.md` (`:900`, `:1025`, `:1066`, `:1074`, `:1182`, `:1231`).

`spec/12:349` is GC concurrency rule 6, which restates the backstop's row-selection predicate; editing `:328` without it would leave the rule pinning the sweep to the narrower predicate. `spec/12:346` is GC concurrency rule 3, which rests its idempotency argument on the raw MinIO `DeleteObject` verb and names checkpoint rotation and TTL expiration explicitly. `spec/10:175` is BarrierAck rule 5, which carries a fifth statement of the backstop's row-selection predicate, and `spec/10:155`'s generation-stale-cleanup sentence carries a sixth, in the same words.

**Change (the unification).** Rewrite the §10.1 partial-manifest subsection so the chunked upload is the path for every checkpoint trigger rather than for the tier-cap and BarrierAck-deadline triggers alone:

- The intent-row write (`:130`) happens at the start of **every** checkpoint attempt. Drop the scoping clause "whose upload path is the chunked partial-upload path (i.e., eviction / preStop / barrier-triggered checkpoints subject to the tier cap or to the BarrierAck deadline)".
- `partial_object_key_prefix` becomes `object_key_prefix`, and the column carries exactly one value: the string prefix `{tenant_id}/checkpoint/{session_id}/{checkpoint_id}-chunk-`. Chunk keys are `object_key_prefix + {n}.{chunk_encoding}` for `n` in `[0, chunk_count)`. The column is renamed at `:130`, `:132`, `:133`, `:135`, `:137`, `:141`, `:155`, and `:157`, and the supersede-on-write delete at `:137` derives its key set the same way instead of listing under a prefix. `:132` is the BarrierAck-handler sentence of the finalisation step, which names the column and is otherwise edited by S3.
- Chunk objects are therefore named `{checkpoint_id}-chunk-{n}.{chunk_encoding}` with `{n}` a zero-padded five-digit monotonic index, replacing `partial-{n}.{chunk_encoding}` at `:139`, at `spec/04:235`, at `spec/04:280` (the manual-recovery key logging), and in the `:155` reassembly steps. That filename is the `PartID` handed to the `ArtifactStore`, so the object key keeps the canonical four-segment §12.5 format `/{tenant_id}/{object_type}/{session_id}/{filename}` (`spec/12:307`) and the `lenny-blob://` URI round-trips through `blobstore.ParseURI`. The object-type segment is the singular `checkpoint`, matching the `blobstore` object-type enum (`pkg/blobstore/blobstore.go:151-153`). Prefix scoping on S3 and MinIO is string-prefix scoping rather than directory scoping, so no path segment is added and the five-segment key the §5 non-goals reject never appears.
- `checkpoint_timeout_at` (`:130`, `:141`) is populated on every intent row and carries the attempt's own deadline: the §10.1 Stage 2 tier cap on the gateway-drain path, `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` on the barrier path, the pod-declared `budget_ms` deadline on the §4.6.1 agent-pod eviction path, whose slots are all reserved at handoff time and executed serially inside that one budget (C7), and the §4.4 60-second checkpoint timeout (`spec/04:245`, `checkpoint.CheckpointTimeout`, `pkg/checkpoint/checkpoint.go:158-160`) on the deadline-less triggers (periodic and seal), which §4.4 already applies to "all checkpoint paths". The rule behind the four values is one rule: the column records the wall clock by which the attempt that owns the row settles, so a peer reading the row can tell a running attempt from an abandoned one. A NULL value for the deadline-less triggers would leave the §12.5 backstop with no way to tell an in-flight attempt from an abandoned one, which is the discriminator the backstop bullet below rests on.
- `manifest_reason` (`:130`, `:141`) is populated at finalisation for `partial = true` rows and is NULL for `partial = false` rows. It is no longer `NOT NULL`. S4 reduces its value set.
- The `:126` framing paragraph is reworded so a partial manifest is the outcome of a truncated stream rather than a separate flow, and `:151`'s reference to "the coexisting full-checkpoint rows in the same `checkpoint_manifest` table" becomes a statement of the table's contents rather than an aside.
- Supersede-on-write (`:137`) gains a `coordination_generation` guard. Its shipped predicate keys only on `partial = true AND deleted_at IS NULL AND session_id = $session AND slot_id = $slot AND checkpoint_id != $new_checkpoint_id`, with no generation term, and the fencing `spec/10:155` describes is a read-side fence on the resume path alone. The manifest row and the chunk objects are shared across replicas, and `spec/10:165` states that the in-memory lease cache produces false-positive barriers for sessions just handed off, so a stale coordinator can reach the supersede transaction while the new coordinator's upload is in flight. The predicate therefore gains the following conjunct, which is the single form every other section of this proposal repeats verbatim:

  ```
  AND (coordination_generation < $new_generation
       OR (coordination_generation = $new_generation
           AND (manifest_reason IS NOT NULL OR checkpoint_timeout_at < now())))
  ```

  `$new_generation` is the authoritative `sessions.coordination_generation`, read in the same transaction that commits the intent row and written onto the row, and the staged `spec/10:137` text says so. It is the counter the `spec/10:33` handoff CAS writes and the value `CoordinatorFence` announces to the pod (`pkg/gateway/coordination/coordfence/coordfence.go:144`), so the row's generation and the pod's fence generation are the same number. The `coordination_lease` mirror is not that number: the sweeper upserts the generation it read before `RecordHandoff` bumped it and the upsert is best-effort (`pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:109`, `:145`, `:150`, `:169-171`, `:178-194`), so a mirror-sourced row would carry the victim's own generation and the strictly-higher arm below would never fire.

  An attempt that finds an active row at a **higher** generation aborts rather than superseding it, which is the CPS-006 fence. A **strictly higher** generation supersedes unconditionally, because a strictly higher generation exists only after a §10.1 handoff, and the handoff's `CoordinatorFence` (`spec/10:35`) is a hard precondition of every gateway→pod RPC, which P1 and C1 make true of the `Checkpoint` stream by putting `coordination_generation` on the request and validating it at the adapter, so the displaced writer's upload is fenced at the pod: its remaining chunk `Put`s are wasted bytes the widened backstop sweeps from the soft-deleted row, and its counter and finalising `UPDATE`s no-op under the `deleted_at IS NULL` guard and abort the attempt (S3). Without that arm the `spec/10:165` replacement coordinator and the C7 termination handler could never supersede the row of the coordinator they replaced (the victim's row carries no `manifest_reason` and sits inside its own deadline, which is the definition of an upload still running), their intent-row INSERT would collide with `partial_manifest_active_uniq`, and every handed-off session would take the minimal-state fallback with a healthy object store and a live pod. At **equal** generation the settled-row term decides, and two attempts of the same generation are the common case: the periodic ticker (`checkpointer.go:122`, `:173`) is an independent goroutine that nothing pauses on drain, `prestop.Hook.run` fires the barrier as its first drain act (`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/prestop/prestop.go:354`), and both read the same `sessions.coordination_generation` for the session. A same-generation row whose attempt is still running is never superseded and the attempt that would have superseded it aborts instead, because no fence stands between two same-generation writers. `manifest_reason` is NULL on an intent row and is written non-NULL by the `partial = true` finalisation (S3, C4), so the first arm admits every row whose attempt has stopped, including one the lock preemption above cut short and one whose archive stream truncated well inside its deadline; the second arm admits a row whose attempt has passed its own deadline without finalising, which is an attempt abandoned by a crashed writer. A term keyed on `checkpoint_timeout_at` alone would not do this, because the column is written once at intent-row INSERT and no settlement rewrites it, so a preempted or early-truncated attempt would leave an active row inside its own deadline that no later attempt could supersede: the drain-time barrier flush and the C5 eviction checkpoint would abort on the guard, and their intent-row INSERT would collide with `partial_manifest_active_uniq`. Without the guards, a stale or same-generation concurrent writer would soft-delete the live row and `SoftDelete` the chunks of a running upload under it, after which the victim's finalising `UPDATE` no-ops under its own `deleted_at IS NULL` guard and the checkpoint is lost silently. The guard leaves one residual window, closed by two mechanisms stated below. A superseded writer that still believes it holds the lease can land the `PutObject` for the one index whose counter `UPDATE` committed before the supersede, and that chunk then sits under a soft-deleted row. The writer closes it on the primary path: its next counter `UPDATE` affects zero rows, which aborts the attempt (S1) and takes the abort sweep over the key set it derives from the row it holds (`spec/04:249` as staged by S1). When the superseded writer has no further chunk to write, the same zero-row observation lands on its finalising `UPDATE`, which carries the `checkpoint_id = $id AND deleted_at IS NULL` guard and is likewise an abort (S3), so the writer records no session-record stamp and no retention row over a chunk set the new coordinator has already deleted. The backstop closes it on the crash path: its row-selection predicate is widened to soft-deleted rows inside the tombstone-retention window, below. A per-`(session_id, slot_id)` single-flight lock in the gateway's upload driver orders the periodic ticker (`pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:122-133`), `Seal` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:91`), the barrier dispatcher (`pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier/barrier.go:184`), and the eviction path within one replica, and every one of them mints, supersedes, and INSERTs inside it (C2). It is the mechanism that orders same-generation attempts, which the generation fence cannot do; the generation guard, the settled-row term, and `partial_manifest_active_uniq` are what hold across replicas.

**Change (quota reservation and the hard stream cap, spec/11:35).** `spec/11:35` mandates the atomic Redis reservation before any checkpoint byte reaches MinIO and states that "for checkpoint writes the adapter supplies the tar size from the workspace-size probe before upload begins". The gateway is now the first writer, so the sentence is restated: the adapter reports the probed workspace size to the gateway in the leading `CheckpointStart` frame (P1), and the gateway reserves that byte count before the first chunk `Put`.

The same paragraph's **hard stream cap** sentence is restated to cover the checkpoint path, which it could not cover before this change because no checkpoint byte crossed the gateway. The declared size is now a pod self-report, and `spec/11:41` refuses to let a pod's self-report drive quota accounting, so the reservation alone is bypassable: an adapter that sends `CheckpointStart{probed_bytes: 1}` and then streams an arbitrarily large archive would have every chunk written with `projected ≤ storageQuotaBytes` never re-evaluated. The gateway therefore wraps the inbound `Checkpoint` archive stream in an `io.LimitedReader` set to `remaining_quota_bytes = storageQuotaBytes − storage_bytes_used`, exactly as the workspace-upload path already does (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload.go:337`, and the over-stream abort at `:442`). An adapter that streams past the cap has its attempt aborted with `STORAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` and takes the abort sweep. `CheckpointStart.probed_bytes` is the advisory pre-check that sizes the reservation, and the cap is what enforces the quota.

The rejection code (`STORAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED`) and the atomicity of the Lua script are unchanged. The release-on-failure sentence ("If the upload subsequently fails, the gateway decrements the reservation") is amended for the chunked checkpoint path, where a failed attempt can leave committed chunks behind: a chunk `Put` never increments the Redis counter (`cataloging.Store.Put` inserts the catalog row and issues no quota adjustment, `pkg/blobstore/cataloging/cataloging.go:143-176`), so the committed bytes sit on the counter as the unreleased part of the reservation, and each chunk's `SoftDelete` releases exactly its own `artifact_size_bytes` (`cataloging.go:240-268`). On an abort with `N` committed chunks the gateway therefore releases only `probed_bytes − workspace_bytes_uploaded` and lets the abort sweep's per-chunk `SoftDelete` release the rest. Releasing the whole reservation in addition to the per-chunk deletes would decrement the same bytes twice and drift `storage_bytes_used` permanently below actual usage, since nothing reconciles the counter until a Redis restart rehydrates it from Postgres (`pkg/gateway/quota/storagequota/recovery.go:33-40`); releasing nothing would leak up to `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` per aborted attempt and drive a tenant to `STORAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` across repeated aborts during a MinIO outage. On a finalisation (`partial = false` or `partial = true`) the gateway releases the same remainder, and the committed chunks keep their bytes on the counter through their `artifact_store` rows until they are deleted. When the attempt commits no chunk the whole reservation is released.
