`pkg/adapter/oplock.go` (`Begin` `:57-73`, the pod-global op-lock a fenced stream must release before the new coordinator's `Checkpoint` contends for it); `pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/coordinatorfence.go` (`Client.CoordinatorFence` `:48-54`, which stamps the new `CoordinatorFenceRequest.slot_id`), `pkg/gateway/coordination/coordfence/coordfence.go` (`FenceClient` `:62-64`, `Fencer.Fence` `:137`, and `fence` `:143`, which carry the slot through the retry/relinquish loop), and `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go` (`CoordinationFencer` `:822`) with `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go` (`fenceResumedPod` `:3176-3180`), which are the fence's only senders and which pass no slot today, so the field P1 adds would arrive empty and the adapter's `useSlot` (`pkg/adapter/slot.go:39-41`) would take the pod-global branch on every fence; `cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go` (`:258`); `cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:146`; `cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go:118`; `pkg/adapter/checkpoint_test.go` and `pkg/adapter/resume_test.go`, both of which drive the deleted seams and the renamed messages in every case and are rewritten onto the streaming handlers (§6, §10).

**Change (the generation gate on the byte RPCs).** `Checkpoint` and `Resume` validate `coordination_generation` against the slot's `lastFenced` state before they do any other work, returning `FailedPrecondition: coordinator_handoff_stale` exactly as `CheckpointBarrier` does today (`pkg/adapter/coordination.go:143-159`), and accepting the request when the slot's fence state is uninitialized. The C2 drivers stamp the intent row's `coordination_generation` on both requests.

**Change (the fence cancels the superseded generation's in-flight work).** The entry gate alone does not make `spec/10:37` true of the applied implementation, and the strictly-higher-generation supersede arm rests on more than it can deliver. The gate runs once, when the RPC arrives, and a displaced coordinator's `Checkpoint` stream is in flight precisely because it arrived and passed the gate before the fence advanced `lastFenced`; nothing re-evaluates it. The adapter's only in-flight-cancellation path fires on a generation gap greater than 1 (`coordination.go:91-95`), while the `spec/10:33` CAS bumps by exactly one, and even that path is documented as unimplemented (`coordination.go:62-64`, `:92-95`). The victim's handler therefore holds the pod-global op-lock it takes at `checkpoint.go:178` (`s.ops.Begin(ctx, opCheckpoint)`, released by `defer` at `:190`) for the whole life of its stream, up to the 60-second `checkpoint.CheckpointTimeout` (`pkg/checkpoint/checkpoint.go:160`), and the new coordinator's eviction `Checkpoint` for the same slot blocks in `opLock.Begin` (`pkg/adapter/oplock.go:57-73`) or is coalesced into `codes.Aborted` (`checkpoint.go:180-186`) and drops the slot to the `workspace_lost = true` minimal-state fallback against a healthy object store and a quiescable pod, which is the outcome `leasestore.Steal` was added to prevent (P1, C7).

C1 therefore builds the cancellation the restated `spec/10:36` mandates (S3). The `Checkpoint` and `Resume` handlers register a cancel func for their own stream, keyed on `(session_id, slot_id)` and tagged with the `coordination_generation` they passed the entry gate with, on the same per-slot state that carries `lastFenced` and `initialized`. `CoordinatorFence` (`coordination.go:67-119`), which touches only `s.coord` today and never `s.ops`, invokes every registered cancel func whose generation is below the fence's `new_generation` **before** it records the new generation and acknowledges, so the victim's handler returns and its `defer release()` frees the op-lock while the fence is still in flight, strictly before the new coordinator issues its first RPC (`spec/10:35` makes the fence ack a hard precondition of step 3). A cancelled stream ends with `FailedPrecondition: coordinator_handoff_stale`, the same status the entry gate returns, so the gateway-side driver takes one abort arm for both. The two together are what make `spec/10:37` true and what license the strictly-higher-generation supersede (S2, §2).

**Change (produce).** `Checkpoint` resolves the request's `slot_id` first: on a concurrent pod it validates through `checkSlotSession(sessionID, slotID)` and resolves the slot's roots (`workspaceRootForSlot`, `pkg/adapter/slot.go:119`, plus that slot's `/sessions` subtree), and on a single-session pod it keeps `checkSession` and the pod-global roots, which is the `useSlot(slotID)` branch every other slot-scoped handler already takes (`pkg/adapter/attach.go:42`). Without that routing the handler cannot serve a `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pod at all: its pod-global gate can never pass there (`pkg/adapter/session.go:234-236`) and `checkpointRoots()` would archive the wrong tree. It then runs the existing quiesce and the pre-checkpoint size probe against the resolved root, sends the probed byte count in the leading `CheckpointStart` frame, archives the two-root bundle (the resolved workspace root plus the `/sessions` tmpfs, `checkpointRoots()` at `checkpoint.go:311-323`, which takes the resolved root as its input rather than `s.WorkspaceRoot`) in the `CheckpointRequest.chunk_encoding` the gateway selected into the gRPC server stream as `archive_chunk` frames, and closes with a `CheckpointSummary` carrying the total archived byte count. C2 puts the manifest row's `slot_id` on the request.

**Change (fence and barrier).** `CoordinatorFence` (`coordination.go:72`) and `CheckpointBarrier` (`coordination.go:137`) take the same `useSlot` branch, and the fence's `lastFenced` / `initialized` state moves from the pod-global `coordinationState` (`coordination.go:26-34`) onto the per-slot state, so each session on a concurrent pod is fenced at its own `coordination_generation` (P1). Without it the C7 handoff cannot fence a concurrent pod and every one of its slots falls to the minimal-state fallback. An adapter that cannot produce the requested encoding fails the stream rather than substituting another one, because the encoding is already committed on the manifest row and is the suffix of every derived chunk key (P1). `archiveAndStore` loses its sink and becomes `archiveAndStream`. The `CheckpointSink`, `CheckpointAborter`, and `CheckpointSource` interfaces, the `Server.Checkpoints`, `Server.CheckpointAborter`, and `Server.Restorer` fields, and both `codes.Unimplemented` branches are deleted.

**Change (lifecycle result).** `archiveAndStore` today converts its storage result into the `cpStatus` handed to `s.Lifecycle.CompleteCheckpoint` (`checkpoint.go:412-418`). The adapter no longer observes the upload, so `checkpoint_complete.status` reports whether the archive stream completed, matching the S1 redefinition of the event across `spec/04:721`, `schemas/lifecycle-events.schema.json:91-103`, `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md:541`, and `docs/runtime-author-guide/integration-levels.md:145-175`. The adapter releases the runtime from quiescence as soon as its archive stream ends, regardless of what the gateway's upload does afterwards.

**Change (restore).** `Resume` reads the `ResumeStart` frame and resolves its `slot_id` first, exactly as `Checkpoint` does: on a concurrent pod it validates through `checkSlotSession(sessionID, slotID)` and resolves the slot's root with `workspaceRootForSlot` (`pkg/adapter/slot.go:119`), and on a single-session pool it keeps the pod-global path. This replaces the shipped `claimSession(sessionID)` (`resume.go:49`), which sets the pod-global session and returns `codes.Unavailable` for the second slot of a concurrent pod (`pkg/adapter/session.go:363-368`), and the pod-global `s.WorkspaceRoot` (`resume.go:40`), against which the `expected_workspace_root` guard is compared (`:63`) and which is not the slot's tree. Without that routing the restore side keeps the pod-global gate the produce side sheds, and the per-slot checkpoints C7 writes have no restore path. It then applies the existing `expected_workspace_bytes` / `workspace_size_limit_bytes` and `expected_workspace_root` pre-extraction guards against the resolved root, and feeds the concatenated `archive_chunk` bytes into one decompress-then-untar pipeline. Extraction targets a staging directory that is a **sibling of the resolved workspace root**, so the rule holds for both `/workspace/current` and `/workspace/slots/{slotId}/current` (`spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:373`, `:392`), and the staging directory is renamed onto the workspace root only after the pipeline completes with end-truncation as the sole observed error. The archive's `/sessions` tree is extracted to a staging path and renamed onto the tmpfs in the same commit step; on the abort path both staging trees are removed and neither the workspace root nor `/sessions` is mutated.

**Change (barrier).** `flushBarrierCheckpoint` (`coordination.go:193-222`) stops producing a checkpoint. The `CheckpointBarrier` handler quiesces and acknowledges immediately, so the unary handler never blocks on a stream the gateway has yet to open. `archiveAndStore` and `runAbortCleanup` drop out of the barrier path. The gateway side of the flush lives in `pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier`, which is the package that actually sends the barrier and reads the ack: `Coordinator.dispatchOne` (`barrier.go:184-210`) allocates the barrier id, calls `Dispatcher.Send` (`barrier.go:82-87`), and persists the ack's `CheckpointRef` into `sessioncheckpointmeta` (`sessioncheckpointmeta.go:43-45`), and `PodDispatcher.Send` (`wiring.go:44-61`) is the caller of `adapterclient.Client.CheckpointBarrier`. `prestop.BarrierDispatcher` (`prestop.go:203-205`) is `Dispatch(ctx context.Context) error` with no session, request, or ack in its signature, and `fireBarrier` (`prestop.go:472-479`) only calls it, so it carries no seam at which a per-session `checkpoint_id` could be minted or an ack observed. `prestop.fireBarrier` stays the bounded fan-out caller; the per-session mint, the intent-row commit, the `checkpoint_id` on `CheckpointBarrierRequest`, and the on-ack drive of the C2 upload driver all land in `barrier.Coordinator` (C2). `CheckpointBarrierResponse.checkpoint_ref` echoes the gateway-minted `checkpoint_id` the barrier request carried, so `barrier.Ack.CheckpointRef` and `sessioncheckpointmeta.Record.CheckpointRef` are restated as that echoed id rather than as a pod-produced storage reference.

**Change (metrics).** `recordStorageFailure` (`checkpoint.go:380-400`) and `CheckpointTriggerLabel` (`server.go:241-245`) move to the gateway with the upload, and `lenny_checkpoint_orphaned_objects_total` moves with the abort sweep (its only emitter today is `runAbortCleanup`, `checkpoint.go:297-298`). `CheckpointPoolLabel` (`server.go:232-235`) and `CheckpointLevelLabel` (`:236-240`) stay adapter-side: `recordSizeExceeded` (`checkpoint.go:265-266`, `:274-275`) needs both, `spec/04_system-components.md:255` mandates the pool and level labels on `lenny_checkpoint_size_exceeded_total`, and `pkg/adapter/credentials.go:149` reads `CheckpointPoolLabel`.

**Change (wiring).** `cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go` today assigns `WorkspaceRoot`, `WorkspaceBase`, `ArtifactsRoot`, and `SessionsRoot` and leaves `CheckpointMetrics`, `CheckpointEvents`, `WorkspaceSize`, `WorkspaceSizeLimitBytes`, and the pool and level labels nil or zero. Wire all of them, so the retained adapter-side telemetry (the size probe, the `checkpoint.skipped` event) is live rather than dead after the sink deletion.

### C2. Add the gateway-side checkpoint upload and restore drivers behind the ArtifactStore

**Target:** `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go` (`snapshot` `:236-291`, whose first act is the `Registry.Get` that the stale-binding check extends; `Run` `:122-133` and `Sweep` `:173-178`, the serial periodic producer that becomes a bounded worker pool; `recordRetention` `:311-334`, whose `Rotate` call at `:331` discards the rotated-out records) and `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/registry.go` (`Remove`, whose only caller today is `PodExecutor.Release` at `pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:313`, so a binding whose pod is destroyed while the session lives on has no reclaimer) and `partialcleanup.go` (`PartialChunkDeleter` `:24-30`; `CleanupPartialManifest` `:94-101`; `PartialCleaner.CleanupAfterResume` `:145-157`) with its shipped unit suite `partialcleanup_test.go` (`stubChunkDeleter` `:15-22`, which implements the deleted `DeleteByPrefix`; `seedManifest` `:45-53`; the prefix-deletion assertion at `:71-72`; and `TestCleanupPartialManifestNilDeleterSkipsMinIO` `:146`, which pins the nil-deleter skip this change removes); a new restore driver package under `pkg/gateway/checkpoint`; `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointretention/checkpointretention.go` (`Rotate` `:188`) ; `pkg/gateway/storage/retentiongc/retentiongc.go` (`ArtifactDeleter` `:67`, the per-session delete at `:252`) and its wiring at `cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go:665-670`; `pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier` (`Target` `barrier.go:59-70`, which gains `Pool` and `SlotID`; `Dispatcher.Send` `barrier.go:85-87`; `Metrics` `barrier.go:100-102`; `Coordinator.Dispatch` `barrier.go:164-166` and `dispatchOne` `barrier.go:183-210`; `PodDispatcher.Send` and `MirrorTargetLister.Targets` `wiring.go:44-61` and `:112-143`, the two target producers that populate the new fields; and the shipped `barrier_test.go` and `wiring_test.go`, whose fakes carry the pre-widening signatures) and `pkg/gateway/session/sessioncheckpointmeta` (`Record.CheckpointRef` `:43-45`); `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/prestop/prestop.go` (`fireBarrier` `:472-479`); `pkg/blobstore/blobstore.go` (the `Store` interface `:306-321`, `TenantScoped` `:834-933`, and `MemoryStore` / `FilesystemStore`), `pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go` (`Put` `:259`, `Get` `:487`, `SoftDelete` `:786`, `putRetryBackoffs` `:44`), `pkg/blobstore/cataloging/cataloging.go`, and the other four backends `blobproviderflags.Resolve` (`pkg/blobstore/providerflags/providerflags.go:105-150`) can return (`pkg/blobstore/s3/s3.go`, whose `Put` at `:222-223` opens on `context.Background()`; `pkg/blobstore/gcs/gcs.go`; `pkg/blobstore/azureblob/azureblob.go`; and `pkg/blobstore/fsstore.go`), plus the startup assertion in `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go` (`:1035-1090`); `pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go` (`Checkpoint` `:466-477`; `CheckpointBarrier`; `Resume` `:618+`); `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/binder.go` (`Binder.Resume` `:1534-1539`, `ResumeRequest`, and `ResumeResult` `:657-667`); `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go` (`resumeOnPod` `:3084-3131`, the `WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref` gate and the `CheckpointID` it passes); `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go` (`SessionResponse` `:2021-2032` and `toResponse` `:3141-3172`, the §15.1 envelope and its projection, neither of which carries a reason field today) with `pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.json` (the `Session` schema `:110-136`) and the three client SDK session types (`sdks/client/go/lenny/types.go:62`, `sdks/client/python/lenny/types.py:106`, `sdks/client/typescript/src/types.ts:59`); `pkg/gateway/quota/storagequota/failover.go` (`Reserve` `:91`); `pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics` (`lenny_checkpoint_partial_total` and `IncCheckpointPartial`, `gatewaymetrics_podlifecycle.go:112-117` and `gatewaymetrics.go:1247-1256`; `IncCheckpointStorageFailure` `gatewaymetrics.go:608-613`; the `lenny_gc_tombstones_pruned_total` `table` label values, `gatewaymetrics_retention.go:67-73`; and `gatewaymetrics_elicitation_test.go:207` and `:234`, the two shipped tests that pin the hard-coded `reason` and the `partial_manifest` table label); `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go`, `replay.go`, `session_subresources.go`; `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go` (`:1062`, `:1090`, `:1842`, `:1878`), `cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go` (`:599-610`, `:617`, `:621`), `cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go` (`:665-670`).

**Change (upload driver).** The upload driver lives in `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer`, which already owns the `Registry`, `Sessions`, `Retention`, `Metrics`, and `Deadline` seams and is already wired at `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1878`. A new sibling package is not added for it.

**Change (the reservation retires a stale registry binding before it writes anything).** The reservation transaction already reads the session row for its authoritative `coordination_generation` (§3). It also reads `pod_assignment` and compares it against the `SandboxName` of the registry binding it is about to checkpoint (`podsession.BindResult.SandboxName`, `binder.go:487-488`; the column carries the Sandbox name, `start.go:753`). On a disagreement the binding names a pod the session no longer has, so the driver calls `Registry.Remove(sessionID)`, returns `ErrNoBinding`, and commits nothing: no mint, no supersede, no intent row, no RPC. The driver also drops the binding when the `Checkpoint` RPC itself fails because the pod is gone (the dial fails, or the binder can no longer resolve the Sandbox), on the same abort path that soft-deletes the attempt's zero-chunk row. This is the only reclaimer a destroyed pod's bindings have: `Registry.Remove` has one caller in the tree, `PodExecutor.Release` (`pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:313`), which runs on the session's teardown and on the serving replica alone, while an evicted session stays non-terminal and is resumed onto a replacement pod by a possibly different replica (`start.go:3150`). Without the reclaimer, every tick of `Checkpointer.Sweep` on the replica that still holds the old binding would reserve a checkpoint against a pod that no longer exists, commit an intent row, and hold an active same-generation row carrying no `manifest_reason` and inside its deadline, which is the row the supersede predicate refuses to displace, so it would abort the session's genuine checkpoint on its replacement pod on every tick (C7). One attempt runs per `(session_id, slot_id)` at a time, behind a single-flight lock the driver owns, because `snapshot` (`:236`) holds no per-session lock and the periodic ticker (`:122-133`), `Seal` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:91`), the barrier dispatcher (`barrier.go:184`), and the C5 eviction path all reach it for the same session. Acquiring the lock preempts: a producer that finds an attempt in flight for the pair cancels that attempt's upload context and waits for it to settle its finalisation or its abort sweep on the detached context, so the rows the new attempt supersedes are always rows whose attempt has stopped running. Inside the lock the driver mints `checkpoint_id`, supersedes prior active partial rows for the pair under the single S2 predicate (`coordination_generation < $new_generation OR (coordination_generation = $new_generation AND (manifest_reason IS NOT NULL OR checkpoint_timeout_at < now()))`), which admits every row of a strictly older generation (already fenced at the pod by the §10.1 handoff) and, at equal generation, a row whose attempt has finalised (`manifest_reason IS NOT NULL`, including the attempt this reservation just preempted) or whose deadline has elapsed, and which aborts the reservation against a same-generation row whose attempt is still running or against any row of a higher generation, commits the intent row, opens the server-streaming `Checkpoint` RPC, reserves the `CheckpointStart` frame's `probed_bytes` against the tenant's storage quota through `storagequota.Failover.Reserve` (`failover.go:91`, the same gate `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload.go:724` uses) before the first `Put`, wraps the frame stream in an `io.LimitedReader` at the tenant's remaining quota headroom (`upload.go:337`, over-stream abort at `:442`) so an under-declared `probed_bytes` cannot bypass the §11.2 quota gate, slices the frame stream at `chunk_size_bytes`, commits the `chunk_count = n + 1` `UPDATE` before the `PutObject` for index `n`, `Put`s each chunk, advances `workspace_bytes_uploaded` as each chunk commits, resolves the at-most-one index whose `Put` outcome it did not observe with a `Stat` on the derived key before it finalises (counting the object when the `Stat` reports it present at the byte length the driver was writing, soft-deleting it and lowering the count when the `Stat` definitively reports it absent or at another length, and keeping the reserved count without a delete when the `Stat` itself fails, S1), finalises, and releases the unused remainder of the reservation. It finalises `partial = false` only when the terminal `CheckpointSummary` arrived inside the deadline and `workspace_bytes_uploaded` equals `CheckpointSummary.size_bytes`; EOF without a summary, or a summary that disagrees with the committed byte count, finalises `partial = true` with `manifest_reason = 'timeout'` (S3). A failed counter `UPDATE`, a rejected reservation, a `Put` error, or a stream that runs past the quota cap aborts the attempt and takes the abort sweep. On the abort path the driver releases `probed_bytes − workspace_bytes_uploaded` and lets the sweep's per-chunk `SoftDelete` release the committed bytes, so no byte is decremented twice and none leaks (S2).

The deadline the driver is given (the C5 tier cap, or `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` on the barrier path) bounds the archive stream and the chunk `Put`s alone. The finalising UPDATE, the `chunk_count == 0` soft-delete, the abort sweep, and the quota release run on a `context.WithoutCancel` derivation of that context under their own bounded budget, because otherwise every write the driver owes after the deadline fires is issued on a cancelled context and fails with `context.DeadlineExceeded`, leaving the row unfinalised, the chunks undeleted, and the reservation leaked (C5).

**Change (the periodic producer becomes a bounded worker pool, and one semaphore bounds every producer).** `Checkpointer.Sweep` (`:173-178`) calls `Checkpoint` synchronously for every bound session in one goroutine, and `Run` (`:122-133`) fires one `Sweep` per tick. That loop is instant today only because `Checkpoint` returns `codes.Unimplemented`; under this change each iteration relays a whole workspace through the gateway, so a serialized sweep of `maxSessionsPerReplica` sessions cannot finish inside `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds` at the §4.4 SLO (`400 × 2s = 800s` against a 600s interval at Tier 3), and every session on the replica would carry a last-checkpoint age above the interval, pinning `lenny_checkpoint_stale_sessions` and firing `CheckpointStale` (`spec/16:459`) permanently. `Sweep` therefore dispatches its sessions across a bounded worker pool sized by `gateway.checkpointWorker.concurrency` (S2), reporting per-session failures through `OnError` as it does today. The same semaphore is the single bound on concurrent uploads for the whole replica: the seal (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:91`), the barrier flush (C2's per-pod gate), and the eviction producers (C5, C7) acquire it too, so a drain cannot open more concurrent relays than the knob allows. It bounds concurrency alone and does not serialize a session against itself, which stays the job of the per-`(session_id, slot_id)` single-flight lock. The sustained invariant it satisfies is the one S2 states at `spec/17:1221-1225`: `maxSessionsPerReplica × checkpoint_duration ≤ periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds × checkpointWorker.concurrency`.

**Change (a cancellable chunk-I/O surface, and the driver owns the retry ladder).** No verb on `blobstore.Store` takes a context (`pkg/blobstore/blobstore.go:307-321`: `Put(u URI, mimeType string, data io.Reader)`, `Get(u URI)`, `Stat(u URI)`), and `miniostore.Store.Put` opens with `ctx := context.Background()` (`pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:259`) and runs its own fixed ladder of `time.Sleep` backoffs (`putRetryBackoffs = {1s, 5s, 30s}`, `miniostore.go:44`, retry loop at `:338-368`), with `Get` (`:487`) and `SoftDelete` (`:786`) opening the same way. A single chunk `Put` against an unreachable MinIO therefore blocks for roughly 36 seconds of uncancellable sleeps plus four network attempts, and nothing the driver holds can cut it. As staged without this change the first chunk `Put` would overrun both the 30-second default Stage 2 tier cap and the 30-second eviction retry budget before the driver regained control, `checkpoint.RetryBudgetFor(TriggerEviction)` could never be the budget that governs a MinIO retry, the sequential per-session preStop fan-out would overrun `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`, and the detached-context finalisation, the `chunk_count == 0` soft-delete, the abort sweep, and the quota release would never run under the exact failure (a MinIO outage during a drain) they exist to handle.

The change therefore adds a narrow context-carrying interface next to `Store` (`PutContext(ctx, u, mimeType, r)`, `GetContext(ctx, u)`, `SoftDeleteContext(ctx, u, retention)`), implements it on **every backend `blobproviderflags.Resolve` can return**, and forwards it through `cataloging` and through `TenantScoped` behind the existing `check(u)` tenant guard, following the pattern `TenantScoped.Copy` (`blobstore.go:889`) already uses for a capability the base `Store` does not carry. The base `Store` interface keeps its three verbs and every existing caller is unchanged; only the checkpoint drivers take the context-carrying form.

**Every backend, because MinIO is one of six and the cloud-managed profile is first-class.** `Resolve` (`pkg/blobstore/providerflags/providerflags.go:105`) returns `miniostore`, `blobstore.MemoryStore`, `blobstore.FilesystemStore`, `s3.Store`, `gcs.Store`, or `azureblob.Store` depending on `objectStorage.provider` (`charts/lenny/values.yaml:626-627`; `spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1415` and `:1447` publish the S3, GCS, and Azure profile), and `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1035` wraps whatever it got in `cataloging` (`:1085-1090`). An interface implemented on `miniostore` alone would leave `cataloging` with nothing to forward to on an S3-, GCS-, Azure-, or filesystem-backed gateway, so either no checkpoint could be written at all there, or the driver would fall back to the context-less `Store.Put`, which on the S3 backend also opens with `context.Background()` (`pkg/blobstore/s3/s3.go:222-223`) and therefore cannot be cut by the Stage 2 tier cap or by `RetryBudgetFor(TriggerEviction)`, which is the exact failure this surface exists to remove. Each backend's implementation threads the caller's context into the SDK call it already makes in place of the `context.Background()` it opens today. The fallback rule is stated rather than implied: the gateway asserts the surface on the store `Resolve` returns at startup and **fails closed** if it is absent, rather than degrading to an uncancellable `Put` that a later backend addition would silently reintroduce. The upload driver owns the MinIO retry ladder so that `checkpoint.RetryBudgetFor(trigger)` is the budget that actually governs: each store call is issued under a per-attempt deadline derived from the remaining budget, and the inner `putRetryBackoffs` ladder is bypassed on the checkpoint path (a single attempt per call) so it cannot exceed the outer budget. A `Put` abandoned at the deadline is awaited or cancelled before the abort sweep runs for that index, so the sweep cannot delete a key an abandoned `Put` then re-commits; the sweep runs after the upload goroutine has returned, on the detached context.

**Change (the chunk `Put` is idempotent under the driver's retry ladder).** `blobstore.Store.Put` is write-once: "Overwriting an existing key returns `ErrConflict`" (`pkg/blobstore/blobstore.go:307-312`), and `miniostore.Store.Put` enforces it with a `StatObject` precheck that returns `blobstore.ErrConflict` before any retry logic runs (`miniostore.go:277-283`). The shipped `putRetryBackoffs` ladder is idempotent only because it replays `PutObject` on the already-prechecked key inside one call and never re-runs the `Stat` (`miniostore.go:338-340`). Once the driver owns the ladder, a retry of index `n` re-enters the precheck, and the ordinary partial failure this design creates (a `PutObject` whose body was fully sent but whose response was lost, or one cut by the per-attempt deadline) leaves the object committed server-side. The precheck would then return `ErrConflict`, which is not transient, so per the abort rule above the attempt would abort and the sweep would delete every chunk it had committed. A transient MinIO blip, which is exactly what `RetryBudgetFor(TriggerEviction)` = `{500ms, 5s, 30s}` (`pkg/checkpoint/checkpoint.go:118-122`) exists to ride out, would become a permanently lost checkpoint on the drain path. `PutContext` on the checkpoint path therefore treats an `ErrConflict` on the chunk's own key as success when `Stat` reports the byte length the driver is writing, because a chunk key is derived from the attempt's own `checkpoint_id` and its content is fixed by the index, so a re-`Put` of an identical chunk is a no-op. A conflict whose `Stat` reports a different byte length is a key collision and is an error that aborts the attempt. This is the only case in which `ErrConflict` is not an error on the checkpoint path.

The driver exposes the mint, the supersede, and the intent-row INSERT as a separate reservation entry point, and a second execution entry point that takes the reserved `checkpoint_id` and its committed row and starts at the `Checkpoint` RPC. The reservation entry point accepts the caller's `coordination_generation` transaction, so the C7 termination handler can commit the reservation and the `spec/10:33` CAS together, and the caller's deadline, so that handler can stamp the pod-declared `budget_ms` deadline on a row it reserves now and executes several slots later (S2, C7). The barrier path calls the two in sequence, because the barrier coordinator has to commit the row before it dispatches the barrier in order to put the id on the request, and it must not perform the mint, the supersede, and the INSERT itself: `dispatchOne` (`barrier.go:184`) holds no lock, the periodic ticker is an independent goroutine that nothing pauses on drain (`checkpointer.go:122`, `:173`), and `Hook.run` fires the barrier as its first drain act (`prestop.go:354`), so a barrier that superseded outside the lock would soft-delete the live row and chunk-delete a periodic upload of the same session still running on the same replica. The reservation therefore takes the same per-`(session_id, slot_id)` lock, preempting and settling the in-flight attempt, and holds it until the flush the ack drives settles or the shared `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` deadline fires. Exactly one `checkpoint_id` and one `checkpoint_manifest` row exist per attempt whatever the trigger, and no attempt supersedes the row it is executing: two mints and two intent-row INSERTs for one flush would either collide on `partial_manifest_active_uniq` (C4) or soft-delete and chunk-delete the row the barrier ack correlates against.

**Change (barrier dispatch).** The barrier work lands in `pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier` rather than in `prestop`: `prestop.BarrierDispatcher` is `Dispatch(ctx context.Context) error` (`prestop.go:203-205`) and carries no session, request, or ack, while `barrier.Coordinator.dispatchOne` (`barrier.go:183-210`) is what allocates the barrier id, calls `Dispatcher.Send` (`barrier.go:85-87`), and persists the ack. `Dispatcher.Send` and `adapterclient.Client.CheckpointBarrier` (reached through `PodDispatcher.Send`, `wiring.go:44-61`) widen to carry the gateway-minted `checkpoint_id`, and `barrier.Target` (`barrier.go:59-70`) gains `Pool` and `SlotID`, which the barrier-ack metrics, the `CheckpointBarrierRequest.slot_id` P1 adds, and the driver's per-`(session_id, slot_id)` reservation all need and which neither the shipped `Target` nor the `coordination_lease` mirror carries (S1).

The package's shipped tests implement both widened interfaces with the old signatures and are rewritten with them (§6, §10): `barrier_test.go`'s `fakeDispatcher.Send(ctx, Target, barrierID)` (`:39`) and `fakeMetrics.IncPreStopBarrierTargetSource(source)` (`:55`), and `wiring_test.go`'s three-argument `d.Send(...)` calls (`:85`, `:101`, `:115`, `:134`). `TestDispatchCacheFallbackSource_spec_10_1` (`barrier_test.go:183`) and `TestDispatchTransportErrorNonFatal_spec_10_1` (`:166`) additionally pin the dispatch semantics this change alters, and `pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/checkpointbarrier_test.go:39` pins the ack mapping the widened request changes. For each target, `dispatchOne` calls the C2 driver's reservation entry point before `Send`, because the request has to carry the id the ack echoes. It does not mint, supersede, or INSERT itself: `dispatchOne` holds no lock, and the periodic ticker (`checkpointer.go:122`, `:173`) is an independent goroutine that a drain does not pause, so a barrier that wrote the row outside the driver's single-flight lock would supersede a periodic checkpoint of the same session that is still uploading on the same replica, soft-delete its live row, and chunk-delete its committed chunks under it, which is the loss the generation fence cannot prevent because both attempts read the same lease generation. The reservation takes the lock, preempts and settles the in-flight attempt, and performs the three acts inside it. The commit is the first durable act of the attempt and it runs before the pod's own generation fence can reject a false-positive barrier (the adapter fence runs only on `Send`, `pkg/adapter/coordination.go:153-159`), so the same transaction reads the authoritative `sessions.coordination_generation` (the counter the `spec/10:33` CAS writes and `CoordinatorFence` announces, `pkg/gateway/coordination/coordfence/coordfence.go:144`) rather than the `coordination_lease` mirror, writes it as the row's `coordination_generation`, and takes the generation-and-settlement-fenced supersede: a stale coordinator dispatching a false-positive barrier aborts on the guard instead of soft-deleting the live row and chunk-deleting the new coordinator's in-flight upload under it. The mirror cannot serve as that source: the sweeper upserts the generation it read before `RecordHandoff` bumped it (`coordination.go:109`, `:145`, `:150`, `:169-171`) and the upsert is best-effort (`:178-194`), so a row sourced from it would carry the victim's own generation, the strictly-higher supersede arm would never fire, and the handoff's checkpoint would collide with `partial_manifest_active_uniq`. A failed INSERT, a failed generation read, or a lock the reservation cannot take inside its bounded slice of the deadline does not suppress the barrier: `dispatchOne` still calls `Send` with an empty `checkpoint_id`, so step-2 quiescence fires for that session, the pod acks with an empty `checkpoint_ref`, no flush is driven, and the session falls back to its last successful checkpoint. The skipped flush is counted per session on `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total{trigger="eviction", reason="manifest_write_failed"}` (S1), which is the counter that carries a checkpoint that did not persist, whose `trigger` label already names the gateway-drain class, and whose `reason` label S1 extends with the two values this change's new increment conditions need. It is not counted on `lenny_prestop_barrier_target_source_total{source="cache_fallback"}`: that counter is emitted once per fan-out and reports where the barrier-target set came from (`spec/16:44`), and two of the three degraded triggers occur with a healthy lease read, so it would both miss them and misreport a Postgres outage that did not happen. Without this arm a Postgres failover overlapping a gateway drain would leave every coordinated pod unquiesced, which is the outcome `spec/10:165` and `spec/10:180` rule out. On the ack `dispatchOne` drives the C2 upload driver for that session through the pre-minted entry point, under the shared `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` wall-clock deadline that `prestop.fireBarrier` (`prestop.go:472-479`) already bounds the fan-out by (`bctx`, `prestop.go:475`), which is also the row's `checkpoint_timeout_at`.

**The flush a `dispatchOne` drives is gated per pod, because a barrier `Target` is one session and several targets can share one pod.** `Coordinator.Dispatch` launches one goroutine per target with no bound (`barrier.go:159-178`), and `MirrorTargetLister.Targets` builds one `Target` per `coordination_lease` row and stamps each with the session's `PodAddr` (`wiring.go:112-135`), so on a `maxConcurrentSessions: N` pool the draining replica holds N targets addressed at one pod. Driving the on-ack flush inside that fan-out would open N concurrent `Checkpoint` streams into one adapter, and the adapter's operation lock is pod-global (`ops opLock` on `Server`, `pkg/adapter/server.go:328`): it admits one running operation plus one queued and maps everything beyond that to `codes.Aborted` (`pkg/adapter/oplock.go:37-40`, `pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:178-186`). At `N ≥ 3` the drain-time flush would leave `N − 2` slots aborted before a byte was archived, each finalising as a `chunk_count == 0` row that is soft-deleted, counted on `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total{reason="no_chunks_committed"}`, and fallen through to the last periodic checkpoint, which is the loss the drain-time flush exists to prevent; and it would contradict the `spec/05:542` rule S1 stages, which requires the gateway to drive one slot's `Checkpoint` stream at a time in slot-ID order for a concurrent-session pod. C2 therefore groups the targets by `PodAddr` in `Coordinator.Dispatch` and holds one per-pod gate: the barrier `Send` and its ack stay concurrent across every target, so quiescence still fans out in parallel and the single wall clock still bounds the pass, while the flush a `dispatchOne` drives waits for that pod's gate and the pod's slots flush in slot-ID order. Pods remain parallel with each other. This is the same serialization C7 performs on the agent-pod trigger, reached from the other trigger, and it makes `spec/04:672`'s operation-lock rule (restated pod-global by P1) survivable on a concurrent pod without a second lock inside the adapter. The BarrierAck-timeout finalisation, the `chunk_count == 0` soft-delete, and the abort sweep run on a context detached from `bctx`, for the same reason they are detached from the tier cap on the C5 path (C5). `barrier.Ack.CheckpointRef` and `sessioncheckpointmeta.Record.CheckpointRef` (`sessioncheckpointmeta.go:43-45`) stop meaning "the MinIO checkpoint manifest the barrier flush produced" and become the echoed gateway-minted id. A session whose pod never acks keeps its `chunk_count == 0` row, which the BarrierAck-timeout handler soft-deletes (`spec/10:173`).

**Change (restore driver).** The restore driver is one new package under `pkg/gateway/checkpoint`, called by `podsession.Binder.Resume` at `binder.go:1539`. The binder holds neither the blobstore nor the manifest store today, so this seam is what the new package exists for. The driver loads the manifest row named on the `podsession.ResumeRequest`, derives the chunk keys as `object_key_prefix + {n}.{chunk_encoding}` for `n` in `[0, chunk_count)`, `Get`s them in ascending index order, and pushes them into the client-streaming `Resume` RPC. `ErrNotFound` on any index in `[0, chunk_count)` is the contiguity failure and takes the existing non-terminal fallback to the last successful full checkpoint (`spec/10:155`): the driver resolves the next full checkpoint below the row it just failed on through `SelectLatestFullBefore(tenantID, sessionID, slotID, failedCheckpointID)` (C4), derives and streams that row's chunk set instead, and reports `full`. The exception is the highest index of a `partial = true` row, which may be a reserved-but-unresolved reservation rather than a lost object and is read as the truncation boundary (S1).

**A contiguity failure with no fall-back row is terminal, and it is `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING`.** When `SelectLatestFullBefore` also returns `ErrNotFound`, the session holds manifest rows whose objects are gone, which is exactly the state `spec/25_agent-operability.md:4095` legislates for after a DR promotion to a replication target whose checkpoint objects have not yet replicated: "Sessions whose workspace snapshot is missing transition to `failed` on next resume attempt with error `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING`; the gateway surfaces this to clients via the existing session-state API." The driver therefore reports that it restored nothing and that the rows it holds name absent objects, and the resume fails with `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` and the session transitions to `failed`, rather than falling through to the snapshotless rebuild, which would return HTTP 200 with an empty workspace, `resumeMode: "full"`, and `workspaceLost: false` for a session whose workspace was silently discarded. The snapshotless rebuild is reserved for `SelectForResume` returning `ErrNotFound`, which is the different state of a session that has no manifest row at all and therefore never had a checkpoint to lose. The one arm that outranks the terminal failure is the §4.4 minimal-state record: a session that holds a `session_eviction_state` row resumes `conversation_only` with `workspaceLost: true` (`spec/04:264`), because that record is the designed degradation for a workspace that is known to be gone. The resume's total ordering is therefore: restore what `SelectForResume` selects and report its mode; otherwise, if a `session_eviction_state` row exists, resume `conversation_only`; otherwise, if the session holds manifest rows whose chunk objects are absent, fail with `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING`; otherwise rebuild the workspace from the stored `WorkspacePlan` and resume `full` (C6, C7). This is the detection mechanism the shipped (skipped) test names as undecided: `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/workspace_snapshot_missing_resume_test.go:34-51` asserts the terminal transition and the client-visible code, and its `t.Skip` records that "the detection mechanism (adapter Resume not-found signal vs. gateway pre-resume snapshot probe) and the session-state API field that carries the error are undecided". The mechanism is neither of the two it names: it is the gateway-side chunk `Get` returning `blobstore.ErrNotFound` with no fall-back row, which the restore driver already performs. **The code travels on the §15.1 session envelope, which does not carry a reason field today, so the envelope gains one.** `resumeOnPod` fails the resume and the session row transitions to `failed` with `sessionstore.Session.FailureReason = "WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING"` (`pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:40-46`, the specific coded cause, "distinct from FailureClass: the class is a coarse audit bucket, the reason is a specific cause string"). That column reaches no client today. `SessionResponse`, which the code itself labels "the §15.1 `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` envelope" (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:2021-2032`), declares `FailureClass` and no reason field, and `toResponse` (`:3141-3172`) copies only the class. The `failureReason` JSON tag that does exist in the tree is on `sessionstore.FailureHistory` (`:765-773`), which is the §8.9 delegation-tree per-node projection built by `ProjectNodeAttributes` (`:777`), a different surface. `workspace_seal_timeout` is client-visible through its **class** rather than through the reason (`failWorkspaceSeal` sets both, `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:167-171`), and `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` has no class of its own: `failureClassForReason`'s `default` arm degrades every reason outside the closed catalogue to `runtime_failure` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:393-411`). As staged without an envelope change, a resume against absent chunks would render as `failureClass: "runtime_failure"` with the string appearing in no client-visible body, `spec/25:4095`'s requirement that "the gateway surfaces this to clients via the existing session-state API ([§15.1])" would be unmet, and the un-skipped tier-1 test, which asserts the `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` body contains `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` (`workspace_snapshot_missing_resume_test.go:102-109`), could not pass.

C2 therefore adds `FailureReason string \`json:"failureReason,omitempty"\`` to `SessionResponse` and copies `row.FailureReason` in `toResponse`, and stages the field's parallel representations: the `Session` schema in the hand-authored `pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.json` (`:110-136`), `spec/15:645`'s session-`failureClass` paragraph (which gains the sentence that the envelope also carries the specific coded cause on `failureReason`), and the three client SDK session types, which mirror `failureClass` and carry no reason (`sdks/client/go/lenny/types.go:62`, `sdks/client/python/lenny/types.py:106`, `sdks/client/typescript/src/types.ts:59`). Adding a `workspace_snapshot_missing` value to the `failureClass` enum was rejected: the enum is closed and `spec/07:110` states that adding a class "is a coordinated spec change across §7.1, §7.2, §15.1, and §16", the same paragraph already distinguishes the coarse class from the specific reason string, and `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` is a reason by that definition. Surfacing the reason column the row already carries extends an existing surface rather than widening a closed enum. No §15.5 error-catalog row is added, because `spec/25:4095` surfaces this one through the session-state API rather than as a REST error code, and `docs/api/rest.md`'s `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` section (`:153-159`) lists an illustrative field subset that names neither `failureClass` nor `failureReason`, so it needs no edit. The test is rewritten onto that driver and un-skipped (§6, §10). `spec/25:4095` needs no edit and is confirmed unchanged: its rule holds verbatim over the chunk-set model, where "the workspace snapshot is missing" means the manifest row's derived chunk objects are absent.

**The fallback selector excludes the row that failed, because under S2 a `partial = false` row can itself fail contiguity.** In the shipped model a full checkpoint is one object and cannot fail a contiguity check, which is why `spec/10:155` writes the fall-back rule inside the partial-reassembly path. S2 routes full checkpoints through the same derive-then-`Get` pipeline and `SelectForResume` returns a `partial = false` row directly in the dominant case, so the row that fails contiguity is often the session's newest active full row. A selector that simply returned "the newest active `partial = false` row" would return the row that just failed, the driver would re-derive the same missing key set, and the second retained checkpoint that `spec/12:325`'s latest-2 rotation preserves would be unreachable; the driver would also never reach the terminal `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` arm, because the session does have an active full row. That state is producible by this change's own paths: a rotation or terminal-session delete whose chunk deletes land but whose manifest-row soft-delete does not leaves a live row naming chunk keys whose objects are already gone (S2), and both deletes are multi-step and best-effort (C2). `SelectLatestFullBefore` therefore takes the failed `checkpoint_id` and returns the newest active `partial = false` row **strictly below it** in the total order `(coordination_generation DESC, checkpoint_started_at DESC, checkpoint_id DESC)`, so each contiguity failure walks one step down the retained set and the walk terminates, at the last retained checkpoint that reads or at the terminal `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` arm when none does. Without the exclusion the fallback arm is a no-op on exactly the failure it exists to handle.

**Change (the resume entry condition).** `resumeOnPod` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:3084`) is the only caller of `Binder.Resume` (`:3126`), and it reaches it only when `row.WorkspaceSnapshot != nil && row.WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref != ""` (`:3084`); otherwise it takes the snapshotless rebuild branch (`startOnPod` with the stored `WorkspacePlan`) and returns. The only writer of `WorkspaceSnapshot` is `checkpointer.snapshot` (`checkpointer.go:259-266`), which a `partial = true` finalisation must not reach (below), so a session whose first-ever checkpoint truncated would have no snapshot ref, would never reach the restore driver, and would be rebuilt with an empty workspace. That is the NULL-baseline arm `spec/10:141` and `:155` define and the arm C6 and §6 assert, so the gate has to move off the session column and onto the manifest row.

`resumeOnPod` therefore calls `SelectForResume(tenantID, sessionID, slotID)` (C4) and branches on its result: a restorable row (a `partial = true` row that clears the §10.1 threshold, or a `partial = false` row) takes the restore path, and `ErrNotFound` takes the snapshotless rebuild. The selector applies the threshold gates internally and falls through to the newest active `partial = false` row when the newest partial fails them, so a row that fails the threshold never reaches `resumeOnPod` as a resume-blocking answer. A two-way branch that routed a below-threshold or zero-chunk partial straight to the snapshotless rebuild would be a regression on the shipped behavior: `partial = true` intent rows are committed at the start of every attempt of every trigger, `coordination_generation` advances only on coordinator handoff (`spec/04:157`), so an in-flight or crash-abandoned attempt leaves an active zero-chunk row strictly newer than the session's last successful full row, and the session would be rebuilt with an empty workspace while a valid full checkpoint sat active in the table. Today `resumeOnPod` restores from `row.WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref` in exactly that state. `podsession.ResumeRequest.CheckpointID` carries the selected row's `checkpoint_id` rather than `row.WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref` (`start.go:3131`), so the driver reads exactly the row the gateway selected. `sessions.workspace_snapshot_ref` keeps its other readers (derive, replay, and `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/workspace`, P1) and stops being the resume gate.

**Change (the restore reports the mode it took and the fraction it recovered).** `podsession.ResumeResult` already carries the adapter-reported `Mode` (`binder.go:657-667`), which `resumeOnPod` hands to `classifyResumeWithAdapter` (`start.go:2802`). It gains two gateway-side fields. `RestoreMode` is `partial_workspace` when the driver reassembled a `partial = true` row above the threshold and `full` on every restore of a `partial = false` row, including the fall-through arms (a below-threshold or zero-chunk newest partial, a partial outranked by a strictly newer full row, and a contiguity failure that resolved the next full checkpoint down through `SelectLatestFullBefore`). `RecoveryFraction *float64` is the value `spec/07:138` makes mandatory on the `partial_workspace` mode: the driver computes it as the adapter's `ResumeResponse.restored_bytes` (the post-extraction on-disk total, which is the numerator `spec/10:155` names) over the restored row's frozen `baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes`, and leaves it nil when that baseline is NULL or the mode is `full`. Both values are outputs of the restore because both of their inputs are gone by the time the classifier runs: the manifest row that carries the denominator is soft-deleted by the two cleanup paths (C6), and the numerator exists only inside the adapter's `ResumeResponse`. `resumeOnPod` returns them alongside the adapter's mode, and `emitResumedEvent` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:3247`) widens to take the fraction and populate `resumedEventPayload.WorkspaceRecoveryFraction` (`start.go:3238`), whose only writer today is the synthesized coordinator-handoff frame (`start.go:3264-3269`). Without that widening the emitter takes a mode and nothing else, and every `partial_workspace` resume would publish `session.resumed` with no fraction.

**Change (the partial-manifest counter gets its labels and its emitter).** `lenny_checkpoint_partial_total` is declared at `spec/16:195` with `pool`, `recovered`, `manifest_reason`, and `trigger`, and every consumer this change adds depends on those labels: S1 moves the barrier-timeout partial capture onto it, S4 rescopes its `trigger` set, and the §6 assertions read `trigger="barrier_ack_timeout"` and `trigger="periodic"`. The shipped counter carries none of them. It is registered with `pool` alone (`pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_podlifecycle.go:112-117`), its only helper is `IncCheckpointPartial(pool string)` (`gatewaymetrics.go:1247-1256`), and that helper has no caller in `pkg/` or `cmd/`, so the counter is inert. C2 widens the registration and the helper to the declared label set, emits one increment per manifest row finalised `partial = true` in the upload driver's finalisation arm (S4's increment condition), and flips `recovered` to `true` on the resume that reassembles the row, which is the restore driver's `partial_workspace` arm (C6). The same package authors the `lenny_gc_tombstones_pruned_total` `table` label values (`gatewaymetrics_retention.go:67-73`), whose `partial_manifest` value becomes `checkpoint_manifest` with the renamed table (S1, C4).

`lenny_checkpoint_partial_manifests_superseded_total` gets its emitter in the same package. The counter is registered with `pool` (`gatewaymetrics_checkpoint.go:86-89`) and its helper `IncCheckpointPartialManifestsSuperseded` (`gatewaymetrics.go:408-412`) has no caller anywhere in `pkg/`, `cmd/`, or `tests/`, so the counter is inert today. That is harmless only while no producer of partial manifests exists; S2 puts the supersede step on the hot path of every checkpoint attempt of every trigger, and `spec/16:196` states the increment condition as once per prior partial manifest row soft-deleted by the supersede-on-write step. The upload driver's supersede step therefore calls the helper once per row it soft-deletes, and increments it zero times on the two arms where the predicate refuses the supersede and aborts the reservation (an active row at a higher `coordination_generation`, and a same-generation row carrying no `manifest_reason` and still inside its `checkpoint_timeout_at`), because no row is superseded there. §6 asserts both directions.

Two more counters in the same package are corrected with it. `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_total{pool, outcome}` and `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_duration_seconds{pool}` have **no registration and no emitter anywhere** (S1): C2 registers both in `gatewaymetrics_checkpoint.go` beside the other checkpoint metrics, widens `barrier.Metrics` (`pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier/barrier.go:100-101`, whose entire surface today is `IncPreStopBarrierTargetSource`) with `IncCheckpointBarrierAck(pool, outcome string)` and an ack-duration observer, and emits them from `Coordinator.dispatchOne` (`barrier.go:183-210`) on the ack (`success`), on the `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` expiry (`timeout`), and on a transport error (`error`). The `pool` value those two emissions carry comes from the `Pool` field C2 adds to `barrier.Target` (`barrier.go:59-70`) and populates in `MirrorTargetLister.Targets` (`wiring.go:112-135`) and in the `Fallback` in-memory-cache lister, because neither the shipped `Target` nor the `coordination_lease` mirror it is built from carries a pool (S1). `IncPreStopBarrierTargetSource` (`gatewaymetrics.go:1278-1282`), its registration, and the `barrier.Metrics` method of the same name are **unchanged**: that counter is emitted once per fan-out from `Coordinator.Dispatch` (`barrier.go:164-166`) across every pool the replica coordinates, so a single `pool` label is not expressible on it, and S1 corrects `spec/16:44` to declare `source` alone instead. `pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier` therefore carries a metric change and a `Target` widening as well as the dispatch change. And `IncCheckpointStorageFailure` (`gatewaymetrics.go:608-613`) gains a `reason` parameter instead of hard-coding `retry_exhausted`, so the degraded barrier dispatch can emit `manifest_write_failed` and the zero-chunk fall-through `no_chunks_committed` (S1); the registration is unchanged, because the counter already carries the label. Its only shipped call sites are the adapter-side emitter C1 moves to the gateway (`pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:399`, through the `IncCheckpointStorageFailure(pool, level, trigger string)` seam at `checkpoint.go:79`) and one test in the helper's own package, `TestCheckpointStorageFailureReasonLabel` (`pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_elicitation_test.go:207-221`), which calls the three-argument form and asserts the `reason="retry_exhausted"` value the helper hard-codes. That test stops compiling on the signature change and is rewritten onto the four-argument helper over the full `reason` value set (§6, §10). `TestGCTombstonesPrunedCounter` in the same file (`:234-252`) asserts the `table="partial_manifest"` label value C4 renames to `checkpoint_manifest`, and is restated with it.

**Change (the three `WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref` readers).** `derive.go`, `replay.go`, and `session_subresources.go` become prefix readers per P1. Derive mints a new `checkpoint_id`, copies the parent's derived chunk key set, and inserts the derived session's `partial = false` `checkpoint_manifest` row in the same transaction as the copy, so the invariant holds on the write side: every readable `WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref` prefix has a manifest row behind it, and no reader ever holds a prefix it cannot enumerate.

**Change (tenant scoping and the catalog).** Every `Put`, `Get`, and delete from either driver goes through `blobstore.NewTenantScoped(tenantID, blobs)`, matching the existing callers (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload.go:359`, `derive.go:333`). `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1062` types the store as the raw `blobstore.Store`, so `ErrCrossTenant` is reachable only through the wrapper. The SSE-KMS resolver is inherited from `blobproviderflags.Resolve` (`stores.go:1035-1050`); the tenant-prefix guard is not, and the driver must wrap. The store the drivers wrap is the one production hands the binder (`stores.go:1090` `blobs = blobsCataloged`, `:1842` `Blobs: blobs`), so each chunk `Put` inserts the chunk's `artifact_store` row and each chunk `SoftDelete` releases its bytes (`pkg/blobstore/cataloging/cataloging.go:143-171`, `:240-258`). `pkg/blobstore/cataloging` needs no change: the per-chunk row is the S2 accounting rule.

**Change (delete surface).** Per-key deletion goes through `cataloging.Store.SoftDelete(u blobstore.URI, retention time.Duration) error` (`pkg/blobstore/cataloging/cataloging.go:240`), which forwards to the inner backend's tombstone (`miniostore.Store.SoftDelete` at `miniostore.go:785`) and releases the chunk's `artifact_size_bytes` from the tenant's Redis counter (`cataloging.go:240-268`). That is the delete the S2 accounting rule needs, and the store production hands the gateway (`cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1090`) is exactly `*cataloging.Store`.

`blobstore.Tombstoner` is the wrong assertion for this path in both directions. `*cataloging.Store` does not satisfy it: its `SoftDelete` carries the retention argument, it has no `HardDeleteObject`, and its only compile-time assertion is `blobstore.Eraser` (`cataloging.go:328`). `blobstore.MemoryStore` does satisfy it (`blobstore.go:602`, `:641`). Asserting `Tombstoner` would therefore take the "backend does not implement it" branch on every Postgres-backed gateway and orphan every chunk. `blobstore.TenantScoped` compounds it: the wrapper exposes `Put`, `Get`, `Stat`, `Copy`, `DeleteByTenant`, and `DeleteByUser` (`blobstore.go:862-933`) and no per-key delete at all, so no delete can reach the store through the wrapper as it stands. The same latent mismatch is already visible at `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload.go:591`.

The change adds one forwarding method, `TenantScoped.SoftDelete(u blobstore.URI, retention time.Duration) error`, which runs the existing `check(u)` tenant-prefix guard and delegates to a narrow interface carrying the cataloging signature, following the pattern `TenantScoped.Copy` (`blobstore.go:889`) and `TenantScoped.DeleteByTenant` (`:913`) already use for capabilities the base `Store` does not carry. Every chunk delete then reaches the object store through the same tenant-scoped wrapper as `Put` and `Get`, so the §12.5 rule that tenant-prefix validation lives at the interface boundary holds on the delete path too. When the resolved store does not implement the narrow interface (the gateway running without Postgres, where `blobs` stays the raw `blobstore.MemoryStore`, `stores.go:1062` and `:1085-1090`), the driver skips the sweep and increments `lenny_checkpoint_orphaned_objects_total`, which is the counter's stated purpose. `PartialChunkDeleter.DeleteByPrefix` (`partialcleanup.go:24-30`) is replaced by this per-key form, since no prefix listing survives S2. The package's own unit suite for that code, `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/partialcleanup_test.go`, declares a `stubChunkDeleter` implementing the removed method (`:15-22`) and asserts deletion by prefix (`:71-72`), so it stops compiling here and is rewritten on the per-key surface with C4.

**Change (restore cleanup).** When the restored row has `partial = true`, the driver deletes its chunks and soft-deletes the row after the restore, whether the reconstruction succeeded, failed, or was aborted (`spec/04:237` as widened by S4). When the row has `partial = false`, the restore is read-only against the object store: no chunk deletion, no soft-delete. Full-checkpoint chunk lifetime stays owned by the `session_checkpoints` latest-2 rotation (`checkpointer.go:311`) and the §12.5 GC, with the legal-hold exemption (`spec/12:313`) intact. Deleting a full checkpoint's chunks on restore would erase the fallback target `spec/10:155` names, break the latest-2 retention the proposal keeps, and leave `binder.go:1533`'s documented retry-on-a-fresh-pod with no bytes on the second attempt.

**Change (rotation deletion).** The rotation path issues no object-store call today, so C2 builds the delete rather than widening one. `checkpointretention` is a Postgres catalog package that imports no blobstore: `Rotate` (`pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointretention/checkpointretention.go:188`, and `pgstore.Store.Rotate` at `pgstore/pgstore.go:82`, which issues only `UPDATE session_checkpoints`) flips `Retained` and stamps `DeletedAt` on the rows past the latest-2 cap and returns them, and its sole production caller discards them (`checkpointer.go:331`, `if _, err := c.Retention.Rotate(...)`). Nothing deletes a rotated-out checkpoint's bytes from MinIO on any path today.

`recordRetention` (`checkpointer.go:311-334`) therefore becomes the rotated-out delete site: it consumes the records `Rotate` returns, and for each one resolves that `checkpoint_id`'s `checkpoint_manifest` row, `SoftDelete`s every derived key in `[0, chunk_count)` through the tenant-scoped `ArtifactStore` (which tombstones each object, transitions its `artifact_store` row, and decrements the quota counter by that row's `artifact_size_bytes`), and soft-deletes the manifest row under the `deleted_at IS NULL` guard in the same step. This gives `recordRetention` a blobstore handle and a manifest-store handle it does not hold today. The legal-hold exemption (`checkpointer.go:328-330`, `spec/12:325`) is unchanged: a held session skips `Rotate` and therefore deletes nothing. Without the chunk deletes, every rotated-out checkpoint's chunks stay in MinIO and its bytes stay on the tenant's quota; without the row soft-delete, the `partial = false` row survives its own chunks and every prefix reader that resolves it derives keys for absent objects (S2).

**Change (terminal-session and TTL deletion).** The wired §12.5 sweep is `retentiongc.Collector` (`pkg/gateway/storage/retentiongc/retentiongc.go:252`, `removed, err := a.Delete(ctx, tenant, row.ID)`), whose `artifacts` deleter in production is `blobsCataloged.SoftDeleteSession` (`cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go:665-670`). It deletes per session (`ArtifactDeleter(ctx, tenantID, sessionID)`, `retentiongc.go:67`) rather than per `Record.Ref`, and `cataloging.Store.SoftDeleteSession` (`pkg/blobstore/cataloging/cataloging.go:423-449`) lists the session's catalog rows and tombstones every one with no object-type filter. Because S2 makes every chunk a catalogued `artifact_store` row of its session, that sweep already collects the chunk objects under `/{tenant_id}/checkpoint/{session_id}/` without a per-ref change. What it does not do is touch the `checkpoint_manifest` table, so as it stands a collected session would keep live `partial = false` rows naming chunk keys whose objects are tombstoned, which is exactly the state S2's accounting rule forbids: the hard-prune predicate keys on `deleted_at IS NOT NULL`, so the row is never physically removed either, and every prefix reader that resolves it takes the contiguity-failure branch instead of finding no artifact.

The collector therefore gains a companion manifest deleter alongside the `artifacts` one: a `retentiongc.Artifact{Name: "checkpoint_manifest"}` whose `Delete` soft-deletes every `checkpoint_manifest` row for the collected session under the `deleted_at IS NULL` guard, registered in the same `arts` slice at `cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go:665-670`. Chunk objects and catalog rows keep going through `SoftDeleteSession`, so no object-type-aware deleter is introduced and the existing sweep is extended rather than replaced. The §12.5 tombstone hard-prune then removes both the chunk rows and the manifest rows after `gc.tombstoneRetentionSeconds`.

**Change (size accounting, and what a partial finalisation must not write).** On the `partial = false` arm the `CheckpointSummary` frame supplies the total archived byte count, and `checkpointer.snapshot` writes it into `WorkspaceSnapshot.Bytes` exactly as it writes `result.SizeBytes` today, so the §7.2 `workspaceRecoveryFraction` denominator and the §10.1 preStop tier-cap input (F-7.3.21) do not regress.

The `partial = true` arm writes none of it. `snapshot` today reaches the `Sessions.Update` that stamps `WorkspaceSnapshot` and `LastSuccessfulCheckpointAt` (`checkpointer.go:259-276`) and then `recordRetention` (`:286`) on any non-error return, and a deadline-truncated checkpoint is a non-abort outcome for the driver, so as staged a truncated archive would become the session's `WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref`, its `last_successful_checkpoint_at`, its `last_checkpoint_workspace_bytes`, and a retained row in the latest-2 catalog. That contradicts the retained spec (`spec/04:259` stamps the timestamp "on every **successful** checkpoint", `spec/10:108` reads `last_checkpoint_workspace_bytes` "updated after each **successful** checkpoint", and `spec/04:235` says a partial manifest "is not a valid checkpoint; it is a recovery aid"), and it would make the truncated row the fallback target `spec/10:155` resolves to, let the rotation evict a genuine full checkpoint in its favour, and feed a truncated byte count into the next attempt's frozen `baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes` and its Stage 2 cap tier. It is also unsatisfiable on its own terms: a truncated stream carries no terminal `CheckpointSummary`, so there is no byte count to write. The upload driver therefore returns the finalisation arm to `snapshot`, which skips the `Sessions.Update` stamp and `recordRetention` entirely when the row finalised `partial = true` (S3).

`snapshot` reports that arm to its caller as an error rather than as a success, because a truncated capture is a failed checkpoint for every caller that keys on the return value. `Checkpointer.Seal` is a thin wrapper over `snapshot` (`checkpointer.go:210-216`) and `sealWorkspace` treats a nil return as a completed export (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:91-95`), so a `partial = true` seal reported as success would release the pod with the session's previous checkpoint standing as its exported workspace, which `spec/07:112` forbids ("The workspace is always exported to durable storage before the pod is released"). On the error the §7.1 bounded-backoff loop retries inside `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` and, on window exhaustion, transitions the session to `failed` with reason `workspace_seal_timeout`, emits the `workspaceSealFailed` audit event, and observes `lenny_workspace_seal_duration_seconds{outcome="timeout"}` (`seal.go:98-108`), which is the behaviour the shipped code already gives a failed export. The periodic sweep reports the same error through `OnError` (`checkpointer.go:173-176`), and the `NotifyPodTerminating` handler counts the slot as unsettled (C7). The row, its committed chunks, and its `lenny_checkpoint_partial_total` increment are retained on that arm regardless: the error is what the caller sees, and the partial row is what a later resume reassembles.

The same three writes are skipped when the finalising `UPDATE` affects zero rows. `checkpointer.snapshot` runs `c.Sessions.Update` (`checkpointer.go:253-266`, writing `Ref: result.CheckpointID`), `row.LastSuccessfulCheckpointAt` (`:277`), and `c.recordRetention` (`:288`) on any non-error return, and none of the three carries a generation or row-liveness guard. The generation fence admits the legitimate direction, where a newer coordinator at generation `N + 1` supersedes the still-running upload of the old coordinator at generation `N` after the handoff `spec/10:165` models, and the supersede deletes that row's chunk objects (`spec/10:137`). If the old writer's last chunk has already committed there is no further counter `UPDATE` to observe `rows_affected == 0`, so the abort-on-zero-rows rule the driver applies to the counter never fires. The driver therefore treats a zero-row finalising `UPDATE` as an abort (S3): it reports the abort arm to `snapshot`, which writes neither the `Sessions.Update` stamp nor the retention row, and it releases the reservation remainder and takes the abort sweep over its own derived key set. Without it the superseded writer records a phantom newest checkpoint over chunks the new coordinator has already deleted, which pollutes the frozen `baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes` denominator and the Stage 2 cap-tier input, makes the latest-2 rotation count the phantom as one of the two retained checkpoints, and hands every prefix reader a chunk set that no longer exists.

### C4. Replace session_partial_checkpoint_manifest with the unified checkpoint_manifest table

**Target:** `migrations/0062_session_partial_checkpoint_manifest.up.sql` and `.down.sql`; `migrations/0150_partial_manifest_active_uniq.up.sql`; `tests/tier2_component/migrations/prod_columns_test.go` (`:164`, the migration-contract row `{migration: "0062", table: "session_partial_checkpoint_manifest", create: true}`, and the `0150` comment at `:421-422`, both of which name the old table and which `TestProdMigrationsApplyExpectedSchema` and `TestProdMigrationsRollBackPerStep` assert against a real Postgres); `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/partialmanifeststore/partialmanifeststore.go` (the `Record` type `:45-72`, the `Store` interface whose `Get`, `SoftDelete`, and `HardDelete` all take a `generation int64` (`:119`, `:135`, `:145`) and whose `LatestActive` takes no slot (`:125`), and `MemoryStore`); `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/partialmanifeststore/pgstore/pgstore.go` (all eight methods, `:59`, `:140`, `:165`, `:193`, `:208`, `:239`, `:249`, `:265`); `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/partialcleanup.go`; **the three shipped test files that drive those surfaces directly and stop compiling on the re-key**: `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/partialmanifeststore/partialmanifeststore_test.go` (the store package's own unit suite: it builds `Record{… Generation: 7, PartialObjectKeyPrefix: …}` (`:22-27`), calls the generation-keyed `Get(ctx, "acme", "sess_42", 7)` (`:31`), and pins the pre-change resume selector in `TestLatestActiveReturnsHighestGeneration` (`:96`)), `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/partialcleanup_test.go` (whose `stubChunkDeleter` (`:15-22`) implements the `DeleteByPrefix` method C2 deletes, whose `seedManifest` (`:45-53`) builds rows on the generation key with `PartialObjectKeyPrefix`, whose success case asserts deletion by prefix (`:71-72`), and whose `TestCleanupPartialManifestNilDeleterSkipsMinIO` (`:146`) pins the nil-deleter skip that is T-4.4.16 itself), and `cmd/lenny-gateway/main_test.go` (`TestHardPrunePartialManifests` `:657-703`, which drives `hardPrunePartialManifests` against a `MemoryStore` seeded with generation-keyed `Record`s and reads them back with `store.Get(ctx, "acme", "sess-a", 1)`); `tests/tier2_component/stores/partialmanifeststore_test.go` (`:31`, `TestPartialManifestStoreContract`, which pins the old row key, the old `LatestActive` predicate, and migration 0062's table) and `tests/tier2_component/stores/partialcleanup_component_test.go` (`:35-46`, whose `minioPrefixDeleter` implements the deleted `DeleteByPrefix`, discovers chunks with `ListObjects`, and issues raw `RemoveObject` calls); `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:998-1002`; `cmd/lenny-gateway/main.go:1072` (`hardPrunePartialManifests`); `cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go:803-841` (the leader-gated GC loop that today registers only the tombstone hard-prune).

**Change (migrations).** Rewrite `0062` in place as `CREATE TABLE checkpoint_manifest` with the full §10.1 column set, and `0150` in place as the corrected unique index. The repository edits migrations in place (commit `32b4d7bb` edited `0148`), and no deployment exists in the wild, so no third create-then-drop migration is added. The new table keeps the RLS posture, the `lenny_tenant_guard` policy, and the `lenny_app` grants that `0062` already carries.

The rename is a contract change and its shipped contract test is rewritten with it. `tests/tier2_component/migrations/prod_columns_test.go` applies the production migrations against a real Postgres and asserts, forward and on rollback, that `0062` creates and drops exactly `session_partial_checkpoint_manifest` (`:164`, driving `TestProdMigrationsApplyExpectedSchema` at `:540-553` and `TestProdMigrationsRollBackPerStep` at `:560-583`). Both fail on the first run after the rename. The row becomes `{migration: "0062", table: "checkpoint_manifest", create: true}` and asserts the new columns (`checkpoint_id`, `slot_id`, `partial`, `chunk_count`, `chunk_size_bytes`, `chunk_encoding`, `object_key_prefix`, `checkpoint_timeout_at`, `manifest_reason`), and the `0150` comment at `:421-422` is restated on the renamed table. The file is also what satisfies `scripts/lint-migrations.sh`, which requires every migration number to be referenced by number in a test under that directory, so leaving it would fail the lint as well as the suite.

Columns: `tenant_id`, `checkpoint_id` (UUID), `session_id`, `slot_id` (`'default'` sentinel for `maxConcurrentSessions: 1`), `coordination_generation`, `recovery_generation`, `partial BOOLEAN NOT NULL`, `chunk_count`, `chunk_size_bytes`, `chunk_encoding`, `workspace_bytes_uploaded`, `baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes` (nullable), `object_key_prefix`, `checkpoint_started_at`, `checkpoint_timeout_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL` (the attempt's own deadline: the Stage 2 tier cap, the BarrierAck deadline, the pod-declared `budget_ms` deadline on the agent-pod eviction path, or the §4.4 60-second checkpoint timeout for the deadline-less triggers, per S2), `manifest_reason TEXT NULL CHECK (manifest_reason IS NULL OR manifest_reason = 'timeout')` plus `CHECK (partial OR manifest_reason IS NULL)` (a row carries a reason only once it finalises partial), `created_at`, `deleted_at`. `PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, checkpoint_id)`.

Indexes:

- `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX partial_manifest_active_uniq ON checkpoint_manifest (tenant_id, session_id, slot_id) WHERE partial = TRUE AND deleted_at IS NULL`. The shipped `0150` predicate is `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` with no `partial = TRUE` term, so once full-checkpoint rows live in the same table, two successive full rows for one session (which the latest-2 rotation deliberately keeps) would collide on it. The index is tenant-first, matching the repository convention (`0062:60-63`, `0150:23-25`); `spec/10:147` writes the tenant-less form and S2 notes the tightening. `slot_id` is in the key because `spec/10:141` mandates the column and `spec/10:137`'s supersede-on-write keys on `(session_id, slot_id)`.
- `CREATE INDEX ON checkpoint_manifest (tenant_id, session_id, slot_id, coordination_generation DESC, checkpoint_started_at DESC) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`. This table becomes the lookup for every restore, and the S2-restated `spec/10:155` selector reads the newest active row for `(session_id, slot_id)` in the total order `(coordination_generation DESC, checkpoint_started_at DESC, checkpoint_id DESC)`; `DeleteByUser` (`pgstore.go:249-251`) also keys on `session_id`. The primary key serves neither. The `checkpoint_started_at` column is in the index key because `coordination_generation` advances only on coordinator handoff (`spec/04:157`), so it does not separate a session's two retained full rows or a full row from a later partial written under the same coordinator.
- The `(partial, deleted_at)` index for the §12.5 backstop sweep is retained, extended with `checkpoint_timeout_at` so the sweep's liveness term (S2) is served by the same index.

**Change (Go).** The store's row key moves from `(tenant_id, session_id, generation)` to `(tenant_id, checkpoint_id)`, so every method on the `Store` interface changes, `MemoryStore` is widened alongside `pgstore` because it is the live non-Postgres binding (`cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1002`), and `PartialCleaner` (`partialcleanup.go`) plus `hardPrunePartialManifests` (`cmd/lenny-gateway/main.go:1072`) re-key. `Record` gains `checkpoint_id`, `slot_id`, `partial`, `chunk_count`, `chunk_size_bytes`, `chunk_encoding`, `object_key_prefix`, `baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes`, `checkpoint_timeout_at`, and `manifest_reason`. `DeleteByUser` and `DeleteByTenant` keep their contract unchanged: the §12.8 erasure path must keep working against the new table. `PartialCleaner`'s nil chunk deleter (T-4.4.16) is filled with the C2 delete surface.

Three shipped test files sit directly on those surfaces and take their packages' builds down the moment the re-key lands, so each is rewritten with it (§6, §10). `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/partialmanifeststore/partialmanifeststore_test.go` is the store package's own unit suite, keyed on `(tenant_id, session_id, generation)` throughout; it is rewritten onto the `(tenant_id, checkpoint_id)` key, the `object_key_prefix` field, and the `SelectForResume`, `SelectLatestFullBefore`, and `ListSweepable` selectors, with `TestLatestActiveReturnsHighestGeneration` re-scoped onto the `partial = TRUE` `LatestActive` the cleaner keeps. `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/partialcleanup_test.go` is the cleaner's unit suite: its `stubChunkDeleter` implements the `DeleteByPrefix` method C2 removes and its assertions read the deleted prefixes, so it is rewritten onto the per-key `SoftDelete` surface over the derived key set `[0, chunk_count)`, and `TestCleanupPartialManifestNilDeleterSkipsMinIO` is deleted rather than rewritten, because the nil-deleter skip it pins is the T-4.4.16 defect this change closes. `cmd/lenny-gateway/main_test.go`'s `TestHardPrunePartialManifests` seeds generation-keyed rows and reads them back by generation; it is rewritten onto the re-keyed `hardPrunePartialManifests` and the reason-agnostic hard-prune predicate S2 stages at `spec/12:353`, so it asserts the prune reaches a soft-deleted `partial = false` row as well as a `partial = true` one.
