The write runs on a context **detached** from the slot's tier cap (`context.WithoutCancel` plus its own bounded budget), for the reason C5 states for the finalisation and the abort sweep and for one more: the Postgres write's own budget is 60 seconds (`spec/04:278`) and the MinIO retry budget that precedes it is 30 seconds, so the two cannot both fit inside a 30-second tier cap, and a fallback write issued on the cancelled cap context would return `context.DeadlineExceeded` immediately. This is requirement (a) of the three that travel with the trigger, and it is the reason the grace-period arithmetic in S3 carries a `postgresFallbackBudgetSeconds` term per slot rather than assuming the tier cap subsumes it.

**On the retry-exhaustion and failed-handoff arms the write runs inside the RPC; on the budget-expiry arm it runs after the RPC returns.** The first two arms fire while the pod's grace period still has the slot's `postgresFallbackBudgetSeconds` in it, which is exactly what the S3 floor sizes it to cover, so the write completes before the gateway moves to the next slot and `spec/04:286`'s "publish `session.lost` before the preStop hook exits" holds literally. The budget-expiry arm cannot work that way. `budget_ms` is defined as the wall clock the adapter will hold the pod open, with the drain margin and the demote-and-exit reserve already subtracted (P1), and the proto doc declares it "an upper bound the gateway MUST NOT exceed", so at the instant it elapses the gateway has zero grace period left: every remaining second is committed to the adapter's teardown and to the kubelet. A 60-second Postgres write cannot be drawn from it. Holding the RPC past `budget_ms` to make room would consume the reserve and the margin and get the pod SIGKILLed before the adapter demotes its SDK, which is the outcome the S3 floor exists to prevent.

The gateway therefore **returns `NotifyPodTerminating` at `budget_ms`** with the remaining margin intact, and completes the fallback write for every unsettled slot on a gateway-owned context detached from the RPC and from the pod's lifetime, under its own §4.4 60-second budget; the total-loss `session.lost` publish, on that budget's exhaustion, runs on the same context. The cut slot's own bookkeeping runs there too, on the same gateway-owned clock: the handler cancels that slot's upload, and the driver's finalising `UPDATE` (`partial = true`, `manifest_reason = 'timeout'`), its `chunk_count == 0` soft-delete, its abort sweep, and its storage-quota release all execute after the RPC has returned (S3, C5). None of them needs the pod, and none of them may be charged to a grace period that has zero seconds left. Nothing in the write needs the pod: it is a Postgres row recording that the workspace is gone, and the pod's departure is what makes it true. `spec/04:286`'s ordering is therefore restated rather than kept verbatim (S3): the gateway publishes `session.lost` before the pod's preStop hook exits **when the fallback runs inside the RPC**, and completes the record and the publish on its own detached budget when the pod's declared budget elapsed first. The alternative, keeping the ordering unconditional, makes the arm unsatisfiable whenever the Postgres write needs more than the zero seconds the arm leaves it, and the minimal-state record is what separates a `conversation_only` resume from a session that comes back reporting `workspaceLost: false` over a workspace that died with its pod.

A gateway-drain checkpoint reaches the same driver and must not reach this writer. The driver takes the fallback arm only when the trigger's termination class destroys the workspace, which is the agent-pod class alone (C6). The `snapshotWithTrigger` call from `prestop.CheckpointFn` (C5) and the call from the `NotifyPodTerminating` handler both pass `checkpoint.TriggerEviction`, so the trigger enum does not distinguish them and a second discriminator is needed: the fallback is a property of the **call site** rather than of the trigger, so the writer is invoked by the `NotifyPodTerminating` handler on its own failure return and is not reachable from `prestop.CheckpointFn`. The `Checkpointer` does not hold the writer at all, which makes the constraint structural rather than conditional.

**Change (requirement (b): the resume precedence rule, before the lookup is wired).** `classifyResume` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:3214-3219`) returns `ResumeConversationOnly` on the bare presence of a `session_eviction_state` row, ahead of every other arm. `spec/04:264` requires the opposite ordering: "If a previous successful full checkpoint exists, the session is resumed from that checkpoint … If only the minimal state record exists," the session resumes `conversation_only`. The classifier gains that predicate before `Options.EvictionStateLookup` is assigned in `cmd/`, because wiring the lookup first would make every session that ever took one fallback resume `conversation_only` even after a later clean checkpoint.

The rule is the one `spec/04:264` states, with no generation qualification: the eviction-state row wins only when the session has **no restorable checkpoint at all**. C6 already makes the classification an output of the restore, so the predicate needs no second store read to express it. `resumeOnPod` reaches the restore path exactly when `SelectForResume` returns a row and takes the snapshotless rebuild exactly when it returns `ErrNotFound`, which is the case where the session has neither a restorable partial nor an active `partial = false` row (C4). The eviction-state arm therefore applies exactly when the resume restored nothing: when the restore driver restored no bytes and a `session_eviction_state` row exists, the mode is `conversation_only` with `workspaceLost: true`; when it restored a checkpoint, its reported `RestoreMode` is the mode and the eviction row does not outrank it. "Restored nothing" covers both `SelectForResume` returning `ErrNotFound` and the terminal absent-chunks arm C2 defines, and on the second of those the eviction row outranks the `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` failure, because `spec/04:264`'s minimal-state record is the designed degradation for a workspace that is known to be gone and a `conversation_only` resume is strictly better for the client than a `failed` session. `HasEvictionState` (`evictionstatestore.go`) keeps its boolean signature, because the comparison the presence check cannot express is no longer needed.

A generation comparison was considered and rejected. Keying the arm on the record's `recovery_generation` (requiring a full row at or above it) is stricter than `spec/04:264` and discards durable workspaces: `recovery_generation` is bumped on every pod recovery, so the ordinary sequence of a checkpoint at generation N, a resume to N+1, and an eviction at N+1 with the object store down leaves every full row strictly below the record's generation, and the session would resume `conversation_only` while holding a restorable checkpoint. `spec/04:287` confirms the intent in the other direction: a session with `had_prior_checkpoint: true` is "resumable from an earlier snapshot". Keying it on `SelectForResume`'s result was also rejected: that selector returns the newest restorable row in the total order and may legitimately return a `partial = true` row, so it cannot answer a question about `partial = false` rows, and in the common case the rule exists for (an eviction row, a durable full checkpoint, and a newer restorable partial) it would return the partial and the classifier could not decide.

**Change (requirement (c): the eviction-state row is superseded by a later successful checkpoint).** `evictionstatestore.Delete` (`evictionstatestore.go:234`) has no non-test caller, and `spec/04:282` cleans the table only at terminal state, so without a clear-on-success path one fallback write makes every later resume of that session `conversation_only` with `workspaceLost: true` forever, discarding a workspace the session in fact holds. The C2 upload driver therefore deletes the session's `session_eviction_state` row on every `partial = false` finalisation, in the same best-effort post-finalisation step as the retention-catalog INSERT and the `sessions.last_successful_checkpoint_at` stamp (`checkpointer.go:305-311`), which already carries the rule that "a catalog or rotation failure does not unwind the successful snapshot".

**`spec/04:282` is edited with it, on both of the things it says.** The paragraph states that "The `session_eviction_state` table is cleaned up when the session reaches a terminal state", which the new trigger falsifies, and it carries an ordering mandate for the eviction-context object: when `last_message_context` exceeded 2 KB it was stored as a MinIO object at `/{tenant_id}/eviction/{session_id}/context` (`pkg/gateway/storage/evictionfallback/evictionfallback.go:53`, `:86-92`), paired with an `artifact_store` row of `artifact_type = eviction_context` and a `StorageQuotaSink.Adjust(tenant, +size)` (`:139-165`), and the gateway "deletes the corresponding MinIO object … atomically as part of the same terminal-state cleanup sequence — the object deletion is issued before the Postgres row is removed". The row is the only in-band pointer to that key, `evictionstatestore.Delete` only stamps `deleted_at` and issues no object-store call (`pgstore/pgstore.go:160-162`), and `Get` filters `deleted_at IS NULL` (`:131-132`), so a clear-on-success that removed the row without the object would permanently defeat the terminal-state object deletion: the later cleanup would read `ErrNotFound`, never learn the key, and leave up to 64 KB per session on the tenant's `storage_bytes_used` until the object-type-blind `SoftDeleteSession` terminal sweep reclaimed it, which is the §12.5 backstop reserved for objects that escape the path.

`spec/04:282` therefore states two cleanup triggers rather than one: a later `partial = false` finalisation supersedes the record, and terminal-state cleanup retires whatever remains. Both own the context-object delete, and both issue it before the Postgres row is removed. C7's post-finalisation step reads `last_message_context` first and, when it is an object key, soft-deletes the object through the tenant-scoped `ArtifactStore` (transitioning its `artifact_store` row and releasing its `artifact_size_bytes`) before it calls `evictionstatestore.Delete`. The step stays best-effort in both halves: a failed object delete leaves the row and the object for the next successful checkpoint or for terminal-state cleanup, and the §12.5 backstop is the outer guarantee, which is the same failure posture `spec/04:282` already assigns to the terminal-state sequence. A delete failure leaves the row and the next successful checkpoint retries it; the precedence rule (b) is what keeps that transient state from degrading a resume, because a full checkpoint at or above the record's `recovery_generation` already beats the row whether or not the delete landed. The two requirements are therefore complementary rather than redundant: (b) makes a stale row harmless and (c) stops it accumulating.

**Change (the grace-period default and the webhook rule).** `pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go`'s `defaultTerminationGraceSeconds` (`:75`) rises from 120s to 180s, and `pkg/admission/pool_config_validator/validator.go`'s `decideTerminationBudget` (`:585-660`, which is its doc comment as well as its body) enforces the S3 budget rule:

```
session mode:
  floor = maxConcurrentSessions
            × (max(MaxTieredCheckpointCapSeconds(workspaceSizeLimitBytes), 90) + postgresFallbackBudgetSeconds)
        + preStopDrainMarginSeconds
        + sdkDemoteReserveSeconds

service mode:
  floor = preStopDrainMarginSeconds + sdkDemoteReserveSeconds
```

Six edits to the shipped function. (1) The multiplier and the mode split: `effectiveMaxConcurrent` (`validator.go:459-462`) returns 1 for every session-mode pool and therefore never multiplies the floor for the only class that runs serialized per-slot eviction checkpoints, so `SessionPolicy` gains `MaxConcurrentSessions *int32` (S3) and `effectiveMaxConcurrent` returns it for session mode. A service-mode pool takes the two-term floor instead: it materializes no workspace (`spec/05:391`), its session is claimless (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create.go:326`), so `ListActiveByPod` resolves no session for its terminating pod and no eviction checkpoint and no Postgres fallback run on it (S3). (2) The per-slot term gains the 60s `postgresFallbackBudgetSeconds` and is floored at the 90s tier the gateway must select for a session with no prior checkpoint (`spec/10:108`, `prestop.MaxTierBudget`). (3) `barrierAck` leaves the sum, because it bounds a gateway drain rather than the agent pod's own termination (S3). (4) `minStreamDrainSeconds` (30) becomes `preStopDrainMarginSeconds` (10), the value the podspec actually reserves (`podspec.go:77-82`), and the sum gains `sdkDemoteReserveSeconds` (`sdkDemoteTimeoutSeconds + sdkDemoteGraceMarginSeconds`, 10s at the defaults, `podspec.go:84-93`), which is the demote-and-exit window the adapter spends after `NotifyPodTerminating` returns and which is serial with the gateway's checkpoint window rather than inside it. (5) The floor is compared against the pool's **effective** grace period rather than only against an explicitly set one: the `spec.TerminationGracePeriodSeconds != nil` guard at `validator.go:644` is dropped, and the comparison reads `effective := defaultAgentTerminationGraceSeconds; if spec.TerminationGracePeriodSeconds != nil && *spec.TerminationGracePeriodSeconds > 0 { effective = *spec.TerminationGracePeriodSeconds }`, where `defaultAgentTerminationGraceSeconds` is a new validator constant mirroring the podspec's `defaultTerminationGraceSeconds` (180s). Without it a `maxConcurrentSessions: 2` pool that omits the field is admitted (its 320s floor sits below the 600s warning threshold), renders pods with the flat 180s default, and is SIGKILLed part-way through its second slot's serialized eviction checkpoint (S3). The guard can only be dropped because the writer path S3 adds makes `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` settable on a pool: a `termination_grace_period_seconds` column on the pools table, the field on `poolstore.Pool` and its pgstore, the admin-API pool create and update payloads, and the `PoolStoreSource.toConfig` mirror onto `SandboxTemplateSpec.TerminationGracePeriodSeconds` (`poolstoresource.go:65-96`). (6) The doc comments are restated with the code, because the tier-11 assertion compares the rule's statement in `decideTerminationBudget`, in `DecideTemplate`, and in the package doc against the spec. Five doc sites state the retired floor or the retired mode rule: the `decideTerminationBudget` comment (`:591-600`, which states the retired formula, and `:602-612`, whose first bullet states the retired `is set and below the floor` guard); the **package doc comment** (`validator.go:16-25`), which restates the retired floor `maxConcurrent × max_tiered_checkpoint_cap + checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds + 30` verbatim and asserts that "a session-mode pool uses a multiplier of 1"; `DecideTemplate`'s own doc comment (`:406-411`), which restates the same formula and the same multiplier collapse; and `DecideTemplate`'s inline budget rationale (`:437-444`), which asserts that "the rule is not subject to the executionMode the deployer happened to pick", the mode-independence the two-arm split falsifies (a service-mode pool now carries neither the tier cap nor the Postgres fallback). All five are restated on the agent-pod floor: the session/service split, the `maxConcurrentSessions` multiplier, the `postgresFallbackBudgetSeconds` and `sdkDemoteReserveSeconds` terms, the dropped `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` term, and the effective-grace comparison. C7's edit range for the file therefore covers `:1-35`, `:396-446`, `:452-462`, and `:585-660`.

The CRD mirror lands where the CRD is actually written. `poolstoresource.go:124-130` writes the controller's internal `PoolConfig` and never the CRD spec; `sessionPolicyToCRD` (`:145-159`, called at `:89-90`) is the only renderer of `lennyv1.SessionPolicy`, and it returns nil for a pool with no recycle scrub control, which is every ordinary non-recycling pool. C7 edits that function so it emits a non-nil `SessionPolicy` carrying `MaxConcurrentSessions` whenever the store row sets it, independently of the recycle guards, and restates its doc comment (S3). Without that edit the field exists on the Go type, the webhook reads nil, and every non-recycling `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pool is admitted at the collapsed 170s floor.

**The two deployer-facing pages that document the pool's fields are edited with the rule**, because the dropped guard turns a currently-valid documented configuration into a `422`. `docs/reference/configuration.md` states each field's "default value, description, and validation rules" (`:11`), carries no `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` row in either its Pool configuration table (`:70-79`) or its sessionPolicy table (`:87-105`), and gives `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions` the Validation cell "`> 1` requires `acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation: true`" (`:87`), which is accurate against the shipped `!= nil` guard and false afterwards: a pool satisfying everything the page states, at the 180s default, is rejected at `maxConcurrentSessions: 2` against its 320s floor. The page gains a `pool.terminationGracePeriodSeconds` row carrying the 180s default and the two-arm floor as its validation rule, and the `maxConcurrentSessions` row's Validation cell names that floor alongside the isolation acknowledgment. `docs/api/admin.md`'s `POST /v1/admin/pools` request body (`:252-268`) and `PUT /v1/admin/pools/{name}` (`:289`) gain the field, which is the surface the deployer raises it through. Without both edits the only remediation for the new fail-closed rejection is undiscoverable from the pages that document the pool.

The `422 INVALID_POOL_CONFIGURATION` rejection, its `lenny_pool_termination_budget_exceeded_total` metric, the >600s node-drain-timeout warning, the `maxTerminationGracePeriodSeconds` hard-ceiling rejection, the BarrierAck-floor rule (`checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds ≥ max_tiered_checkpoint_cap`, the check at `validator.go:628-634` with its doc bullet at `:609-612`), the scope over every `SandboxTemplate.spec` mutation including PoolScalingController SSA applies, and the `lenny-preflight` check are all unchanged. The rejection message names the new terms and the effective value it compared. `terminationGrace` (`podspec.go:1193-1220`) keeps its precedence order and its flat default: the deployer's §5.2 base overrides the default, `maxTerminationGracePeriodSeconds` clamps it down, and the preConnect SDK-demote floor still takes precedence over the clamp. The default stays one constant rather than a formula the podspec evaluates, so the pool's `maxConcurrentSessions` and `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` stay out of `podspec.Inputs` and the budget rule keeps a single evaluator.

Five shipped tests pin the retired floor, the retired guard, or the ceiling rejection the retired floor produced, and are rewritten with the rule. All five apply **service-mode** templates, so the mode split decides what each becomes.

- `TestDecideTemplate_TerminationGraceFloor_spec_5_2_516` (`pkg/admission/pool_config_validator/validator_test.go:308`) pins `// floor = 2*90 + 90 + 30 = 300s` and is rewritten onto the new formula.
- `TestDecideTemplateRejectsBelowGraceFloor_spec_5_2_516` (`tests/tier2_component/admission/pool_config_validator_test.go:182`) applies `ExecutionMode: "service"`, `MaxConcurrent: 8`, and an explicit `TerminationGracePeriodSeconds: 120` (`:185-201`), and asserts a rejection whose reason names an "840s" floor. Under the staged rule that template's floor is the 20s service-mode one, which its 120s grace period clears, so `DecideTemplate` admits it and every assertion inverts (`d.Allowed`, `d.Code != 422`, `d.BudgetExceeded`, and the "840s" reason string). The test is rewritten to carry `executionMode: session` with `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions: 8`, which is the template class the 1220s session-mode floor applies to, and keeps its rejection, its `422`, its `BudgetExceeded`, and its floor-naming assertion against that floor.
- `TestPoolConfigValidatorPropagatesTerminationGraceWarning_spec_5_2_516` (`pkg/admission/webhook/pool_config_validator_test.go:191`) applies `ExecutionMode: "service"`, `MaxConcurrent: 8`, and no `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` (`:191-198`), and asserts it is admitted with one warning naming an "840s" floor. Under the staged rule that pool's floor drops to 20s, which the 180s effective default clears and which is far below the 600s `nodeDrainWarnSeconds` threshold (`pkg/admission/pool_config_validator/validator.go:583`), so the case flips from admit-with-one-warning to admit-with-no-warning: `resp.Allowed` stands and the `len(resp.Warnings) != 1` and `"840s"` assertions are the two that break. The warning-propagation behaviour the test exists to pin is preserved by giving it a session-mode template with `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions: 8`, whose 1220s floor is above the warning threshold, and asserting the warning names that floor.

- `TestPoolConfigValidatorRejectsTerminationGraceCeilingBreach_spec_5_2_516` (`pkg/admission/webhook/pool_config_validator_test.go:215`) applies `ExecutionMode: "service"`, `MaxConcurrent: 8`, and `MaxTerminationGracePeriodSeconds: 600` with no grace period (`:221-223`), and asserts the **ceiling** rejection: not allowed, a 422, and a message naming `spec.maxTerminationGracePeriodSeconds (600s)` (`:227-233`). The shipped floor for that template is `8 × 90 + 90 + 30 = 840s`, which breaches the 600s ceiling; under the service-mode arm its floor is 20s, which does not, so the template is admitted and all three assertions invert. It is re-keyed onto a session-mode template carrying `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions: 8`, whose 1220s floor breaches the same 600s ceiling, and keeps its rejection, its 422, and its ceiling-naming assertion.
- The `"maxTerminationGracePeriodSeconds breach sets BudgetExceeded"` subtest of `TestDecideTemplate_BudgetExceededDiscriminator_spec_10_1_129` (`pkg/admission/pool_config_validator/validator_test.go:479-489`) pins the same ceiling breach on `ExecutionMode: "service"`, `MaxConcurrent: 8`, `MaxTerminationGracePeriodSeconds: 600`, asserting a rejection naming "exceeds spec.maxTerminationGracePeriodSeconds" and `d.BudgetExceeded`. It inverts for the same reason and is re-keyed the same way, preserving the discriminator the test exists to pin: a ceiling breach sets `BudgetExceeded` and the BarrierAck-floor rejection does not.

The `"service mode carries no pool-config invariant"` case of `TestTemplate` (`validator_test.go:232`) applies `executionMode: service, maxConcurrent: 4` with no grace period and expects admission; it **keeps** that expectation, because the service-mode floor is 20s and the effective default is 180s. The case is what pins the mode split, so §6 asserts it explicitly rather than leaving it to the table.

`TestPoolStoreSourceLeavesSessionPolicyNilWithoutScrubControl` (`pkg/controller/poolscaling/poolstoresource_test.go:263`) asserts that a session-mode pool with no scrub control renders `SessionPolicy = nil`, which the mirror above falsifies for any pool carrying `maxConcurrentSessions`. It is rewritten: a row with no recycle block and no `maxConcurrentSessions` still renders nil, and a row carrying `maxConcurrentSessions` renders a `SessionPolicy` carrying it and no recycle block.

## 5. Non-goals

- **No presigned URLs, per-pod scoped S3/STS credentials, MinIO/S3 §4.9 credential-lease provider, agent-namespace egress rule to MinIO, or MinIO ingress allow for agent namespaces.** The gateway-mediated path needs none of these, and `spec/12_storage-architecture.md:307` forbids relocating tenant-prefix enforcement into a caller.
- **No streaming or multipart `Put` on `blobstore.Store`.** The 16 MiB chunk boundary keeps every backend's `io.ReadAll` (`pkg/blobstore/s3/s3.go:231`, `pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:321`) bounded, so the three verbs of the `Store` interface at `pkg/blobstore/blobstore.go:306-321` keep their signatures and every existing caller is unchanged. The buffering is deliberate, for retry replay (`miniostore.go:320-324`). C2 does add a separate context-carrying interface (`PutContext`, `GetContext`, `SoftDeleteContext`) alongside `Store`, implemented by every backend `blobproviderflags.Resolve` can return and forwarded through `cataloging` and `TenantScoped`, because no verb on `Store` takes a context and each backend opens its own `context.Background()` (`miniostore.go:44`, `:259`, whose 36-second retry ladder is uncancellable; `s3.go:222-223`), which no checkpoint deadline or retry budget could otherwise bound.
- **No prefix-listing method on `blobstore.Store`.** `chunk_count` and `chunk_encoding` on the manifest row make every chunk key derivable, and a `Get` on a missing index is the contiguity check, so no consumer needs `ListObjectsV2`. All three of the spec's current listing consumers (the restore contiguity check at `spec/10:155`, the supersede delete at `spec/10:137`, and the §12.5 GC backstop at `spec/12:328`) are converted to derivation by S2, along with the `spec/10:139` rationale clause that names prefix listing as the reassembly mechanism, and the counter-reservation ordering (S1) is what makes the derived key set a superset of the committed objects.
- **No per-chunk `artifact_store` bypass.** The chunk drivers `Put` through the production `cataloging` store rather than around it, so the catalog row is written by the same code path as every other artifact and no checkpoint row is keyed on a bare prefix. The alternative (Put through the un-cataloged inner store and hand-write one prefix-keyed row in the finalising transaction) produces a row whose URI fails `blobstore.ParseURI`, which the `cataloging` GC sweeps skip (`pkg/blobstore/cataloging/cataloging.go:386-391`), leaving the row un-GC-able and its bytes permanently on the tenant's quota.
- **No widening of the `blobstore` URI grammar to admit a slashed `PartID`** (the dropped C3). Every backend interpolates `u.PartID` raw into the object key (`pkg/blobstore/s3/s3.go:214`, `miniostore/miniostore.go:218`, `gcs/gcs.go:214`, `azureblob/azureblob.go:228`, `blobstore.go:529`), so a slash would land the object under the right prefix, but `URI.String()` percent-escapes it (`blobstore.go:296-303`) and `ParseURI` then decodes it back into a four-segment path that falls into the `ErrInvalidURI` default branch (`blobstore.go:233-256`). Production wraps the store in `cataloging` (`cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1085-1091`), whose GC sweeps re-parse the catalog URI and skip any row that fails to parse (`pkg/blobstore/cataloging/cataloging.go:387`, `:437`), so a slashed `PartID` would make every chunk permanently un-GC-able, and `fsstore` would collapse the slash into a single escaped filename (`pkg/blobstore/fsstore.go:107-115`, `:138`). S2's flat `{checkpoint_id}-chunk-{n}` `PartID` needs no blobstore change, keeps the canonical four-segment §12.5 key format, and follows the precedent at `cmd/lenny-gateway/legalholdescrow.go:94`, `:219`, which flattens a multi-segment escrow key into one `PartID` segment.
- **No single-object fast path for small workspaces.** A one-chunk prefix is the same path with `chunk_count = 1`; a second upload path would reintroduce the full-versus-partial asymmetry this change removes.
- **No permanent-error short-circuit in `sealWorkspace`.** The only permanent seal error identified is the `codes.Unimplemented` that C1 deletes, so after this change the loop succeeds on the first attempt. `failWorkspaceSeal` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:143-181`) transitions the session to `failed` regardless of whether the loop gave up in 5s or 300s, so failing faster would not make §6.2 occupancy-zero recycling reachable; C1 and C2 making the seal succeed is what does. A third `outcome` label value would also contradict `spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:112` and `spec/16_observability.md:227`, which enumerate the label as `success` and `timeout`, and `spec/07:112` bounds the retry window deliberately so that a permanent MinIO outage cannot hold pods in `draining` indefinitely.
- **No gateway-side agent-pod informer.** The agent-pod eviction trigger ships (S3, C7), driven by the pod's own preStop hook rather than by a gateway watch on pods. RBAC is not a ground for the rejection: the gateway `ClusterRole` already grants `get`, `list`, `watch`, and `patch` on core `pods` cluster-wide (`charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:176-178`, `:191-203`), so an informer would widen no Kubernetes surface. The informer is rejected on two grounds. It needs a per-replica ownership and dedup design, because every gateway replica receives every delete event while only the session's coordinator may checkpoint it, and no informer or watch machinery exists in `pkg/gateway` to build that on. And it races the kubelet: the watch event and the termination grace clock both start at the `DELETE`, so the gateway learns of the eviction concurrently with the pod and has no mechanism to hold the pod open while it checkpoints. The preStop signal has the opposite property, because the kubelet blocks the container's `SIGTERM` on the hook's exit.
- **No unhealthy-threshold drain trigger.** `DrainSandbox` (`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:598`) stamps `lenny.dev/drain-request` and does not terminate a pod carrying live sessions (`spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:178`), so it is not an eviction seam; the rationale is in §2 and C5. When such a pod does eventually terminate, its preStop hook fires and the C7 signal covers it as an agent-pod eviction like any other.
- **No pre-scale-down checkpoint driver.** `checkpoint.TriggerPreScaleDown` stays unimplemented and its `trigger` label value stays unemitted. `pkg/controller/warmpool` is a separate binary with no checkpointer and no gateway channel, and no spec section defines the driver, so wiring it means designing a controller-to-gateway surface. A scale-down that terminates a pod carrying live sessions already runs that pod's preStop hook and is therefore already checkpointed by the C7 signal, as an agent-pod eviction; what stays unbuilt is a checkpoint taken *before* the controller decides to release, which no spec section requires.
- **No retention-catalog fold.** `session_checkpoints` stays the retention and rotation catalog and `checkpoint_manifest` stays the upload and restore manifest, so a successful checkpoint writes two rows. Folding rotation into the manifest would touch the §12.5 GC rules and the `artifact_store` tombstone sweep, both of which key on `session_checkpoints`, for a consolidation nothing in this change needs. The rationale is in §2.
- **No `manifest_reason` drop.** The column is kept and single-valued (`timeout`). The rationale is in §2, and the supersede transaction's settled-row term keys on it (S2).
- **No new pod-local query surface into the adapter.** The `prestop` subcommand keeps its `{signal, alive, sleep, logf}` dependency set (`cmd/lenny-adapter/prestop.go:31-36`) and learns nothing about the adapter's op-lock or its sessions. The termination signal travels adapter→gateway on the channel the adapter already holds, so the hook's contract with the adapter stays "signal it, then wait for it to exit" (C7). The sidecar `runtime` container's `prestop-wait` hook narrows that contract rather than widening it: it waits for the adapter to exit and does not signal it, and its liveness signal is the adapter's existing TCP listener rather than a new endpoint, a new port, or a query API.
- **No new listener, port, or NetworkPolicy edge in the agent pod.** The `runtime` container's wait-only hook polls `127.0.0.1:50051`, the port the adapter already binds, across the network namespace the pod's containers already share. A preStop `httpGet` or `tcpSocket` handler was considered and rejected: `tcpSocket` returns as soon as the connection is established and is therefore not a barrier, and `httpGet` would need a new HTTP listener and a second container port, which the agent pod's §13.2 NetworkPolicies do not admit: `allow-pod-egress-base` permits TCP 50051 and DNS alone (`spec/13:80-114`, `:91-100`) and the gateway's ingress row admits agent namespaces on TCP 50051 (`spec/13:216`), so a second port is a new NetworkPolicy edge.
- **No gateway-side `LifecycleChannel` consumer for adapter control events.** None exists today, and the eviction seam chosen here does not need one.
- **No incremental or differential checkpoints.** `spec/04_system-components.md:253` defers them.
- **No fix for the concurrent-slot release path's indifference to the session disposition** (`pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:313-317` → `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:525-557` never consults it, so a failed session on a `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pod does not retire the pod, contradicting `spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:24`). The defect is verified and independent of the checkpoint gap, and it belongs with the §5.2 slot-lifecycle work in proposal 0035.

## 6. Testing

The change reaches tier 0 and tier 1 for the touched packages plus higher tiers per `.claude/rules/test-coverage.md`: tier 1 for the pool-config webhook's grace-budget decision function; tier 2 for the Postgres-backed manifest store, the `artifact_store` accounting, the rotation and TTL sweeps, the resume classifier, the pool-config admission decision through the real CRD codec, the new pool column's migration contract, and the admin-API and `poolstore` writer path for `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`; tier 3 for the `Checkpoint`, `Resume`, `CheckpointBarrier`, and lifecycle-event contracts and for the §15.1 session envelope against the hand-authored OpenAPI document; tier 4 for the multi-service produce-store-restore flow, the cross-replica eviction notification, the workspace-download endpoint, and derive; tier 5 for the kind drain and eviction journeys and the admission rejection; tier 7 for the gateway-driven barrier flush; tier 8 for the MinIO outage; tier 9 for tenant isolation on the chunk keys; tier 10 for the adapter conformance surface; and tier 11 for spec/code consistency.

- **tier-1 gateway is the only object-store caller (spec-named-failure path, S1/C1):** In `pkg/adapter`, assert by construction that the adapter package imports no `blobstore` and no S3 client, and that `Checkpoint` and `Resume` compile with no storage seam. Drive `Checkpoint` with a stream whose consumer returns an error mid-archive and assert the adapter aborts, restores the runtime, and issues no storage call. `// spec: 13.1 (no standing credentials, gateway-mediated file delivery), 10.1 (chunk uploads are gateway-issued). // diagnosis: a failure means the agent pod regained an object-store dependency §13 forbids.`
- **tier-1 chunk slicing and the one-chunk heap bound (boundary path, C2):** In the upload driver, assert that an archive stream of exactly `chunk_size_bytes`, of `chunk_size_bytes + 1`, and of zero bytes produce `chunk_count` of 1, 2, and 0 respectively, that the zero-byte case soft-deletes the intent row and falls through to the §4.4 storage-failure path (`spec/10:132`), and that at most one chunk body is resident at a time. `// spec: 10.1 (chunked-object storage model, chunk_count == 0 finalisation).`
- **tier-1 intent-row-first ordering under a mid-upload gateway crash (spec-named-failure path, C2/C4):** Assert no `PutObject` is issued before the intent-row INSERT commits, and that a driver killed after chunk 2 leaves a `partial = true, deleted_at IS NULL` row whose derived key set the §12.5 backstop can sweep. The non-happy path is an orphaned chunk with no referencing row. `// spec: 10.1 (intent-row-first ordering, orphan prevention).`
- **tier-1 the summary frame is the finalisation gate (boundary path, S3/C2/C4):** Assert that a stream carrying a terminal `CheckpointSummary` whose `size_bytes` equals the committed byte count sets `partial = false` with the committed counters; that a stream reaching EOF without a summary, and one whose summary disagrees with the committed byte count, each finalise `partial = true` with `manifest_reason = 'timeout'` rather than claiming a complete checkpoint; and that a Postgres failure on the finalising UPDATE leaves the row `partial = true` rather than a `partial = false` row with an incomplete chunk set. The non-happy path is a truncated archive that becomes the session's full checkpoint. `// spec: 10.1 (finalisation), 4.4 (checkpoint atomicity).`
- **tier-1 counter reservation precedes the chunk Put (spec-named-failure path, S1/C2):** Assert the `chunk_count = n + 1` `UPDATE` commits before the `PutObject` for index `n` is issued, that a failed counter `UPDATE` aborts the attempt instead of continuing the upload, and that a driver killed between the counter `UPDATE` and the `PutObject` leaves a row whose derived key set covers every committed object and whose reserved-but-uncommitted index deletes as a no-op. The non-happy path is a committed chunk past the last counter update, which no sweep can enumerate. `// spec: 10.1 (chunk uploads, orphan prevention).`
- **tier-1 finalisation resolves the chunk whose Put outcome it never saw (spec-named-failure path, S1/C2):** With the per-attempt deadline firing after index `n`'s body was fully sent but before its response was observed, and the object therefore committed server-side, assert the driver `Stat`s the derived key on its detached bookkeeping context before the finalising `UPDATE`, counts the chunk (so `chunk_count` keeps the reserved value and `workspace_bytes_uploaded` includes its bytes), and leaves no object outside `[0, chunk_count)`. Assert the second case: an index whose `Stat` definitively reports the object absent, or present at a different byte length, is soft-deleted and the count is lowered. Assert the third case, which is the dominant one on the deadline path this resolution exists for: with the object store unreachable at the deadline, so that the resolving `Stat` returns a transport error rather than `blobstore.ErrNotFound` (`pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:513-517`), the driver keeps the **reserved** `chunk_count`, issues no soft-delete (which would fail on its own `StatObject` precheck anyway, `miniostore.go:785-793`), and leaves the index inside the derived key set, so the abort sweep, the supersede delete, and the §12.5 backstop all still reach it. Assert a resolved or unresolved chunk does not make a truncated archive `partial = false`, since the terminal `CheckpointSummary` gate is unchanged and `workspace_bytes_uploaded` advances only on an observed `Put`. Assert the restore's reading of an unresolved index: on a `partial = true` row whose highest index is absent, the driver reassembles the objects it did read and does not take the contiguity-failure arm, while an absent index below the highest one does take it. The non-happy path is a finalising `UPDATE` that lowers `chunk_count` past a committed object, which then has neither a manifest-derived name nor an `artifact_store` row (`cataloging.Store.Put` inserts the row only on success, `pkg/blobstore/cataloging/cataloging.go:143-171`), so no sweep can reach it and its bytes stay on the tenant's counter forever. `// spec: 10.1 (finalisation, chunk_count as the reserved index count), 12.5 (artifact_store accounting).`
- **tier-2 artifact_store rows per checkpoint (boundary path, C2):** Against Postgres with the production `cataloging` store, assert a finalised `chunk_count = N` checkpoint has exactly `N` `artifact_store` rows whose `artifact_size_bytes` sum to `workspace_bytes_uploaded`, that a `partial = true` checkpoint's chunks are catalogued the same way, that no row is keyed on a bare prefix (every row's URI round-trips through `blobstore.ParseURI`), and that the tenant's `storage_bytes_used` matches the reserved-then-reconciled total. The non-happy path is a double count or an un-GC-able prefix row. `// spec: 12.5 (artifact_store covers all MinIO-backed artifacts), 11.2 (storage quota).`
- **tier-1 quota reservation precedes the first chunk (fail-closed path, C2):** Assert the driver reserves `CheckpointStart.probed_bytes` before the first `Put`, that a reservation rejection aborts the checkpoint with no byte written to the object store, that the unused remainder is released at finalisation, and that the whole reservation is released when the attempt commits no chunk. `// spec: 11.2 (pre-upload size check, atomic reservation).`
- **tier-1 the hard stream cap closes the under-declaration bypass (fail-closed path, S2/C2):** With an adapter that declares `CheckpointStart{probed_bytes: 1}` and then streams past the tenant's remaining quota headroom, assert the driver aborts with `STORAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, commits no chunk beyond the cap, and takes the abort sweep. The non-happy path is a pod that writes an arbitrarily large workspace into MinIO by underreporting its probe. `// spec: 11.2 (hard stream cap, storage quota). // diagnosis: a failure means the §11.2 quota gate is bypassable from inside the agent pod.`
- **tier-2 abort accounting returns the tenant's counter to its pre-checkpoint value (spec-named-failure path, S2/C2):** Against Postgres with the production `cataloging` store and a live quota counter, abort an attempt that has committed two chunks (separately: a stream error, a `Put` error, and a failed counter `UPDATE`) and assert that with the object store reachable `storage_bytes_used` returns exactly to its pre-checkpoint value, with no double decrement and no leaked reservation. Assert the sweep's failure behaviour separately, because the committed chunk bytes leave the counter only through the per-chunk `SoftDelete` and that call cannot run against an unreachable store: `cataloging.Store.SoftDelete` returns the inner tombstone's error before it reads the catalog row or releases the bytes (`pkg/blobstore/cataloging/cataloging.go:230-236`, `:262-268`), and `miniostore.Store.SoftDelete` fails on its `StatObject` precheck rather than taking the missing-object no-op (`pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:785-793`). With the object store unreachable during the sweep, assert instead that the unused remainder `probed_bytes − workspace_bytes_uploaded` is released, that the committed chunks' bytes stay on the counter because the chunks still exist, that `lenny_checkpoint_orphaned_objects_total` increments, and that those bytes are reclaimed later by the sweep that owns the chunks (the terminal-session `SoftDeleteSession` pass and the §12.5 backstop). The non-happy path is a counter that drifts below actual usage, which no path reconciles until a Redis restart rehydrates from Postgres. `// spec: 11.2 (release on failure), 12.5 (artifact_store accounting), 4.4 (orphaned-objects counter).`
- **tier-1 the abort sweep deletes the chunks it committed (spec-named-failure path, S1/C2):** With two chunks committed, assert that an archive-stream error, a `Put` error, and a failed counter `UPDATE` each make the gateway issue an `ArtifactStore` soft-delete for every index in `[0, chunk_count)` including the reserved-but-uncommitted index, soft-delete the manifest row under the `deleted_at IS NULL` guard, and leave no `artifact_store` row un-tombstoned and no byte of the attempt on the tenant's quota counter; and that a deadline fire instead finalises `partial = true` and retains the chunks. The non-happy path is an aborted attempt whose chunks stay in MinIO under a soft-deleted row. `// spec: 4.4 (checkpoint abort cleanup), 10.1 (derived chunk keys, finalisation on deadline fire).`
- **tier-1 a retried chunk `Put` is idempotent against the write-once store (spec-named-failure path, C2):** With a chunk `Put` whose first attempt commits the object server-side but is observed as a timeout or is cut by the per-attempt deadline, assert the driver's retry of that index succeeds rather than aborting: the `StatObject` precheck's `ErrConflict` (`pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:277-283`) is treated as success when `Stat` reports the byte length being written, the attempt runs to a finalised checkpoint, and no committed chunk is swept. Assert that an `ErrConflict` whose `Stat` reports a different byte length does abort the attempt. The non-happy path is a transient MinIO blip on the drain path turning into a permanently lost checkpoint, which is the failure `RetryBudgetFor(TriggerEviction)` exists to ride out. `// spec: 4.5 (write-once immutability), 4.4 (eviction retry budget).`
- **tier-1 the orphan counter fires when the sweep cannot delete (spec-named-failure path, C1/C2):** Assert a chunk soft-delete failure during the abort sweep, and a resolved store that carries no per-key delete surface, each increment `lenny_checkpoint_orphaned_objects_total` with its `pool` and `trigger` labels. This is the counter's only emitter after C1 deletes `runAbortCleanup`. `// spec: 4.4 (orphaned-objects counter).`
- **tier-2 the delete path runs against the production store (spec-named-failure path, C2):** Against Postgres with the tenant-scoped `cataloging` store production wires (`cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1090`), assert that a chunk delete actually removes the object and transitions its `artifact_store` row rather than taking the orphan-and-skip branch, that no `ListObjects` call and no raw `RemoveObject` is issued on the path, and that a cross-tenant chunk URI is rejected by the wrapper before any delete is issued. The non-happy path is a type assertion that never matches the production store, which would orphan every chunk on every Postgres-backed gateway. `tests/tier2_component/stores/partialcleanup_component_test.go` is rewritten into this assertion: its `minioPrefixDeleter` (`:35-46`) implements the `DeleteByPrefix` surface C2 removes, discovers chunks by `ListObjects`, and issues the raw `RemoveObject` the staged `spec/04:249` text declares not permitted. `// spec: 12.5 (tenant isolation, artifact_store lifecycle), 4.4 (checkpoint abort cleanup).`
- **tier-1 one attempt per session at a time (concurrent path, C2):** Assert an eviction-trigger checkpoint racing an in-flight periodic checkpoint for one session serialises on the single-flight lock, that neither attempt's supersede step deletes a chunk of an upload still running, and that no chunk is left under a soft-deleted prefix. Assert the same for the barrier-dispatch racer specifically, which reaches the row through a different call path: with a periodic checkpoint of one session mid-chunk, fire `prestop.Hook.run`'s barrier for that session at the same `coordination_generation` and assert the barrier's reservation takes the same lock, cancels and settles the periodic attempt before it mints, and deletes no chunk of an upload still running. Assert the reservation then commits its own intent row and finalises: the preempted periodic attempt settles well inside its recorded `checkpoint_timeout_at`, leaving either a `partial = true, manifest_reason = 'timeout'` row that the barrier's supersede matches through the `manifest_reason IS NOT NULL` arm or, at `chunk_count == 0`, a soft-deleted row, so the barrier neither aborts on the supersede guard nor collides with `partial_manifest_active_uniq`. Assert the same for a checkpoint that follows an archive stream truncated seconds into a 60-second deadline. Assert separately, on a row belonging to a different attempt, that a supersede whose target row carries no `manifest_reason` and whose `checkpoint_timeout_at` has not elapsed (an upload still running on another replica) aborts the superseding attempt instead of clobbering it. The non-happy path is a drain-time barrier chunk-deleting an in-flight periodic checkpoint of the same session on the same replica, which the generation fence admits because both attempts read the same lease generation, and the mirror failure of a barrier or eviction checkpoint that takes no checkpoint at all because the row it preempted still sits inside its own deadline. `// spec: 10.1 (supersede-on-write, at-most-one-active-partial invariant, false-positive barriers on handoff).`
- **tier-1 the periodic sweep is a bounded worker pool (boundary path, S2/C2):** Assert `Checkpointer.Sweep` drives up to `gateway.checkpointWorker.concurrency` uploads at once and never more, that a seal, a barrier flush, and an eviction checkpoint acquire the same semaphore so the replica-wide bound holds across every producer, and that the bound does not serialize a session against itself (which is the single-flight lock's job). Assert the completion invariant the knob exists for: a sweep of `maxSessionsPerReplica` sessions, each taking the §4.4 SLO duration (`spec/04:253`), finishes inside `periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds` at the configured concurrency, and that a concurrency of 1 against the Tier 3 figures (400 sessions, 600s interval, 2s per checkpoint) does not, leaving sessions with a last-checkpoint age above the interval. The non-happy path is the shipped serial `for` loop (`checkpointer.go:173-178`), which under this change relays a whole workspace per iteration and pins `lenny_checkpoint_stale_sessions` at `maxSessionsPerReplica`, firing `CheckpointStale` (`spec/16:459`) permanently at the top tier. `// spec: 17.8 (per-replica checkpoint relay budget and the sweep-completion inequality), 4.4 (checkpoint duration SLO), 16.5 (CheckpointStale).`
- **tier-1 tenant-prefix guard on every chunk verb (fail-closed path, C2):** Assert that an upload or restore driver constructed against the raw `blobstore.Store` rather than `blobstore.NewTenantScoped` cannot reach `Put` (a driver-level guard), and that a chunk `Put` under a tenant prefix other than the caller's returns `ErrCrossTenant`. `// spec: 12.5 (tenant isolation lives at the interface boundary). // diagnosis: a failure means the checkpoint path can write across a tenant boundary.`
- **tier-1 T4 fail-closed SSE-KMS on the chunk path (fail-closed path, C2):** With the tenant at `workspaceTier: T4` and the tenant-scoped KMS key unavailable, assert the chunk `Put` returns `ErrClassificationControlViolation`, no chunk commits, and the checkpoint falls through to the §4.4 storage-failure path rather than writing under the deployment-wide key. `// spec: 12.5 (T4 failure behavior when the KMS key is unavailable).`
- **tier-1 restore derives keys and detects a gap (spec-named-failure path, C2):** With `chunk_count = 4` and chunk 2 absent, assert the restore driver detects the contiguity failure, aborts before any adapter frame is sent, and deletes nothing on a `partial = false` row. Assert the fallback is reachable and taken: the driver resolves the newest active `partial = false` row strictly below the failed row through `SelectLatestFullBefore`, streams that checkpoint's chunk set to the adapter, and reports `full`, so the session comes back with the bytes of a durable checkpoint. Assert the terminal arm: a session whose manifest rows all name absent objects and which holds no `session_eviction_state` row (the post-DR-promotion state `spec/25:4095` legislates for) fails the resume with `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` and transitions to `failed`, surfaced on `GET /v1/sessions/{id}`, rather than returning 200 with an empty workspace and `resumeMode: "full"`. Assert the same session **with** a `session_eviction_state` row resumes `conversation_only` with `workspaceLost: true` instead, which is the `spec/04:264` degradation and outranks the terminal failure. Assert the snapshotless rebuild is reached only when `SelectForResume` returns `ErrNotFound`, which is a session with no manifest row at all. `TestResumeWithMissingWorkspaceSnapshotFailsWithSnapshotMissing_spec_25_11` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/workspace_snapshot_missing_resume_test.go:52`), whose `t.Skip` records the detection mechanism as undecided and whose `absentSnapshotSource` implements the `adapter.CheckpointSource` seam C1 deletes, is rewritten onto the restore driver's absent-chunk arm and un-skipped: the mechanism is the gateway-side chunk `Get` returning `blobstore.ErrNotFound` with no fall-back row. Assert the arm on a **full** row as well as on a partial one, because S2 makes a `partial = false` row a chunk set that can fail contiguity and it is the row `SelectForResume` returns in the dominant case: with the session's newest full checkpoint missing a chunk and a second retained full checkpoint intact, the restore comes back with the second checkpoint's bytes rather than looping on the broken row. The non-happy path is a fallback selector that returns the row that just failed, so the driver re-derives the same missing key set, the older retained checkpoint of the latest-2 rotation is unreachable, and the arm never terminates at the snapshotless rebuild. `// spec: 10.1 (reassembly contiguity check, fall back to the last successful full checkpoint).`
- **tier-3 the session envelope carries the reason the terminal arm reports (boundary path, C2):** The assertion above reads `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` out of a `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` body, and the shipped §15.1 envelope has no field that can carry it: `SessionResponse` declares `failureClass` alone (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:2021-2032`), `toResponse` copies only the class (`:3141-3172`), and `failureClassForReason`'s `default` arm maps every reason outside the closed §7.1 catalogue to `runtime_failure` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:393-411`). Assert the envelope carries `failureReason` with the row's coded cause on every `failed` session, that `workspace_seal_timeout` keeps reporting both its class and its reason so no existing consumer regresses, that the `failureClass` enum is unchanged and a session failed with `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` reports `failureClass: "runtime_failure"` with the reason carrying the code, and that the response validates against the `Session` schema in `pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.json` (`:110-136`), which is hand-authored and therefore drifts silently. Assert the three client SDK session types deserialize the field (`sdks/client/go/lenny/types.go`, `sdks/client/python/lenny/types.py`, `sdks/client/typescript/src/types.ts`). The non-happy path is a code the driver reports, the row stores, and no client-visible body ever shows, which leaves `spec/25:4095` unsatisfied while every gateway-side test passes. `// spec: 15.1 (session envelope), 25 (WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING is surfaced through the session-state API), 7.1 (the class is a coarse bucket and the reason is the specific cause).`
- **tier-1 restore cleanup is scoped to partial rows (spec-named-failure path, C2):** Assert that restoring a `partial = true` row deletes its chunks and soft-deletes it on success, on failure, and on a mid-resume abort; and that restoring a `partial = false` row deletes nothing and soft-deletes nothing, so a second resume attempt on a fresh pod still finds the bytes. The non-happy path is a full checkpoint erased by its own restore. `// spec: 4.4 (partial-checkpoint cleanup), 7.2 (mid-resume terminal collapse), 12.5 (latest-2 retention).`
- **tier-1 gateway-minted checkpoint_id (boundary path, P1/C2):** Assert `CheckpointRequest.checkpoint_id` is non-empty and equals the id on the intent row, and that the adapter has no path to mint one. `// spec: 10.1 (the gateway mints the checkpoint_id UUID).`
- **tier-1 Resume first-frame contract (error path, P1/C1):** Assert the adapter rejects a `Resume` stream whose first frame is an `archive_chunk` with `InvalidArgument`, rejects a second `ResumeStart` the same way, and rejects a stream whose `expected_workspace_bytes` exceeds `workspace_size_limit_bytes` before extracting any byte. `// spec: 4.4 (hard workspace size limit), 4.7 (Resume contract).`
- **tier-1 restore staging is a sibling of the resolved workspace root (boundary path, C1):** Assert extraction stages beside `/workspace/current` on a single-session pool and beside `/workspace/slots/{slotId}/current` on a concurrent pool, that a mid-stream extraction error leaves both the workspace root and `/sessions` unmutated, and that the `/sessions` tree commits in the same rename step as the workspace. `// spec: 6.4 (/sessions tmpfs), 6.2 (per-slot workspace roots), 10.1 (atomicity of extraction failure).`
- **tier-1 eviction trigger and retry budget (spec-named-failure path, C5):** Assert the gateway preStop hook's `prestop.CheckpointFn` reaches `snapshotWithTrigger` with `checkpoint.TriggerEviction`, labels `lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds{trigger="eviction"}`, selects `checkpoint.RetryBudgetFor(TriggerEviction)` (`{500ms, 5s, 30s}`) rather than the periodic budget, and passes the hook's per-session tier-cap budget through as the row's `checkpoint_timeout_at`; and assert `triggerForSource` no longer exists. The non-happy path is a gateway-drain checkpoint that silently takes the shorter periodic retry budget. `// spec: 4.4 (eviction retry budget), 10.1 (preStop stage 2 tier cap), 16.1 (trigger label). // diagnosis: a failure means the eviction trigger is still dead code (T-4.4.20).`
- **tier-1 the bookkeeping survives the deadline that cut the upload (spec-named-failure path, C5/C2):** With the per-session tier cap firing mid-upload, assert the driver still commits the `partial = true, manifest_reason = 'timeout'` finalisation with the committed `chunk_count`; that an attempt cut before its first chunk still soft-deletes its `chunk_count == 0` row; that an aborted attempt still soft-deletes every derived chunk key through the `ArtifactStore`; and that the tenant's `storage_bytes_used` returns to its pre-checkpoint value in all three cases. The non-happy path is a driver whose finalisation, sweep, and quota release all run on the cancelled context and return `context.DeadlineExceeded`, leaving an unfinalised row, undeleted chunks, and a reservation that only a Redis restart reconciles. `// spec: 10.1 (finalisation on deadline fire, CPS-010), 11.2 (release on failure), 4.4 (checkpoint abort cleanup).`
- **tier-1 a failed gateway-drain checkpoint loses nothing (spec-named-failure path, C5/C6):** With the object store unreachable for the whole eviction retry budget, assert the gateway-drain checkpoint fails, increments `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total{trigger="eviction"}` and no `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total`, writes no `session_eviction_state` row, publishes no `session.lost`, and leaves `classifyResume` at `ResumeFull` for a session that still has a workspace snapshot. The agent pod and its workspace survive a gateway drain, so a `workspace_lost = true` record on this path would discard an intact workspace. `// spec: 10.1 (coordinator handoff re-adopts the still-running pod), 4.4 (the minimal-state record represents a lost workspace).`
- **tier-1 the restore driver evaluates the §10.1 restore predicate and reports the mode it took (spec-named-failure path, C2/C4/C6):** Assert the restore driver reassembles and reports `partial_workspace` for the newest active `partial = true` row whose `chunk_count > 0` and whose `workspace_bytes_uploaded` is at or above `baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes * partialRecoveryThresholdFraction`; that a session whose first-ever checkpoint truncated (`baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes IS NULL`, `workspace_bytes_uploaded > 0`, `chunk_count > 0`) also reassembles and reports `partial_workspace`, with `workspaceRecoveryFraction` omitted from `session.resumed` per `spec/10:155`; and that each of a `partial = false` newest row, a `chunk_count == 0` row left by an in-flight or crash-abandoned attempt, a below-threshold partial, a partial outranked by a **strictly newer** `partial = false` row, and a contiguity failure reports `full` instead. Assert that in each of those `full` arms the session is restored with the bytes of its last successful full checkpoint rather than rebuilt empty through `startOnPod`: with a live `partial = false` checkpoint plus a strictly newer `chunk_count == 0` or below-threshold `partial = true` row, `SelectForResume` returns the full row, `resumeOnPod` takes the restore path, and the workspace file the full checkpoint carried is present on the resumed pod. The non-happy path is a non-restorable intent row routing the resume to the snapshotless rebuild and discarding a live full checkpoint, which the intent-row-first ordering makes the common case for every session resumed mid-checkpoint. Assert also that a partial finalised after a full checkpoint at the same `coordination_generation` reports `partial_workspace` rather than `full`, which is the case the shipped "at or above" comparison would have swallowed on every stable-coordinator session, and that the driver's reported `RecoveryFraction` is `restored_bytes / baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes` on that arm and nil when the baseline is NULL. `// spec: 10.1 (resume-row selection, strictly-newer full-wins fencing, reassembly threshold including the NULL-baseline degenerate arm, fall back to the last successful full checkpoint, CPS-010), 7.2 (session.resumed resumeMode and workspaceRecoveryFraction).` The eviction-row arm of the classifier is asserted separately, below.
- **tier-1 the resume classification survives both cleanups (spec-named-failure path, C2/C6):** Drive a resume that reassembles a `partial = true` row through the wired `handleResume` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:2661`), with the restore driver's own partial cleanup (`:2710`) and `PartialCleaner.CleanupAfterResume` (`:2786`) both running, and assert the emitted `session.resumed` carries `resumeMode: "partial_workspace"` and the `workspaceRecoveryFraction` the restore driver reported, even though the manifest row is soft-deleted by the time the classifier runs (`:2802`). Assert the fraction reaches `resumedEventPayload` through the widened `emitResumedEvent` (`start.go:3247`) rather than being recomputed, and that no code path re-reads the manifest table after `:2710` to decide the mode or the fraction. Assert the same resume emits `lenny_checkpoint_partial_total` with `recovered="true"` and the restored row's `manifest_reason` and `trigger`, which is the counter's second emission site (C2) and the only one that flips `recovered`; and that a resume falling through to a full checkpoint emits no `recovered="true"` increment. The non-happy path is a counter whose `recovered` label is declared, registered, and never written, which the tier-11 label assertion would not catch. The non-happy path is a `partial_workspace` event with the fraction omitted, which `spec/07:138` makes mandatory on that mode. The non-happy path is a classifier that re-queries the store after the restore has destroyed the row, which makes `ResumePartialWorkspace` unreachable and reports `full` for every partial recovery. `TestResumeEmitsPartialWorkspaceWhenPartialManifestPresent` and its `fakePartialLookup` fake (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resumed_event_test.go:52-145`) are rewritten into this assertion: the test constructs `sessionserver.Options{PartialManifestLookup: …}`, which C6 deletes, so it stops compiling, and it derives `resumeMode: "partial_workspace"` from the bare presence check this change replaces with the restore driver's `RestoreMode`. `TestResumeEmitsConversationOnlyWhenEvictionStatePresent` (`:92`) is rewritten against C7's precedence rule, which makes the eviction row decisive only when `SelectForResume` returns `ErrNotFound`. `// spec: 7.2 (session.resumed resumeMode), 10.1 (partial reassembly), 4.4 (partial-checkpoint cleanup, the eviction-record precedence rule).`
- **tier-1 the coordinator handoff carries the partial recovery fraction forward (spec-named-failure path, C6):** Resume a session `partial_workspace` with a recovery fraction of 0.4, then synthesize the coordinator-handoff reattach for the same session and assert its `session.resumed` frame carries `resumeMode: "coordinator_handoff"`, `workspaceLost: false`, and `workspaceRecoveryFraction: 0.4` read from the session's `session_checkpoint_meta` row, rather than the hard-coded `1.0` the shipped `handoffResumedFrame` publishes (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:3264-3269`). Assert a session that has never resumed `partial_workspace` still gets `1.0`, which is what `events_test.go:446` pins today, and that a later `full` restore writes NULL to the column so the next handoff reports `1.0` again rather than a fraction that no longer describes the workspace. Assert the write goes through the narrow `SetWorkspaceRecoveryFraction` writer and leaves the row's `barrier_id` and `checkpoint_ref` intact, which the shipped whole-row `Upsert` would clobber. The non-happy path is a client told its workspace is intact (`workspaceRecoveryFraction: 1.0`) on every handoff after a partial recovery, which is what the three retained statements (`spec/07:138`, `spec/10:392`, `spec/15:1421`) forbid and what no producer prevents today. `// spec: 7.2 (the fraction is carried forward verbatim across a coordinator handoff), 10.4 (the synthesized session.resumed sources the fraction from session_checkpoint_meta), 15.4 (the handoff frame set).`
- **tier-1 a truncated first checkpoint reaches the restore driver (spec-named-failure path, C2):** With a session whose only checkpoint is a `partial = true` row above the threshold and whose `sessions.workspace_snapshot_ref` is therefore empty, assert `resumeOnPod` takes the restore path with the selected `checkpoint_id` on `podsession.ResumeRequest` rather than the snapshotless `startOnPod` rebuild (`start.go:3084`), and that the restored workspace carries the reassembled bytes rather than an empty tree. The non-happy path is the shipped `WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref` gate sending every NULL-baseline partial recovery down the rebuild branch, which makes the §10.1 degenerate arm unreachable. `// spec: 10.1 (NULL baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes degenerate arm), 7.2 (resume on a replacement pod).`
- **tier-2 checkpoint_manifest store against Postgres (concurrent path, C4):** Against the containerized Postgres harness, assert `partial_manifest_active_uniq` rejects a second active `partial = true` row for the same `(tenant_id, session_id, slot_id)` and admits two `partial = false` rows for the same session (the latest-2 rotation), that the `CHECK (partial OR manifest_reason IS NULL)` constraint rejects a `partial = false` row carrying a reason, that a concurrent supersede-on-write racer observes the unique violation and retries, that `SelectForResume` returns the newest active row for `(tenant_id, session_id, slot_id)` in the total order `(coordination_generation DESC, checkpoint_started_at DESC, checkpoint_id DESC)` and prefers a `partial = false` row over the highest active partial only when it is strictly newer in that order, that with two retained `partial = false` rows at the same `coordination_generation` it deterministically returns the newer one, that a newest `partial = true` row failing the `chunk_count > 0`, `workspace_bytes_uploaded > 0`, or threshold gate makes it return the newest active `partial = false` row instead and `ErrNotFound` only when no full row exists either, that it uses the `(tenant_id, session_id, slot_id, coordination_generation DESC, checkpoint_started_at DESC)` index, and that RLS blocks a cross-tenant read. Assert also that `SelectLatestFullBefore` excludes the `checkpoint_id` it is handed and returns the next-newest active `partial = false` row in the same order, and `ErrNotFound` when the failed row is the session's only full row. `TestPartialManifestStoreContract` (`tests/tier2_component/stores/partialmanifeststore_test.go:31`), which pins the `(tenant_id, session_id, generation)` row key, the `deleted_at IS NULL`-only `LatestActive` predicate, and migration 0062's old table, is rewritten into this assertion. The store package's own unit suite, `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/partialmanifeststore/partialmanifeststore_test.go`, drives the same surfaces against `MemoryStore` and stops compiling on the re-key (the generation-keyed `Get` at `:31` and `TestLatestActiveReturnsHighestGeneration` at `:96`); it is rewritten onto the `(tenant_id, checkpoint_id)` key, `object_key_prefix`, and the three new selectors, and it carries the tier-1 half of every assertion above that does not need Postgres. `cmd/lenny-gateway/main_test.go`'s `TestHardPrunePartialManifests` (`:657`) seeds generation-keyed rows and reads them back by generation; it is rewritten onto the re-keyed `hardPrunePartialManifests` and asserts the reason-agnostic hard-prune predicate S2 stages at `spec/12:353` reaches a soft-deleted `partial = false` row as well as a `partial = true` one, which is the row class the unification introduces and the shipped `partial = true` predicate structurally excludes. `// spec: 10.1 (partial unique index, supersede-on-write, resume-row selection and full-wins fencing), 12.5 (tombstone hard-prune), 12.8 (RLS).`
- **tier-2 a stale-generation supersede cannot clobber a live attempt (concurrent path, S2/C2):** Against Postgres, with two gateway replicas driving a checkpoint for one session, assert that a supersede transaction whose `coordination_generation` is below the active row's aborts rather than committing, leaves the higher-generation row active and its chunks intact, and that the higher-generation attempt's finalising `UPDATE` still commits; that a superseded writer's next counter `UPDATE` affects zero rows and aborts the attempt before that index's `PutObject`; and that a chunk it committed before the supersede is deleted by its own abort sweep (which runs from the row the driver holds, whether or not that row is still active) and, when the writer crashes before sweeping, by the backstop through its widened soft-deleted-row arm, so no chunk survives under a soft-deleted row. The non-happy path is a stale coordinator on a draining replica soft-deleting the new coordinator's row and chunk-deleting its upload under it, after which the victim's finalising `UPDATE` no-ops silently. `// spec: 10.1 (supersede-on-write, coordination_generation fencing, false-positive barriers on handoff). // diagnosis: a failure means split-brain can lose a completed checkpoint silently.`
- **tier-2 a superseded writer records no phantom checkpoint (concurrent path, S3/C2):** The mirror image of the assertion above, in the direction the generation fence permits. Against Postgres, let a newer-generation coordinator supersede an in-flight upload after that upload's last chunk has committed, so the victim reaches its finalising `UPDATE` with no further counter `UPDATE` left to observe the supersede. Assert the victim's finalising `UPDATE` affects zero rows under its `checkpoint_id = $id AND deleted_at IS NULL` guard and that the driver treats that as an abort: no `partial = false` row is written, `sessions.workspace_snapshot_ref`, `last_successful_checkpoint_at`, and `last_checkpoint_workspace_bytes` are unchanged, no `session_checkpoints` retention row is inserted and no rotation runs, the reservation remainder is released, the abort sweep runs over the victim's derived key set, and the checkpoint the session resolves afterwards is the new coordinator's. The non-happy path is a phantom newest checkpoint whose chunk set the new coordinator already deleted, which every prefix reader resolves into absent objects and which the latest-2 rotation counts as one of the two retained checkpoints. `// spec: 10.1 (supersede-on-write, coordination_generation fencing, finalisation), 4.4 (last_successful_checkpoint_at is stamped on every successful checkpoint), 12.5 (latest-2 rotation).`
- **tier-1 the resume-time partial cleaner never touches a full checkpoint (spec-named-failure path, C4):** Assert `PartialCleaner.CleanupAfterResume` selects only through the `partial = TRUE AND deleted_at IS NULL` predicate, so a resume of a session whose newest manifest row is `partial = false` deletes no chunk and soft-deletes no row, and a second resume on a fresh pod still finds the bytes. The non-happy path is the already-wired cleaner (`cmd/lenny-gateway/sessionsrv.go:217`, invoked on every resume at `start.go:2786`) deleting the session's full checkpoint once its nil `Deleter` is filled. The cleaner's shipped unit suite, `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/partialcleanup_test.go`, is rewritten into this assertion: its `stubChunkDeleter` (`:15-22`) implements the `DeleteByPrefix` method C2 removes and its success case reads the deleted prefixes (`:71-72`), so the stub moves onto the per-key `SoftDelete` surface over the derived key set `[0, chunk_count)` and the assertions become per-key ones. `TestCleanupPartialManifestNilDeleterSkipsMinIO` (`:146`) is deleted rather than rewritten: the nil-deleter skip it pins is T-4.4.16, which this change closes, so a cleaner constructed with no delete surface is a wiring defect rather than a supported mode. `// spec: 4.4 (partial-checkpoint cleanup is scoped to the partial manifest), 12.5 (latest-2 retention).`
- **tier-2 §12.8 erasure against the new table (spec-named-failure path, C4):** Assert `DeleteByUser` and `DeleteByTenant` still remove every manifest row for the subject after the re-key from `(tenant, session, generation)` to `(tenant, checkpoint_id)`. The non-happy path is an erasure request that silently misses rows. `// spec: 12.8 (GDPR erasure).`
- **tier-3 Checkpoint and Resume stream contracts (boundary path, P1):** In `tests/tier3_contract`, assert `Checkpoint` returns a leading `CheckpointStart` carrying the probed byte count, then zero or more `archive_chunk` frames, then exactly one terminal `CheckpointSummary`; that a stream closed without a summary is treated as truncated (`partial = true`); that a stream whose first frame is not `CheckpointStart` is rejected; that `Resume` requires `ResumeStart` first; and that `ResumeStart.slot_id` round-trips, so a `Resume` against a `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pod validates the session against that slot and extracts against that slot's workspace root rather than the pod-global one. The non-happy path is a per-slot checkpoint that no restore path can consume, because the shipped `ResumeRequest` names no slot and the handler's `claimSession` returns `codes.Unavailable` for a second slot. `// spec: 4.7 (adapter contract table), 5.2 (checkpoints are per-slot), 6.2 (per-slot workspace roots), 11.2 (the probe feeds the reservation).`
- **tier-3 Checkpoint, CoordinatorFence, and CheckpointBarrier are per-slot on a concurrent pod (boundary path, P1/C1):** Assert `CheckpointRequest.slot_id` reaches the adapter, that a `Checkpoint` against a `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pod validates the session against that slot (`checkSlotSession`) rather than against the pod-global identity, and that it archives only that slot's `/workspace/slots/{slotId}/current` plus that slot's `/sessions` subtree, with a second slot's file absent from the stream. Assert the same routing on `CoordinatorFence` and `CheckpointBarrier`, and that two sessions on one pod at independent `coordination_generation` values are each fenced without either rejecting the other as stale. Assert the fence's **sender** carries the slot as well as its receiver: the slot the caller hands `coordfence.Fencer.Fence` reaches `CoordinatorFenceRequest.slot_id` through `adapterclient.Client.CoordinatorFence` unchanged, on the resume path (`fenceResumedPod`) and on the C7 handoff path. The non-happy path is a `slot_id` the receiver reads and no sender writes, under which `useSlot` (`pkg/adapter/slot.go:39-41`) takes the pod-global branch on every fence and the per-slot routing is unreachable. The non-happy path is the shipped pod-global gate (`pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:149`, `pkg/adapter/coordination.go:72`), which the code itself documents as unreachable on a concurrent pod (`pkg/adapter/session.go:234-236`), so every checkpoint and every fence on the class C7 exists for fails with `FailedPrecondition` and every slot falls to the minimal-state fallback. `// spec: 5.2 (checkpoints are per-slot), 6.2 (per-slot workspace roots), 10.1 (coordinator handoff and the generation fence).`
- **tier-3 a displaced coordinator's Checkpoint is fenced at the pod (spec-named-failure path, P1/C1):** Assert the adapter rejects a `Checkpoint` whose `coordination_generation` is below the slot's `lastFenced` with `FailedPrecondition: coordinator_handoff_stale`, exactly as `CheckpointBarrier` does (`pkg/adapter/coordination.go:143-159`), and accepts one whose slot has not been fenced yet; assert the same on `ResumeStart`. With a displaced coordinator's `Checkpoint` stream in flight against a terminating pod, assert that the `CoordinatorFence` carrying the higher generation **cancels that stream before it acknowledges**: the victim's handler returns `FailedPrecondition: coordinator_handoff_stale`, its `defer release()` frees the pod-global op-lock (`pkg/adapter/server.go:328`, `checkpoint.go:178-190`), and the fence's ack is observed by the gateway only afterwards, so the new coordinator's eviction `Checkpoint` for that slot is neither queued behind the victim in `opLock.Begin` (`pkg/adapter/oplock.go:57-73`) nor rejected with `codes.Aborted`. Assert the entry gate alone does not achieve this, by asserting the victim's stream is cut rather than merely left to expire at its own 60-second deadline (`pkg/checkpoint/checkpoint.go:160`). The non-happy path is a stale coordinator's upload holding the op-lock through the pod's grace period, which drops a slot with a healthy object store and a quiescable pod to the `workspace_lost = true` minimal-state fallback. `// spec: 10.1 (the coordinator fence is a precondition of every gateway→pod RPC; no window admits two coordinators), 4.7 (Checkpoint contract).`
- **tier-3 the gateway owns `chunk_encoding` (boundary path, P1/C1/C2):** Assert `CheckpointRequest.chunk_encoding` is non-empty and equals the `chunk_encoding` on the manifest row the gateway INSERTed before it opened the stream; that `CheckpointStart` carries no encoding field; that the bytes the adapter streams decode under the encoding the row records; and that an adapter which cannot produce the requested encoding fails the stream rather than substituting another. The non-happy path is a manifest row whose `chunk_encoding` suffix disagrees with the payload, which mis-drives the restore's decompress step and every derived chunk key. `// spec: 10.1 (a fixed chunk_encoding is chosen once at intent-row INSERT time; the suffix reflects the on-disk payload).`
- **tier-3 CheckpointBarrier is quiesce-and-acknowledge (boundary path, P1/C1):** Extend `tests/tier3_contract/adapter_checkpointbarrier/checkpointbarrier_wire_test.go`, which already pins the barrier wire contract, so it asserts the adapter returns `CheckpointBarrierAck(barrier_id, checkpoint_ref)` as soon as it has quiesced, that `checkpoint_ref` carries the gateway-minted `checkpoint_id` from the barrier request and never a pod-minted storage reference, and that the adapter writes no object and opens no checkpoint of its own. `// spec: 10.1 (CheckpointBarrier protocol), 4.7 (CheckpointBarrierAck fields).`
