- **tier-1 a Postgres outage during a drain still quiesces every pod (spec-named-failure path, S1/C2):** With Postgres unreachable during a gateway drain, assert every coordinated pod still receives a `CheckpointBarrier` (with an empty `checkpoint_id`) and quiesces, that `lenny_prestop_barrier_target_source_total{source="cache_fallback"}` increments once for the fan-out whose lease read failed, that the per-session skipped flush increments `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total{trigger="eviction"}` rather than the per-fan-out target-source counter, that no `checkpoint_manifest` row is written and no `Checkpoint` stream is opened, and that each session falls back to its last successful checkpoint. Assert separately that a degraded dispatch whose lease read **succeeded** (a failed intent-row INSERT, or a lock the reservation cannot take inside its slice of the deadline) leaves `lenny_prestop_barrier_target_source_total{source="cache_fallback"}` at zero and still counts the skipped flush, since stamping `cache_fallback` there would report a Postgres outage that did not happen. The non-happy path is an intent-row INSERT that is a hard precondition of `Send`, which would suppress the barrier for every session and leave tool calls dispatching into a replica seconds from SIGKILL. `// spec: 10.1 (degraded barrier set on a failed coordination-lease read, the barrier bounds the interruption window to one in-flight tool call). // diagnosis: a failure means a Postgres failover overlapping a drain silently removes step-2 quiescence.`
- **tier-2 the non-object-store failure reasons do not page on-call as a MinIO outage (spec-named-failure path, S1/C2):** Assert a degraded barrier dispatch (`reason="manifest_write_failed"`) and a zero-chunk fall-through (`reason="no_chunks_committed"`) each increment `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` and neither fires `CheckpointStorageUnavailable`, because the alert's expression excludes both reasons, and that `/v1/admin/health` therefore keeps its `objectStore` component `healthy` while MinIO's `HeadBucket` probe succeeds. Assert the mirror: a `reason="retry_exhausted"` or `reason="minio_unreachable"` increment does fire the alert and does flip the component to `unhealthy`. The non-happy path is a Postgres failover overlapping a gateway drain paging on-call with "Checkpoint upload to MinIO failed after all retries", routing them to the MinIO runbook, and marking the object store unhealthy while it is serving. `// spec: 16.5 (alert catalog), 25 (health components derive unhealthy from a firing critical alert).`
- **tier-1 a barrier flush commits exactly one manifest row (spec-named-failure path, C2):** Assert that a barrier flush mints one `checkpoint_id` and commits one `checkpoint_manifest` row, that the id on `CheckpointBarrierRequest` equals the id on the finalised row and the one echoed in the ack's `checkpoint_ref`, and that `lenny_checkpoint_partial_manifests_superseded_total` does not increment on a clean drain. The non-happy path is a driver that mints a second id and supersedes the row the ack correlates against, deleting its chunks under it. `// spec: 10.1 (intent-row-first ordering, partial unique index, supersede-on-write).`
- **tier-1 the supersede counter counts the rows the supersede actually retires (boundary path, S1/C2):** The counter's negative assertion above passes vacuously against the shipped code, because `IncCheckpointPartialManifestsSuperseded` (`pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics.go:408-412`) has no caller in the tree and this change gives it its first one, so the positive direction is asserted here. On the single-flight racer (above) and on the cross-replica supersede (below), assert a supersede of an active `partial = true` row increments `lenny_checkpoint_partial_manifests_superseded_total{pool}` exactly once per row soft-deleted, which is the `spec/16:196` increment condition, and that each of the predicate's two abort arms increments it zero times: an active row at a higher `coordination_generation`, and a same-generation row carrying no `manifest_reason` and still inside its `checkpoint_timeout_at`. The non-happy path is a counter that is registered, declared, and never written, which the tier-11 registered-and-emitted gate would pass on a single emitter anywhere and which neither the increment condition nor the abort arms would pin. `// spec: 16.1 (partial manifests superseded), 10.1 (supersede-on-write).`
- **tier-3 lifecycle `checkpoint_complete` is an archive-capture result (boundary path, S1/C1):** Assert the adapter emits `checkpoint_complete` with `status: "ok"` when its archive stream closes cleanly and `status: "failed"` when the archive fails, independently of what the gateway's upload does afterwards, and that the emitted event validates against `schemas/lifecycle-events.schema.json`. `// spec: 4.7 (lifecycle channel events).`
- **tier-7 gateway-driven barrier flush across pods (spec-named-failure path, C1/C2):** With several coordinated sessions on one replica, drive the preStop barrier fan-out and assert all flushes run under the single `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` wall-clock deadline; that a session still uploading when the deadline fires finalises `partial = true` with `manifest_reason = 'timeout'` and `lenny_checkpoint_partial_total{trigger="barrier_ack_timeout"}`; that a session whose pod never acked keeps a `chunk_count == 0` row that is soft-deleted with a fall-through to the last periodic checkpoint; that `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_total{outcome="timeout"}` increments for the pod that never acked; and that a pod which acked inside the deadline but whose gateway-driven flush the deadline then cut increments `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_total{outcome="success"}` exactly once and no `partial_captured`, with the partial finalisation counted on `lenny_checkpoint_partial_total{trigger="barrier_ack_timeout"}` instead. The non-happy path is one pod counted under both `success` and `partial_captured`, which corrupts the barrier success rate the counter exists to compute. Assert the companion histogram on that same discriminating pod, because C2 gives `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_duration_seconds` its first emitter and S1 simultaneously redefines the interval it measures (`spec/16:42` today defines it over checkpoint flushing): assert the histogram observes the send-to-ack interval alone, so the pod that acked quickly records a value far below the duration of the flush the deadline then cut, while `lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds{trigger="eviction"}` carries that flush, and assert one observation per barrier target including the `outcome="timeout"` pod. The non-happy path is an emitter that observes at flush completion, which compiles, satisfies the tier-11 registered-and-emitted check, and silently reinstates the retired flush-inclusive semantics, falsifying both the staged `spec/16:42` text and the `PDBBlockedEvictions` clause (b) diagnosis (`spec/16:456`), which after the decoupling reads the two histograms as disjoint intervals. Assert the concurrent-pod arm separately, because a barrier `Target` is one session and several targets share one pod: on a `maxConcurrentSessions: 8` pool whose eight sessions all sit on one pod, drive the fan-out and assert all eight barriers are sent and acked concurrently, that the gateway drives the eight flushes **one at a time in slot-ID order** against that pod, that all eight finalise with their chunk sets committed, and that no slot returns `codes.Aborted` from the adapter's pod-global `opLock.Begin` (`pkg/adapter/oplock.go:37-40`, `pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:178-186`) and no slot finalises `chunk_count == 0` with `reason="no_chunks_committed"`. Assert two pods still flush in parallel with each other. The non-happy path is an unbounded per-session fan-out opening eight concurrent `Checkpoint` streams into one adapter, which the pod-global operation lock admits two of and aborts the rest, losing six of the eight drain-time checkpoints the flush exists to take. `// spec: 10.1 (checkpoint flush, BarrierAck-timeout partial-capture path CPS-007, barrier-ack outcome set), 5.2 (eviction checkpoints are serialized across the slots of one pod, in slot-ID order), 4.7 (the adapter's pod-global operation lock), 16.1 (barrier ack outcomes; the barrier-ack duration histogram measures pod quiescence and excludes the flush), 16.5 (PDBBlockedEvictions clause (b)).`
- **tier-2 rotation deletes the chunk set of the checkpoint it rotates out (concurrent path, C2):** Rotation issues no object-store call today (`checkpointretention` imports no blobstore, and `checkpointer.go:331` discards the records `Rotate` returns), so assert the new path: rotating a session past the latest-2 cap makes `recordRetention` consume the rotated-out records, delete every derived `chunk-{n}` key in `[0, chunk_count)` under that checkpoint's `object_key_prefix`, soft-delete each chunk's `artifact_store` row exactly once under the `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` guard, decrement the Redis quota counter by the summed `artifact_size_bytes` exactly once, and soft-delete the checkpoint's `checkpoint_manifest` row under the same guard so the row does not outlive its chunks; and that a legal-hold session rotates nothing and deletes nothing. The non-happy path is a rotated-out checkpoint whose chunks stay in MinIO and on the tenant's quota forever, or whose `partial = false` row is never retired. `// spec: 12.5 (latest-2 rotation, GC concurrency rules 2 and 5, legal hold).`
- **tier-2 the terminal-session sweep retires the manifest rows with the chunks (spec-named-failure path, C2):** Against Postgres with the production `cataloging` store, run the §12.5 retention GC over a terminated session past its resume window and assert that `SoftDeleteSession` tombstones every chunk object and its `artifact_store` row, that the companion `checkpoint_manifest` deleter soft-deletes every one of the session's manifest rows in the same pass so no live row survives naming tombstoned chunks, that the tombstone hard-prune then removes both the chunk rows and the manifest rows after `gc.tombstoneRetentionSeconds` regardless of the rows' `partial` value, and that a prefix reader resolving a collected session finds no manifest row rather than a live row naming absent objects. The non-happy path is the object-type-blind `SoftDeleteSession` (`cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go:665-670`) deleting the chunks while a live `partial = false` row keeps naming them, which the hard-prune's `deleted_at IS NOT NULL` predicate never reaches. `// spec: 12.5 (terminal-session checkpoint deletion, tombstone hard-prune).`
- **tier-2 the backstop reaches a chunk under a soft-deleted row (spec-named-failure path, S2/C4):** Against Postgres, commit a chunk under a manifest row that a generation-fenced supersede has already soft-deleted, **leaving the session live and non-terminal**, which is the only state a supersede is produced in, then advance past the row's `created_at + maxResumeWindowSeconds` and run a backstop cycle. Assert the row is selected through the widened `deleted_at IS NULL OR deleted_at > now() - gc.tombstoneRetentionSeconds` arm and through §12.5 entry condition (b), evaluated on the row's own `created_at` rather than on the session's state, so the sweep reaches it without the session ever becoming terminal. Assert that a sweep implemented as a session-record join for both entry conditions would leave the row and its chunk unreachable until the hard-prune destroyed the only name the chunk has. Assert further that the chunk is soft-deleted through the `ArtifactStore` with its `artifact_store` row transitioned and its bytes released, and that the pass leaves the already-soft-deleted row's `deleted_at` unchanged (the terminating `UPDATE` affects zero rows and the pass is chunk-delete-only). Assert a row whose `deleted_at` predates the tombstone-retention window is not selected, so the widened arm terminates. The non-happy path is a chunk named only by a row no sweep predicate selects, whose bytes stay on the tenant's `storage_bytes_used` after the hard-prune removes the row. `// spec: 12.5 (GC backstop sweep, GC concurrency rule 6, tombstone hard-prune), 10.1 (crash semantics).`
- **tier-2 the backstop does not sweep a live seal or periodic checkpoint (spec-named-failure path, S2/C2/C4):** Against Postgres, run a backstop cycle against a terminal session whose `Seal` checkpoint is mid-upload (intent row committed, two chunks committed, `checkpoint_timeout_at` in the future) and assert the cycle deletes no chunk, soft-deletes no row, and that the seal's finalising `UPDATE` still commits `partial = false`; then advance past `checkpoint_timeout_at + gc.cycleIntervalSeconds` on an abandoned row and assert the same cycle does sweep it. Run the same pair for a periodic checkpoint in flight when its session terminates. The non-happy path is a 15-minute GC cycle landing inside the seal window and destroying the artifact `spec/07:212` designates as the terminal session's `final_workspace_ref`. `// spec: 12.5 (GC backstop entry conditions), 7.1 (seal-and-export), 10.1 (finalisation).`
- **tier-1 a chunk `Put` against an unreachable object store is cut at the deadline (spec-named-failure path, C2/C5):** Assert that with the object store unreachable, the first chunk `Put` is cut at the tier cap rather than blocking for `miniostore`'s uncancellable 36-second ladder (`miniostore.go:44`, `:259`), that the retry ladder the driver runs is `checkpoint.RetryBudgetFor(trigger)` rather than `putRetryBackoffs`, and that the finalisation, the abort sweep, and the quota release all complete inside the remaining grace period. Assert the same over **every** backend `blobproviderflags.Resolve` can return, not `miniostore` alone: table-drive the deadline case across `miniostore`, `s3`, `gcs`, `azureblob`, `FilesystemStore`, and `MemoryStore`, each wrapped in `cataloging` and `TenantScoped` as production wires them (`cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1035`, `:1085-1090`), and assert the caller's context cuts the in-flight `Put` on each. Assert the fail-closed rule: a resolved store that does not implement the context-carrying surface is rejected at gateway startup rather than falling back to the context-less `Store.Put`, whose S3 implementation opens on `context.Background()` (`pkg/blobstore/s3/s3.go:222-223`) and cannot be cut at all. The non-happy path is a drain on an S3-backed gateway whose first chunk overruns both ceilings, is SIGKILLed, and leaves an unfinalised row, undeleted chunks, and a leaked reservation, which the MinIO-only test would never see. `// spec: 10.1 (Stage 2 tier cap), 4.4 (eviction retry budget), 11.2 (release on failure), 17.9 (cloud-managed object-storage backends).`
- **tier-1 a truncated periodic checkpoint is countable and changes no session state (spec-named-failure path, S3/S4/C2):** Assert that a periodic checkpoint whose archive stream reaches EOF without a terminal `CheckpointSummary` finalises `partial = true` with `manifest_reason = 'timeout'` and increments `lenny_checkpoint_partial_total` with the legal `trigger = "periodic"` label; and that it leaves `last_successful_checkpoint_at`, `WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref`, `last_checkpoint_workspace_bytes`, and the `session_checkpoints` retention catalog unchanged. The non-happy path is a truncated archive recorded as the session's last successful checkpoint, which then becomes the fallback target, the frozen `baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes` denominator, and the Stage 2 cap-tier input. `// spec: 10.1 (finalisation), 4.4 (last_successful_checkpoint_at is stamped on every successful checkpoint), 16.1 (partial-manifest trigger label).`
- **tier-4 `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/workspace` reassembles the chunk set (boundary path, P1/C2):** Assert the endpoint streams a byte-identical `tar.gz` reassembled from a multi-chunk prefix, and returns the documented error rather than a truncated body when an index in `[0, chunk_count)` is absent. `// spec: 15.1 (workspace snapshot download), 10.1 (chunk derivation).`
- **tier-4 derive and replay read a prefix (spec-named-failure path, P1/C2):** Assert `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/derive` copies every chunk of the parent's derived key set, inserts the derived session's `partial = false` `checkpoint_manifest` row in the same transaction, and still surfaces `DERIVE_SNAPSHOT_UNAVAILABLE` when a parent chunk is missing; assert `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/workspace` on the derived session reassembles its own chunk set into a byte-identical archive, which is what the derived manifest row exists to make possible; assert a failed copy leaves neither a partially written chunk nor a manifest row for the derived session; and assert the replay snapshot reader resolves a prefix ref through its manifest row. The non-happy path is a derived session holding a prefix with no manifest row, which no reader can enumerate. `// spec: 7.1 (session derivation, derive copy semantics, partial-copy cleanup), 10.1 (chunk derivation), 15.1 (workspace snapshot download).`
- **tier-4 produce → store → restore round trip (spec-named-failure path, C1/C2/C4):** On the compose stack, checkpoint a session with a populated workspace and a `/sessions` tree, then resume it onto a fresh pod and assert the restored tree is byte-identical, `resumeMode: "full"`, and `chunk_count > 1` for a workspace larger than one chunk. Separately, truncate the archive stream at the deadline and assert the resume yields `partial_workspace` with a `workspaceRecoveryFraction` computed against the frozen `baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes`. `tests/tier4_integration/checkpoint_minio_atomicity_test.go` is rewritten here. It is a live tier-4 test that assigns the deleted `Server.Checkpoints` and `Server.Restorer` fields (`:187`, `:264`, `:324`), so it stops compiling after C1, and both of its assertions are inverted by this change: `TestCheckpointWritesMetadataOnlyAfterMinIOUpload` (`:153`) pins the metadata record following the MinIO upload, while the intent row is now the first durable act, and `TestCheckpointDiscardedWhenMinIOUploadFails` (`:296`) pins a failed upload leaving no record, while a truncated attempt now deliberately retains its committed chunks under a `partial = true` row. Its `minioCheckpointBridge` and both functions are rewritten onto the gateway-driven model (intent-row-first ordering, and a failed upload leaving a `partial = true` row with its committed chunks), and their two `tests/spec-map.json:230-231` entries are updated with them. `// spec: 4.4 (checkpoint atomicity), 10.1 (reassembly on resume, intent-row-first ordering), 7.2 (session.resumed).`
- **tier-4 seal succeeds and the pod recycles (spec-named-failure path, C1/C2):** Assert that on a single-session recycling pool, `sealWorkspace` completes on the first attempt rather than timing out at 300s, the session does not transition to `failed`/`workspace_seal_timeout`, and `binder.go:1829` reaches the recycle branch. This is the §6.2 occupancy-zero recycling path that the `Unimplemented` checkpoint made unreachable. `// spec: 7.2 (seal-and-export invariant), 6.2 (occupancy-zero recycling).`
- **tier-1 a truncated seal is a failed export (spec-named-failure path, S3/C2):** The chunked driver makes `partial = true` a third outcome on the seal path beside success and error, so pin what `sealWorkspace` does with it. With the seal's archive stream cut at the driver's deadline so the row finalises `partial = true` with `manifest_reason = 'timeout'`, assert `Checkpointer.Seal` returns a non-nil error to `sealWorkspace` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:91-95`), that the §7.1 bounded-backoff loop retries inside `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds`, and that on window exhaustion the session transitions to `failed` with reason `workspace_seal_timeout`, emits the `workspaceSealFailed` audit event, and observes `lenny_workspace_seal_duration_seconds{outcome="timeout"}` (`seal.go:98-108`). Assert the truncated seal stamps no `WorkspaceSnapshot`, no `last_successful_checkpoint_at`, and no retention row, and that its committed chunks and its `partial = true` row survive for the resume path. The non-happy path is a truncated capture observed as `sealOutcomeSuccess`, which releases the pod and leaves the session's previous checkpoint standing as the terminal session's exported workspace, which is the loss `spec/07:112` exists to prevent. `// spec: 7.1 (seal-and-export invariant, workspace_seal_timeout), 10.1 (finalisation), 4.4 (a partial manifest is not a valid checkpoint).`
- **tier-5 gateway drain checkpoints its coordinated sessions (spec-named-failure path, C5):** On kind, drain the gateway replica coordinating a live session and assert its preStop hook completes an eviction-trigger checkpoint for that session (`lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds{trigger="eviction"}` observed, the eviction retry budget selected), that the session is re-adopted by the replacement coordinator with `resumeMode: "coordinator_handoff"` and `workspaceLost: false` on its still-running pod (`spec/10:392`), and that the checkpoint the drain took is the artifact a subsequent pod-loss resume restores from. The non-happy path is a drain that leaves no fresh checkpoint because the checkpoint took the periodic budget or never ran. `// spec: 4.6 (disruption protection), 10.1 (preStop stage 2, coordinator handoff), 4.4 (eviction checkpoint).`
- **tier-5 node drain checkpoints a bound agent pod and the session resumes with its workspace (spec-named-failure path, S3/P1/C7):** On kind with `gateway.replicas: 2`, `kubectl drain` the node carrying a bound agent pod whose session has written a file into its workspace, and assert the pod's preStop hook signals the gateway (`GatewayControl.NotifyPodTerminating` observed), the gateway drives an eviction-trigger checkpoint against that pod's adapter before the pod exits (`lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds{trigger="eviction"}` observed, `checkpoint.RetryBudgetFor(TriggerEviction)` selected), the checkpoint's chunk set is committed to MinIO, and `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/resume` brings the session up on a fresh pod with `resumeMode: "full"`, `workspaceLost: false`, and the file intact. The two replicas are pinned so the journey exercises the notification landing on either replica rather than only on the coordinator. This lifts the `t.Skip` at `tests/tier5_e2e_kind/checkpoint_resume_test.go:74` (`TestCheckpointThenResumeRestoresWorkspace`), whose stated precondition is exactly this trigger, and it needs the `sessiondriver.Resume` helper the skip also names. The non-happy path is a drain that SIGKILLs the pod before the checkpoint commits, which is the loss the whole trigger exists to prevent. `// spec: 4.6.1 (disruption protection for agent pods), 4.4 (eviction checkpoint), 7.2 (resume on a replacement pod).`
- **tier-5 the MinIO-outage variant degrades to the Postgres minimal state (fail-closed path, C7):** On kind, with MinIO unreachable for the duration of the eviction retry budget, drain the node carrying a bound agent pod and assert the gateway exhausts `RetryBudgetFor(TriggerEviction)`, increments `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total{trigger="eviction"}`, writes the `session_eviction_state` minimal-state row, increments `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total`, and that the session resumes on a fresh pod with `resumeMode: "conversation_only"` and `workspaceLost: true`. Assert the Postgres write completes inside the pod's remaining grace period rather than being cancelled by the tier cap that cut the upload, which is the detached-context requirement C7 builds. This lifts the `t.Skip` at `tests/tier5_e2e_kind/checkpoint_resume_test.go:95` (`TestCheckpointResumeMinIOOutageFallsBackToPostgresMinimalState`) and needs the MinIO-outage injector that skip also names. The non-happy path is a fallback write issued on the cancelled tier-cap context, which returns `context.DeadlineExceeded` and loses the session outright. `// spec: 4.4 (eviction fallback: Postgres minimal state record, Postgres fallback retry during failover), 12.4 (MinIO unavailability during checkpoint).`
- **tier-5 the checkpoint fits inside terminationGracePeriodSeconds (boundary path, S3/C7):** On kind, with a workspace sized into the 90s tier cap, drain the node and assert the pod is not SIGKILLed mid-upload: the checkpoint's terminal `CheckpointSummary` is received and the manifest row finalises `partial = false` before the kubelet's grace deadline, the pod's `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` satisfies the S3 floor rather than the old 120s default, and both containers' preStop hooks return only after the adapter exits. Assert the same for a `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pool, whose `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` the webhook forced the deployer to raise to at least the floor (an unset field on such a pool is rejected at admission, so no pod runs the serialized per-slot checkpoints under the 180s default), and for a session with no prior checkpoint on a `workspaceSizeLimitBytes: 100Mi` pool, whose first checkpoint runs under the 90s tier the gateway must select rather than the pool's 30s tier cap. The non-happy path is a grace period sized against the old formula or against the pool's own tier cap, under which the 90s tier plus the 60s Postgres fallback overruns the grace period and the kubelet kills the pod with an unfinalised row and undeleted chunks. `// spec: 4.6.1 (terminationGracePeriodSeconds budget), 5.2 (per-slot checkpoint budget, serialized eviction checkpoints), 10.1 (Stage 2 tier cap, the 90s tier for a session with no prior checkpoint).`
- **tier-1 the podspec renders the termination surface the trigger depends on (boundary path, C7):** In `pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec`, assert `Build` returns `terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 180` by default and the `--timeout=170s` that follows from it on the `adapter` and embedded hooks; that the sidecar `runtime` container carries a preStop `exec` hook whose command is the staged `/lenny/drain/lenny-adapter prestop-wait`, resolvable in a stock third-party runtime image because the binary is mounted rather than assumed (the hook must not be `preStopDrainHook`, whose `lenny-adapter prestop` command is absent from `Runtime.spec.image` and whose `runPreStop` would SIGTERM that container's own PID 1, which is the runtime process); that the sidecar pod carries the `drain-bin` `emptyDir` and the initContainer that stages the binary into it, mounted read-only on the `runtime` container; that the embedded pod carries neither, since there the adapter is PID 1; that `shareProcessNamespace` stays unset on both layouts; and that the sidecar `adapter` container and the embedded container both carry `LENNY_TERMINATION_GRACE_SECONDS` equal to the rendered grace period, since that env var is the only source of the `budget_ms` ceiling on the whole eviction fan-out. Six shipped tests pin the 120s default or the `--timeout=110s` that follows from it and are rewritten here: `TestBuildDefaultsTerminationGraceTo120_spec_4_6_1` (`podspec_test.go:440`), `TestBuildIgnoresCeilingAboveDefault_spec_4_6_1` (`:469`), `TestBuildAddsPreStopDrainHook_spec_4_6_1` (`:487`, which pins `--timeout=110s` and whose case table covers only the `adapter` and embedded containers), the `"zero base ignored"` case of `TestBuildAppliesTerminationGraceBaseOverride_spec_5_2` (`:540`), the `"preconnect default grace unaffected"` case of `TestBuildPreConnectFloorsTerminationGrace_spec_6_1_67` (`:572`), and `TestReconcileCreatesPodForNewSandbox` (`pkg/controller/sandbox/controller_test.go:347`), which asserts the rendered pod's `terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 120` and is the only site outside `podspec/` the default reaches. The non-happy path is a runtime container whose hook cannot run, or whose hook kills the process it exists to hold open. `// spec: 4.6.1 (preStop hook on every container), 13.1 (no shared PID namespace).`
- **tier-3 a Full-level sidecar runtime survives to quiesce (spec-named-failure path, C7):** With the sidecar `runtime` container's wait-only hook in place, assert a Full-level runtime is still running when the gateway opens the `Checkpoint` stream during pod termination, completes the `checkpoint_request` / `checkpoint_ready` handshake, and lets the eviction checkpoint finalise `partial = false`; and assert the hook returns only after the adapter's listener stops accepting, having sent the runtime no signal. The non-happy path is a runtime SIGTERMed at t=0, whose quiesce fails with `codes.Internal` (`pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:409-411`) and drops every Full-level sidecar session to the minimal-state fallback on the one path the trigger exists for. `// spec: 4.6.1 (preStop hook on every container), 4.7 (cooperative checkpoint quiesce).`
- **tier-1 the eviction-state row does not outrank a restorable checkpoint (spec-named-failure path, C7):** Assert `classifyResume` returns the restore driver's own mode, and never `ResumeConversationOnly`, for a session that has a `session_eviction_state` row and a restorable checkpoint, in each of three states the shipped and the once-staged generation-keyed predicates would both have failed: a full checkpoint at the record's `recovery_generation`; a full checkpoint at a **lower** `recovery_generation` than the record's, which is the routine checkpoint-at-N / resume-to-N+1 / eviction-at-N+1 sequence; and a full checkpoint plus a strictly newer restorable `partial = true` row, where the resume reassembles the partial and reports `partial_workspace`. Assert `ResumeConversationOnly` is returned only when the resume restored nothing, which is `SelectForResume` returning `ErrNotFound` or the terminal absent-chunks arm, and that on the second of those it outranks the `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` failure. The non-happy path is a session holding a durable workspace that resumes `conversation_only` with `workspaceLost: true`. `// spec: 4.4 ("If a previous successful full checkpoint exists, the session is resumed from that checkpoint"; "resumable from an earlier snapshot"), 7.2 (session.resumed resumeMode).`
- **tier-2 a later successful checkpoint supersedes the eviction-state row and takes its context object with it (spec-named-failure path, C7):** Against Postgres with the production `cataloging` store, write a `session_eviction_state` row for a session, then run a checkpoint that finalises `partial = false` and assert the driver deletes the row in its post-finalisation step, so the next resume is **not** `conversation_only`. Run the same case for a fallback row whose `last_message_context` exceeded 2 KB and therefore lives as the MinIO object `/{tenant_id}/eviction/{session_id}/context` with its own `artifact_store` row: assert the driver soft-deletes the object through the tenant-scoped `ArtifactStore` **before** it deletes the Postgres row, that the object's `artifact_store` row is tombstoned, and that the tenant's `storage_bytes_used` returns to its pre-fallback value. Assert that a failed object delete or a failed row delete leaves both in place without changing the resume outcome, because the precedence rule already makes the newer full checkpoint win. The non-happy path is a row deleted while its context object survives, which destroys the only in-band pointer to that key and leaves its bytes on the tenant's counter until the terminal `SoftDeleteSession` sweep, and its mirror, one fallback write making every later resume of that session `conversation_only` with `workspaceLost: true` forever. `// spec: 4.4 (the minimal state record is a fallback, not a permanent session property; the context object is deleted before the Postgres row), 12.5 (session_eviction_state cleanup).`
- **tier-3 NotifyPodTerminating is a blocking checkpoint barrier (boundary path, P1/C7):** In `tests/tier3_contract`, assert the adapter sends `NotifyPodTerminating` on SIGTERM **before** any part of its teardown, that its gRPC server answers a `Checkpoint` stream opened by the gateway while the notification is in flight, that the intra-pod lifecycle channel is still open for that stream so a Full-level runtime completes the `checkpoint_request` / `checkpoint_ready` handshake, that the adapter runs none of `ShutdownDemoteSDK`, `lifecycle.Close()`, or `GracefulStop` (`cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:396-401`) until the RPC returns or its own deadline fires, and that a gateway which is unreachable does not block the drain past that deadline. Assert the adapter subtracts `LENNY_DEMOTE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS + 5s` and `preStopDrainMarginSeconds` from the grace period **when it computes `budget_ms`**, and that it applies that same value as its own RPC deadline, so the ceiling the gateway is handed is the wall clock the adapter actually honours and a pod in `sdk_connecting` whose gateway is unreachable or hangs still completes `DemoteSDK` and exits before the kubelet SIGKILL, which is the §6.1 sequence and its grace-sizing rule. The non-happy path is a `budget_ms` that declares a ceiling the adapter abandons the call before reaching, which cuts a gateway that planned its serialized slot loop against the declared value. Assert the request carries the pod name and a `budget_ms` the gateway treats as a ceiling, and that the gateway resolves the bound sessions from `sessions.pod_assignment` rather than from the request. Assert the RPC is answered by the **composed** gateway `GatewayControl` server rather than by a handler type constructed in the test: dial the server `newGatewayControlServer` builds (`cmd/lenny-gateway/main.go:582`, registered at `:700`) and assert `NotifyPodTerminating` returns a real response rather than `codes.Unimplemented`. The non-happy path is a handler wired outside the one registered `GatewayControlServer` (`leasecontrol.Service`, which embeds `UnimplementedGatewayControlServer`), which compiles, passes every direct-construction unit test, and answers `Unimplemented` in production, so the trigger never fires. The non-happy path is an adapter that closes its lifecycle channel before the gateway can quiesce the runtime, which fails the checkpoint with `codes.Internal` on exactly the path it exists for, and its mirror, an adapter that spends the whole grace period waiting on a hung gateway and abandons the SDK mid-connection. `// spec: 4.6.1 (preStop signal), 6.1 (SIGTERM during sdk_connecting), 4.7 (GatewayControl contract, checkpoint quiesce handshake).`
- **tier-4 the notification checkpoints every slot on a replica that does not coordinate the pod (concurrent path, C7):** With two gateway replicas and a session whose coordination lease is **held and actively renewed** by replica B, deliver `NotifyPodTerminating` to replica A and assert the eviction-trigger checkpoint is taken for every slot bound to the pod: replica A resolves the sessions from `sessions.pod_assignment`, dials that pod's adapter through `podsession.Binder.AdoptForCheckpoint` (assert the connection is built from the Sandbox CR's `status.podIP`, since the `pod_assignment` column carries a Sandbox name and not an address, and that a pod A can neither resolve nor dial is a failed slot that takes the minimal-state fallback rather than a silent skip), takes the live lease through `leasestore.Steal` (asserting first that `Acquire` returns `ErrHeld` against the same lease, which is why the primitive exists), runs the rest of the §10.1 handoff (the `coordination_generation` CAS and the reservation of that slot's checkpoint in the same transaction, then the `CoordinatorFence` over that connection), and drives the `Checkpoint` stream under the new generation and the reserved `checkpoint_id`, which the pod accepts. Assert replica B stands down: its next sweep's `Acquire` for that session returns `ErrHeld` (which is the call the coordination sweeper renews with, `coordination.go:10-14`, `:132-137`; `leasestore.Renew` has no coordination-path caller), and it stops renewing and stops mirroring the session into `coordination_lease`. Assert the `ErrHeld` arm drops **no** `podsession.Registry` binding, in both of the states it fires in: a replica that takes `ErrHeld` for a session it never coordinated (the ordinary multi-replica case, since the sweeper races to `Acquire` every non-terminal session while the binding is written by whichever replica served start or resume) keeps its binding, so its `Checkpointer.Sweep` keeps checkpointing that session and `PodExecutor.Release` still drains its pod and releases its slot at teardown; and the replica displaced by the `Steal` keeps its binding until it retires it itself. Assert the window the reservation-inside-the-handoff closes, which is the one the generation number cannot: with replica A's handoff complete for every session of the pod and A part-way through its serialized slot loop, fire replica B's periodic ticker for a slot A has not reached yet, and assert B's attempt reads the **post-CAS** `sessions.coordination_generation` (so the pod's entry gate would have admitted its `Checkpoint`, since the gate rejects only a generation below the slot's `lastFenced`), meets A's reserved intent row for that slot at equal generation with no `manifest_reason` and inside its `checkpoint_timeout_at`, and aborts at the reservation: B commits no row, opens no `Checkpoint` RPC, takes no share of the adapter's pod-global op-lock, and A's eviction checkpoint for that slot finalises `partial = false`. Assert the reserved row's `checkpoint_timeout_at` is the pod-declared `budget_ms` deadline rather than the slot's tier cap, so the last slot of a `maxConcurrentSessions: 8` pod is not readable as abandoned by B's supersede or by a §12.5 backstop cycle while A is still working through the earlier slots. The non-happy path is a handler that reserves at execution time: B's tick then takes the active row, A aborts on the settled-row term, and the slot writes `session_eviction_state` with `workspace_lost = true` against a healthy object store and a live, quiescable pod, which is the loss the trigger exists to prevent. Assert what replica A does with the bindings it holds: when `NotifyPodTerminating` returns, A holds no `podsession.Registry` binding for any session of the terminating pod, including bindings that were already in its registry before the RPC arrived, and has closed every adapter connection it dialed, so A's `Checkpointer.Sweep` drives no further checkpoint and reserves nothing against a pod that no longer exists. Assert the bindings on **every other** replica are retired too, which is the state no handler can reach: after the pod is gone and the session has resumed onto a replacement pod, assert replica B's next `Checkpointer.Sweep` tick reserves nothing for that session, because the reservation reads `sessions.pod_assignment`, finds it naming a Sandbox other than the one on B's binding (`podsession.BindResult.SandboxName`), drops the binding through `Registry.Remove`, and returns `ErrNoBinding` before it mints, supersedes, or INSERTs; and that a tick fired in the window **before** the resume, where `pod_assignment` still names the destroyed Sandbox, drops the binding on the `Checkpoint` RPC's dial failure after soft-deleting its own zero-chunk row, so at most one aborted attempt per stale binding is produced. The non-happy path is the shipped registry, whose only `Remove` caller is `PodExecutor.Release` (`pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:313`) on the session's teardown: the binding then names a destroyed pod for the session's whole remaining life, and every tick commits an intent row the supersede predicate refuses to displace, aborting the session's genuine checkpoint on its replacement pod forever. Drive a genuinely in-flight cross-replica upload through the handoff: with replica B mid-chunk on an unfinalised row (no `manifest_reason`, `checkpoint_timeout_at` in the future), assert replica A's strictly-higher-generation supersede is **admitted** under the `coordination_generation < $new_generation` arm, that replica B's in-flight `Checkpoint` stream is cancelled at the pod by replica A's `CoordinatorFence` before that fence acknowledges (`FailedPrecondition: coordinator_handoff_stale`, the `spec/10:36` rule C1 builds) so it releases the adapter's op-lock and replica A's eviction `Checkpoint` is neither queued behind it nor `Aborted`, that replica B's next zero-row counter or finalising `UPDATE` aborts its attempt and sweeps its own chunks rather than leaving them under a soft-deleted row, and that an **equal**-generation attempt against the same row aborts on the guard instead. Assert the generation the superseding row carries is the post-CAS `sessions.coordination_generation` and is strictly greater than the victim row's, rather than the `coordination_lease` mirror value, which the sweeper writes from its pre-bump read (`coordination.go:150`, `:169-171`) and which would make the two rows equal and the supersede abort. Assert the takeover through the value production actually wires: the handler holds a `*leasestore.Failover` (`cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1272`), so drive the same handoff with Redis unavailable and assert `Steal` routes to the `pgstore` advisory-lock arm, displaces the holder, and lets the checkpoint proceed, rather than dropping every slot to the minimal-state fallback during a Redis outage (`spec/12:217`). Assert the bind gate and the `Steal` gate are evaluated separately, since the binding and the lease are held by uncorrelated replicas: deliver `NotifyPodTerminating` to the replica that **holds the lease and no registry binding** (the sweeper won the lease race while another replica served the session's start) and assert it still dials the pod through `AdoptForCheckpoint`, binds it, and checkpoints every slot, rather than skipping the bind because it already coordinates the session and then failing in `Checkpointer.snapshot` with `ErrNoBinding` (`checkpointer.go:236-239`) and writing `session_eviction_state` for a live pod with a healthy object store; and assert the replica that holds **both** the lease and the binding runs neither the dial nor the `Steal`. Assert that the `coordination_generation` CAS and the `CoordinatorFence` run in **all four** of those states, including the two in which the receiving replica already holds the lease: with replica A holding the lease and no binding while replica B holds the binding and is mid-chunk on an unfinalised row at the current generation, assert A's eviction attempt carries a strictly higher generation, supersedes B's row through the `coordination_generation < $new_generation` arm, cancels B's stream at the pod with the fence so the adapter's op-lock is released, and finalises `partial = false`, rather than reading B's own generation, aborting on the equal-generation settled-row term, contending with B for the op-lock, and writing `session_eviction_state` for a live pod with a healthy object store. The single-flight lock cannot cover this state, because it is process-local and A and B are different replicas. Assert replica A drives no checkpoint under a lease it has not taken, that a slot whose handoff cannot complete takes the minimal-state fallback rather than being silently skipped, and that a pod with no bound sessions produces no checkpoint and returns immediately. Assert the handoff completes on a `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pod: each session's `CoordinatorFence` carries its slot and is validated against that slot's session (P1, C1), and two sessions on one pod at independent `coordination_generation` values are both fenced, which the shipped pod-global fence state and its pod-global session gate (`pkg/adapter/coordination.go:26-34`, `:72`) cannot do. The non-happy path is a replica that cannot take a live lease and therefore skips or fails every slot, which makes the whole trigger a minimal-state fallback for most terminating pods in any multi-replica deployment and which a single-replica test cannot catch. `// spec: 4.6.1 (the gateway resolves and checkpoints the pod's sessions), 10.1 (coordinator handoff, lease takeover on the termination path, generation fencing, supersede-on-write, sticky routing is an optimization).`
- **tier-4 the pod-declared budget is a hard ceiling on the fan-out (boundary path, S3/C7):** On a `maxConcurrentSessions: 8` pod whose `budget_ms` expires part-way through the serialized slot loop, assert the handler cancels the in-flight slot's upload so it finalises `partial = true` with `manifest_reason = 'timeout'` rather than staying unfinalised, attempts no further slot after the budget elapses, and **returns `NotifyPodTerminating` at `budget_ms`** with the drain margin and the demote-and-exit reserve intact, so the adapter's teardown completes inside the grace period. Assert the cut slot's bookkeeping is durable on the gateway's own clock rather than charged to the pod's spent grace period: after the pod is gone, the row carries `manifest_reason = 'timeout'` and the committed `chunk_count`, the abort sweep's chunk deletes have issued for an attempt that committed none, and the tenant's `storage_bytes_used` carries no leaked `probed_bytes` reservation. The non-happy path is a bookkeeping budget computed from the remaining `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`, which is zero on exactly this arm, leaving the row unfinalised and the reservation reconciled only on a Redis down-to-up edge (`pkg/gateway/quota/storagequota/recovery.go:29-40`). Assert the fallback record is nonetheless durable: the handler writes a `session_eviction_state` row and increments `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total` for every slot that did not settle, on a gateway-owned context detached from the RPC and from the pod's lifetime, under its own §4.4 60-second Postgres budget, and the rows are present after the pod is gone. Assert the same for the total-loss arm: with Postgres unavailable for the whole 60s budget, `session.lost` with `reason: "eviction_total_loss"` is published on that detached context rather than inside the RPC. Assert the §4.4 manual-recovery key logging on that arm, which C7 is the first producer of and which `evictionfallback.Writer` derives entirely from caller-supplied fields (`pkg/gateway/storage/evictionfallback/evictionfallback.go:602-621` reads `p.CommittedMinIOKeys`, defaults a nil slice to empty, and buckets the counter off its length): with the object store down after two chunks committed, assert the handler passes the failed attempt's derived committed keys into `evictionfallback.WriteParams`, so the WARN log carries `committed_minio_keys` naming exactly the `{checkpoint_id}-chunk-{n}.{chunk_encoding}` objects that landed (the S2 names rather than `partial-{n}`), carries the row's `chunk_encoding` and `generation`, and increments `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_partial_keys_logged_total{keys_committed="1+"}`; and assert the mirror, an attempt that committed no chunk emitting the same log with an empty list and `keys_committed="0"`. Assert `had_prior_checkpoint` on `lenny_session_eviction_total_loss_total` is set from the session's durable-checkpoint state at the same call site. The non-happy path is a handler that passes a nil key slice, which is the easiest implementation because the committed key set lives inside the upload driver that just failed rather than in the handler: it compiles, writes the row, publishes `session.lost`, passes every other assertion here, and permanently empties the only in-band record of which chunks survived the outage, which is what `spec/04:280` exists to produce and what makes the `keys_committed="1+"` bucket unreachable. Assert, separately, that the retry-exhaustion and failed-handoff arms **do** complete their fallback write and their publish before the RPC returns, because those arms fire while the slot's `postgresFallbackBudgetSeconds` is still inside the grace period the S3 floor sized. The non-happy path is a handler that holds the RPC past the declared budget to fit a 60-second Postgres write into a window that has zero seconds left, and is SIGKILLed with the SDK still connected; its mirror is a handler that returns at `budget_ms` and writes no record, which loses the session outright. `// spec: 4.6.1 (the gateway returns when the budget elapses), 4.4 (eviction fallback, the 60-second Postgres retry budget, the total-loss publish), 5.2 (serialized per-slot eviction checkpoints), 6.1 (the demote-and-exit reserve).`
- **tier-1 the pool-config webhook's grace floor (boundary path, S3/C7):** On `pcv.DecideTemplate`, assert the session-mode floor is `maxConcurrentSessions × (max(max_tiered_checkpoint_cap, 90) + 60) + 10 + 10` and that it is compared against the pool's **effective** grace period: that a `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` pool at the new 180s `defaultTerminationGraceSeconds` is admitted (`1 × (90 + 60) + 10 + 10 = 170 ≤ 180`) and the same pool at 160s is rejected with `422 INVALID_POOL_CONFIGURATION`; that the multiplier reads `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions`, so a `maxConcurrentSessions: 8` pool carries a 1220s floor rather than the 170s the shipped `effectiveMaxConcurrent` (`validator.go:459-462`) would compute for it; that a `maxConcurrentSessions: 2` pool **with no `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` set at all** is rejected, because its 320s floor exceeds the 180s default the podspec would render for it and the shipped `spec.TerminationGracePeriodSeconds != nil` guard (`validator.go:644`) admits it silently today, with the rejection message naming the effective value it compared and the pool field the deployer must raise; that a pool with `workspaceSizeLimitBytes: 100Mi` (whose `max_tiered_checkpoint_cap` is 30s) carries the same 170s floor rather than 110s, because the gateway must select the 90s tier for a session with no prior checkpoint; that `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` no longer enters the floor, so raising it does not raise the grace-period requirement; that the BarrierAck-floor rule (`checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds ≥ max_tiered_checkpoint_cap`) still rejects; and that the >600s warning and the `maxTerminationGracePeriodSeconds` hard-ceiling rejection still fire against the new floor (`8 × 150 + 10 + 10 = 1220s`), with the ceiling breach setting `BudgetExceeded` and the BarrierAck-floor rejection not setting it, which is the discriminator `TestDecideTemplate_BudgetExceededDiscriminator_spec_10_1_129` (`validator_test.go:476`) pins on a service-mode template today and which is re-keyed onto a session-mode one. Assert the **service-mode arm** separately, because it is the arm that must not carry the checkpoint terms: a grace-less `executionMode: service, maxConcurrent: 4` pool and a grace-less `maxConcurrent: 8` one are both admitted against their 20s floor, while a grace-less `maxConcurrentSessions: 2` session-mode pool is rejected. Assert the ceiling rule on the same arm: a grace-less `executionMode: service, maxConcurrent: 8` template carrying `maxTerminationGracePeriodSeconds: 600` is now **admitted** with no `BudgetExceeded`, because its 20s floor is below the ceiling, where the shipped 840s floor breached it; that is the behaviour change the mode split introduces on the ceiling rule, and it is what inverts `TestPoolConfigValidatorRejectsTerminationGraceCeilingBreach_spec_5_2_516` (`pkg/admission/webhook/pool_config_validator_test.go:215`) and the ceiling subtest of the discriminator test. The non-happy path is a service-mode pool charged `maxConcurrent × 150s` for eviction checkpoints and Postgres fallbacks it never performs (it materializes no workspace and its session is claimless, so `ListActiveByPod` resolves nothing for its terminating pod), which turns a currently-admitted pool into a 422 that the PoolScalingController cannot bypass and that wedges the pool in the §4.6.3 stuck-pool path. Assert the floor leaves a usable window: a session-mode pool admitted **exactly at** its floor still gives the gateway `maxConcurrentSessions × (max(cap, 90) + 60)` of wall clock after the adapter's demote-and-exit reserve and the kubelet's drain margin are subtracted, which is what the additive `sdkDemoteReserveSeconds` term buys and what a floor summing only the checkpoint and drain terms would have cut by 10s off the tail of the last slot's Postgres fallback. `TestDecideTemplate_TerminationGraceFloor_spec_5_2_516` (`validator_test.go:308`), which pins the old `2*90 + 90 + 30 = 300s` formula, is rewritten here; the `"service mode carries no pool-config invariant"` case of `TestTemplate` (`validator_test.go:232`) **keeps** its admit expectation and is what the service-mode assertion above pins. `// spec: 5.2 (per-slot checkpoint budget), 10.1 (CRD validation rule, the 90s tier for a session with no prior checkpoint), 4.6.1 (terminationGracePeriodSeconds budget), 6.1 (the demote-and-exit reserve).`
- **tier-2 the admission decision through the real CRD codec (boundary path, S3/C7):** In `tests/tier2_component/admission`, assert that a `SandboxTemplate` carrying `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions` round-trips through the generated CRD schema and reaches `decideTerminationBudget` with the multiplier intact (the field is new, so a codec that drops it would silently restore the collapsed-to-1 floor), that a session-mode pool below the floor is rejected with a reason naming the effective grace period and the computed floor, and that a service-mode pool with no `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` is admitted against its 20s floor. `TestDecideTemplateRejectsBelowGraceFloor_spec_5_2_516` (`tests/tier2_component/admission/pool_config_validator_test.go:182`) applies a service-mode template with an explicit 120s grace period and asserts a rejection against an "840s" floor; under the staged rule that template clears its 20s service-mode floor and is admitted, so the test is rewritten onto a session-mode template carrying `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions: 8` and keeps its rejection against the 1220s floor. The non-happy path is a CRD field the webhook cannot read, which is the hole the shipped `effectiveMaxConcurrent` leaves open. `// spec: 4.6.3 (CRD field ownership), 5.2 (per-slot checkpoint budget), 10.1 (CRD validation rule).`
- **tier-2 the PoolScalingController renders the fields the webhook reads (spec-named-failure path, S3/C7):** On `PoolStoreSource.ListPoolConfigs`, assert that a session-mode poolstore row with `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions: 8` and **no recycle block** renders `SandboxTemplate.spec.sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions: 8`, that a row with the field unset renders no multiplier (the webhook then defaults to 1), that a row carrying both `maxConcurrentSessions` and a scrub profile renders both, and that a row setting `termination_grace_period_seconds` renders `spec.terminationGracePeriodSeconds`. Assert the rendered template then carries the resulting floor through `decideTerminationBudget` and is admitted. The non-happy path is `sessionPolicyToCRD`'s recycle and scrub nil guards (`poolstoresource.go:145-152`) leaving `spec.sessionPolicy` nil for every ordinary non-recycling pool, which makes the multiplier unreadable at admission and silently restores the collapsed 170s floor while the CRD-codec assertion above still passes, because that assertion hand-builds the template rather than exercising the producer. `TestPoolStoreSourceLeavesSessionPolicyNilWithoutScrubControl` (`poolstoresource_test.go:263`), which pins the nil render, is rewritten here. `// spec: 4.6.3 (SandboxTemplate.spec.* is PoolScalingController-owned and reconciled from the Postgres pool definitions), 4.6 (sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions >= 1), 5.2 (per-slot checkpoint budget).`
- **tier-2 the new pool column's migration contract (boundary path, S3/C7):** Add a `prodMigrationSchema` row for the migration that adds `termination_grace_period_seconds` (`{migration: "01NN", table: "sandbox_warm_pools", columns: []string{"termination_grace_period_seconds"}}`, `tests/tier2_component/migrations/prod_columns_test.go:20-25`, following the per-column convention the `0040` row already uses for this table at `:65`), so `TestProdMigrationsApplyExpectedSchema` asserts the forward `ALTER TABLE` against a real Postgres and `TestProdMigrationsRollBackPerStep` asserts the `.down.sql` drops exactly that column. Without the row `scripts/lint-migrations.sh` fails the build outright, because it rejects any migration number no test under that directory references (`scripts/lint-migrations.sh:87-88`), which is the same rule C4's rename edit already satisfies for `0062`. `// spec: 5.2 (deployers must set terminationGracePeriodSeconds).`
- **tier-2 the grace period is settable end to end (fail-closed path, S3/C7):** The dropped `!= nil` guard makes every session-mode pool with `maxConcurrentSessions ≥ 2` a `422 INVALID_POOL_CONFIGURATION` until the deployer raises `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`, so the remediation path is asserted rather than assumed. Assert `POST /v1/admin/pools` and `PUT /v1/admin/pools/{name}` accept `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` on the §15.1 payloads (`pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/pools.go:91-92`, `:237-238`), persist it to the pool row, return it on `GET`, and reject a non-positive value, with the same tri-state PUT semantics the pool's other budget fields carry; and assert the `poolstore` pgstore round-trips the column and reads back nil when unset, so an unset pool keeps the 180s effective default. Chain the two to the admission outcome: a `maxConcurrentSessions: 2` pool created through the admin API with `terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 320` is admitted by `decideTerminationBudget`, and the same pool created without it is rejected with `422 INVALID_POOL_CONFIGURATION`. The non-happy path is a fail-closed rejection whose only remediation is a field the wire drops or the store never persists, which wedges the pool in the §4.6.3 stuck-pool state. `// spec: 15.1 (admin pool CRUD), 5.2 (per-slot checkpoint budget), 4.6.3 (stuck pool).`
- **tier-5 the API server rejects a pool below the grace floor (fail-closed path, S3/C7):** On kind, apply a `SandboxTemplate` whose `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` violates the new floor and assert the admission webhook rejects it with `422 INVALID_POOL_CONFIGURATION` and increments `lenny_pool_termination_budget_exceeded_total`, and that the same template at the floor is admitted. `// spec: 10.1 (CRD validation rule), 5.2 (per-slot checkpoint budget).`
- **tier-5 task-mode recycle scrubs the workspace between sessions (spec-named-failure path, C1/C2):** Lift the `t.Skip` at `tests/tier5_e2e_kind/execution_modes_test.go:110` (`TestTaskModeRecycleScrubsWorkspaceBetweenSessions`). Its body is already written and its stated precondition is the unwired `CheckpointSink` that C1 deletes and C2 replaces: with the seal succeeding, the session no longer transitions to `failed`/`workspace_seal_timeout`, §6.2 occupancy-zero recycling becomes reachable, and the test's second session lands on the same recycled pod with a scrubbed workspace. No new assertions are needed. `// spec: 5.2 (sequential pod reuse), 6.2 (occupancy-zero recycling), 7.1 (seal-and-export).`
- **tier-8 MinIO outage during checkpoint (fail-closed path, C2/C5/C7):** With MinIO down for the duration of an eviction checkpoint, assert the gateway retries with the eviction budget and gives up at whichever of the tier cap and the retry budget expires first, and assert the two triggers diverge exactly where they should. On a **gateway drain**, the failure increments `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total{trigger="eviction"}` (the counter S1 widens to cover every trigger), increments no `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total`, writes no `session_eviction_state` row, and falls back to the last successful checkpoint, leaving the session resumable from it on its still-running pod. On an **agent-pod eviction**, the same failure additionally writes the minimal-state row, increments `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total`, and degrades the resume to `conversation_only` with `workspaceLost: true`. With MinIO down mid-upload after two chunks committed, assert the manifest row stays `partial = true` and the backstop sweep reclaims the chunks. The non-happy path is a routine gateway rolling update writing a lost-workspace record for sessions whose pods are still running. `// spec: 12.5 (MinIO unavailability during checkpoint), 4.4 (checkpoint storage failure, eviction fallback), 10.1 (fall back to the last successful checkpoint).`
- **tier-9 chunk keys are tenant-scoped (fail-closed path, C2):** Assert every chunk key of every checkpoint sits under `/{tenant_id}/checkpoint/{session_id}/`, that a restore driver bound to tenant `acme` cannot `Get` a chunk of tenant `globex`, and that `DeleteByTenant` removes the chunks of one tenant only. `// spec: 12.5 (tenant isolation), 13.2 (isolation). // diagnosis: a failure means checkpoint bytes crossed a tenant boundary.`
- **tier-10 adapter conformance for the streaming surface (spec-named-failure path, C1):** Assert a conforming adapter streams an archive on `Checkpoint`, restores from a `Resume` stream, emits `lenny_checkpoint_size_exceeded_total` with `pool` and `level` when the pre-checkpoint probe trips, and issues no object-store call on any path. `// spec: 4.7 (runtime adapter), 4.4 (workspace size limit).`
- **tier-11 spec/code consistency (spec-named-failure path, S1/S2/S4):** Assert no `spec/` line attributes a checkpoint `PutObject`, `GetObject`, `DeleteObject`, or `AbortMultipartUpload` to the adapter, and no `spec/` line attributes a checkpoint *upload* to the pod, to the adapter, or to the in-pod runtime SDK in prose that names no verb (the `spec/10:124` BarrierAck-floor rationale is one line this catches, arguing the floor from a pod that "needs 90s to upload a 512 MB workspace", and the §15.7 SDK-responsibility bullet at `spec/15:2531` is the other, offering the pod "helpers for … uploading checkpoints"), so the assertion's scope covers §15.7 as well as §4, §10, and §12; assert `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_total{outcome="timeout"}` is described identically at `spec/10:124`, `spec/10:177`, and `spec/16:41`, as a pod that did not acknowledge quiescence by the deadline rather than as a slow upload, and that no `spec/16` row, in the §16.1 inventory or in the §16.5 alert catalog, attributes checkpoint-flush time to `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_duration_seconds` (`spec/16:42`, whose interval ends at the ack and therefore contains no flush, and `spec/16:456`, whose `PDBBlockedEvictions` diagnosis correlates a saturated preStop checkpoint cap against that histogram and is retargeted at `lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds{trigger="eviction"}`); assert `manifest_reason` carries a single value across `spec/10:141`, `spec/10:157`, `spec/16:195`, and the migration `CHECK` constraint; assert the strings `partial_object_key_prefix`, `partialChunkSizeBytes`, `ListObjectsV2`, "listed by prefix", `DeleteObject`, and `DeleteObjects` appear nowhere in `spec/`, `migrations/`, or `pkg/` on the checkpoint path, so no surviving passage mandates a store capability `blobstore.Store` does not expose or a raw bucket delete that bypasses the `artifact_store` row and the quota decrement; assert `terminated_during_resume` and `resume_snapshot_close` appear nowhere in `spec/`, `pkg/`, `migrations/`, or `tests/spec-map.json`; assert the `trigger` label set on `lenny_checkpoint_partial_total` covers every trigger that can commit an intent row, so no partial row class is unrepresentable; assert every `spec/` statement of the §12.5 backstop's row-selection predicate (`spec/10:133`, `spec/10:141`, `spec/10:155`, `spec/10:175`, `spec/12:328`, `spec/12:349`) carries the widened soft-deleted-row conjunct and that no line states the backstop sweep as `partial = true AND deleted_at IS NULL` alone; assert the `table` label's value set on `lenny_gc_tombstones_pruned_total` is stated identically at `spec/16:197`, at `spec/12:353` (which carries a second, independent declaration of it in the hard-prune paragraph), and on the Go registration (`pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_retention.go:67-73`), so no site keeps naming the renamed table; assert no `spec/` line addresses a checkpoint as a single object key or a checkpoint copy as a single-object copy (`final_workspace_ref`, `Record.Ref`, the §7.2 terminal-seal paragraphs, the §7.1 and §12.5 derive-copy paragraphs, and the §15.5 `DERIVE_SNAPSHOT_UNAVAILABLE` row with its `docs/reference/error-catalog.md` mirror all name the `object_key_prefix` and its chunk set); assert no `spec/` line states a per-pod or per-session count of checkpoint *objects* that equates one retained checkpoint with one MinIO object (`spec/12:325`, `:336`, and `:338` all count retained checkpoints and multiply by `chunk_count`), which the single-object-addressing assertion above does not reach because it is scoped to key addressing; assert no `spec/` line mandates chunk deletion or a manifest-row soft-delete after a `partial = false` restore, since the §10.1 Cleanup rule and its §4.4 twin are both scoped to `partial = true` rows; assert `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` is described identically on its four mirrors (`spec/16:200`, `docs/reference/metrics.md`, `docs/operator-guide/observability.md`, and `pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go`) and that none of them scopes it to non-eviction checkpoints; assert that neither the `CheckpointStorageUnavailable` alert summary nor its description nor the `spec/16` catalog row nor the `docs/reference/metrics.md` alert-catalog row nor the rendered mirrors scope the alert to eviction, now that the counter it fires on covers every trigger; assert that no alert summary, alert description, `spec/16` catalog row, `docs/reference/metrics.md` alert-catalog row, or runbook claims a Postgres minimal-state fallback on the gateway-drain path; assert no `spec/` line defines `chunk_count` as the count of committed chunks, now that the counter is reserved before the `PutObject` it names; assert the agent pod gained no object-store reachability, which is the security argument of the whole change: no NetworkPolicy in `spec/13_security-model.md` or `charts/lenny/templates/agent-network-policies.yaml` permits agent-namespace egress to MinIO or to port 9443, `spec/13:220`'s MinIO ingress list still admits only the gateway, `lenny-ops`, and `lenny-backup` pods, the §4.9 credential-lease provider set (`spec/04:1117-1118`) carries no S3, MinIO, or STS provider, and no `sdks/runtime/` package imports an object-store client (this is the assertion that fails if a later change quietly reintroduces a pod-side S3 path); assert `checkpoint_id` is described as gateway-minted at every site that names its origin (`schemas/lenny-adapter.proto`, `docs/api/internal.md`, `spec/10:130`) and that no example anywhere in `schemas/examples/`, `docs/`, or `spec/` shows it in a non-UUID format; assert the `resumeMode` value set is stated identically at `spec/07:138`, `docs/client-guide/wire-format.md`, `docs/client-guide/streaming.md`, `docs/runtime-author-guide/lifecycle.md`, and `docs/getting-started/concepts.md`, and that `workspaceRecoveryFraction` appears in every one of them; assert the adapter's operation-lock scope is stated identically at `spec/04:672`, at `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md:86`, and on the `ops opLock` declaration in `pkg/adapter/server.go:328`, so no surviving statement in the tree describes it as per-session while the shipped lock is one field on `Server` and every displaced-coordinator argument reads it as pod-global; assert the agent-pod grace-period budget rule is stated identically at `spec/04:489`, `spec/04:625`, `spec/05:542`, `spec/10:116-122`, `spec/16:132`, the `pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1/sandboxtemplate_types.go` field docs, and `decideTerminationBudget` (`pkg/admission/pool_config_validator/validator.go`, its doc comment as well as its body), including the `sdkDemoteReserveSeconds` term, the session-mode `maxConcurrentSessions` multiplier, and the service-mode arm that carries neither the tier cap nor the Postgres fallback, that no `spec/` line, CRD field doc, or Go doc comment (including `validator.go`'s package doc) states the floor without the demote-and-exit reserve, that no `spec/` line or Go doc comment charges a service-mode pool a per-slot checkpoint or Postgres-fallback term for a pod that materializes no workspace, that `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions` is a `SandboxTemplate` CRD field the webhook can read so the multiplier the rule names is the one the code evaluates, that no `spec/` line or CRD field doc says the webhook adds `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` to the agent pod's grace floor, that no `spec/` line justifies the rule by SIGKILL during a **gateway** drain, that `spec/10:167` and `spec/10:177` name the gateway pod's drain budget rather than a CRD rule, that the rule is stated everywhere as a comparison against the pool's effective grace period rather than against an explicitly set one, that the podspec's `defaultTerminationGraceSeconds` satisfies the rule at `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` and that the validator's mirror of that constant agrees with it, and that `charts/lenny/crds/lenny.dev_sandboxtemplates.yaml` and `pkg/embedded/crds/lenny.dev_sandboxtemplates.yaml` are byte-identical and regenerated from the Go source; assert the §10.1 supersede predicate is stated identically in `spec/10:137` and in every code and test site that repeats it (`coordination_generation < $new_generation OR (coordination_generation = $new_generation AND (manifest_reason IS NOT NULL OR checkpoint_timeout_at < now()))`), so no site states the guard as a bare `coordination_generation <= $new_generation` conjunction and no site claims a strictly-newer coordinator may not supersede a running upload; assert no `spec/` line, Go doc comment, or test comment claims that the `coordination_generation` fence or the pod's entry gate excludes a displaced coordinator's **later** attempt, since that attempt re-reads the advanced generation from the session row and the gate rejects only a generation below the slot's `lastFenced`, and assert the §4.6.1 termination handler's ordering is stated identically at `spec/04:489`, in `spec/10:30-37`, and in the handler's code (the reservation of every slot commits in the same transaction as the CAS, before any slot is driven); assert `spec/06:67`'s SIGTERM sequence begins with the `NotifyPodTerminating` signal and reserves `LENNY_DEMOTE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS + 5s` for the demote-and-exit steps, and that `pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:1203-1212` and `sandboxtemplate_types.go:174` mirror it; assert every label a `spec/16` row declares is registered on the counter in `pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics` and every label the code registers is declared (three counters disagree today and this change reconciles all three: `lenny_checkpoint_partial_total` declares `pool`, `recovered`, `manifest_reason`, and `trigger` and registers `pool` alone, which C2 corrects on the registration; `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` registers `reason` and declares it nowhere, which S1 corrects on the `spec/16:200` declaration; and `lenny_prestop_barrier_target_source_total` declares `pool` and `source` at `spec/16:44` and at `docs/reference/metrics.md:40` and registers `source` alone at `gatewaymetrics_podlifecycle.go:142-144`, which S1 corrects on both declarations, because the counter is emitted once per fan-out across every pool the replica coordinates (`barrier.go:164-166`) and a single `pool` value is not expressible there); assert the label set of `lenny_prestop_barrier_target_source_total` is stated identically on all three of `spec/16:44`, `docs/reference/metrics.md:40`, and the Go registration, so the docs mirror cannot keep declaring a label the counter does not carry; assert every `reason` value the code emits on `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` is in the value set `spec/16:200` declares and every declared value has an emitter, which covers the four values the `IncArtifactUploadError` rollup produces (`minio_unreachable`, `auth`, `quota_exceeded`, `other`) as well as the two this change adds (`manifest_write_failed`, `no_chunks_committed`); assert every counter and histogram this change relies on is registered in `pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics` and has at least one non-test emitter, which `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_total` and `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_duration_seconds` have neither of today (they appear only as name-and-type rows in `pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go:127-128`, which registers nothing); and assert `chunkSizeBytes`, `partialRecoveryThresholdFraction`, and `checkpointWorker.concurrency` each have a `spec/17` row, a `charts/lenny/values.yaml` key, a `values.schema.json` entry, and a gateway flag, so no checkpoint knob is spec-only. `// spec: 10.1, 13.1, 16.1, 16.5, 17.8. // diagnosis: a failure means the reassigned actor, the reduced enum, the reserved counter, the widened counter, the grace-period budget, or the pod's forbidden object-store reachability drifted between the spec, the charts, the docs, and the code.`

`tests/tier2_component/stores/partial_manifest_terminated_during_resume_test.go` and its `tests/spec-map.json:169` entry are deleted rather than un-skipped, because S4 removes the row they assert. The mid-resume counter semantics stay covered by `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/coordination_fence_test.go` and `failure_test.go:380`.

`pkg/adapter/checkpoint_test.go` and `pkg/adapter/resume_test.go` are built end to end on the seams C1 deletes and the messages P1 renames, so neither compiles after the change and both are rewritten in full.

**Ten further shipped test files construct the deleted seams and stop compiling with them, in six packages outside `pkg/adapter/checkpoint_test.go` and `resume_test.go`.** Each is named here and in §10 with what replaces it, because a `Server.Checkpoints` or `Server.Restorer` assignment, or a fake typed as `adapter.CheckpointSink` or `adapter.CheckpointSource`, takes its whole test package's build down the moment C1 lands.

- `pkg/adapter/coordination_test.go`: `stubCheckpointSink` (`:219-230`) and the two cases that drive it, `TestCheckpointBarrierAcksMatchedGeneration` (`:236`, `s.Checkpoints = stubCheckpointSink{id: "ck-1"}` at `:242`) and `TestCheckpointBarrierEmptyCheckpointOnSinkError` (`:303`, `:306`), pin an ack whose `checkpoint_ref` is a pod-produced storage reference from a pod-side barrier flush, which C1 and P1 invert. The fake and both cases are **deleted** with the seam. The file's fence cases are **rewritten** onto the per-slot `lastFenced` state C1 moves off the pod-global `coordinationState`, onto the quiesce-and-ack barrier, and onto the fence-driven cancellation of an in-flight `Checkpoint` (C1).
- `pkg/adapter/tracing_external_test.go` (`:241`, `:262`): rewritten onto the streaming handler; its `fakeCheckpointSink` is deleted with the seam.
- `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer_test.go` (23 cases; `stubSink` at `:43-52`, assigned at `:94`, `:156`, `:228`, and `:663`) and `bytes_test.go` (`:36`): the unit suite of the package C2 and C5 rewrite. `TestCheckpointRecordsTheWorkspaceSnapshot` (`:90`) asserts `row.WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref == "ckpt-7"`, the adapter-minted id this change abolishes. Both files are rewritten against the C2 upload driver: the gateway mints the `checkpoint_id`, `WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref` carries the `object_key_prefix`, and there is no adapter sink.
- `pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client_test.go` (`countingSink` at `:818-836`, assigned at `:839` and `:862`; `srv.Restorer` at `:899` and `:937`): rewritten onto the server-streaming `Checkpoint` and the client-streaming `Resume`. The case that asserts an adapter-returned `CheckpointResponse.CheckpointID` (`:846-850`) and `TestResumeRejectsAMissingCheckpointSource` (`:975`) are deleted with the message and the branch they pin.
- `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/binder_test.go` (`stubRestorer` at `:46-51`, assigned at `:507` and `:1671`): rewritten onto the C2 restore-driver seam at `binder.go:1539`, with `ResumeRequest.CheckpointID` and `SlotID` and the new `ResumeResult.RestoreMode` and `RecoveryFraction`.
- `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start_pod_test.go` (`:1332`, `:1567`, `:1678`, `:1737`), `resume_external_effect_regression_test.go` (`:103`), and `workspace_snapshot_missing_resume_test.go` (`:58`): rewritten onto `SelectForResume` plus the restore driver. The last of these is also un-skipped and rewritten onto the terminal `WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` arm (above).
- `tests/tier4_integration/checkpoint_resume_test.go` (`:107`): the live tier-4 restore coverage, rewritten onto the gateway-driven chunk-set restore alongside `checkpoint_minio_atomicity_test.go`.

**Four more shipped test files break on the interface widenings rather than on the seam deletion, and are named for the same reason.**

- `pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier/barrier_test.go` and `wiring_test.go`: `fakeDispatcher.Send(ctx, Target, barrierID)` (`barrier_test.go:39`), `fakeMetrics.IncPreStopBarrierTargetSource(source)` (`:55`), and the four three-argument `d.Send(...)` calls in `wiring_test.go` (`:85`, `:101`, `:115`, `:134`) all carry the pre-widening signatures. The fakes gain the `checkpoint_id` and the slot on `Send`, `fakeMetrics` gains `IncCheckpointBarrierAck` and the ack-duration observer, and `barrier.New` gains the driver-reservation seam. `TestDispatchCacheFallbackSource_spec_10_1` (`:183`) survives on its own terms (the target-source counter keeps its signature and its per-fan-out rule, S1) and `TestDispatchTransportErrorNonFatal_spec_10_1` (`:166`) is extended with the `outcome="error"` increment. `TestDispatchHappyPath_spec_10_1` (`:63`) gains the reservation-before-`Send` and drive-on-ack assertions.
- `pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/checkpointbarrier_test.go` (`:39`, `TestCheckpointBarrierMapsAck_spec_10_1`): rewritten onto the request that carries the gateway-minted `checkpoint_id` and the slot, and the ack that echoes it.
- `pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_elicitation_test.go`: `TestCheckpointStorageFailureReasonLabel` (`:207`) calls the three-argument `IncCheckpointStorageFailure` (`:212`) and asserts the hard-coded `reason="retry_exhausted"` (`:221`), so it stops compiling on the four-argument helper. It is rewritten to assert one series per legal `reason` value (`retry_exhausted`, `kms_unavailable`, `tier_store_mismatch`, `minio_unreachable`, `auth`, `quota_exceeded`, `other`, plus `manifest_write_failed` and `no_chunks_committed`), which is the value set `spec/16:200` declares after S1. `TestGCTombstonesPrunedCounter` (`:234`) asserts `lenny_gc_tombstones_pruned_total{table="partial_manifest"}` (`:252`) and is restated on the renamed `checkpoint_manifest` label value (C4).
- `pkg/gateway/coordination/coordfence/coordfence_test.go` and `pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/coordinatorfence_test.go`: the fence's sender widens to carry the slot (P1), so `fakeFenceClient.CoordinatorFence(_ context.Context, _ string, gen int64)` (`coordfence_test.go:24`) no longer satisfies `FenceClient` and the `adapterclient` case drives a signature that no longer exists. Both are rewritten on the slot-carrying sender, and `coordfence_test.go` gains an assertion that the slot the caller passes reaches `CoordinatorFenceRequest.slot_id` unchanged, since an empty value there routes the adapter back to the pod-global gate that can never pass on a concurrent pod.
