- `pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/failover_test.go` and `pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination_mirror_test.go`: `LeaseStore` has two test implementations besides its three production ones, and C7 adds a seventh method to the interface. `fakeLeaseStore` (`failover_test.go:19-58`) implements exactly the six shipped methods and is passed to `NewFailover(primary, fallback LeaseStore, …)` (`:77`, `:114`), and `fakeLeases` (`coordination_mirror_test.go:22-31`) is passed to `NewSweeper(…, leases leasestore.LeaseStore, …)` (`:87`, `:137`, `:155`). Both gain `Steal`, and `failover_test.go` gains a primary-then-fallback routing assertion for it. `coordination_mirror_test.go` needs nothing beyond the new method: the sweeper's `ErrHeld` arm keeps its stand-down-and-skip behaviour and takes no registry seam (C7).

- Deleted with the seams and the emitters they drive (C1): `TestCheckpointAbortCleanupRunsOnSinkFailure` (`pkg/adapter/checkpoint_test.go:263`), `TestCheckpointAbortCleanupBumpsOrphanedCounterOnDeleteFailure` (`:326`), and `TestCheckpointAbortCleanupSkippedOnSuccess` (`:344`), which drive `runAbortCleanup` and the `CheckpointAborter` interface; `TestCheckpointUnimplementedWithoutSink` (`:242`) and `TestResumeUnimplementedWithoutACheckpointSource` (`pkg/adapter/resume_test.go:187`), which assert the two `codes.Unimplemented` branches C1 removes; `TestCheckpointSurfacesSinkError` (`:250`), whose sink no longer exists; and `TestCheckpointBumpsStorageFailureCounterOnSaveFailure` (`:287`) and `TestCheckpointSkipsStorageFailureForEviction` (`:310`), which assert the adapter-side `recordStorageFailure` and `CheckpointTriggerLabel` emitter C1 moves to the gateway. The gateway-side abort-sweep, orphan-counter, and `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` assertions above replace their coverage on the new actor.
- Rewritten onto the streaming handlers: the remaining `checkpoint_test.go` cases (the session-gate rejections at `:215`, `:224`, and `:233`, the `/sessions` bundling at `:143`, the lifecycle-channel quiesce at `:164`, and the five size-precheck cases at `:361`, `:394`, `:423`, `:441`, and `:456`, all of which C1 keeps adapter-side) are driven against a fake stream consumer rather than a `CheckpointSink`, and all eleven `resume_test.go` cases (which build `*adapterv1.ResumeRequest`, call the unary `s.Resume`, and assign `s.Restorer`) are driven against the client-streaming `Resume` with a `ResumeStart` first frame, preserving their `expected_workspace_root`, non-idle-pod, `/sessions` extraction, and claim-release assertions on the slot-resolved paths C1 introduces.

## 7. Findings closed on application

- **T-4.4.12** (`TEST-GAPS.md:604`, "Production CheckpointSink is never wired: the §4.4 checkpoint-to-MinIO write path is Unimplemented in every production binary", OPEN) is closed by S1, P1, C1, and C2. Its "Needs human input" note asks for exactly this change-proposal-scale product build.
- **T-4.4.13** (`TEST-GAPS.md:612`, "Production adapter Resume path is also unwired", OPEN) is closed by P1, C1, and C2.
- **T-4.4.14** (`TEST-GAPS.md:620`, "Gateway never drives the §4.4 eviction-checkpoint trigger on individual agent-pod eviction", OPEN) is closed by S3, P1, C5, and C7. C5 makes the eviction trigger, the eviction retry budget, and the `trigger="eviction"` label reachable on the gateway's own preStop path, and C7 gives the finding's own subject, individual agent-pod eviction, its trigger: the pod's preStop hook signals the gateway over `NotifyPodTerminating` and the gateway drives the checkpoint against the terminating pod's adapter under `checkpoint.RetryBudgetFor(TriggerEviction)`. The finding's "Needs human input" note asks for the choice of mechanism; §2 records the decision and the rejected gateway-informer alternative.
- **T-4.4.16** (`TEST-GAPS.md:635`, "Gateway wires PartialCleaner with a nil Deleter", OPEN) is closed by C2 and C4, which give the cleaner the gateway-side delete surface and, in the same step, re-scope its row selection to `partial = TRUE AND deleted_at IS NULL` so that filling the deleter does not make every resume delete the session's full checkpoint.
- **T-4.4.20** (`TEST-GAPS.md:664`, "checkpointer triggerForSource comment references a non-existent snapshotWithTrigger method", OPEN) is closed by C5, which adds `snapshotWithTrigger` and deletes `triggerForSource`.
- **T-4.2.1** (`TEST-GAPS.md:405`, "Partial-checkpoint manifest with `manifest_reason = 'terminated_during_resume'` has no test at any tier", OPEN) is closed as a **spec defect** rather than as a test gap. S4 removes the requirement: the aborted snapshot replay writes no manifest row and commits no chunks, so the row the spec mandated could never be written and there is nothing to test. No tier-2 test is owed, and `tests/tier2_component/stores/partial_manifest_terminated_during_resume_test.go` and its `tests/spec-map.json:169` entry are deleted rather than un-skipped (§6). The finding's own "Needs human input" note (`TEST-GAPS.md:411`) asks precisely whether the requirement or the test is wrong; the answer is the requirement.
- **T-4.6.4** (`TEST-GAPS.md:790`, "Node-drain disruption protection verifies replenishment but not checkpoint-before-eviction", OPEN) is closed by S3, C7, and the tier-5 node-drain assertion in §6. The finding's "Needs human input" note (`TEST-GAPS.md:796`) asks for the intended mechanism for firing the preStop eviction checkpoint on a node drain and whether it should be scoped through one change proposal alongside T-4.4.12, T-4.4.13, and T-4.4.14; this proposal is that scope, and §2 records the mechanism.
- **T-JRN.9** (`TEST-GAPS.md:11700`, "The checkpoint-then-resume client journey is not exercised on Kind and the tier4 scaffold cites a non-existent test file", OPEN) has its tier-5 half closed: the two `t.Skip`s in `tests/tier5_e2e_kind/checkpoint_resume_test.go` (`:74`, `:95`) lift against the trigger C7 wires, and both journeys are asserted end to end in §6. The finding's tier-4 half (the scaffold's reference to a test file that does not exist on disk) is a separate scaffold defect and stays open against that half alone.

## 8. Resolved in adversarial review

Subsequent adversarial review rounds populate this section. The drafting pass applied the following convergence revisions before first review:

- **S1's target list corrected in both directions.** The draft targeted `spec/10:130`, `:132`, `:133`, `:137`, `:155`, `:157` and `spec/04:235`, `:237`, none of which carry adapter-worded object-store text; editing them risked churning the `MAX(coordination_generation)` fencing rule and the supersede transaction. It omitted `spec/04:263` and `:264` (which attribute `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` and `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total` to the adapter, inside the range S1 claimed) and `spec/10:167` (which has the adapter trigger the barrier-flush checkpoint). The staged S1 targets the adapter-worded passages and nothing else, and the new invariant sentences it proposed for §4.4 and §10.1 are replaced with a cross-reference to `spec/13:12-13` and `spec/12:307`.
- **S2 gained the artifact-store accounting rule.** `spec/12:321` states that `artifact_store` covers checkpoints, `spec/12:348` hangs checkpoint rotation and TTL deletion off `artifact_store` rows, and `spec/10:135` deliberately carves partial-chunk bytes out of that regime. Making every checkpoint chunked would have moved every checkpoint's bytes out of the catalogued regime silently. S2 gained the accounting rule, rotation and TTL delete the derived chunk key set, and `checkpointretention.Record.Ref` and `sessionstore.WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref` carry the prefix. (The prefix-keyed single row this pass proposed is superseded by Pass 1, which puts one `artifact_store` row on each chunk object.)
- **The five-segment object key was rejected and the C3 URI widening dropped.** S2's original prefix added a fifth path segment against the four-segment §12.5 format (`spec/12:307`), and C3 would have widened the `blobstore` URI grammar to carry it. A slashed `PartID` breaks `ParseURI` round-tripping and would make every chunk un-GC-able through the `cataloging` sweep. The staged layout uses the flat `{checkpoint_id}-chunk-{n}.{chunk_encoding}` `PartID`, which needs no blobstore change.
- **S3 shrunk to the two elements that rest on real defects.** Gateway minting of `checkpoint_id` is already normative at `spec/10:130` and needs no second statement in §4.4; the `terminated_during_resume` producer is already named in the spec (the gap was code-only, and S4 now shows the row cannot exist at all); and bundling the retention INSERT, the latest-2 rotation, and the `last_successful_checkpoint_at` stamp into the finalising transaction would invert `checkpointer.go:305-311`'s documented best-effort contract and hand a fully uploaded checkpoint to the GC on a rotation failure. What remains is the `spec/04:489` preStop rewrite and the `spec/10:132` clean-EOF arm.
- **C7 replaced by the spec-only S4.** The mandated `terminated_during_resume` manifest row has no writer because the replay path is read-then-delete (`spec/10:155`, `spec/04:237`); the only row in hand at collapse time is the one being restored, which under S2 is usually the `partial = false` full checkpoint that `spec/07:214` step 2 designates as the session's `final_workspace_ref`. Retagging it would invalidate the artifact the same paragraph relies on. The audit need is already met by the terminal session row plus the `coordination_generation` bump (`start.go:3397`). S4 removes the mandate instead of building the phantom writer.
- **C2's delete-after-restore narrowed to partial rows.** The draft deleted every chunk and soft-deleted the row after every restore, citing `spec/04:237`. That rule is scoped to the partial manifest. Applied to `partial = false` rows it would erase the full checkpoint, leaving `spec/10:155`'s own fallback target and `binder.go:1533`'s retry-on-a-fresh-pod with no bytes.
- **C2's "no new enforcement code" claim corrected.** Only the SSE-KMS resolver is inherited from `blobproviderflags.Resolve`. `stores.go:1062` hands out the raw backend, and `ErrCrossTenant` is reachable only through `blobstore.NewTenantScoped`, which every existing caller wraps per call. The drivers must wrap.
- **`ListObjectsV2` dropped from the restore and abort paths.** `blobstore.Store` exposes no prefix listing, and the draft's use of it would have required a new interface across every backend, contradicting the non-goal that the `Store` interface is unchanged. `chunk_count` makes the keys derivable and a missing `Get` is the contiguity check.
- **C2 collapsed from two new packages into one.** `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer` already is the gateway-side checkpoint driver and is already wired; the upload driver goes there. Only the restore driver becomes a new package, because `podsession.Binder` holds neither the blobstore nor the manifest store.
- **C1 gained the barrier path, the orphaned-objects counter, and the correct label split.** `pkg/adapter/coordination.go:199` is a third consumer of `s.Checkpoints`; `CheckpointBarrier` is unary, so an adapter-side flush cannot route bytes through a gateway-owned upload. `lenny_checkpoint_orphaned_objects_total` moves with the abort sweep because deleting `CheckpointAborter` deletes its only emitter. `CheckpointPoolLabel` and `CheckpointLevelLabel` stay adapter-side for `recordSizeExceeded` and `spec/04:255`; only `CheckpointTriggerLabel` and `recordStorageFailure` move.
- **C1's restore staging rule corrected to the real roots.** The archive is a two-root bundle (`checkpoint.go:311-323`), and per-slot pools use `/workspace/slots/{slotId}/current`, where `/workspace/current` does not exist. Staging is a sibling of the resolved workspace root, and the `/sessions` tree commits in the same rename step.
- **C4's rationale corrected and its schema made satisfiable.** The claim that `0150` would reject a legitimate concurrent per-slot write is false (a slot hosts one session, `spec/05:542`). The defect that bites is the missing `partial = TRUE` term in its predicate once full rows share the table. The draft's `manifest_reason TEXT NOT NULL` is unsatisfiable when the intent row is written before the outcome is known, so the column is nullable with a `CHECK`, `checkpoint_timeout_at` is nullable, and S2 stages the matching `spec/10:141` amendment. A `(tenant_id, session_id, slot_id, coordination_generation DESC)` index is added for the now-primary read path, the migrations are rewritten in place, and the full eight-method store surface plus `MemoryStore` is named.
- **C5 cut to the defect it names.** The agent-pod informer, the `pkg/controller/warmpool` pre-scale-down seam, and the `prestop.go` inversion are dropped. The preStop premise was wrong (`GracefulStop` drains in-flight RPCs, so the hook's doc comment is already accurate) and the sketched mechanism does not exist (no pod-local socket). The `binder.go` Release path was the wrong call site: it runs after the session has terminated and `sealWorkspace` has already checkpointed. (This pass moved the eviction checkpoint to `DrainSandbox`; Pass 1 rejects that seam and moves it to the gateway's own preStop hook.)
- **C6's seal short-circuit dropped and its lookup wiring added.** The permanent seal error it guarded is the `Unimplemented` C1 deletes; failing faster would not reach §6.2 recycling, and `outcome="permanent"` contradicts `spec/07:112` and `spec/16:227`. The draft's claim that the fallback consumer "is already wired" is false: `Options.EvictionStateLookup` is never assigned in `cmd/`, so wiring only the writer would produce a row nothing reads and a `resumeMode: "full"` claim for a lost workspace.
- **The Resume streaming question resolved in favour of client-streaming.** The alternative (a separate gateway→adapter chunk-push RPC that a unary `Resume` waits on) adds an RPC and an ordering dependency between two calls for one logical operation.

### Pass 1 (2026-07-13, automated)

- **`object_key_prefix` had two incompatible values.** S2 assigned the column both `/{tenant_id}/checkpoint/{session_id}/{checkpoint_id}/` and the flat `{tenant_id}/checkpoint/{session_id}/{checkpoint_id}-chunk-`, and §3 repeated the first. The trailing-slash form produces the five-segment key §5 rejects and `spec/12:307` forbids. The flat prefix is now the single stated value in §2, §3, S2, and the staged `spec/10:130` and `:141` text, and chunk keys derive as `object_key_prefix + {n}.{chunk_encoding}`.
- **The `artifact_store` accounting rule was impossible against the wired store.** Production hands the gateway the `cataloging` decorator (`cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1085-1090`), whose `Put` inserts one row per object key (`cataloging.go:143-171`), so the staged "exactly one row keyed on `object_key_prefix`" would double-count every chunk and produce a prefix-keyed row the `cataloging` GC sweeps skip (`cataloging.go:386-391`). S2 now states one `artifact_store` row per chunk object, removes the `spec/10:135` partial-chunk carve-out instead of preserving it, and routes rotation, TTL, and backstop deletion through the same per-chunk soft-delete. A tier-2 assertion on the row count per finalised checkpoint is added.
- **The spec passages hanging off the agent-pod preStop checkpoint were unedited.** S3 now edits `spec/04:264` (the eviction retry-budget bullet's title and rationale), `:286` (the total-loss publish deadline), `:491` (the PDB rationale), `:493` (the finalizer's condition (b), which would otherwise wedge a `Sandbox` in `Terminating`), `spec/12:296` (the MinIO-outage-and-node-drain risk), and `spec/17:11` (the agent-pod deployment row).
- **The `partialChunkSizeBytes` rename orphaned its defaults row.** The knob is renamed to `chunkSizeBytes` at `spec/10:139`, `:141`, and `:153`, and the §17.8.1 quick-reference row (`spec/17:919`) is renamed and relabelled "Checkpoint chunk size" so it covers every trigger. `spec/17_deployment-topology.md` is added to S1 and to §10.
- **`spec/10:133` and `:137` kept the old column name.** Both are added to S2's target list, `:137`'s supersede delete derives its key set from `object_key_prefix` plus `chunk_count`, and the sentence asserting `:137` is untouched is removed.
- **Dropping `ListObjectsV2` left three consumers mandating it.** S2 now converts the §12.5 GC backstop (`spec/12:328`), the crash-semantics rule (`spec/10:133`), and the resume-time cleanup discovery clause (`spec/04:237`) to the same derivation, so no surviving passage requires a store capability `blobstore.Store` does not expose.
- **Fire-and-forget chunk counters would orphan chunks permanently.** With prefix listing gone, `chunk_count` is the sole enumeration source. S1 now rewrites `spec/10:131` so the counter `UPDATE` for index `n` commits before the `PutObject` for index `n`, making the derived key set a superset of the committed objects, and a failed counter update aborts the attempt. §2, §3, C2, and a tier-1 ordering test carry the same predicate.
- **`DrainSandbox` was the wrong eviction seam.** It does not terminate a pod with live sessions (`spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:178`), its only caller has already released the session's slot reservation, it carries no pod-to-sessions enumeration, it is reachable only on `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pools, and it runs on an unrelated client's request context. C5 drops it.
- **C5 missed the gateway preStop hook, the already-wired eviction driver.** `prestop.CheckpointFn` is documented as the §4.4 eviction-trigger checkpoint (`prestop.go:207-212`) and is wired to the `Checkpointer` (`httpsurface.go:559-561`, `:617`), so it was about to keep the periodic label and the periodic retry budget. C5 routes it through `snapshotWithTrigger(…, TriggerEviction)` and threads the per-session tier-cap budget into the upload driver. T-4.4.14's agent-pod half is closed by C7.
- **S1 and C1 specified two different `CheckpointBarrierAck` points.** The ack is now immediate on quiescence. `CheckpointBarrierRequest` gains the gateway-minted `checkpoint_id`, the gateway commits the intent row before dispatching the barrier and drives the `Checkpoint` stream on ack, and the BarrierAck-timeout path finalises on the gateway's own deadline. `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/prestop` is added to C1, C2, and §10.
- **A derived session's snapshot prefix had no manifest row.** Derive now mints a `checkpoint_id`, copies the parent's derived chunk key set, and inserts the derived session's `partial = false` `checkpoint_manifest` row in the same transaction, so every readable `WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref` prefix has a row behind it.
- **Concurrent checkpoints of one session could clobber each other.** Nothing serialised the periodic ticker, `Seal`, and the eviction path against one session, so a supersede would delete the chunks of an upload still running and leave them under a soft-deleted row that no sweep predicate selects. The upload driver takes a per-`(session_id, slot_id)` single-flight lock, supersede-on-write is scoped to rows from attempts that are no longer running, and a tier-1 race test is added.
- **The gateway-driven upload bypassed the §11.2 quota reservation.** `CheckpointFrame` gains a mandatory leading `CheckpointStart { probed_bytes, chunk_encoding }` carrying the adapter's existing workspace-size probe, the gateway reserves before the first chunk `Put` through `storagequota.Failover.Reserve`, and `spec/11:35` is restated. `spec/11_policy-and-controls.md` and the quota wiring are added to S2, C2, and §10.
- **`spec/05:542` still assigned the per-slot upload to the adapter.** S1 now rewords the eviction-checkpoint-ordering rule so the gateway serializes the per-slot chunk uploads in slot-ID order, and S1's claim to name every adapter-worded object-store passage is dropped.
- **`checkpoint_complete` promised an upload result the adapter can no longer produce.** S1 rewrites the remainder of `spec/04:263` (the adapter resumes the runtime when its archive stream ends; the gateway owns the upload retries) and redefines `checkpoint_complete` as the archive-capture result across `spec/04:721`, `schemas/lifecycle-events.schema.json`, `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md`, and `docs/runtime-author-guide/integration-levels.md`, with a tier-3 lifecycle-event contract assertion.
- **The abort sweep would have deleted a deadline-truncated checkpoint's chunks.** The staged `spec/04:249` text listed the deadline as an abort, which would erase the partial artifact the same proposal finalises. The sweep is scoped to attempts that leave no usable row, and the deadline fire is stated as a finalisation.
- **The retained half of `spec/04:249` was false under the intent-row-first model.** The sentences claiming that no Postgres record is written for an aborted checkpoint and that an orphaned object is unreferenced are replaced with the intent-row reality: an aborted attempt leaves a `partial = true` row the gateway soft-deletes, and the §12.5 backstop reclaims any row that escapes.
- **`docs/reference/adapter-contract.md` was in no edit list.** Its `Checkpoint`, `Resume`, `CheckpointBarrier`, and `CheckpointBarrierAck` rows are added to P1 and C1, so an adapter author is not led to implement the adapter-side flush C1 removes.
- **Tests were added for the readers and paths the change alters:** the workspace-download endpoint, derive, and replay against a multi-chunk prefix; the gateway-driven barrier flush and its BarrierAck-timeout partial finalisation; the latest-2 rotation and the TTL GC deleting the derived chunk key set exactly once under the `deleted_at IS NULL` guard, with the legal-hold exemption; the `artifact_store` row count per checkpoint; the quota reservation; and the counter-reservation ordering.

### Pass 2 (2026-07-13, automated)

- **C2's delete surface asserted an interface the production store does not implement.** `*cataloging.Store`, which `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1090` hands the gateway, does not satisfy `blobstore.Tombstoner`: its `SoftDelete` carries a retention argument (`cataloging.go:240`), it has no `HardDeleteObject`, and its only assertion is `blobstore.Eraser` (`cataloging.go:328`). `blobstore.MemoryStore`, named in the proposal as the non-implementer, is the one type that does implement it (`blobstore.go:602`, `:641`), and `blobstore.TenantScoped` carries no per-key delete at all (`blobstore.go:862-933`). As staged, every chunk delete on a Postgres-backed gateway would have taken the orphan-and-skip branch and left every checkpoint chunk in MinIO with its `artifact_store` row live. C2 now names `cataloging.Store.SoftDelete(u, retention)` as the delete, adds a `TenantScoped.SoftDelete` forwarding method carrying the existing tenant guard, replaces `PartialChunkDeleter.DeleteByPrefix`, and adds a tier-2 assertion that the delete runs against the production store.
- **The `object_key_prefix` rename skipped `spec/10:132`.** The BarrierAck-handler sentence in the finalisation step names the column, and S3 declared that sentence unchanged, so the applied spec would have kept a column name that no longer exists and the proposal's own tier-11 assertion would have failed. `:132` is added to S2's rename list, and S3 drops the sentence's "the adapter otherwise stops uploading" clause with it.
- **The eviction retry budget was re-argued against a figure smaller than the budgets it covers.** S1 cited the 60s gateway preStop drain timeout (`spec/17:966`) as accommodating the combined 30s MinIO and 60s Postgres retry budgets. Clause (b) of `spec/04:264` keeps `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` (240s at Tier 1/2, 300s at Tier 3), reattributed to the gateway pod, which is the figure the adjacent Postgres-fallback paragraph (`spec/04:278`) already argues its own budget against.
- **The tier cap and the eviction retry budget were not reconciled.** The preStop hook wraps each per-session checkpoint in the §10.1 Stage 2 tier cap (`prestop.go:403-405`, `checkpoint_adapter.go:28-30`), which is 30s at the default tier (`prestop.go:88-90`), and the eviction retry budget is 30s total. C5 now names both as ceilings on the same attempt and states which outcome each expiry produces. C7 runs the §4.4 Postgres fallback write on a context detached from the per-session cap, because the two budgets cannot fit inside it.
- **C6 wrote a `workspace_lost = true` record on a path where the workspace survives.** The C5 eviction trigger is the gateway replica's own drain, which does not terminate the agent pod: the session is re-adopted through a coordinator handoff with its workspace intact (`spec/10:178`, `:392`). Routing a failed drain checkpoint into `evictionfallback` would have written a lost-workspace row and published `session.lost` for every session a draining replica coordinates during a transient MinIO blip. C6 therefore keeps the writer off the gateway-drain path; C7 hangs it off the agent-pod eviction trigger, which is the class that does destroy the workspace. The tier-5 assertion expects `resumeMode: "coordinator_handoff"`, the tier-8 assertion expects a fall-back to the last successful checkpoint, and a tier-1 assertion pins that a failed drain checkpoint writes no row and leaves `classifyResume` at `ResumeFull`.
- **The wired eviction-state lookup was presence-only and permanent.** `classifyResume` returns `ResumeConversationOnly` on the bare presence of a `session_eviction_state` row (`start.go:3214-3219`), with no comparison against the session's checkpoints, and nothing clears the row after a later successful checkpoint (`evictionstatestore.Delete` has no non-test caller; `spec/04:282` cleans only at terminal state). `spec/04:264` requires a prior successful full checkpoint to win. Both the precedence rule and a clear-on-success path are built in C7, before `Options.EvictionStateLookup` is assigned.
- **The barrier flush minted two `checkpoint_id`s and committed two intent rows.** C2 had the barrier dispatcher mint and INSERT, and then invoke an upload driver specified to mint and INSERT for every trigger. The second INSERT either collides with `partial_manifest_active_uniq` or supersedes the row the ack correlates against and deletes its chunks. The driver now has an entry point that takes an already-minted id and an already-committed row, the barrier path uses it, and §3, C2, and the staged `spec/10:167` text all state that one id and one row serve an attempt and that no attempt supersedes its own row. A tier-1 assertion pins the single row and the unchanged superseded-manifests counter on a clean drain.
- **The barrier mechanism was anchored on a seam that cannot carry it.** `prestop.BarrierDispatcher` is `Dispatch(ctx) error` (`prestop.go:203-205`) with no session, request, or ack. The mint, the intent-row commit, the `checkpoint_id` on the request, and the ack are all in `pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier` (`barrier.go:82-87`, `:184-210`, `wiring.go:44-61`), which was in no target or files-touched list. C1, C2, and §10 re-anchor on that package plus `sessioncheckpointmeta`, `prestop.fireBarrier` stays the bounded fan-out caller, and §6 names the existing `tests/tier3_contract/adapter_checkpointbarrier/checkpointbarrier_wire_test.go`.
- **BarrierAck-timeout rule 4 claimed the same input state as rule 3.** With the gateway committing the intent row before it dispatches the barrier, a pod that hangs during quiescence always has a row, which is the state S1 reassigned to rule 3. `spec/10:174` is added to S1: its antecedent becomes the residual case where the INSERT never committed and no barrier was dispatched, and its pod-as-uploader rationale is dropped.
- **The abort path double-counted the committed chunk bytes.** A chunk `Put` issues no quota increment (`cataloging.go:143-176`), so the committed bytes sit on the counter as the unreleased part of the reservation, while each chunk's `SoftDelete` releases its own `artifact_size_bytes` (`cataloging.go:240-268`). Firing both the §11.2 release-on-failure rule and the per-chunk delete would decrement the same bytes twice. S2, §3, and C2 now state that an abort releases only `probed_bytes − workspace_bytes_uploaded`, `spec/11:35`'s release sentence is amended for the chunked path, and a tier-2 assertion pins the tenant's counter returning to its pre-checkpoint value.
- **The §11.2 quota gate was bypassable from inside the pod.** The reservation was sized entirely from the adapter's self-reported `probed_bytes`, and the `spec/11:35` hard stream cap was not restated for the checkpoint path. `spec/11:41` refuses to let a pod self-report drive quota accounting, and the workspace-upload path already implements the cap (`upload.go:337`, `:442`). S2 restates the cap for the inbound `Checkpoint` stream, C2's driver wraps the frame stream at the tenant's remaining headroom, and a tier-1 fail-closed assertion covers an adapter that declares one byte and streams past the headroom.
- **`spec/10:155` still mandated `/workspace/current.partial` as the staging path.** That directory does not exist on `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pools (`spec/06:373`, `:392`), which is the correction C1 already carries on the code side. S2 gains an edit restating step (4) in terms of the resolved workspace root, with the `/sessions` tree committing in the same rename step.
- **The §4.7 `CheckpointBarrier` contract row was in no edit list.** `spec/04:656` is the normative source `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md:68` mirrors, and it carries the same "flushes a best-effort checkpoint" mandate the change removes from the adapter. It is added to P1 and to §10.
- **The gateway-side abort sweep had no test, while the three adapter tests covering the abort path are deleted with `runAbortCleanup`.** §6 gains tier-1 assertions that a stream error, a `Put` error, and a failed counter `UPDATE` each delete every derived chunk key and soft-delete the row, that a deadline fire does not take the sweep, and that a failed delete or a store without the delete surface increments `lenny_checkpoint_orphaned_objects_total`. §6 and §10 record the deletion of `TestCheckpointAbortCleanupRunsOnSinkFailure`, `TestCheckpointAbortCleanupBumpsOrphanedCounterOnDeleteFailure`, and `TestCheckpointAbortCleanupSkippedOnSuccess`.

### Pass 3 (2026-07-13, automated)

- **The wired resume classifier was presence-only and would have misclassified every in-flight and crash-abandoned checkpoint.** `sessionserver.PartialManifestLookup` is `HasActivePartialManifest(ctx, tenantID, sessionID) (bool, error)` (`sessionserver.go:672`), and `classifyResume` returns `ResumePartialWorkspace` on that boolean alone (`start.go:3221-3226`). S2 makes the intent row a `partial = true` row committed at the start of every attempt of every trigger, so a session resumed mid-checkpoint, or after a gateway crash mid-upload (`chunk_count = 0`, `deleted_at IS NULL`), would classify `partial_workspace` while the restore driver in fact reads the last successful full checkpoint. C6 now widens the lookup to the `spec/10:155` predicate: selection at `MAX(coordination_generation)` for `(tenant_id, session_id, slot_id)`, a `partial = false` row at or above the partial's generation winning, and the `chunk_count > 0` plus reassembly-threshold gate. The classifier and the C2 restore driver read the same row through the same selector. §3 step 5 and the tier-1 classifier assertion carry the same predicate.
- **C6's "no new interface" claim did not compile.** `partialmanifeststore.Store` carries `Put`, `Get`, `LatestActive`, `SoftDelete`, `ListSoftDeletedBefore`, `HardDelete`, `DeleteByUser`, and `DeleteByTenant` (`partialmanifeststore.go:95-153`) and no `HasActivePartialManifest`, and reusing `LatestActive`'s `deleted_at IS NULL` predicate (which is also `0150`'s index predicate, `migrations/0150_partial_manifest_active_uniq.up.sql:24-26`) would return true for every session that has ever checkpointed once `partial = false` rows share the table. C4 adds `SelectForResume(ctx, tenantID, sessionID, slotID)` to the `Store` interface, `MemoryStore`, and `pgstore`, and C6 drops the claim.
- **The `partial = false` row had no deletion path.** Nothing soft-deleted a full-checkpoint row (the restore is read-only, and rotation and TTL deleted only chunks), and the §12.5 hard-prune predicate for manifest rows is `partial = true AND deleted_at IS NOT NULL AND …` (`spec/12:353`), which structurally excludes the row class the unification creates. Every rotated-out and TTL-expired checkpoint would have left a live row naming chunk keys whose objects were already deleted. S2 adds `spec/12:326` and `:353` to its target list so every checkpoint delete path soft-deletes the manifest row alongside its chunks and the hard-prune becomes reason-agnostic, C2's rotation and TTL change carries the row soft-delete, and the tier-2 rotation assertion pins it.
- **The chunk-delete verb was a hard bucket delete in the spec text and a quota-releasing soft-delete in the code sections.** `cataloging.Store.SoftDelete` tombstones the object, transitions its `artifact_store` row, and releases its bytes (`cataloging.go:240-268`); a raw `DeleteObject` touches neither the row nor the Redis counter, so the abort accounting S2 builds on the soft-delete arm would leak `workspace_bytes_uploaded` per aborted, superseded, rotated, and TTL-expired checkpoint. The staged `spec/04:249` text, the S1 counter paragraph, the S2 GC-backstop bullet, and the abort-sweep tests now name the `ArtifactStore` soft-delete, and S2 gains an edit replacing the retained hard-delete sentences at `spec/10:137`, `spec/10:157`, and `spec/04:237`. The idempotency argument survives against `miniostore.SoftDelete`'s missing-object no-op (`miniostore.go:788-793`).
- **`chunk_encoding` was committed on the intent row before the actor that reported it had spoken.** The gateway INSERTs the row (and derives keys from its suffix) before it opens the `Checkpoint` stream, while `CheckpointStart` carried the encoding the adapter chose, and `spec/10:139` mandates that the encoding "is chosen once at intent-row INSERT time". P1 removes the field from `CheckpointStart` and puts `chunk_encoding` on `CheckpointRequest`: the gateway selects it (constant `tar.gz`, the only encoding `workspace.ArchiveTree` produces, `pkg/adapter/workspace/tree.go:45-57`) and the adapter must stream in it or fail. The obligation is stated in the RPC doc comment and in `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md`, and a tier-3 assertion pins the row's suffix against the streamed bytes.
- **"Clean stream EOF" admitted a truncated archive as a full checkpoint.** The staged `spec/10:132` finalisation keyed on EOF alone, so an adapter that ends its server stream with an OK status after a truncated archive would get a `partial = false` row over an incomplete chunk set, which is then the session's full checkpoint and the fallback target of every other branch. The terminal `CheckpointSummary` frame becomes the completion gate in §2, §3, S2, S3, and C2: `partial = false` requires the summary inside the deadline and `workspace_bytes_uploaded == CheckpointSummary.size_bytes`, and a stream without a summary or with a disagreeing count finalises `partial = true` with `manifest_reason = 'timeout'`. The tier-1 finalisation assertion is rewritten around the summary.
- **The deadline that cut the checkpoint also cancelled the context its bookkeeping runs on.** `run` and `CheckpointFnFor` both wrap the attempt in `context.WithTimeout` (`prestop.go:403-405`, `checkpoint_adapter.go:28-30`), so the finalising UPDATE, the `chunk_count == 0` soft-delete, the abort sweep, and the quota release would every one fail with `context.DeadlineExceeded` on the dominant drain path, leaving the row unfinalised, the chunks undeleted, and up to one `probed_bytes` per drained session leaked onto the tenant's counter until a Redis restart rehydrates it (`recovery.go:29-40`). C5, C2, §3 step 4, and the staged `spec/10:132` text now state that the tier cap and the retry budget bound the archive stream and the chunk `Put`s alone and that the bookkeeping runs on a `context.WithoutCancel` derivation under its own budget, on the barrier path too (`prestop.go:475`). - **No metric represented a failed gateway-drain eviction checkpoint.** `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` is defined as the non-eviction counter (`spec/04:263`, `spec/16:200`) and `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_fallback_total` means a Postgres fallback actually occurred, which the gateway-drain path never does, so the class had no signal and the tier-8 test asserted a counter the spec excluded it from. The counter already carries a `trigger` label, so S1 widens it to every trigger rather than adding a metric, and corrects the `CheckpointStorageUnavailable` description, which today tells the on-call that a Postgres minimal-state fallback was attempted and that resumed sessions are `conversation_only`. The alert's name, severity, expression, and runbook target are unchanged.
- **`spec/07:212` still addressed a checkpoint as one object key.** It sets a terminal session's `final_workspace_ref` to "this checkpoint's object key" and `:222` repeats it, two lines from the sentence S4 rewrites, so the applied spec would carry the chunk-set model and the deleted single-object model in one numbered list. Both are added to P1 and to §10 and restated as the checkpoint's `object_key_prefix`. The tier-11 consistency assertion is extended to grep `spec/` for single-object checkpoint addressing.

The §2 decision, S2's target list, and the `// spec:` tags on the two quota tests now all name §11.2.

The primary path closes the same window without the backstop: the superseded writer's next counter `UPDATE` affects zero rows, which aborts the attempt before that index's `PutObject` and takes the abort sweep.
- **The §12.5 backstop's terminal-session arm selected the live intent row of the seal checkpoint.** `Seal` runs after the session row is terminal (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:395` → `checkpointer.go:210`), and S2 makes the `partial = true` intent row the first durable act of every attempt, so from the seal's INSERT until its finalising `UPDATE` the row satisfies the backstop's entry condition (a) with no liveness discriminator. A leader-elected GC cycle landing in that window would delete the chunks and soft-delete the row, and the seal's finalising `UPDATE` would no-op under its own guard, destroying the artifact `spec/07:212` designates as the terminal session's `final_workspace_ref`. The same race hits any periodic checkpoint in flight when its session terminates. S2 now populates `checkpoint_timeout_at` on every intent row (the §4.4 60-second checkpoint timeout for the deadline-less triggers, `spec/04:245`, reversing the earlier NULL rule) and adds `checkpoint_timeout_at < now() - gc.cycleIntervalSeconds` (the existing GC-cycle knob, rather than a new one) to the terminal-session arm; C4's column becomes `NOT NULL` and its backstop index carries the term; a tier-2 assertion pins that a cycle against an in-flight seal deletes nothing and that the seal still finalises `partial = false`.
- **`spec/10:141` still defined `chunk_count` as the count of committed chunks.** S1 rewrites the ordering at `:131` so the counter `UPDATE` for index `n` commits before the `PutObject` for index `n`, which makes `chunk_count` an upper bound, but every enumerated edit to the schema paragraph touched other clauses, so the applied spec would have asserted the superset invariant at `:131` and the exact-committed-count invariant at `:141` about the same column. An implementer following `:141` literally would advance the counter after each `Put` and reintroduce the committed-but-uncounted chunk the change calls unreachable. S1 now restates the `chunk_count` column as the reserved chunk-index count, keeps the `chunk_count = 0` initial-state sentence, and the tier-11 consistency assertion greps `spec/` for any line defining the column as the committed count.
- **The resume classifier read the manifest row after the restore had already destroyed it.** `handleResume` runs `resumeOnPod` (`start.go:2710`, reaching the restore driver's own partial cleanup) and `PartialCleaner.CleanupAfterResume` (`:2786`, which soft-deletes the row at `partialcleanup.go:100`) before `classifyResumeWithAdapter` (`:2802`) reaches `classifyResume` and its lookup (`:3221`). Any selector scoped to `deleted_at IS NULL` therefore cannot see the row the resume just reassembled, so `ResumePartialWorkspace` would be unreachable on every production resume and every partial recovery would emit `resumeMode: "full"` with no `workspaceRecoveryFraction`. C2's restore driver now reports the mode it actually took on `podsession.ResumeResult.RestoreMode`, `classifyResumeWithAdapter` classifies from that report, and C6 deletes `PartialManifestLookup`, its `classifyResume` arm, and its `Options` field rather than widening them: the §10.1 predicate lives once, in the driver that acts on it. A tier-1 assertion drives the outcome through the wired `handleResume` with both cleanups running.
- **The restore driver was reachable only through a session column a partial finalisation never writes.** `resumeOnPod` gates the restore path on `row.WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref != ""` (`start.go:3084`), whose only writer is `checkpointer.snapshot` (`checkpointer.go:259-266`), which C2 forbids on the `partial = true` arm. A session whose first-ever checkpoint truncated therefore has no ref, takes the snapshotless rebuild branch, and never reaches the restore driver, so the NULL-baseline recovery arm that `spec/10:141` and `:155` define and that C6 and two tests assert could never restore. C2 now gates the restore path on `SelectForResume` returning a restorable row and passes that row's `checkpoint_id` on `podsession.ResumeRequest` instead of `row.WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref`; `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go` (`resumeOnPod`, `:3084-3131`) is added to C2's target list and to §10, and a tier-1 assertion pins that a truncated first checkpoint reaches the driver rather than the rebuild path.
- **The `CheckpointStorageUnavailable` summary stayed scoped to eviction while the counter it fires on was widened to every trigger.** S1 corrected only the `Description` (`pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:353`), leaving `Summary: "Checkpoint upload to MinIO failed during eviction"` (`:352`) as the first line on-call sees, so a failed periodic or seal checkpoint upload would page with an eviction headline over a trigger-agnostic body. S1 now corrects both fields to a trigger-agnostic form, drops "during eviction" from the `spec/16:417` catalog row, names the rendered mirrors that `make generate` re-renders, and the tier-11 assertion checks the summary as well as the description. The alert's name, severity, expression, and runbook target are unchanged.

### Pass 6 (2026-07-13, automated)

- **The resume entry condition routed a non-restorable partial row to the snapshotless rebuild, discarding a live full checkpoint.** S2 makes the `partial = true` intent row the first durable act of every attempt of every trigger, and `coordination_generation` advances only on coordinator handoff (`spec/04:157`), so an in-flight or crash-abandoned attempt leaves an active zero-chunk row strictly newer than the session's last successful full row. `SelectForResume` returned that row, and C2's two-way branch sent it to the snapshotless rebuild (`startOnPod` with the stored `WorkspacePlan`, `start.go:3084-3097`), so the session came back with an empty workspace while a restorable full checkpoint sat active in the table, which is strictly less reliable than the shipped `row.WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref` gate. The fallback target that §3, C6, the §6 tests, and the retained `spec/10:155` all name had no selector and no caller. `SelectForResume` now applies the §10.1 restorability gates itself and falls through to the newest active `partial = false` row, returning `ErrNotFound` only when neither class exists; C4 adds `SelectLatestFull` for the restore driver's contiguity and reassembly-failure arms; §3 step 5, C2's entry condition and restore driver, and C6's `full` arms all state that "reports `full`" means the last successful full checkpoint was restored; and the tier-1 assertions pin that a session holding a live full checkpoint plus a strictly newer zero-chunk or below-threshold partial resumes with the full checkpoint's bytes.
- **`spec/10:141` kept stating the GC backstop's predicate as `partial = true AND deleted_at IS NULL`, which S2 widens everywhere else.** The sentence "Cleanup predicates remain reason-agnostic — both the primary path and the backstop sweep key on `partial = true AND deleted_at IS NULL`" is a fourth normative statement of the predicate, and no staged edit touched it, so the applied spec would have carried the widened predicate at `spec/12:328`, `spec/12:349`, and `spec/10:133` and the narrow one at `:141`. An implementer following `:141` builds the narrow sweep, and the chunk a superseded writer commits after its row is soft-deleted stays on the tenant's `storage_bytes_used` permanently once the hard-prune removes the row. S2 now anchors that sentence explicitly, restates the `partial: true` field entry as the boolean column of the unified table, and the tier-11 assertion checks every `spec/` statement of the backstop predicate.
- **The partial-workspace resume emitted no `workspaceRecoveryFraction`.** `spec/07:138` makes the fraction mandatory on `resumeMode: "partial_workspace"`, and this change is the first to make that mode reachable, but C6 plumbed only `RestoreMode`: `emitResumedEvent` takes a mode and nothing else (`start.go:3247-3256`), and the field's only writer today is the synthesized coordinator-handoff frame (`start.go:3264-3269`). Both of the fraction's inputs are unavailable at classification time by C6's own argument, since the denominator lives on a manifest row both cleanup paths soft-delete and the numerator exists only in the adapter's `ResumeResponse.restored_bytes`. `podsession.ResumeResult` now carries `RecoveryFraction *float64` alongside `RestoreMode`, the driver computes it from `restored_bytes` over the row's frozen `baseline_full_checkpoint_bytes` (nil when the baseline is NULL, per `spec/10:155`), `emitResumedEvent` widens to populate `resumedEventPayload.WorkspaceRecoveryFraction`, and the emitter is added to C6's target list, to §10, and to the tier-1 assertion.
- **The driver-owned chunk retry ladder was not idempotent against the write-once store.** Chunk keys are deterministic, `blobstore.Store.Put` returns `ErrConflict` on overwrite (`blobstore.go:307-312`), and `miniostore.Store.Put` enforces it with a `StatObject` precheck that runs before any retry logic (`miniostore.go:277-283`). The shipped `putRetryBackoffs` ladder is idempotent only because it replays `PutObject` inside one call without re-running the `Stat` (`miniostore.go:338-340`), and C2 bypasses that ladder. A `Put` whose body was fully sent but whose response was lost, or one cut by the per-attempt deadline (which C2 itself concedes can land the object), would make the next retry of that index return `ErrConflict`, abort the attempt, and sweep every committed chunk, so a transient MinIO blip on the drain path became a permanently lost checkpoint. C2 now states that `PutContext` on the checkpoint path treats an `ErrConflict` on the chunk's own key as success when `Stat` reports the byte length being written and errors only on a length mismatch, S2's implementer note is reconciled with in-attempt retries, and a tier-1 assertion covers a first attempt that commits server-side but is observed as a timeout.
- **Making the intent-row INSERT a hard precondition of barrier dispatch destroyed the quiescence barrier under a Postgres outage.** `spec/10:165` designs the barrier to fire on a degraded target set precisely because a transient failover or PgBouncer outage can overlap a drain, and `spec/10:180` rests the interruption-window bound on that barrier firing. With the INSERT and the same-transaction `coordination_lease` read as preconditions of `Send`, such an outage would suppress the barrier for every coordinated pod, leaving tool calls dispatching into a replica seconds from SIGKILL. `spec/10:165` is added to S1's target list, and S1's staged `:167` text, C2's `dispatchOne`, and §3 step 1 now carry a degraded arm: the barrier is dispatched with an empty `checkpoint_id`, the pod quiesces and acks with an empty `checkpoint_ref`, no flush is driven, the session falls back to its last successful checkpoint (the rule-4 outcome), and the dispatch is counted on the existing `lenny_prestop_barrier_target_source_total{source="cache_fallback"}` counter. A tier-1 assertion pins that every pod still quiesces with Postgres unreachable.
- **The spec's derive-copy passages still copied a checkpoint as one MinIO object.** P1 rewrites derive into a chunk-set copy plus a manifest-row insert, but the four passages that define the copy itself were in no edit list: `spec/07:81` ("reads the parent's MinIO object and writes a new independent object"), `spec/07:92` (the derive-lock immutability argument and the "any partially written destination object" cleanup rule), `spec/12:331`, and `spec/12:938`. An implementer following them copies one object and leaves the derived prefix with no manifest row, which P1 itself calls unenumerable and which breaks `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/derive` and the derived session's `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/workspace`. All four are added to P1's target list and to §10 and restated on the (prefix + manifest row) pair, the tier-4 derive test asserts the derived manifest row and the derived session's workspace download, and the tier-11 grep now covers single-object checkpoint copies as well as single-object checkpoint addressing.

### Pass 7 (2026-07-13, automated)

- **The §4.5 derive-copy passage was a fifth single-object copy site and was in no edit list.** `spec/04:312` ("Workspace lineage") has the gateway copy "the parent's workspace snapshot bytes into a new MinIO object scoped to the derived session's own path", which is the same single-object copy as the four passages P1 already restates, and its opening clause identifies a snapshot by "a content-addressed hash (SHA-256 of the tar archive)". Leaving it would put the chunk-set model at `spec/07:81` and `spec/12:331` and the single-object model at `spec/04:312` in one applied spec, and would fail the proposal's own tier-11 assertion that no `spec/` line describes a checkpoint copy as a single-object copy. `spec/04:312` is added to P1's target list and to §10, restated as the chunk-set copy plus the derived session's `partial = false` manifest row, with the checkpoint's identity restated on its `checkpoint_id`.
- **The §12.5 checkpoint-storage sizing guidance still counted one MinIO object per retained checkpoint.** `spec/12:336` frames the per-pod object count as depending on the execution mode alone and `spec/12:338` states that a pod with `maxConcurrentSessions: 8` "carries up to 16 checkpoint objects steady-state", which the chunked-object unification falsifies by up to 32x at the 512 MiB `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` with the 16 MiB `chunkSizeBytes` default. Both lines are added to S2's target list and to §10 and restated as counts of retained checkpoints, each stored as `ceil(workspace_bytes / chunkSizeBytes)` objects. The byte-based aggregate ceiling and the legal-hold bullet need no change.
- **The BarrierAck-floor CRD rule still attributed the 90s upload to the pod and defined `outcome="timeout"` as a slow pod upload.** `spec/10:124` argues the floor from a pod that "legitimately needs 90s to upload a 512 MB workspace" and tells operators that `lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_total{outcome="timeout"}` distinguishes "legitimate slow uploads from unresponsive pods", which contradicts the staged `:169`, `:177`, and `spec/16:41`, where the ack signals quiescence alone and a cut flush is counted on `lenny_checkpoint_partial_total{trigger="barrier_ack_timeout"}`. The tier-11 assertion did not catch it because it is verb-scoped and `:124` names no verb. `:124` is added to S1's target list and to §10 with its rationale re-argued on the gateway-driven flush and its observability sentence restated, the floor rule and the rejection message unchanged, and the tier-11 assertion is extended to cover verb-less upload attributions and the three statements of the ack counter's `timeout` outcome.
- **The barrier path's supersede ran outside the upload driver's single-flight lock.** C2 put the mint, the supersede, and the intent-row INSERT in `barrier.Coordinator.dispatchOne` (`barrier.go:184`), which holds no lock, while the only cross-actor guard was the generation fence, which admits equality. The periodic ticker is an independent goroutine that nothing pauses on drain (`checkpointer.go:122`, `:173`) and `Hook.run` fires the barrier as its first drain act (`prestop.go:354`), so a drain-time barrier would soft-delete the live row and chunk-delete a periodic upload of the same session still running on the same replica, and the victim's finalising `UPDATE` would no-op silently. The driver now exposes the mint, supersede, and INSERT as a reservation entry point that takes the same per-`(session_id, slot_id)` lock and preempts the in-flight attempt (cancelling it and waiting for it to settle) before it mints; `dispatchOne` calls it rather than writing the row itself, and a lock it cannot take inside its slice of the deadline falls into the existing degraded arm so quiescence still fires. The supersede predicate also gains the `checkpoint_timeout_at < now()` liveness term S2 already introduces for the backstop, so a row whose attempt is still inside its own deadline is never superseded. §2, §3 step 1, S2, and C2 all state that same-generation attempts are ordered by the lock rather than by the fence, and the tier-1 race test gains the barrier-dispatch racer.
- **The finalising UPDATE and the session-record stamp were unfenced against a supersede by a newer coordinator.** The generation guard admits the legitimate direction, where a newer coordinator supersedes the still-running upload of the old one and deletes its chunks (`spec/10:137`, `:165`). If the old writer's last chunk has already committed there is no further counter `UPDATE` to observe `rows_affected == 0`, so the proposal's only stated closure never fired: the writer's finalising `UPDATE` no-opped and `checkpointer.snapshot` proceeded to stamp `WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref`, `LastSuccessfulCheckpointAt`, and `last_checkpoint_workspace_bytes` (`checkpointer.go:253-266`, `:277`) and to insert a retention row (`:288`) naming a chunk set that no longer exists. The staged §10.1 finalisation text, S2's residual-window paragraph, §3 step 4, and C2 now state that the finalising `UPDATE` carries the `checkpoint_id = $id AND deleted_at IS NULL` guard and that a zero-row result is an abort: no `partial = false` row, no session-record stamp, no retention INSERT, reservation remainder released, abort sweep taken. A tier-2 assertion covers the mirror image of the existing split-brain test.
- **The §12.5 backstop sweep was load-bearing for three orphan invariants and three tests, and nothing built it.** C4 added `ListSweepable` with no caller, and the repository has no sweep over `partial = true` rows: the only partial-manifest worker is the leader-gated tombstone hard-prune (`cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go:803-841`, `hardPrunePartialManifests` at `main.go:1072`), which issues no object-store call and destroys the only name a chunk has. C4 now builds the sweep as a second leader-gated entry beside the hard-prune on the same `gc.cycleIntervalSeconds` ticker: it calls `ListSweepable`, joins each row against session state for the two §12.5 entry conditions, soft-deletes every derived chunk key through the tenant-scoped `ArtifactStore`, and soft-deletes the row under the `deleted_at IS NULL` guard. `cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go:803-841` is added to C4's target list and to §10, so the three §6 backstop assertions have a target.
- **The tier-2 abort-accounting assertion was unsatisfiable with the object store unreachable.** Committed chunk bytes leave the tenant's counter only through the per-chunk `SoftDelete`, and that call cannot run against an unreachable store: `cataloging.Store.SoftDelete` returns the inner tombstone's error before it reads the catalog row or releases the bytes (`cataloging.go:230-236`), and `miniostore.Store.SoftDelete` fails on its `StatObject` precheck rather than taking the missing-object no-op (`miniostore.go:785-793`). The proposal's own rule routes that outcome to the orphan branch, so the counter cannot return to its pre-checkpoint value and the only way to pass the assertion is the double decrement S2 forbids. The assertion is split: with the store reachable the counter returns exactly to its pre-checkpoint value, and with the store unreachable the remainder is released, the committed bytes stay on the counter, `lenny_checkpoint_orphaned_objects_total` increments, and the bytes are reclaimed by the sweep that owns the chunks.
- **`spec/16:418` still told operators that `CheckpointStorageUnavailable` is the eviction-fallback alert.** The `MinIOUnavailable` row's cross-reference reads "the checkpoint-path eviction fallback has its own alert — `CheckpointStorageUnavailable`", which is false on every path this change builds once S1 widens the counter to every trigger and C6 establishes that the gateway-drain eviction performs no Postgres fallback. Leaving it would also fail the proposal's own tier-11 assertion that no `spec/16` catalog row scopes the alert to eviction. `:418` is added to S1's target list and to §10, reworded to name the alert by what it now covers.

### Pass 8 (2026-07-13, automated)

- **The single-flight preemption and the supersede guard's liveness term could not both hold.** `checkpoint_timeout_at` is written once at intent-row INSERT and no settlement rewrites it, so a periodic checkpoint preempted seconds into its 60-second window, or one whose archive stream truncated early, settles as an active `partial = true` row whose recorded deadline is still in the future. A supersede term keyed on `checkpoint_timeout_at < now()` matches no such row, so the drain-time barrier flush and the C5 eviction checkpoint would abort on the guard and their intent-row INSERT would collide with `partial_manifest_active_uniq`, and `Seal` would wedge for up to the previous attempt's deadline. The term becomes `(manifest_reason IS NOT NULL OR checkpoint_timeout_at < now())`, using the discriminator C4 already carries: `manifest_reason` is NULL on an intent row and non-NULL on a finalised partial, so the first arm admits every settled row and the second admits every abandoned one, while a row that is running (no reason, deadline not elapsed) still aborts the attempt that would supersede it. §2, §3 step 1, S2's supersede bullet and the staged `spec/10:137` predicate, C2's upload driver and barrier reservation, and §10 carry the same predicate, and the tier-1 race test now asserts that the barrier's reservation commits and finalises after preempting the periodic attempt, that a checkpoint following an early-truncated stream does the same, and separately that a row whose attempt is still running aborts its superseder.
- **GC concurrency rule 3 (`spec/12:346`) kept the raw `DeleteObject` as the rotation and TTL delete verb.** It rests the stale-leader convergence argument on the MinIO `DeleteObject` API, states the terminal state as "the object is absent in MinIO", and adds that "no object tagging is required on the MinIO side", all of which the `ArtifactStore` soft-delete falsifies: the object is tombstoned with a `PutObjectTagging` call (`pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:785-804`) and is absent only after the tombstone hard-prune. An implementer following rule 3 would hard-delete a rotated-out or TTL-expired chunk and bypass the `artifact_store` row and the Redis decrement, which is the leak S2's accounting rule exists to prevent, and the proposal's own tier-11 grep would fail on the line. `:346` is added to S2's target list and to §10 and restated on the soft-delete, whose missing-object no-op (`miniostore.go:788-793`) carries the same idempotency argument.
- **The §12.5 latest-2 retention rule (`spec/12:325`) carried a third statement of the falsified object count.** Pass 7 corrected `:336` and `:338` but missed the normative retention bullet two lines above them, which states that a pod with `maxConcurrentSessions: 8` "may retain up to 16 checkpoint objects (8 slots × 2)". The applied spec would have stated two different object counts for one pod configuration, and an operator sizing MinIO object counts and `storageQuotaBytes` reads the retention rule first and under-counts by up to 32×. `:325` is added to S2's target list and to §10 and restated as 16 retained checkpoints of `chunk_count` objects each, and the tier-11 assertion is extended to catch any `spec/` line equating one retained checkpoint with one MinIO object.
- **The §10.1 resume-time Cleanup rule (`spec/10:157`) was never scoped to `partial = true` rows.** Its opening sentence is unconditional ("Regardless of reassembly outcome, the coordinator deletes every chunk under `partial_object_key_prefix` … then soft-deletes the Postgres manifest row"), and today the column name is the scope. After the rename to `object_key_prefix`, which the unified table carries on every row, the sentence mandates deleting a full checkpoint's chunks and soft-deleting its row on every restore, which C2 forbids and which S4's parallel edit to `spec/04:237` forbids, so an implementer following §10.1 would erase the session's full checkpoint and the `spec/10:155` fallback target on its first restore. S2 gains an edit scoping the paragraph to `partial = true` rows and stating that a `partial = false` row is read-only on restore, and the tier-11 assertion now requires that no `spec/` line mandates chunk deletion after a `partial = false` restore.
- **BarrierAck rule 5 (`spec/10:175`) was frozen while stating the narrow backstop predicate.** S1 declared it unchanged and it states the §12.5 backstop sweep as `partial = true AND deleted_at IS NULL` alone, which S2 widens at four other sites, so the applied spec would have carried four widened statements and one narrow one and would have failed the proposal's own tier-11 predicate assertion. `:175` is removed from S1's unchanged list, added to S2's target list and to §10, reworded on the widened predicate, and added to the tier-11 enumeration.
- **The §15.7 runtime-SDK contract still gave the pod checkpoint-upload helpers.** `spec/15:2531` offers the in-pod SDK "helpers for … uploading checkpoints", so the applied spec would have said the pod issues no object-store call (`spec/10:131`, `spec/04:489`) and that the pod-side SDK uploads checkpoints, and the tier-11 upload-attribution assertion would have failed on it, because that assertion was verb-scoped and §15.7 names no verb. `spec/15_external-api-surface.md:2531` is added to S1's target list and to §10, restated so the SDK provides workspace materialization helpers alone, and the tier-11 assertion's scope now covers §15.7.
- **The `DERIVE_SNAPSHOT_UNAVAILABLE` catalog still addressed the checkpoint as one object.** `spec/15:1056` defines the code as a missing snapshot *object* and defines `details.snapshotRef` as "the missing object path", and `docs/reference/error-catalog.md:150` mirrors it, while P1 makes the ref a prefix that names no object and makes the missing artifact one chunk. Both are the surface clients branch on and both were in no edit list. They are added to P1's target list and to §10 and restated on the chunk-set model, with P1 stating that the emitter (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go:343`) keeps putting the parent checkpoint's `object_key_prefix` in `details.snapshotRef`.
- **The minio-failure runbook promised a Postgres checkpoint fallback in its opening summary.** `docs/runbooks/minio-failure.md:31` is the first line an operator paged by `CheckpointStorageUnavailable` reads (the alert's `runbook_url` resolves to this page, `pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:349-354`), and it tells on-call that "checkpoint storage falls back to Postgres" for exactly the failures the widened alert now surfaces, contradicting the corrected `:106` bullet in the same file and failing the proposal's own tier-11 runbook assertion. S1 staged only `:106`; `:31` is added to S1's target list and to §10 and corrected in the same terms.

C6's target list, its body, and §10 named a `cmd/` removal that does not exist and omitted the three `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go` sites that must be deleted for the package to compile (`:273`, `:1258-1262`, `:1671`). All four removal sites are now named in `sessionserver.go`, and the `cmd/lenny-gateway` entry records that no edit is needed there.

### Pass 9 (2026-07-13, operator decisions and the exhaustive blast-radius fold-in)

The review loop had been finding one or two uncovered edit sites per round, which is a convergence failure rather than a series of discoveries: the proposal was being reviewed against a partial blast radius. This pass folds an exhaustive sweep of `spec/`, `docs/`, `schemas/`, `sdks/`, `charts/`, and `TESTING.md` into the staged changes in one shot, and resolves the three open decisions. §11 records the sweep's bucket-to-change mapping and the three sites deliberately left out.

- **The agent-pod eviction trigger ships (was D2).** The operator selected the pod-to-gateway termination signal over the gateway-side pod informer. The informer is rejected on the record in §2 and §5: it needs a per-replica ownership and dedup design with no existing informer machinery in `pkg/gateway` to build on, and it races the kubelet's grace period rather than holding the pod open. (Pass 14 removed a third ground, new pod RBAC, which is false: the gateway `ClusterRole` already carries `pods: get, list, watch, patch` cluster-wide.) S3 stages the spec, P1 adds `GatewayControl.NotifyPodTerminating`, and C7 builds the pod side, the gateway handler, the `evictionfallback.Writer` call site on a detached context, the `spec/04:264` resume precedence rule, and the `session_eviction_state` supersede. T-4.4.14, T-4.6.4, and the tier-5 half of T-JRN.9 close, and three tier-5 `t.Skip`s lift.
- **The grace-period arithmetic did not close and now does.** `spec/04:489` sets the agent pod's `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` default at 120s while `spec/04:264` clause (b) and `spec/04:278` justify a 30s MinIO retry budget and a 60s Postgres fallback budget against 240s and 300s, which are the **gateway** pod's figures. Against 120s, a single slot at the 90s tier cap (which `spec/10:108` **requires** for a session with no prior checkpoint) plus the 60s fallback plus the 10s preStop drain margin needs 160s. S3 states a webhook rule with a per-slot `postgresFallbackBudgetSeconds` term, promotes `spec/05:542`'s advisory `max(cap, 60)` sentence into the formula, and raises the default to 180s. A tier-5 assertion pins that the pod is not SIGKILLed mid-upload. (Pass 10 corrects the rule's per-slot checkpoint term to `max(max_tiered_checkpoint_cap, 90)`, drops its `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` term, and reconciles it with the second statement of the same rule at `spec/10:116-122`; the formula as it ships is stated in S3.)
- **The retention catalog stays separate (was D1) and `manifest_reason` stays single-valued (was D3).** Both are recorded in §2 and §5. §9 is now empty.
- **Bucket E is the security argument and it is now stated explicitly.** The sweep confirms that `spec/13`'s NetworkPolicies and the rendered chart already forbid agent-pod egress to MinIO, that MinIO's ingress list already excludes agent pods, and that no §4.9 lease provider issues an object-store credential. §2 records that none of those sites changes, and the tier-11 assertion fails the build if any of them does. A reviewer reading only §13 would conclude nothing is broken today; the contradiction this change removes lives in §4, §10, and §12, which assign the pod calls §13 forbids.
- **Bucket H's metric owners, subjects, and cardinalities are staged.** Nine `spec/16` inventory rows change emitter or subject, and each carries its `docs/reference/metrics.md` mirror and the runbook page its alert routes to. Two pre-existing label defects surface and are corrected rather than inherited: `spec/12:315` documents a `reason` label `spec/16:200` does not declare, and `spec/16:274` and `spec/04:287` declare two different label sets for `lenny_session_eviction_total_loss_total`, a counter C7 gives its first emitter. `lenny_checkpoint_orphaned_objects_total` gains a per-key increment rule, without which a partially failed abort sweep would understate the orphan count by up to `chunk_count`.
- **The published checkpoint-metric table attributes no emitter.** `docs/reference/metrics.md`'s `## Checkpoint metrics` section (`:181-195`) lists eleven counters with no emitter and no scrape-target note, so a reader cannot tell that the counters `spec/04:263` and `:264` assign to the adapter are unscrapable in the shipped topology. Moving the emitters to the gateway settles it, and S1 states the attribution in that section. (Pass 11 corrects this bullet: the adapter-emitted-and-unscraped paragraph at `:172` scopes the separate `## Adapter metrics` section and its two SO_PEERCRED rows, names no checkpoint counter, and is accurate as written, so it is confirmed unchanged rather than rewritten.)
- **The chunked model's capacity consequences were unstated.** `spec/17`'s MinIO sizing is a bandwidth budget with a checkpoint *write rate*; chunking multiplies the `PutObject` **request** rate by `ceil(workspace_bytes / chunkSizeBytes)` and gateway-mediated I/O funnels every byte through a gateway replica, with the drain burst landing on one. S2 adds a request-rate row, a per-replica relay budget, a drain-burst bound, and a `checkpointWorker.concurrency` knob. `spec/12:292`'s delete-marker lifecycle, sized for one marker per checkpoint, is re-derived for `chunk_count` markers.
- **Three checkpoint knobs were spec-only.** `partialChunkSizeBytes` and `partialRecoveryThresholdFraction` are declared at `spec/17:919-920` and have no Helm value, no schema entry, and no gateway flag. S2 gives them, and the new `checkpointWorker.concurrency`, a real configuration surface, and the tier-11 assertion fails if a checkpoint knob exists only in the spec.

### Pass 10 (2026-07-14, automated)

- **The new grace-period budget rule contradicted a second, normative statement of the same webhook rule that was declared unchanged.** `spec/10:116-122` states the same `SandboxWarmPool` CRD rule against the same field (`max_tiered_checkpoint_cap + checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds + 30 > terminationGracePeriodSeconds`, a 210s floor at defaults), it is the line the shipped validator cites and implements (`pkg/admission/pool_config_validator/validator.go:437`, `:636`), and `spec/10:167` and `:177` restate it. S3 replaced only the §5.2 statement and §10 declared `spec/10:100-124` unchanged, so the applied spec would carry two mutually exclusive formulas for one webhook on one field and the new 180s default would be rejected by a rule the proposal never touched. S3 now edits `spec/10:116-122`, `:167`, and `:177`, and the two budgets are separated by the workload they bound: the webhook rule is the **agent** pod's, and the 210s arithmetic is the **gateway** pod's own drain budget, which its 240s Helm value covers and which no webhook enforces because the gateway pod carries no `SandboxTemplate`.
- **The budget rule under-budgeted every small-workspace pool.** `max_tiered_checkpoint_cap` is derived from `spec.workspaceSizeLimitBytes` (30s at ≤ 100 MB, `validator.go:549-566`), but the deadline the gateway actually applies is the 90s maximum tier for any session with no prior checkpoint (`spec/10:108`, `prestop.MaxTierBudget` at `prestop.go:93-96`, `:378-384`), which is every session's first checkpoint. The staged floor admitted a 100 MB pool at `1 × (30 + 60) + 10 = 100s` against a real worst case of 160s, and the deleted `max(cap, 60)` hedge no longer covered it because the `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` term that absorbed it is gone. The per-slot term becomes `max(max_tiered_checkpoint_cap, 90)`, stated identically in S3, C7, `spec/05:542`, the CRD field docs, and the webhook, with a tier-1 assertion on a `workspaceSizeLimitBytes: 100Mi` pool.
- **`checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds`'s CRD field doc asserted webhook behavior the change removes, and was declared unchanged.** The field doc tells deployers that "the pool-config webhook adds it to the per-pod `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` floor", which is what `validator.go:636` does and what the new rule stops doing. S3 now edits it, strips that clause, keeps the BarrierAck-floor rule it still governs, and states why the term leaves the agent-pod floor: it is a gateway-drain deadline that consumes none of the agent pod's grace period.
- **The CRD edits targeted a generated file and omitted the embedded mirror.** `charts/lenny/crds/*.yaml` is rendered by `make generate` from the Go doc comments (`Makefile:78`), so a YAML-only edit is reverted on the next generate, and `pkg/embedded/crds/lenny.dev_sandboxtemplates.yaml` must stay byte-identical to the chart copy (`pkg/embedded/crds/crds_test.go:80`). S3 retargets the field-doc edits at `pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1/sandboxtemplate_types.go` and §10 records the chart CRD as regenerated and the embedded copy as re-copied.
- **S3 staged an edit to a `charts/lenny/values.yaml` helper comment that does not exist.** The file's only `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` key is the gateway Deployment's (`:2402-2408`), no chart template renders an agent-pod grace period, and `spec/05:542`'s pointer at "a helper formula in `values.yaml` comments" is already false. The target is dropped and `spec/05:542`'s pointer is retargeted at the `SandboxTemplate` field docs, where the formula is published.
- **`spec/17:976-979`'s node-drain formula is gateway-scoped and must not follow the agent pod's new default.** It is tied to the gateway Deployment's `maxUnavailable: 1` PDB (`spec/17:973`) and evaluated at the gateway's Tier-3 300s (`:979`). S3 listed it as a target whose "figures follow the new default", which would publish a wrong operator-facing drain estimate. It is now confirmed unchanged, on gateway-pod scope.
- **`NotifyPodTerminating` landed on an arbitrary replica and the "forwards" arm named a seam that does not exist.** The adapter dials the load-balanced `lenny-gateway` Service with no session affinity against a two-replica default, while `podsession.Registry` is process-local, `Checkpointer.snapshot` returns `ErrNoBinding` without a local binding, and the pod's generation fence rejects a non-coordinator. No gateway-peer RPC exists anywhere in `schemas/`, `pkg/gateway`, or `spec/10`, and the alternative arm ("skips") made the trigger a silent no-op for most terminating pods in any multi-replica deployment, which a single-replica kind test would not catch. C7's handler now resolves the pod's sessions from `sessions.pod_assignment` and takes over coordination of each through the §10.1 handoff before checkpointing, which needs no new RPC, no new RBAC, and no new network edge; the interaction with the S2 supersede guard is stated (the generation bump fences the old coordinator's in-flight upload, which aborts on its next zero-row `UPDATE`); a slot whose handoff fails or whose budget elapses takes the minimal-state fallback; and a tier-4 assertion drives the notification into a non-coordinating replica. The gateway-driven eviction `Checkpoint` is a cooperative quiesce for any Full-level runtime (`pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:203-206`, `:409-411`), so it would fail with `codes.Internal` on exactly the path the trigger exists for and drop every such session to the minimal-state fallback. C7 gives the `runtime` container a hook of its own, §2 and the staged `spec/04:489` text state that every container carries one, and a tier-3 assertion pins the handshake completing during termination. (Pass 11 corrects the mechanism: reusing `preStopDrainHook` there is impossible, because its `lenny-adapter prestop` command is absent from the third-party runtime image and `runPreStop` signals its own container's PID 1, which there is the runtime process. The `runtime` container gets a wait-only hook instead.)
- **The notification was staged "before `GracefulStop`", which is after the lifecycle channel is closed.** The SIGTERM handler runs `ShutdownDemoteSDK` and `lifecycle.Close()` before `srv.GracefulStop()` (`cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:396-401`), and `Close` fails every pending lifecycle request and rejects every later one with `errLifecycleClosed` (`pkg/adapter/lifecyclechannel.go:558-561`, `:605-614`), so the gateway-driven `Checkpoint` would reach a live gRPC server and a dead quiesce channel. C7 and the staged §4.6.1 text now put the RPC ahead of all three teardown steps, and the tier-3 assertion requires the lifecycle channel to still be open for a stream the gateway opens while the notification is in flight.
- **The resume precedence rule was stricter than the `spec/04:264` rule it cited, and named a selector that cannot evaluate it.** Keying the eviction-state arm on a full row whose `recovery_generation` is at or above the record's discards a durable workspace on the routine checkpoint-at-N / resume-to-N+1 / eviction-at-N+1 sequence, which `spec/04:264` ("If a previous successful full checkpoint exists") and `spec/04:287` ("resumable from an earlier snapshot") both forbid; and `SelectForResume` returns the newest restorable row in the total order, which may be a `partial = true` row, so it cannot answer a question about `partial = false` rows. Because C6 already makes the classification an output of the restore, the rule reduces to what the restore did: the eviction row wins only when the resume restored nothing, which is the snapshotless-rebuild path `SelectForResume`'s `ErrNotFound` selects. `HasEvictionState` keeps its boolean signature and the tier-1 assertion covers all three states the two rejected predicates would have failed.
- **`lenny_checkpoint_partial_total` is registered with one label and has no emitter, and its package was in no edit list.** `spec/16:195` declares `pool`, `recovered`, `manifest_reason`, and `trigger`; `pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_podlifecycle.go:112-117` registers `pool` alone, and `IncCheckpointPartial` (`gatewaymetrics.go:1247-1256`) has no caller. - **The `reason` label was reconciled in the wrong direction.** S1 deleted `reason="kms_unavailable"` from `spec/12:315` as a pre-existing defect, but the counter is registered with four labels including `reason` (`gatewaymetrics_sessionlifecycle.go:282-286`) and three call sites emit it, including the T4 fail-closed write rejection (`gatewaymetrics.go:616-631`, wired at `cmd/lenny-gateway/metricsbackfill.go:243-244`). `spec/12:315` describes shipped behavior and `spec/16:200` is the incomplete declaration, so the reconciliation is reversed: `spec/16:200` and its `docs/reference/metrics.md` mirror gain the `reason` label and `spec/12:315` keeps its identification. The substitute the proposal offered, `T4KmsKeyUnusable`, reads a different metric (the leader-elected probe gauge, `pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:368-372`).
