S1 and C2 now count the skipped per-session flush on `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total{trigger="eviction"}`, the counter S1 widens to cover every trigger; `spec/16:44` stays as written and the tier-1 assertion pins both arms.
- **`gateway.partialRecoveryThresholdFraction` already had a `docs/reference/configuration.md` row with a conflicting definition.** `:31` defines it over "expected manifest entries" and names `lenny_gateway_partial_recovery_below_threshold_total`, a metric that exists nowhere in the tree, so adding the knob as new would leave the page with two rows for one key. S2 rewrites the existing row on the §10.1 byte-fraction threshold and adds rows only for the two keys that have none.

S1 grouped it with three names that exist nowhere and deleted its runbook diagnosis step, which would have removed the only per-checkpoint size signal an operator has and redirected them to a per-tenant aggregate gauge that cannot answer the question. The step is kept and only its emitter framing changes; S1's and §11's "names nothing that exists" claim is narrowed to the two knobs that do not exist.
- **`docs/api/internal.md`'s `service RuntimeAdapter` block declared the unary `Checkpoint` a second time, outside every edited range.** `:89-90` would keep advertising `rpc Checkpoint(CheckpointRequest) returns (CheckpointResponse);` after the proposal deletes that message, on the one page whose stated audience is runtime adapter authors, and the page is authored rather than generated. It is added to P1's target list and to §10.

After this change the manifest row is the first durable act of every attempt and a stored partial is the source of the `partial_workspace` resume the same page's `:98` edit introduces. The tier-5 node-drain journey pins `gateway.replicas: 2`, and §10 records that `TestDecideTemplate_TerminationGraceFloor_spec_5_2_516` is rewritten to the new formula.

### Pass 11 (2026-07-14, automated)

- **The sidecar `runtime` container's preStop hook could not run, and would have killed the process it exists to hold open.** C7 reused `preStopDrainHook`, whose exec command is `["lenny-adapter", "prestop", …]` (`podspec.go:1229-1245`). A preStop `exec` handler runs inside its own container: the `runtime` container runs the third-party `Runtime.spec.image` (`podspec.go:236-237`), which carries no Lenny binary and mounts no volume holding one (`runtimeMounts`, `:509-518`), so the kubelet's exec fails with `FailedPreStopHook` and SIGTERMs the container at t=0. Even with the binary present, `runPreStop` signals and polls PID 1 of its own container (`cmd/lenny-adapter/prestop.go:12-16`, `:99-101`), which there is the runtime process, and it cannot see the adapter's PID because `shareProcessNamespace` is unset by design (`podspec.go:485-487`, `spec/04:243`, `spec/13:16-23`). Either way a Full-level sidecar session, the dominant third-party layout, drops to the minimal-state fallback on the one path the trigger exists for. C7 now gives the `runtime` container a **wait-only** hook: an initContainer stages the statically linked adapter binary (`Dockerfile:21`) into a `drain-bin` `emptyDir` the container mounts read-only, and the hook execs it as `prestop-wait`, which signals nothing and polls the adapter's existing listener on `127.0.0.1:50051` across the pod's shared network namespace until it stops accepting. §2, §3, the staged §4.6.1 text, §5, C7, §10, and the §6 assertions are restated on it, and §6 gains a tier-1 podspec bullet.
- **The `NotifyPodTerminating` handler could not take the coordination lease from a live coordinator.** C7 staged a "`coordination_lease` CAS", but the authoritative lease is the Redis TTL lease, whose `Acquire` returns `ErrHeld` against a different holder (`pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/leasestore.go:125-141`), and the Postgres `coordination_lease` table is a mirror with no compare-and-swap on its interface (`pkg/gateway/coordination/coordlease/coordlease.go:5-9`, `:61-84`). `spec/10:30` and `:178` describe acquiring an unheld or lapsed lease. A live coordinator renews continuously, so the handoff would have failed for roughly `(N − 1) / N` of terminating pods and every one of those slots would have taken the workspace-destroying minimal-state fallback with a healthy object store and a live pod, which is worse than the shipped behavior at every replica count above one. C7 adds `leasestore.Steal`, a Lua compare-and-set beside `Acquire`, called only by this handler; S3 states the §10.1 rule that permits a takeover on the termination path. The steal is safe on machinery the store and the spec already carry: `Renew` already returns `ErrNotHeld` when "another replica took over" (`leasestore.go:149-151`), `Release` is already a no-op for a non-holder (`:170-172`), and `CoordinatorFence` is already a hard precondition of every gateway→pod RPC (`spec/10:35`). The peer-forwarding RPC stays rejected, now on the ground that one Lua script on an existing store interface replaces a new RPC, an addressing scheme, and a NetworkPolicy edge.
- **The supersede predicate forbade exactly the supersede C7 depended on.** C7 claimed the generation bump admits the supersede of the previous coordinator's in-flight upload, while §2, §3, and S2 all stated a conjunction whose settled-row term (`manifest_reason IS NOT NULL OR checkpoint_timeout_at < now()`) has no generation escape, so a still-running row would abort the eviction attempt instead. The predicate now reads, identically in §2, §3, S2, C2, C7, and the §6 assertions: `coordination_generation < $new_generation OR (coordination_generation = $new_generation AND (manifest_reason IS NOT NULL OR checkpoint_timeout_at < now()))`. A strictly-newer coordinator supersedes a running row, which is safe because the §10.1 `CoordinatorFence` has already fenced that writer at the pod and its counter and finalising `UPDATE`s no-op under the `deleted_at IS NULL` guard; a same-generation racer, behind no fence, still waits and aborts. The §6 tier-4 assertion now drives a genuinely in-flight cross-replica upload through the handoff and pins both arms.
- **The grace-period floor was enforced only when the deployer set the field.** `decideTerminationBudget` guards its rejection with `spec.TerminationGracePeriodSeconds != nil` (`validator.go:644`), the CRD field is `+optional` with no default and no defaulting webhook, and the podspec falls back to a flat constant with no `maxConcurrentSessions` term (`podspec.go:75`, `:1193-1197`). A `maxConcurrentSessions: 2` pool that omits the field would be admitted with no rejection and no warning (its 310s floor sits under the 600s node-drain warning) and rendered with 180s of grace, and the kubelet would SIGKILL it part-way through the second slot's serialized eviction checkpoint. The hole is inert today only because `Checkpoint` returns `codes.Unimplemented`; C7 makes the agent pod's grace period load-bearing. The guard is dropped: the webhook compares the pool's **effective** grace period (the deployer's value, or the podspec default) against the floor. The default stays one constant rather than a formula the podspec evaluates, so the budget rule keeps a single evaluator.
- **`spec/16:132` restated the superseded webhook inequality and was in no edit list.** The §16.1 row for `lenny_pool_termination_budget_exceeded_total` inlines `max_tiered_checkpoint_cap + checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds + 30 > terminationGracePeriodSeconds` as its increment condition and labels its cross-reference "tiered cap + BarrierAck budget". S3 now restates both on the agent-pod floor, and the tier-11 budget-rule assertion enumerates the row.
- **`spec/04:625` justified the webhook's budget rule by SIGKILL during a gateway drain.** After S3 splits the two budgets, the rule bounds the agent pod's own termination and the gateway's 210s drain budget is not an admission rule at all. S3 rewords the §4.6.3 rationale onto the agent pod's own termination; the `checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds` floor rule stays as the second rule of the set.
- **`spec/06:67` fixed the adapter's SIGTERM sequence as beginning with `DemoteSDK` and sized the grace period against that sequence alone.** C7 puts a blocking gateway RPC ahead of it whose deadline can consume most of the grace period, so both the ordering and the `LENNY_DEMOTE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS + 5s` sufficiency claim become false. S3 restates the sequence with the signal as step 0, bounds the adapter's deadline so the demote-and-exit budget is reserved whatever the gateway does, and restates the grace-sizing sentence against the S3 floor; the mirrored comments in `podspec.go` and `sandboxtemplate_types.go` follow, and the §6 tier-3 assertion covers an `sdk_connecting` pod whose gateway is unreachable.
- **S1 misread `docs/reference/metrics.md:172`.** That paragraph scopes the `## Adapter metrics` section, whose table carries the two SO_PEERCRED counters and no checkpoint counter, so the "latent contradiction" did not exist and the staged edit would have stranded those two rows and declared the pod's adapter-emitted set to be a counter that lives in a different section. The section is recorded as confirmed unchanged, and the emitter and scrape-target statement lands in the `## Checkpoint metrics` section instead, mirroring the staged `spec/16:199` row.
- **The `disaster-recovery.md` edits were anchored on the wrong lines.** The WarmPoolController sentence is at `:279`, not `:245-252`, and `:106-107` is the data-loss impact table rather than the outage list. S1 now corrects the actor at `:279`, scopes `:251`'s unqualified "Eviction checkpoints fall back to Postgres minimal state" to the agent-pod eviction class, and records `:106-107`, `:249`, and `:281` as confirmed unchanged.
- **P1 staged a correction to SDK doc comments that do not exist.** None of the three runtime-SDK lifecycle files mentions a snapshot, an upload, or the artifact store; each describes the callback as quiescing runtime output. The SDK edit is dropped from P1 and from §10, and the three files are recorded as confirmed unchanged, which is what makes the tier-10 and tier-11 no-object-store-client assertions pass.
- **The `checkpoint_id` UUID restatement missed a third doc page.** `docs/runtime-author-guide/integration-levels.md:156`, `:165`, and `:170` carry the same `chk_42` lifecycle frames, and the page was in S1's target list only for the `checkpoint_complete` rewrite at `:145-175`, so the proposal's own tier-11 assertion ("no example anywhere in `schemas/examples/`, `docs/`, or `spec/` shows it in a non-UUID format") would have failed on it. The page is added to P1 and to §10; both edits land in the one `:145-175` pass.
- **The podspec changes had no test at their own tier and invalidated three shipped ones.** The podspec builder is pure in-process logic and already carries a tier-1 suite pinning the values C7 changes: `TestBuildDefaultsTerminationGraceTo120_spec_4_6_1` (`podspec_test.go:440`), `TestBuildIgnoresCeilingAboveDefault_spec_4_6_1` (`:469`), and `TestBuildAddsPreStopDrainHook_spec_4_6_1` (`:487`, `--timeout=110s`). §6 gains a tier-1 podspec bullet covering the 180s default, the 170s hook timeout, the runtime container's wait-only hook and its staged binary, and `LENNY_TERMINATION_GRACE_SECONDS`, which is the sole source of the `budget_ms` ceiling and was pinned at no tier; §10 names the three tests as rewrites.
- **The `budget_ms` overrun arm had no test.** §6 covered the MinIO-retry and failed-handoff fallback arms and the multi-slot case that fits, but nothing asserted what happens when the pod-declared budget elapses with slots unsettled, which is the reachable boundary on a serialized eight-slot loop and the one that decides whether the pod dies with an unfinalised row. A tier-4 bullet now pins the cancellation, the `partial = true, manifest_reason = 'timeout'` finalisation, the per-slot fallback records, and the return inside the declared margin.
- **`lenny_checkpoint_partial_total`'s resume-side `recovered = true` emission had no test.** C2 gives the counter two emission sites and §6 asserted only the finalisation side, so a counter whose `recovered` label is declared, registered, and never written would still pass the tier-11 label assertion. The tier-1 resume-classification bullet now asserts the `recovered="true"` increment on a reassembled partial and its absence on a full-checkpoint fall-through.

### Pass 12 (2026-07-14, automated)

- **The grace-period floor omitted the demote-and-exit reserve, which is serial with the gateway's checkpoint window.** Three claims consume the grace period in sequence: the kubelet's 10s drain margin after the adapter exits (`podspec.go:77-82`), the per-slot checkpoints plus the Postgres fallback, and the `LENNY_DEMOTE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS + 5s` the adapter spends demoting the SDK after `NotifyPodTerminating` returns (`podspec.go:84-93`). The staged floor summed only the first two and claimed the third was "subsumed", so a pool admitted exactly at its floor gave the gateway 10s less than the floor budgets, and the 10s came off the tail of the 60s Postgres fallback, which is the write separating a `conversation_only` resume from the §4.4 total-loss path. The floor gains the reserve as an additive term (`slots × (max(cap, 90) + 60) + preStopDrainMarginSeconds + sdkDemoteReserveSeconds`), which puts the `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` floor at 170s against the new 180s default (a 10s margin rather than the claimed 20s) and the 8-slot floor at 1220s. `budget_ms` is redefined in P1 as the deadline the adapter actually honours, with every serial claim subtracted before the value is declared, so the ceiling the gateway is handed is the wall clock it gets. The webhook cannot read `LENNY_DEMOTE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, so the staged `spec/06:67` text states that raising it above 5s requires raising the pool's `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` by the same amount.
- **The floor's multiplier was keyed on a field the webhook cannot read.** `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions` is not on the `SandboxTemplate` CRD (`sandboxtemplate_types.go:28-34` carries only `Recycle`), and `effectiveMaxConcurrent` (`validator.go:459-462`) collapses to 1 for every session-mode pool, which is the only class that runs the serialized per-slot eviction checkpoints the floor exists to budget (a service-mode pool "materializes no workspace", `spec/05:391`). A `maxConcurrentSessions: 8` pool would have been admitted at the 170s floor against a 1220s worst case. `SessionPolicy` gains `MaxConcurrentSessions`, which `spec/04:416` already requires the CRD to validate and which the PoolScalingController already reads off the poolstore row (`poolstoresource.go:125`) and can mirror into the `SandboxTemplate.spec` it already owns (`spec/04:592`), so the rule the spec states is the rule the webhook evaluates.
- **`validator.go:592-596` was cited as the BarrierAck-floor rule and declared unchanged; it is `decideTerminationBudget`'s doc comment stating the retired floor formula.** The BarrierAck-floor check is at `:628-634` (doc bullet `:609-612`) and is unchanged. The doc comment at `:591-612` states the superseded formula and the superseded "is set and below the floor" guard, both of which this change replaces, so C7's edit range starts at `:585` and the comment is restated with the code, which is what the tier-11 assertion on `decideTerminationBudget` requires.
- **The adapter could not checkpoint a slot.** `CheckpointRequest` carries no slot (`schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:1021-1024`), the handler gates on the pod-global session identity that the code documents as unreachable on a concurrent pod (`pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:149`, `pkg/adapter/session.go:234-236`), and `checkpointRoots()` archives the pod-global workspace root (`checkpoint.go:311-323`), so every `Checkpoint` against the `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pools C7's serialized per-slot loop is built for would fail with `FailedPrecondition`. `CheckpointRequest` gains `SlotId slot_id`, mirroring every other slot-scoped message, and C1 routes the handler through the existing `useSlot` / `checkSlotSession` / per-slot-root pattern.
- **The coordinator handoff could not fence a concurrent-session pod.** `CoordinatorFence` gates on the same pod-global identity (`pkg/adapter/coordination.go:72`), `CoordinatorFenceRequest` carries no slot, and the adapter's fence state is a single pod-global `lastFenced` requiring strictly increasing generations (`coordination.go:26-34`, `:82-89`) while `coordination_generation` is per session, so by C7's own failed-slot rule every slot of every terminating concurrent pod would have taken the `workspace_lost = true` fallback, which is what `leasestore.Steal` exists to prevent. The fence and the barrier gain `slot_id`, route through `checkSlotSession`, and hold their fence state per slot.
- **`Steal` was specified only on the Redis store, which is not the value production wires.** `LeaseStore` has three implementations with compile-time assertions (`leasestore.go:67`, `failover.go:39`, `pgstore/pgstore.go:50`), and `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1272` hands the handler a `*Failover`, so a Redis-only Lua `Steal` would not compile against the interface and would be unreachable through the wired value. It is also a §12.4 durable-fallback role (`spec/12:217`), so a Redis-only `Steal` would drop every terminating pod's sessions to the minimal-state record during a Redis outage. `Steal` is now specified on all three, the package doc's "a lease can never be stolen" invariant names the termination-path exception, and the §6 tier-4 assertion exercises the fallback arm with Redis unavailable.
- **The finalising `UPDATE` could strand a deadline-cut chunk that committed server-side.** The design lowers `chunk_count` to the committed count while the proposal itself establishes that a `Put` cut by the per-attempt deadline can leave the object committed with the driver never seeing the response. Such an object sits outside `[0, chunk_count)` for every manifest-derived sweep and has no `artifact_store` row either, because `cataloging.Store.Put` inserts the row only on success (`cataloging.go:143-171`) and `SoftDeleteSession` enumerates catalog rows (`:423-425`), so it is permanently orphaned on the design's common path. The driver now resolves the at-most-one unobserved index with a `Stat` on its derived key before it finalises, counting the chunk when it is present at the expected byte length and soft-deleting it otherwise. The rule is stated identically in §3, S1, the staged `spec/10:132` text, and C2, with a tier-1 assertion.
- **`lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_total` has no registration and no emitter.** It exists only as a name-and-type row in the metric catalog (`pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go:127`), which registers nothing, and `barrier.Coordinator`'s entire metric surface is `IncPreStopBarrierTargetSource` (`barrier.go:100-101`), so the barrier success rate the `spec/16:41` rationale rests on is uncomputable, the staged `spec/10:124` text pointed operators at a silent counter, and the §6 tier-7 assertion had no target. C2 registers the counter and its companion histogram in `gatewaymetrics_checkpoint.go`, widens `barrier.Metrics`, and emits both from `dispatchOne` on the ack, the timeout, and a transport error.
- **`spec/16:42` defined the ack-duration histogram as measuring checkpoint flushing, which the ack/flush decoupling falsifies.** The ack now returns on quiescence, strictly before the gateway opens the `Checkpoint` stream, so the interval contains no flush. The row is added to S1's target list and restated as the send-to-ack quiescence latency, with the flush attributed to `lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds{trigger="eviction"}`.
- **The `reason` value set for `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total` omitted the shipped rollup's four values, and the change's two new increment conditions had no legal value.** `IncArtifactUploadError` (`gatewaymetrics.go:764-782`, wired at `cmd/lenny-gateway/metricsbackfill.go:247-248`) rolls every failed `ArtifactStore` PUT into the counter with `minio_unreachable`, `auth`, `quota_exceeded`, or `other`, which is a fourth emitter the census missed and the dominant path once every chunk `Put` goes through that store. The degraded barrier dispatch and the zero-chunk fall-through are neither retry exhaustions nor KMS rejections, and the only pool/level/trigger-carrying helper hard-codes `retry_exhausted` (`gatewaymetrics.go:608-613`), so an operator paged during a Postgres failover would have read that MinIO exhausted its retries. The declared value set becomes the full nine (adding `manifest_write_failed` and `no_chunks_committed`), the helper gains a `reason` parameter, and the rollup increments are documented as carrying empty `pool`, `level`, and `trigger`.
- **Three of the five `resumeMode` doc mirrors omitted `workspaceRecoveryFraction`, which the proposal's own tier-11 gate requires on all five.** The field appears in no `docs/` file today. `streaming.md`, `lifecycle.md`, and `concepts.md` now carry it on the same terms as `wire-format.md`.
- **The staged `spec/07:138` restatement left no `resumeMode` arm for a session that restores nothing.** It defined `full` exclusively as a restore from a complete chunk set while the `disaster-recovery.md` edit said a session with no checkpoint resumes `full`, which is what the shipped classifier does (`start.go:3227`) and what C6 and C7 depend on. The restatement gains the §7.3 snapshotless-rebuild arm explicitly, and both sites are worded from one definition.
- **The new MinIO request-rate row's Tier-3 average-workspace figure was understated threefold.** `17 × ceil(100 MB / 16 MiB)` is `17 × 6 = ~102 PUT/s`, not ~34. The row now carries per-tier values derived from the same formula (~1.2, ~10, and ~102 PUT/s) alongside the ~544 PUT/s worst case at `workspaceSizeLimitBytes`.
- **The blast radius of the new floor and the 180s default reached tests and packages the proposal named nowhere.** Four admission tests break (two outside `pkg/admission/pool_config_validator`, one of which flips from admitted to rejected, in a package that appeared in no target list) and six podspec-default tests break (three beyond the three named, one of them in `pkg/controller/sandbox`). §6 gains a tier-2 admission bullet and a tier-2 line in its tier list, and §10 names every invalidated test and adds `pkg/admission/webhook` and `pkg/controller/sandbox/controller_test.go`.

### Pass 15 (2026-07-14, automated)

- **The generation gate on `CheckpointRequest` is an entry-time check and cannot stop a stream already in flight.** The whole cross-replica safety argument for `leasestore.Steal` and for the strictly-higher-generation supersede rested on a displaced coordinator's in-flight `Checkpoint` being rejected at the pod when the fence acks, which no staged change built. A gate runs once, at RPC entry, and the victim's stream is in flight precisely because it passed that gate before the fence advanced; the adapter's only in-flight-cancellation rule fires on a generation gap greater than 1 (`spec/10:36`) while the handoff CAS bumps by exactly one, and even that is documented as unimplemented (`pkg/adapter/coordination.go:62-64`). The victim would hold the pod-global op-lock (`checkpoint.go:178`, released by `defer` at `:190`) for its full 60-second deadline and the new coordinator's eviction `Checkpoint` would queue behind it in `opLock.Begin` or be coalesced into `codes.Aborted`. S3 now restates `spec/10:36` so cancellation is a property of every accepted fence, C1 builds it (the byte handlers register a per-slot cancel func; `CoordinatorFence` invokes every cancel func of a superseded generation before it records the new generation and acknowledges), `pkg/adapter/oplock.go` joins C1's target list, and the §6 tier-3 and tier-4 assertions drive the cancellation rather than assuming it.

S1, the staged `spec/10:132` text, C2, §3, and §6 now carry a third arm: an unresolved index keeps the reserved `chunk_count` and is not soft-deleted, so it stays reachable to every sweep. The staged edit would have dropped live bindings on the replica serving a healthy session: the session would stop being checkpointed by anyone, its pod and slot would leak (`PodExecutor.Release` is the only `Registry.Remove` caller and returns nil when the binding is absent, `pod.go:313-316`), and message dispatch, interrupt, upload-to-session, expiry warnings, and tree recovery would all lose the adapter. The unbind is removed and the sweeper is unchanged. The wasted work it was meant to save is bounded by the predicates the proposal already carries: a displaced replica's next attempt reads the post-CAS generation, meets the new coordinator's active row at equal generation with no `manifest_reason` and inside its deadline, and aborts on the settled-row term, while its `Checkpoint` RPC is rejected by the pod's entry gate. §2, C7, §6, and §10 are restated, and the §6 tier-4 case now asserts the arm drops no binding in either of the two states it fires in.
- **The `NotifyPodTerminating` handler had no adapter connection to the terminating pod and no seam that could produce one.** `CoordinatorFence` takes an `*adapterclient.Client` as a parameter (`coordfence.go:137`) and `Checkpointer.snapshot` reaches the pod through the registry binding's client (`checkpointer.go:237-241`), while the only client producers in the tree are `Binder.connect` and `Binder.reconnect`, both unexported and both resolving the Sandbox CR before dialing `status.podIP` through the composition root's `DialAdapter` closure (`binder.go:1096-1100`, `:1625-1631`, `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:1839`). The column the handler reads carries a Sandbox name rather than an address (`start.go:753`). Every slot on every terminating pod would therefore have fallen to the minimal-state fallback. C7 now adds one exported entry point on the existing producer, `podsession.Binder.AdoptForCheckpoint`, built on the same `resolveSandbox` and `DialAdapter` pair, holds the `*podsession.Binder` on the eviction-checkpoint component (the wiring struct already carries it at `wiring_fields.go:308`), and treats a pod it cannot resolve or dial as a failed slot. §6 asserts the dial and the failed-slot arm.
- **The handler bound the terminating pod into the receiving replica's registry and never removed it.** `Registry` entries are removed only by `PodExecutor.Release` (`pod.go:313-316`), which runs on the serving replica, so the binding would have named a destroyed pod forever and `Checkpointer.Sweep`, which iterates `Registry.Snapshot()` and reads no lease (`checkpointer.go:173-176`), would have committed an intent row per tick for a checkpoint that cannot succeed, holding an active same-generation row that aborts the session's genuine checkpoint on its replacement pod. The handler now removes every binding it created and closes every client it dialed in a `defer` on the way out, leaving a binding that already existed on the replica alone. §2, §3, C7, §6, and §10 carry the rule.
- **The Seal path can finalise `partial = true`, and no section said what `sealWorkspace` does with it.** `sealWorkspace` retries and escalates only on a non-nil error (`seal.go:91-95`) and `Checkpointer.Seal` is a thin wrapper over `snapshot` (`checkpointer.go:210-216`), so a truncated seal reported as success would release the pod with the session's previous checkpoint standing as its exported workspace, which `spec/07:112` forbids. C2 now states that `snapshot` reports the `partial = true` finalisation arm to its caller as an error, so the §7.1 bounded-backoff loop retries and, on window exhaustion, transitions the session to `failed` with reason `workspace_seal_timeout`; the row, its chunks, and its `lenny_checkpoint_partial_total` increment are retained regardless. §6 gains a tier-1 case pinning the arm.

- **The barrier-flush path opened one `Checkpoint` stream per session with an unbounded fan-out, against a pod-global operation lock.** A barrier `Target` is one session (`barrier.go:57-69`) and `MirrorTargetLister.Targets` stamps each with the session's `PodAddr` (`wiring.go:112-135`), so on a `maxConcurrentSessions: N` pool the draining replica holds N targets addressed at one pod, and `Coordinator.Dispatch` launches one goroutine per target (`barrier.go:159-178`). Driving the on-ack flush inside that fan-out opens N concurrent `Checkpoint` streams into one adapter, whose `ops opLock` is a single field on `Server` (`pkg/adapter/server.go:328`) admitting one running operation plus one queued and mapping the rest to `codes.Aborted` (`oplock.go:37-40`, `checkpoint.go:178-186`), so at `N ≥ 3` the drain-time flush loses `N − 2` slots to zero-chunk rows. It also contradicted the `spec/05:542` rule S1 stages and C7 obeys. - **The newly live `partial_workspace` fraction was never persisted, so the coordinator-handoff `session.resumed` still reported `workspaceRecoveryFraction: 1.0`.** `spec/07:138`, `spec/10:392`, and `spec/15:1421` all source the handoff frame's fraction from `session_checkpoint_meta`, whose only writer is the barrier ack path (`barrier.go:203`, `wiring.go:58-60`) and whose ack carries no fraction (`schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:1176-1180`), so the column is always NULL and `handoffResumedFrame` hard-codes `1.0` (`start.go:3264-3269`). That is inert only while the partial arm is unreachable; C4 and C6 make it live, and a session that recovered 40% of its workspace would be told on its next handoff that the workspace is intact. C6 now writes the restore driver's fraction to that column on every restore (the value on `partial_workspace`, NULL on `full`) through a narrow `SetWorkspaceRecoveryFraction` writer, since the shipped whole-row `Upsert` would clobber `barrier_id` and `checkpoint_ref`, and `handoffResumedFrame` reads the row, keeping `1.0` when none is recorded. The three spec statements stay unchanged and gain their first producer, and §6 asserts the carry-forward.
- **The service-mode floor arm inverted two shipped ceiling-breach tests that no ledger named.** `TestPoolConfigValidatorRejectsTerminationGraceCeilingBreach_spec_5_2_516` (`pkg/admission/webhook/pool_config_validator_test.go:215`) and the `"maxTerminationGracePeriodSeconds breach sets BudgetExceeded"` subtest of `TestDecideTemplate_BudgetExceededDiscriminator_spec_10_1_129` (`pkg/admission/pool_config_validator/validator_test.go:479-489`) both apply a service-mode `maxConcurrent: 8` template with `maxTerminationGracePeriodSeconds: 600`, whose shipped 840s floor breaches the ceiling. Under the 20s service-mode arm the templates are admitted and every rejection assertion inverts. - **C7's handoff gate was keyed on the coordination lease while the checkpoint it enables is keyed on the registry binding.** The two are held by uncorrelated replicas, which C7 establishes itself: nothing on the session-start path acquires the lease, the sweeper races `Acquire` on every non-terminal session of every tenant (`coordination.go:109`, `:133-137`), and the binding is written by whichever replica served start or resume (`start.go:2232`, `:3150`). In the state {replica A holds the lease, replica B holds the binding}, which is the majority of sessions at any replica count above one, the RPC landing on A skipped step 2, so A never dialed and never bound the pod and `Checkpointer.snapshot` failed at `Registry.Get` with `ErrNoBinding` (`checkpointer.go:236-239`), dropping every slot to the minimal-state fallback against a healthy object store and a live pod, which is what `leasestore.Steal` was added to prevent. The line is added to P1's docs target list and to §10, restated in the staged `spec/04:672` terms, and the §6 tier-11 parity assertion now compares the lock's scope across `spec/04:672`, the docs page, and the `ops opLock` declaration at `pkg/adapter/server.go:328`.
- **The §4.4 manual-recovery key logging had no assertion, so the committed-key list could ship permanently empty.** `evictionfallback.Writer` derives the `spec/04:280` WARN log and its `keys_committed` bucket entirely from caller-supplied fields (`evictionfallback.go:602-621`), and C7 is its first producer, so a handler that passed a nil key slice would compile, write the row, publish `session.lost`, pass every listed assertion, and defeat the only in-band record of which chunks survived the outage. §6's tier-4 total-loss case now asserts the handler passes the failed attempt's derived committed keys into `WriteParams`, that the log carries the S2 chunk names with the row's `chunk_encoding` and `generation`, that `lenny_checkpoint_eviction_partial_keys_logged_total{keys_committed="1+"}` increments, that an attempt committing no chunk emits the empty-list mirror at `keys_committed="0"`, and that `had_prior_checkpoint` is set from the session's durable-checkpoint state.
- **`lenny_checkpoint_barrier_ack_duration_seconds` gains its first emitter and a redefined interval, and nothing pinned that the observation stops at the ack.** The histogram has no registration and no emitter in the tree (it appears only as a name-and-type row at `pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go:128`), so C2 creates its behaviour from scratch while S1 moves the interval off checkpoint flushing (`spec/16:42`) and onto pod quiescence. An implementation that observed at flush completion would compile, satisfy the tier-11 registered-and-emitted check, and silently reinstate the retired semantics, falsifying the staged `spec/16:42` text and the `PDBBlockedEvictions` clause (b) diagnosis (`spec/16:456`) that depends on the two histograms carrying disjoint intervals. §6's tier-7 case now asserts the histogram observes the send-to-ack interval alone on the pod that acked quickly but whose flush the deadline cut, that `lenny_checkpoint_duration_seconds{trigger="eviction"}` carries the flush, and that one observation is recorded per barrier target including the `outcome="timeout"` pod.

### Pass 19 (2026-07-14, automated)

- **The generation fence could not exclude the displaced replica's *next* attempt, so the eviction checkpoint lost the supersede race to it.** `coordination_generation` is a property of the session row rather than of a replica: a displaced coordinator's next periodic tick re-reads the post-CAS value in the transaction where it would commit its intent row, and the pod's entry gate admits it, because the gate rejects only a generation **below** the slot's `lastFenced` (the shipped barrier gate is an equality check, `pkg/adapter/coordination.go:157`). C7 ran the handoff for every session first and only then checkpointed the slots serially, each under a tier cap of up to 90s plus a 60s fallback, so on a `maxConcurrentSessions: 8` pod the binding-holding replica had minutes in which to fire a periodic tick (`Checkpointer.Sweep` iterates the process-local registry and consults no lease, `checkpointer.go:173-176`, `:236-239`, and nothing told it the pod was terminating). Its intent row is an active same-generation row with no `manifest_reason` inside its deadline, which is exactly the row the supersede predicate refuses to displace, so the handler's own attempt aborted and the slot took the `workspace_lost = true` minimal-state fallback against a healthy object store and a live pod, and its accepted `Checkpoint` stream held the adapter's pod-global op-lock against the next slot. The handler now **reserves every slot at handoff time**, committing the reservation in the same transaction as the `spec/10:33` CAS, and step 3 only executes the pre-minted attempts, which is the two-phase reserve-then-execute the barrier path already uses (C2). The committed row is what excludes the peer, at equal generation, through the settled-row term. The reserved row's `checkpoint_timeout_at` is the pod-declared `budget_ms` deadline rather than the slot's tier cap, so a slot reserved now and executed several minutes later is not readable as abandoned by a peer's supersede or by a §12.5 backstop cycle; S2 and C4 carry the fourth value of that column. The false claims that the entry gate rejects the displaced coordinator's later requests are removed from §2, §3, and C7, the staged `spec/04:489` and `spec/10:30-37` text carries the reservation rule, and §6's tier-4 case asserts a tick fired after the CAS aborts at its own reservation rather than aborting the eviction checkpoint.
- **The binding held by the replica that served the session survived the pod C7 destroys, with no reclaimer.** `Registry.Remove` has exactly one caller in the tree, `PodExecutor.Release` (`pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:313`), which runs on the session's teardown and only on the replica holding the binding. An evicted session stays non-terminal and is resumed onto a replacement pod by whichever replica serves the resume, which puts a new binding in **its** registry (`start.go:3150`) and leaves the old one naming a destroyed pod. C7 asserted that such a binding was owned by `PodExecutor.Release` and left it alone, while arguing two sentences earlier that a binding naming a destroyed pod is destructive under this change: each `Checkpointer.Sweep` tick would reserve a checkpoint, commit an intent row, and hold an active same-generation row that the supersede predicate refuses to displace, aborting the session's genuine checkpoint on its replacement pod. C2's reservation now carries a stale-binding check on the read it already makes: the transaction that reads the session row for `coordination_generation` also reads `pod_assignment` and compares it against the binding's `SandboxName` (`podsession.BindResult`, `binder.go:487-488`), dropping the binding through `Registry.Remove` and returning `ErrNoBinding` before it mints, supersedes, or INSERTs; and the abort path drops the binding when the `Checkpoint` RPC fails because the pod is gone, which covers the window before the resume in which `pod_assignment` still names the destroyed Sandbox. The C7 handler additionally removes **every** binding for the terminating pod from its own registry rather than only those it created. §6's tier-4 case asserts no replica reserves a further checkpoint for a session whose pod is gone.
- **`WORKSPACE_SNAPSHOT_MISSING` was carried on a field the §15.1 session envelope does not serialize.** C2 routed the terminal absent-chunks arm onto `sessionstore.Session.FailureReason` and cited `sessionstore.go:771-773` as its serialization, which is `FailureHistory.FailureReason` inside the §8.9 delegation-tree `NodeAttributes` projection rather than the session envelope. `SessionResponse` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:2021-2032`) declares `failureClass` and no reason field, `toResponse` (`:3141-3172`) copies only the class, and `failureClassForReason` degrades every reason outside the closed §7.1 catalogue to `runtime_failure` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:393-411`), so the code would have appeared in no client-visible body, `spec/25:4095` would have been unmet, and the un-skipped tier-1 test that reads the string out of the `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` body could not pass. C2 now adds `failureReason` to the envelope and to `toResponse` and stages its parallel representations: the `Session` schema in `pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.json`, `spec/15:645`, and the three client SDK session types, which the proposal previously declared unedited. A new `failureClass` enum value was rejected: the enum is closed and `spec/07:110` makes adding a class a coordinated change across four sections, and the same paragraph already distinguishes the coarse class from the specific reason string, which is what this code is.
- **The newly fail-closed grace-period rule had no docs surface.** `docs/reference/configuration.md` documents each pool field's "default value, description, and validation rules" (`:11`), carries no `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` row in either its Pool configuration table (`:70-79`) or its sessionPolicy table (`:87-105`), and states `acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation: true` as the complete admission requirement for `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` (`:87`). After the dropped `!= nil` guard a pool satisfying everything that page states is rejected with `422 INVALID_POOL_CONFIGURATION`, and the field the deployer must raise appears nowhere on it. `docs/api/admin.md`'s pool create body (`:252-268`) likewise carries no such field while C7 gives the admin API the request field. Both pages are added to C7's target list and to §10, with the two-arm floor stated as the new row's validation rule.
- **C4 re-keys the manifest store and C2 deletes `PartialChunkDeleter.DeleteByPrefix`, and three shipped test files that drive those surfaces were in no ledger.** `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/partialmanifeststore/partialmanifeststore_test.go` is the store package's own unit suite, keyed on `(tenant_id, session_id, generation)` (`:22-31`) and pinning the pre-change resume selector (`:96`); `pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/partialcleanup_test.go` declares a `stubChunkDeleter` implementing the deleted `DeleteByPrefix` (`:15-22`), asserts deletion by prefix (`:71-72`), and pins the nil-deleter skip that is T-4.4.16 itself (`:146`); and `cmd/lenny-gateway/main_test.go` drives `hardPrunePartialManifests` against generation-keyed rows (`:657-703`). Each takes its package's build down on application, which is the defect class pass 13 already closed for the tier-2 stores tests. All three are added to C4's and C2's target lists, to §10's rewrite ledger, and to the §6 store and cleaner assertions, and §10's exhaustiveness claim now covers them.
- **The supersede counter's only assertion was a negative one that passes vacuously, and no staged change gave it an emitter.** `IncCheckpointPartialManifestsSuperseded` (`pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics.go:408-412`) has no caller anywhere in the tree, so the assertion that it does not increment on a clean drain would hold even if the supersede step never touched it, and this change moves the counter's increment condition onto the hot path of every checkpoint attempt of every trigger. C2's `gatewaymetrics` ledger now names the emitter (one increment per soft-deleted `partial = true` row, and zero on the two arms where the predicate aborts the reservation instead), and §6 gains the positive assertion beside the negative one.
- **`spec/10:155` carried a sixth statement of the §12.5 backstop's narrow row-selection predicate, which S2's enumeration missed.** Its generation-stale-cleanup sentence is verbatim the one at `spec/10:175` that S2 does widen, so applying the edits as staged would have left five widened statements and one narrow one, which is the state S2 says must not exist and the state its own tier-11 assertion is written to fail on. The sentence is added to S2's backstop block, reworded onto the widened predicate and onto the total order S2 makes the selector, and `spec/10:155` joins the tier-11 enumeration.
- **`spec/12:353` declared `lenny_gc_tombstones_pruned_total`'s `table` label values a second time, and only `spec/16:197`'s copy was staged for the rename.** The hard-prune paragraph S2 already edits closes with "labeled by `table: artifact_store|partial_manifest`", so the applied spec would have declared one counter's value set two ways and named a table that no longer exists. The sentence is folded into S2's `spec/12:353` edit, and the tier-11 label-parity assertion now compares the value set across `spec/16:197`, `spec/12:353`, and the Go registration.

## 9. Open decisions for review

No open decision remains. The decisions this proposal previously carried are resolved and recorded in §2 (Decisions) and §5 (Non-goals):

- **The retention catalog stays separate from `checkpoint_manifest`.** `session_checkpoints` remains the retention and rotation catalog. Folding it would touch the §12.5 GC rules and the `artifact_store` tombstone sweep for a consolidation nothing in this change needs.
- **The agent-pod eviction trigger ships**, driven by a termination signal from the agent pod's existing preStop hook over a new `GatewayControl.NotifyPodTerminating` RPC (S3, P1, C7). A gateway-side pod informer is rejected: it needs a per-replica ownership and dedup design with no existing informer machinery to build on, and it races the kubelet's grace period rather than holding the pod open. Its RBAC cost is zero, because the gateway `ClusterRole` already carries `pods: get, list, watch, patch` cluster-wide (`charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:176-178`), so that ground is dropped from the record. The §4.4 eviction-fallback writer, the `spec/04:264` resume precedence rule, and the `session_eviction_state` supersede all ship with the trigger (C7).
- **`manifest_reason` stays as a single-valued column** (`timeout`), for forward compatibility and because the supersede transaction's settled-row term keys on it.

the `trigger` label rescoped to `tier_cap | barrier_ack_timeout | periodic`, and the increment condition restated as once per manifest row finalised `partial = true`). `:43` (the preStop cap-selection counter) is confirmed unchanged, and so is the increment rule of `:44`: the degraded per-session barrier dispatch is counted on `lenny_checkpoint_storage_failure_total{trigger="eviction"}` rather than on the per-fan-out target-source counter (S1). The only edit to `:44` is the label-set correction stated above, and it lands on the spec row alone. The rule holds verbatim over the chunk-set model, where a missing snapshot is a manifest row whose derived chunk objects are absent, and the shipped skipped test that asserts it is un-skipped and rewritten onto the driver (§6).
